It smashed, dashed and rolled sonorously along the mighty wall, suddenly fainting into an unseen bay, then rolling forth again into the open, passing on like subdued thunder; from the beginning scattering wild echoes, which in turn reechoed criss-wise and cross-wise, an immense maze of vibrations, now passing slowly in decrescendo into a far away rumble and nearby trembling, fainting, dying, as the forest sternly regained its own, and primal stillness came. This display was too dramatic, even for Louis. Once was enough. It seemed too much like an eerie protest, the wildly passionate rejoinder of a living forest disturbed in its primal solitudes of contemplation. Yet the stupendous rhythm, the orchestral beauty of it all, sank deep in Louis’s soul, now become as one with nature’s mood. He wandered from the camp, wishing to be alone, where he might be himself, solitary, in nature’s deep, and commune with venerable immensities that gave forth a voice of haunting stillness which seemed to murmur and at times to chant of an unseen, age-long, immanent, eternal power, which Louis coupled as one with a gentle, sensuous, alluring power to whose moving song of enchantment he had trembled in response, within a bygone springtime in the open.

===Chinese===
路易斯手持火枪，走到湖岸的边缘，在离树木之墙不远的地方停下。在他眼里，这湖长约三英里，宽约四分之三英里。他举起枪，朝正前方放了一响。霎时间响起一片惊人的轰鸣。它在那道巍峨的墙上撞碎、迸溅、铿然滚过，忽然隐入一处看不见的湖湾而微弱下去，随即又滚滚涌回空阔处，像压低了嗓音的雷声一路远去；从头起便撒出狂野的回声，回声又纵横交错地再回响，汇成一团浩大的震颤之阵，此刻在渐弱中缓缓化作远方的隆隆与近旁的颤动，渐弱、渐息，直到森林严峻地收复了它自己的领地，太初的寂静降临。这番景象太富于戏剧性了，即便对路易斯而言也是。一次就够了。它太像一声阴森的抗议，像一片活着的森林在它太初孤寂的沉思中受了搅扰，发出的狂烈而激愤的答辩。然而那惊心动魄的节律、那一切的管弦之美，深深沉入了路易斯的灵魂——他此刻已与自然的心境融为一体。他离开营地信步走去，想独自待着，在那里他可以做他自己，孑然一身，置身自然的深处，与那些令人敬畏的浩大存在交流——它们发出一种萦怀不去的寂静之声，仿佛在低语，又时时像在吟诵一种看不见的、亘古的、内在的、永恒的力量；路易斯把这力量与另一种温柔的、感性的、魅惑的力量合而为一——对后者的动人的魔歌，他曾在一个逝去的春天的旷野里战栗着应和过。

===Original===
The brief camp-life was much the usual thing. Game was scarce, but small speckled trout could be scooped up in quantity from a slow, deep rivulet in a nearby beaver meadow.

Came time to return. The trapper said he could lead them back by an easier way, but it meant a detour of thirty miles to Lyons Falls. They made the distance in three days. They had been away ten days all told; and Louis was exultant that he had made as good a showing as the farm-boy twins.

===Chinese===
短暂的营地生活大体是寻常光景。猎物稀少，但附近一块河狸草甸里，一条流缓水深的小溪（rivulet）中，成群的小斑点鳟鱼可以一捧捧地舀上来。

返程的时候到了。捕兽人说他能领他们走一条好走些的道回去，但那意味着要绕行三十英里才能回到莱昂斯福尔斯。他们三天走完了这段路。前后总共去了十天；路易斯扬眉吐气：他表现得跟那对农家双胞胎一样出色。

===Original===
All too soon came the hour to begin the journey homeward. Goodbyes were said—some of them wistful.

At Albany, Grandpa revealed a plan he had cherished in secret: They were to take the day-boat down the Hudson to New York. Louis was profuse in gratitude as he prefigured coming wonders which he was to see with his very own eyes, and appraise with his own sensibilities. And so it was, as Louis passed almost directly from the sublimity of forest solitudes to the grandeur of the lower Hudson. As they passed West Point, Grandpa said that he had once taught French at the United States Military Academy, and that his pleasure there had been to swim the Hudson, across and back every morning before breakfast. Grandpa’s stock immediately jumped many points, for Louis held prowess in high honor. As they passed the Palisades Louis was astounded as Grandpa explained their nature—huge basalt crystals standing on end. The life on the river all the way down had greatly entertained him; now he came in sight of greater shipping and entered an immense floating activity.

===Chinese===
启程回家的时刻来得太快。道别的话说过了——其中有几句依依不舍。

在奥尔巴尼（Albany），外祖父透露了一个他暗自怀藏已久的计划：他们要乘日航船沿哈得孙河（Hudson）顺流而下，去纽约。路易斯感激不尽，一边预想那些他即将亲眼目睹、并用自己的感官来品鉴的奇观。事情也果真如此，路易斯几乎是径直从森林幽寂的崇高，转入了哈得孙河下游的壮丽。船过西点（West Point）时，外祖父说起他曾在美国军事学院（United States Military Academy）教过法文，他在那里的乐趣，是每天早饭前横渡哈得孙河，游一个来回。外祖父的股票行情当即大涨了许多点，因为路易斯向来崇敬膂力过人的好汉。船过帕利塞兹（Palisades）时，听外祖父讲解它们的本性——一根根直立的巨大玄武岩晶体——路易斯惊得目瞪口呆。一路顺流而下的河上生活让他大饱眼福；此刻他望见更为浩大的船运，驶入了一片浩瀚的水上繁忙。

===Original===
Of New York Louis saw but little; and when Grandpa said it was here they landed when he with his family came from Geneva, Louis took the information deafly, not even inquiring when and why they had moved to Boston. Grandpa felt the hurt of this indifference. Here was this boy, his own cherished grandson, whose fourteen living years had been filled to overflow with vivid episodes, with active thoughts, with dreams, mysteries, prophetic intuitions and rude industrious practicalities, all commingled; here was this boy, ignorant, grossly innocent and careless of the vicissitudes and follies of a seething human world. He shuddered momentarily at the chasm that lay between them. For Grandpa all too well knew the profound significance of a wholly truthful story of any human life, told continuously, without a break, from cradle to old age, could it be known and recorded of any other than one’s self. He knew that the key to the mystery of human destiny and fate lay wrapped and lost within these lived but unrecorded stories. He knew also that Louis was now paying in ignorance the penalty of a sheltered life. Then he told Louis another secret: They were to leave on a Fall River boat and traverse the length of Long Island Sound. Thus, Louis, in renewed joy and ecstasy made his first long trip on the salted sea. Then duly came Boston, Wakefield and the romantic journey’s end.

===Chinese===
纽约，路易斯所见无几；当外祖父说，当年他携全家从日内瓦（Geneva）来美，就是在纽约上的岸，路易斯对这讯息充耳不闻，甚至没有问一句他们究竟是何时、又为何迁往波士顿。外祖父为这份冷漠感到刺痛。眼前是这孩子，他珍爱的外孙，十四年的生命已被鲜活的际遇、活跃的思绪、梦想、谜团、预言式的直觉和粗拙而勤勉的实务填得满满溢出，一切交织混杂；眼前是这孩子，懵懂无知，天真得近乎愚钝，对沸腾人世的沧桑与愚行漠不关心。他为横亘在两人之间的鸿沟打了个寒噤。因为外祖父深知，一个人的生命，若能被旁人知晓并记录，从摇篮到迟暮原原本本、一气呵成地讲述出来，那样一个全然真实的故事蕴含着何等深远的意义。他知道，人类命运与际遇之谜的钥匙，就包裹着、失落在这一个个活过却未被记录的故事里。他也知道，路易斯此刻正以无知偿付着受庇护生活的代价。随后他告诉路易斯另一个秘密：他们将乘一艘法尔里弗（Fall River）航线的船走，纵贯长岛海峡（Long Island Sound）。就这样，路易斯在重焕的欢欣与狂喜中，完成了他生平第一次在咸海上的长途航行。而后如期而至的，是波士顿、韦克菲尔德（Wakefield），和这段罗曼蒂克旅程的终点。

---

===Original===
Louis still had time to brush up rapidly for the high school examinations. He had chosen the English High rather than the Latin High. He was accustomed to thinking and acting for himself, seldom asking advice. His thoughts in mass were directed ever toward his chosen career; and he believed that the study of Latin would be a waste of time for him; the time element was present always as a concomitant of his ambitions. He wished always to advance in the shortest time compatible with sure results. He had no objection to Latin as such, but believed its study suitable only to those who might have use for it in afterlife. He had a keen gift for separating out what he deemed essential for himself.

===Chinese===
路易斯还有时间抓紧温书，备战中学的入学考试。他选了英语中学，而不是拉丁中学。他习惯于自己思考、自己行事，很少征询别人的意见。他的思绪在总体上永远指向他选定的志业；他相信，学拉丁文对他来说纯属浪费时间；时间的因素，始终作为他种种抱负的伴生物而存在。他永远希望在确保成果的前提下，用最短的时间求得进展。他对拉丁文本身并无异议，只是相信它的研习只适合那些日后或许用得上它的人。他有一种敏锐的天赋，善于把对自己而言最要紧的东西分辨出来。

===Original===
On September third, his birthday, he received a letter from Utica, filled with delicate sentiments, encouraging phrases, and concluding with an assurance that the writer would be with him in spirit through his high school days.

The English and Latin High Schools, in those days, were housed in a single building, rather old and dingy, on the south side of Bedford Street; a partition wall separating them, a single roof covering them. The street front was of granite, the side walls of brick. There were brick-paved yards for the recess half-hour with overflow to the street and a nearby bakery. It was a barn-like, repellant structure fronting on a lane as narrow as the prevailing New England mind of its day.

===Chinese===
九月三日，他的生日那天，他收到一封从尤蒂卡（Utica）寄来的信，满纸细腻的情意、鼓励的话语，结尾处向他保证：写信人在他整个中学岁月里，将在精神上与他同在。

那个年月，英语中学和拉丁中学同挤在一栋楼里，相当陈旧而灰暗，坐落在贝德福德街（Bedford Street）南侧；一堵隔墙把它们分开，一片屋顶把它们罩住。临街的正面是花岗岩的，两侧的墙是砖的。有几方砖铺的院子，供半小时的课间休息用，人多了便溢到街上和附近一家面包房里去。这是一栋谷仓般面目可憎的建筑，面朝一条窄巷——窄得一如当年风行的新英格兰头脑。

===Original===
Louis passed the examinations and his name was entered in the year book 1870–71.

He was among those—about forty in all—assigned to a room on the second floor, presided over by a “master” named Moses Woolson. This room was dingy rather than gloomy. The individual desks were in rows facing north, the light came from windows in the west and south walls. The master’s platform and desk were at the west wall; on the opposite wall was a long blackboard. The entrance door was at the north, and in the southwest corner were two large glass-paneled cabinets, one containing a collection of minerals, the other carefully prepared specimens of wood from all parts of the world.

===Chinese===
路易斯通过了考试，他的名字登上了 1870–71 学年的名册。

他被分到二楼一间教室，同班的约有四十人；教室由一位名叫摩西·伍尔森（Moses Woolson）的「教习」（master）主持。这间屋子与其说阴森，不如说灰暗。一张张单人课桌排成行，面朝北，光线来自西墙和南墙的窗户。教习的讲台和讲桌设在西墙；对面的墙上是一块长长的黑板。进门在北面，西南角立着两座大玻璃柜，一座陈放着一批矿物标本，另一座是精心制备的世界各地木材标本。

===Original===
The new class was assembled and seated by a monitor, while the master sat at his desk picking his right ear. Louis felt as one entering upon a new adventure, the outcome of which he could not forecast, but surmised would be momentous.

Seated at last, Louis glanced at the master, whose appearance and makeup suggested, in a measure, a farmer of the hardy, spare, weather-beaten, penurious, successful type—apparently a man of forty or under. When silence had settled over the mob, the master rose and began an harangue to his raw recruits; indeed he plunged into it without a word of welcome. He was a man above medium height, very scant beard, shocky hair, his movements were panther-like, his features, in action, were set as with authority and pugnacity, like those of a first mate taking on a fresh crew.

===Chinese===
新生班级由一名班长（monitor）领着集合就座，教习则坐在讲桌后面抠着他的右耳。路易斯觉得自己像正踏上一场新的冒险，其结局他无从预料，却估摸着必定关系重大。

终于坐定，路易斯打量了教习一眼：此人的相貌和体格，多少显出一种吃苦耐劳、精瘦干枯、风吹日晒、吝啬而发迹的农夫类型——看年纪四十岁上下。待喧哗的人群静了下来，教习站起身，对着他这批生瓜蛋子新兵开始了训话；的确，他连一句欢迎的话都没有，径直就开了火。他中等偏高的身材，胡须极稀，一头乱蓬蓬的头发，动作如黑豹般轻捷；五官一旦活动起来，便凝着威严与好斗的神色，像一位接收新水手的大副。

===Original===
He was tense, and did not swagger—a man of passion. He said, in substance: “Boys, you don’t know me, but you soon will. The discipline here will be rigid. You have come here to learn and I’ll see that you do. I will not only do my share but I will make you do yours. You are here under my care; no other man shall interfere with you. I rule here—I am master here—as you will soon discover. You are here as wards in my charge; I accept that charge as sacred; I accept the responsibility involved as a high, exacting duty I owe to myself and equally to you. I will give to you all that I have; you shall give to me all that you have. But mark you: The first rule of discipline shall be **silence**. Not a desktop shall be raised, not a book touched, no shuffling of feet, no whispering, no sloppy movements, no rustling. I do not use the rod, I believe it the instrument of barbarous minds and weak wills, but I will shake the daylight out of any boy who transgresses, after one warning. The second rule shall be **strict attention**: You are here to *learn*, to *think*, to *concentrate* on the matter in hand, to hold your minds steady. The third rule shall cover **alertness**. You shall be awake all the time—body and brain; you shall cultivate promptness, speed, nimbleness, dexterity of mind. The fourth rule: You shall learn to **listen**; to *listen* in *silence* with the *whole* mind, not part of it; to listen with your *whole heart*, not part of it, for sound listening is a basis for sound thinking; sympathetic listening is a basis for sympathetic, worthwhile thinking; accurate listening is a basis of accurate thinking. Finally you are to learn to **observe**, to **reflect**, to **discriminate**. But this subject is of such high importance, so much above your present understanding, that I will not comment upon it now; it is not to be approached without due preparation. I shall not start you with a jerk, but tighten the lines bit by bit until I have you firmly in hand at the most spirited pace you can go.” As he said this last saying, a dangerous smile went back and forth over his grim set face. As to the rest, he outlined the curriculum and his plan of procedure for the coming school year. He stressed matters of hygiene; and stated that a raised hand would always have attention. Lessons were then marked off in the various books—all were to be “home lessons”—and the class was dismissed for the day.

===Chinese===
他浑身紧绷，却不虚张声势——一个有血性的人。他的话大意是：「小子们，你们还不认识我，但很快就认识了。这里的纪律将是铁一般的。你们是来学习的，我会盯着的。我不但要做好我这一份，还要叫你们做好你们那一份。你们在我的照管之下；任何别人不得插手。这里是我说了算——我是这里的主宰——你们很快就会明白。你们是我监护下的子弟；我把这份监护当作神圣之事来接受；我把由此而来的责任，当作一桩我对自己、也同样对你们欠下的崇高而苛严的本分来接受。我会把我的一切都给你们；你们也须把你们的一切都给我。但你们给我记住：纪律的第一条是**沉默**。不许掀起一块课桌盖，不许碰一本书，不许蹭脚，不许交头接耳，不许有懒散的动作，不许窸窣作响。我不动戒尺，我认为那是野蛮头脑和软弱意志的器具；但谁若犯了规矩，警告一次之后，我会把他打得天昏地暗。第二条是**严格专心**：你们到这里来是来*学习*、来*思考*、来*专注*于眼前的事务的，要把你们的心智稳住。第三条管的是**机警**。你们要时时醒着——身子醒着，脑子也醒着；你们要培养敏捷、迅速、轻捷与心智的灵巧。第四条：你们要学会**倾听**；在*沉默*中用*整个*心智去*倾听*，而不是只用一部分；用你们的*整个心*去倾听，而不是只用一部分——因为健全的倾听是健全思考的根基；富于同情的倾听是富于同情的、有价值的思考的根基；准确的倾听是准确思考的根基。最后，你们要学会**观察**，学会**沉思**，学会**辨别**。但这个题目事关重大，远在你们目前的理解之上，我现在不作讲解；没有应有的准备，不得轻率涉足。我不会猛地一拽就让你们起步，而要一点一点收紧缰绳，直到你们以自己最奋发的步速，被我稳稳握在手中。」说到最后这一句时，一抹危险的微笑在他严峻紧绷的脸上来回掠了一掠。至于其余，他概述了课程表和来学年的施教方案。他强调了卫生诸事；并申明，举起的手永远会得到照应。随后各科功课在书上一一划定——全部是「家庭功课」——当天的课就此解散。

===Original===
Louis was amazed, thunderstruck, dumbfounded, overjoyed! He had caught and weighed every word as it fell from the lips of the master; to each thrilling word he had vibrated in open-eyed, amazed response. He knew now that through the years his thoughts, his emotions, his dreams, his feelings, his romances, his visions, had been formless and chaotic; now in this man’s utterances, they were voiced in explosive condensation, in a flash they became defined, living, real. A pathway had been shown him, a wholly novel plan revealed that he grasped as a banner in his hand, as homeward bound he cried within: *At last a Man!*

Louis felt the hour of freedom was at hand. He saw, with inward glowing, that true freedom could come only through discipline of power, and he translated the master’s word of discipline into its true intent: *Self Discipline of self power.* His eager life was to condense now in a focusing of powers: What had the words meant;—“silence,” “attention,” “promptness,” “speed,” “accurate,” “observe,” “reflect,” “discriminate,” but powers of his own, obscurely mingled, uncoordinated, and, thus far, vain to create? Now, in the master’s plan, which he saw as a ground plan, he beheld that for which, in the darkness of broad daylight, he had yearned so desperately in vain; that for which, as it were with empty, outstretched hands, he had grasped, vaguely groping; as one seeing through a film, that for which he had hungered with an aching heart as empty as his hands. He had not known, surely, what it was he wished to find, but when the master breathed the words that Louis felt to be inspired: “You are here as wards in my charge; I accept that charge as sacred; I accept the responsibility involved as a high exacting duty I owe to myself and equally to you. I will give to you all that I have, you shall give me all that you have,”—a veil was parted, as it were by magic, and behold! there stood forth not alone a man but a **teacher** of the young.

===Chinese===
路易斯惊愕了、震住了、目瞪口呆了、狂喜不禁！教习唇边落下的每一个字，他都接住并掂量过；每一个令人震颤的字眼，他都睁着眼睛、惊异地应声共鸣。他现在明白了：这些年来，他的思想、他的情感、他的梦、他的感触、他的罗曼史、他的幻象，一直无形而混沌；如今在这人的言辞里，它们被以爆发的浓缩一一道出，霎时间变得清晰、鲜活、真实。一条路径已向他指明，一套全然新颖的方略已向他揭开，他把它像一面旗帜一样攥在手里，在回家的路上，他在心里呼喊：*终于有了一个大丈夫！*

路易斯感到自由的时刻近在手边。他怀着内心的灼热看清了：真正的自由只能经由对力量的训导而来；他把教习所说的纪律，译解成它的真义：*对自身力量的自我训导。*他热切的生命此刻要凝聚成一次力量的聚焦：那些字眼——「沉默」「专心」「敏捷」「速度」「准确」「观察」「沉思」「辨别」——除了是他自己的种种力量、晦暗地混杂着、失于协调、至今徒然无从创造之外，还能是什么？如今，在教习的方案里——在他眼中那是一张总平面图——他望见了他在光天化日的黑暗中苦苦渴求而不得的东西；望见了那仿佛用一双伸出的空手曾经抓握、茫然摸索的东西；像一个隔着一层薄膜看物的人，望见了那曾令他以一颗与手同样空空的、作痛的心去饥渴以求的东西。他固然并不知道他想找的究竟是什么，可当教习吐出那些路易斯觉得如受神启的字句：「你们是我监护下的子弟；我把这份监护当作神圣之事来接受；我把由此而来的责任，当作一桩我对自己、也同样对你们欠下的崇高而苛严的本分来接受。我会把我的一切都给你们；你们也须把你们的一切都给我」——一层帷幕仿佛被魔法分开，看哪！站在那里的不止是一个人，而是一位教诲青年的**教师**。

===Original===
On board the train for Wakefield, Louis took account of himself; he viewed the long, loop-like journey he had but recently completed, still fresh and free in memory’s hold. He had gathered in, as though he had flung and drawn a huge lassoo, the Berkshires, the Mohawk and its valley, Little Falls, the Black River, the Moose River, the primeval forest, and the Falls, the Hudson, the Catskills, the Palisades, New York Harbor, Long Island Sound; he had voyaged by rail, by river, and by sea. All these things, these acts, with their inspiring thoughts and emotions and reveries he had drawn into himself and shaped as one single imposing drama, ushering in a new and greater life. Or, in a sense reversed, his “child-domain,” holding, within the encircling woods, his ravine, his rivulet, his dam, his lovely marsh, his great green field, his tall, beauteous, slender elm; land of his delight, paradise of his earth-love, sequestered temple of his nature-worship, sanctuary of his visions and his dreams, had seemed at first, and hopefully, to extend itself progressively into a larger world as far as Newburyport and Boston, there, however, to stop, to remain fixed and bound up for seven long years, held as by a sinister unseen dam, the larger, urgently growing Louis, held also back within it, impatient, repressed, confined, dreaming of power, storing up ambition, searching for what lies behind the face of things, agitated and at times morose, malignant. When, of a sudden, the dam gives way, the child-domain so far enlarged, rushes forth, spreading over the earth, carrying with it the invisible living presence of Louis’s ardent soul, pouring its power of giving and receiving far and wide over land and sea, encompassing mountains and broad valleys, great rivers, turbulent waterfalls, a solemn boundless forest enfolding a lustrous lake, and again a noble river mountain-banked, an amazing harbor, and the great salt waves of the sea itself.

===Chinese===
在开往韦克菲尔德的火车上，路易斯盘点了自己；他回望那段刚刚走完的、环带般绵长的旅程，它在他的记忆里依然新鲜而自由。他像抛出又收拢了一条巨大的套索，把伯克希尔山（Berkshires）、莫霍克河（Mohawk）及其河谷、利特尔福尔斯（Little Falls）、黑河（Black River）、穆斯河、原始森林和那道瀑布、哈得孙河、卡茨基尔山（Catskills）、帕利塞兹、纽约港、长岛海峡尽收怀中；他乘过火车，行过河，航过海。这一切事物、这一切行动，连同它们激发的思绪、情感与遐想，都被他吸纳入自身，塑成了一出单一而恢弘的戏剧，引领他走进一段新的、更宏大的生活。或者，把意义倒过来说：他的「童年领地（child-domain）」——环抱的树林里容着他的沟谷、他的小溪、他的水坝、他可爱的沼泽、他绿色的大草地、他颀长美丽的榆树；他的欢愉之乡，他的大地之爱的乐园，他礼拜自然的幽僻神殿，他的幻象与梦的圣所（sanctuary）——起初曾满怀希望地渐次扩展，一直扩到纽伯里波特和波士顿那样大的世界；然而到了那里便止步了，被禁锢、被捆绑了整整七年，像被一道不祥的无形水坝拦住；那个日渐长大的、急迫生长的路易斯，也被一并困在其中，焦躁，压抑，局促，梦想着力量，积攒着抱负，探求着事物面目背后的东西，激动不安，时而阴郁，时而恶狠狠。突然之间，水坝溃决了，已然扩展至此的童年领地奔涌而出，漫布大地，携着路易斯炽热灵魂那看不见的活的临在，把它给予与领受的力量远远地泼洒到陆地与海洋之上，环抱了群山与宽谷、大河与奔泻的瀑布、一座围拥着明净湖泊的庄严无边的森林，又环抱了一条群山夹岸的壮丽大河、一座令人惊叹的港湾，和大海自身那伟大的咸浪。

===Original===
Thus were the boundaries extended; thus were the power and splendor of Mother Earth revealed in part; thus was provided deep and sound foundation for the masterful free spirit, striding in power, in the open, as the genius of the race of purblind, groping, striving, ever hoping, ever dreaming, illusioned mankind.

And thus it seemed to Louis that he was becoming stronger and surer of himself. Reverting to the words of the master, he dared affirm that this very power was within him, as a ward in his charge; that he must accept that charge as sacred; that he must accept the responsibility involved as a high exacting duty he owed to himself and equally to it; that he must give to it his all, to insure that it might give to him its all.

===Chinese===
疆界就这样扩展开了；大地母亲的力量与壮丽就这样部分地显豁了；就这样，为一颗在旷野中挟着力量阔步前行的、自主而自由的心灵，打下了深厚而坚实的根基——它正是那半盲的、摸索的、奋进的、永远怀着希望、永远做梦、执于幻象的人类种族的天才。

于是路易斯觉得自己正在变得更强、更笃定。回到教习的那些话上，他敢于断言：这股力量就在他自己身上，是他监护下的子弟；他必须把这份监护当作神圣之事来接受；必须把由此而来的责任，当作一桩他对自己、也同样对它欠下的崇高而苛严的本分来接受；他必须把自己的全部给它，以确保它可能把它的全部给他。

===Original===
And Louis now saw clearly and in wonder, that a whim of his Grandpa, not the Rice Grammar School, had prepared him to meet Moses Woolson on fair terms. With confident assurance he awaited the beginning of what he foresaw was to be a long and arduous disciplinary training, which he knew he needed, and now welcomed.

===Chinese===
而路易斯此刻又惊又明地看清：是外祖父的一时兴起，而不是赖斯文法学校，使他得以旗鼓相当地迎接摩西·伍尔森。他怀着笃定的信心，等待着一场他预见将是漫长而艰苦的训导的开始——他知道自己需要它，如今更对它表示欢迎。

===Original===
That evening he told Grandpa what he thought of Moses Woolson and his plan; and Grandpa, with inward seeing eyes, smiled indulgence at his grandson seated on his knee, one hand about his neck, as he mused aloud: “My dear child, allowing for the rosy mist of romance through which an adolescent like yourself sees all things glorified, I will say that in the whole wide world it is true there may be found a few such men as you portray; but as a venerable and prudent Grandpa I shall reserve the right to wait awhile that we may see how the ideal and the real agree. But you go at it just the same, regardless of what may be passing in the back of my bald head.” And Louis laughed, and kissed and hugged his Grandpa, and settled to his lessons, as grandma knitted by the student lamp, as uncle Julius thrummed away on a helpless guitar and sang the melancholy sentimental ditties of the day, and as Grandpa, in slippers, gazed with incredulity at a boy on the floor oblivious of them all.

===Chinese===
当晚，他把他对摩西·伍尔森及其方略的看法讲给外祖父听；外祖父慧眼内视，对着坐在他膝上、一手搂着他脖子的外孙，宽容地微笑着，朗声自语道：「我亲爱的孩子，姑且扣除你这样一位少年透过它看什么都熠熠生辉的那层玫瑰色的罗曼雾气，我可以说，茫茫世界上确实找得出几个你描绘的这种人；但作为一位德高望重而审慎的外祖父，我要保留等一等的权利，好看看理想与实际究竟合不合拍。不过你还是照旧放手去干，别管我这颗秃脑瓜的背后转着什么念头。」路易斯笑了，亲吻外祖父、搂住外祖父，然后安顿下来做功课；外祖母在学生灯（student lamp）旁织着毛线，舅舅朱利乌斯（Julius）在一把任其摆布的吉他上砰砰乱弹，唱着当日的伤感小曲，外祖父趿着拖鞋，难以置信地望着地板上一个把他们全都忘在脑后的男孩。

===Original===
As it has but little import in this story, we shall pass over the breaking-in period of Moses Woolson’s class, and begin an exposition of Moses Woolson’s plan and method, and Louis’s responses thereto at that period the master himself had forecast as “when I have you firmly in hand at the most spirited pace you can go.” Suffice it to say that with great skill in intensive training he had brought them to this point within three months.

The ground work of his plan was set forth in his opening address, and is now to be revealed in its workings in detail.

===Chinese===
由于这件事在本故事里无关宏旨，摩西·伍尔森班上的磨合期我们略过不表，径直开始阐述摩西·伍尔森的方案与方法，以及路易斯在教习本人所预告的那个阶段的响应——那个阶段，他称之为「直到你们以自己最奋发的步速，被我稳稳握在手中」。只需说一句：凭着强化训练的高超手段，他在三个月之内就把他们带到了这一步。

他的方案的基架已在他的开学训话中阐明了，如今要揭示的是它实际运转的细节。

===Original===
The studies on which Louis set the highest value were Algebra, Geometry, English Literature, Botany, Mineralogy and French language. All these subjects were to him revelations. Algebra had startled him; for, through its portal he entered an unsuspected world of symbols. To him the symbol × flashed at once as a key to the unknown but ascertainable. Standing alone, he viewed this × in surprise as a mystic spirit in a land of enchantment, opening vistas so deep he could not see the end, and his vivid imagination saw at once that this ×, expanded in its latent power, might prove the key to turn a lock in a door within a wall which shut out the truth he was seeking—the truth which might dissolve for him the mystery that lay behind appearances. For this ×, he saw, was manipulated by means of things unknown.

===Chinese===
路易斯最看重的功课是代数、几何、英国文学、植物学、矿物学和法语。这些科目对他无一不是启示。代数让他吃了一惊；因为穿过它的大门，他进入了一个始料未及的符号世界。在他眼里，符号 × 旋即闪现为一枚钥匙，通向未知却可知的领域。它独自立在那里，他惊异地把这个 × 视为一方魔境里的神秘精灵，展开的远景深得望不见尽头；而他鲜活的想象力立刻看出，这个 ×，把它的潜在力量展开之后，很可能正是那枚钥匙，能转动一堵墙上一扇门内的锁——正是那堵墙，把他寻求的真理隔绝在外；那真理，或许能为他消解隐藏在现象背后的谜。因为他看出，这个 ×，是用一些未知之物来运算的。

===Original===
Thus he saw far ahead; looking toward the time when he would be mature. Geometry delighted him because of its nicety, its exactitude of relationships, its weird surprises—all like fairy tales, fairy tales which could be proved, and then you said: Q.E.D. He began to see what was meant by a theorem, a postulate, a problem, and that *proof* was a reasoned process based on certain facts or assertions. It was well for him, at the time, that he did not perceive the Euclidian *rigidity*, in the sense that he had noted the fluency of Algebra. As to Botany, had he not always seen trees and shrubs and vines and flowers of the field, the orchard and the garden?

Now he was learning their true story, their most secret intimacies, and the organization of their world. He loved them all the more for this. Mineralogy was new and revealing, the common stones had begun as it were, to talk to him in their own words. Concerning French he was ardent, for he had France in view. English literature opened to him the great world of words, of ordered speech, the marvelous vehicle whereby were conveyed every human thought and feeling from mind to mind, from heart to heart, from soul to soul, from imagination to imagination, from thought to thought; and to his ever widening view, it soon arose before him as a vast treasure house wherein was stored, in huge accumulation, a record of the thoughts, the deeds, the hopes, the joys, the sorrows, and the triumphs of mankind.

===Chinese===
就这样他望向了极远的远方；望向他终将成熟的那一天。几何使他欣喜，因为它精微，因为它的关系的精确，因为它的离奇的惊喜——全都像童话，却是可以证明的童话，证完了你便说：证毕（Q.E.D.）。他开始明白定理、公设、命题各是什么意思，明白*证明*乃是基于某些事实或断言的一个推理过程。他当时未曾察觉欧几里得式的*刚性*，反倒是一桩幸事——在代数那里，他注意到的可是流畅。至于植物学——田野、果园和花园里的树木、灌丛、藤蔓和花草，他不是一向都看在眼里吗？

如今他学的是它们的真实故事、它们最隐秘的私语、它们那个世界的组织构造。他因此愈发爱它们了。矿物学是崭新的、发人深省的，寻常的石块已仿佛开始用自己的语言对他说话。至于法语，他学得火热，因为他心里装着法兰西。英国文学为他打开了词语的伟大世界、井然有序的语言的世界，那辆奇妙的载具——每一缕人类的思想与情感，都由它从心智运往心智，从心运往心，从灵魂运往灵魂，从想象运往想象，从思想运往思想；在他不断拓宽的视野里，它很快又在他面前耸立起一座巨大的宝库，其中浩浩堆积着的，是人类的思想、功业、希望、欢乐、悲伤与凯旋的记录。

===Original===
Moses Woolson was not a deep thinker, nor was Moses Woolson erudite or scholarly, or polished in manners, or sedate. Rather was he a blend of wild man and of poet. But of a surety he had the art of teaching at his fingertips and his plan of procedure was scientific to a degree, so far beyond the pedagogic attainments of his day that he stood unique, and was cordially hated by his craft as lambs might fear and hate a wolf. Today men would speak of such a man as a “human dynamo,” a man ninety-nine percent “efficient.” His one weakness was a temper he all too often let escape him, but his high strung, nervous makeup may be averred in part extenuation, for this very makeup was the source of his accomplishment and power: He surely gave in abundance, with overflowing hands, all that he had of the best to give.

His plan of procedure was simple in idea, and therefore possible of high elaboration in the steady course of its unfolding into action and results. For convenience it may be divided into three daily phases seemingly consecutive, but really interblended; first came severe memory drill, particularly in geometry, algebra, French grammar and in exact English; this work first done at home, and tested out next day in the school room. Second, (first, next day) a period of recitation in which memory discipline and every aspect of alertness were carried at high tension. At the end of this period came the customary half-hour recess for fresh air and easing up. After recess came nature study with open book. Chief among them Gray’s *School and Field Book of Botany*—Louis’s playground; then came a closing lecture by the Master.

===Chinese===
摩西·伍尔森不是深刻的思想家，也不博学，不学者气，谈吐不文雅，举止不沉稳。他毋宁说是野人与诗人的混合体。但可以断言，教学的艺术他烂熟于指掌，他的施教方案在科学性上远远超出他那个时代的教学成就，以至卓然独立，被他的同行们衷心地憎恨——像羔羊惧恨一匹恶狼那样。放在今天，人们会说这样的人是一台「人力发电机」，一个「效率」高达百分之九十九的人。他唯一的弱点是一副常常失控的脾气，但他那高度紧绷、神经质的禀质，或可引作几分开脱之词，因为恰恰是这副禀质，正是他的成就与力量的源泉：他确乎以丰沛之手、源源不断地，把他最好的一切倾囊给出。

他的施教方案立意简单，因而在它稳步展开为行动与成果的进程中，大有高度推衍的余地。为方便起见，可以把它分作每日三个阶段，看似先后相承，实则互相渗透：第一阶段是严格的记诵操练，尤其在几何、代数、法语语法和精确的英语上；这功课先在家里做，次日在教室里检验。第二阶段（次日的第一阶段）是一节背诵课，记诵的纪律与机警的每一个侧面都在其中高度绷紧地运行。这一阶段结束时是惯例的半小时课间休息，透透空气，松一松。休息之后是开卷的自然研习，其中首推格雷（Gray）的《校园与野外植物学手册》（*School and Field Book of Botany*）——那是路易斯的游乐场；最后由教习作一场收尾的讲授。

===Original===
Thus it may be said, there was a period of high tension, followed by a period of reduced tension, and this in turn by a closing period of semi or complete relaxation, as the master reeled off in easy, entertaining talk, one of his delightful lectures. It was in the nature studies, and in these closing lectures, particularly those in which he dwelt upon the great out-of-doors, and upon the glories of English literature, that the deep enthusiasms of the man’s nature came forth undisguised and unrestrained, rising often to the heights of impassioned eloquence, and beauteous awakening imagery. These lectures, or rather, informal talks covered a wide range of subjects, most of them lying beyond the boundaries of the school curriculum.

Thus, in a sense, Moses Woolson’s school room partook of the nature of a university—quite impressively so when Professor Asa Gray of Harvard came occasionally to talk botany to the boys. He did this out of regard for Moses Woolson’s love of the science. The unfailing peroration of these lectures—every one of them, was an exhortation in favor of “Women’s Rights,” as the movement was called at the time; for Moses Woolson was a sincere and ardent champion of womankind. On this topic he spoke in true nobility of spirit.

===Chinese===
因此可以说，先是一段高度紧张的时段，继之以一段张力稍减的时段，再继之以一段半松弛或全然松弛的收尾时段——教习以轻松有趣的家常话，滔滔讲出他一场场令人愉快的讲授。正是在自然研习里，在这些收尾的讲授里——尤其在他细谈伟大的户外、细谈英国文学的光荣的那些时刻——此人天性深处的热忱毫无掩饰、毫无节制地涌了出来，常常升至热烈雄辩的高度与美好而唤醒人心的意象。这些讲授，或者说非正式的谈话，涉及的题目极广，其中大半都在学校课程的边界之外。

因此，在某种意义上，摩西·伍尔森的教室带着一点大学的性质——哈佛的阿萨·格雷（Asa Gray）教授偶尔来给男孩们讲植物学时，这一点尤为令人印象深刻。他这样做，是出于对摩西·伍尔森热爱这门科学的敬重。这些讲授——每一场都不曾例外——结尾总有一段雷打不动的宏论，是为「女权」（Women’s Rights）张目的鼓吹——这个运动当时便叫这个名字；因为摩西·伍尔森是妇女的一位真挚而热烈的捍卫者。在这个题目上，他言谈间透着真正高贵的精神。

===Original===
But the talks that gripped Louis the hardest were those on English literature. Here the master was completely at his ease. Here, indeed, he revelled, as it were, in the careful analysis and lucid exposition of every phase of his subject, copious in quotation, delightfully critical in taking apart a passage, a single line, explaining the value of each word in respect of action, rhythm, color, quality, texture, fitness, then putting these elements together in a renewed recital of the passage which now became a living moving utterance. Impartial in judgment, fertile in illustration and expedient, clear in statement, he opened to view a new world, a new land of enchantment.

===Chinese===
但把路易斯攥得最紧的，还是那些关于英国文学的谈话。在这个领域，教习浑身自在。的确，他简直纵情其中：对题目的每一个侧面都作细密的分析与透亮的阐说，征引浩繁，拆解一段文章、单独一行时妙趣横生地评骘，讲解每个字眼在动作、节奏、色彩、品质、肌理、贴切诸方面的价值，然后把这些元素重新拼合，再把那段文章重新诵读一遍——它于是变成了一句活生生的、流动的言语。裁断不偏不倚，例证与权变层出不穷，陈述清清楚楚，他为人打开了一个新世界，一片新的魔境。

===Original===
One day, to Louis’s amazement, he announced that the best existing history of English literature was written by a Frenchman, one Hyppolite Taine by name. This phenomenon he explained by stating that the fine French mind possessed a quality and power of detachment unknown to the English; that Monsieur Taine further possessed that spiritual aspect of sympathy, that vision, which enabled him to view, to enter freely and to comprehend a work of art regardless yet regardful of its origin in time or place; and he rounded an antithesis of French and English culture in such wise as to arouse Louis’s keenest attention, for the word “culture,” had hitherto possessed no significance for him; it was merely a word! Now his thoughts, his whole being floated o’er the sea to distant France, whereupon he arose from his seat and asked Moses Woolson what culture really meant, and was told it signified the genius of a people, of a race. And what was meant by the *genius* of a people? It signified their innate qualities and powers of heart and mind; that therefore their culture was their own expression of their inmost selves, as individuals, as a people, as a race. Louis was magnificently bewildered by this high concentration. He seemed to be in a flood of light which hid everything from view; he made some sheepish rejoinder, whereupon Moses Woolson saw his own mistake.

===Chinese===
有一天，他宣布现存最好的英国文学史出自一个法国人之手，此人名叫伊波利特·泰纳（Hyppolite Taine）——路易斯闻之愕然。他解释这一现象说，精细的法兰西头脑具有一种英国人无从知晓的超脱的品质与力量；泰纳先生更拥有那种精神层面的同情、那种眼界，使他能够观看、自由地进入并理解一件艺术品，既不论其时空渊源，又顾及其时空渊源；他还把法兰西文化与英格兰文化作成了一组工整的对偶，恰好激起路易斯最敏锐的注意——因为「文化」这个词，此前对他不具备任何意义；它只是一个词罢了！此刻他的思绪、他的整个存在漂洋过海，浮向遥远的法兰西，于是他从座位上站起来，问摩西·伍尔森文化究竟是什么意思，得到的回答是：它意味着一个民族、一个种族的天才（genius）。那么一个民族的*天才*又是什么意思？那意味着他们心灵与头脑先天固有的品质与力量；因此，他们的文化便是他们最内在的自我作为个人、作为民族、作为种族的自我表现。路易斯被这一高度的浓缩弄得壮观地迷乱了。他仿佛置身一片光的洪流，而洪流把一切都遮蔽不见；他讪讪地回了一句什么，摩西·伍尔森这才发觉自己的失误。

===Original===
He came down from his high perch to which he had climbed unwittingly, for it was dead against his theory and practice to talk above the heads of his boys. He thereupon diluted the prior statement with a simply worded illustration, and Louis was glad to find his own feet still on the ground. Then Louis put the two aspects of the statement side by side again, and “culture” became for him a living word—a sheer veil through which, at first, he could but dimly see; but living word and sheer living veil had come from without to abide with him. It seemed indeed as though Moses Woolson had passed on to him a wand of enchantment which he must learn to use to unveil the face of things. Thus Louis dreamed.

===Chinese===
他从方才不知不觉爬上去的高栖上降了下来，因为讲得高出自己男孩们头顶的话，是与他全部的理论与实践格格不入的。于是他用一个措辞浅白的例释把先前那番话稀释了一番，路易斯高兴地发现自己的双脚仍站在地上。随后路易斯把那番话的两个侧面重新并置在一起，「文化」对他便成了一个活着的词——一层轻薄的面纱，起初他只能透过它隐约地看；但这活着的词、这轻薄的活纱，已经从外界来到他这里住下了。真仿佛摩西·伍尔森把一根点化的魔杖传到了他手里，而他还得学着用它去揭开事物的面目。路易斯就这样做着梦。

===Original===
By the end of the school year Moses Woolson through genius as a teacher, had turned a crudely promising boy into, so to speak, a mental athlete. He had brought order out of disorder, definition out of what was vague, superb alertness out of mere boyish ardor; had nurtured and concentrated all that was best in the boy; had made him consciously courageous and independent; had focused his powers of thought, feeling and action; had confirmed Louis’s love of the great out of doors, as a source of inspiration; and had climaxed all by parting a great veil which opened to the view of this same boy, the wonderland of Poetry.

Thus with great skill he made of Louis a compacted personality, ready to act on his own initiative, in an intelligent purposeful way. Louis had the same capacity to absorb, and to value discipline, that Moses Woolson had to impart it, and Louis was not a brilliant or showy scholar. He stood well up in his class and that was enough. His purpose was not to give out, but to receive, to acquire. He was adept in the art of listening and was therefore rather silent of mood. His object was to get every ounce of treasure out of Moses Woolson. And yet for Moses Woolson, the master and the man, he felt neither love nor affection, and it is quite likely that the master felt much the same toward him. What he felt toward the man was a vast admiration, he felt the power and the vigor of his intense and prodigal personality. It is scarcely likely that the master really knew, to the full extent, what he was doing for this boy, but Louis knew it; and there came gradually over him a cumulative reciprocity which, at the end, when he had fully realized the nature of the gift, burst forth into a sense of obligation and of gratitude so heartfelt, so profound, that it has remained with him in constancy throughout the years. There may have been teachers and teachers, but for Louis Sullivan there was and could be only one. And now, in all too feeble utterance he pleads this token, remembrance, to the memory of that **one** long since passed on.

===Chinese===
学年结束时，摩西·伍尔森凭着身为教师的天才，已把一个璞玉浑金的男孩，变成了一位——不妨这么说——心智的运动员。他从紊乱中带出秩序，从含混中带出分明，从单纯的少年热忱中带出超拔的机警；他滋养并凝聚了男孩身上一切最好的东西；使他自觉地勇敢与独立；聚焦了他思考、感受与行动的力量；坐实了路易斯对伟大户外的爱——视之为灵感的源泉；而压轴的一笔，是分开一道巨大的帷幕，向这同一个男孩敞开了诗的奇境。

就这样，他以高超的手段把路易斯锤炼成一个凝实的性格，随时能够凭自己的主动，以聪明而有目的的方式行动。路易斯吸纳的容量、器重纪律的程度，恰如摩西·伍尔森传授的容量与程度；而路易斯并不是一个光芒四射、锋芒外露的学生。他在班里名次靠前，这就够了。他的目的不是输出，而是领受，是获取。他娴于倾听的艺术，因而心绪颇为沉静。他的目标是把摩西·伍尔森身上的宝物一盎司不剩地全数汲取。然而对摩西·伍尔森——对这位教习、这个人——他既不觉得爱，也不觉得亲昵；而教习对他的感觉，多半也相差无几。他对这个人怀有的是浩大的仰慕，他感觉得到他那炽烈而挥霍的个性的力量与活力。教习多半并不真正知道，他从头到尾为这男孩所做的一切意味着什么，可路易斯知道；于是一种日渐累积的回馈渐渐涌上他心头，到最后，当他充分认识到这份馈赠的性质时，它迸发为一股义务感与感激之情，那样由衷，那样深刻，以至经年累月始终不渝地留驻在他身上。天下的教师或许有过一位又一位，但对路易斯·沙利文来说，有过、也可能只有一位。而此刻，他以这过分孱弱的言语，献上这一枚凭证、这一份纪念，献给那位早已逝去的**唯一**。

---

===Original===
Meanwhile a cloud no bigger than a man’s hand arose into the clear blue above the horizon of Henri List’s placid life. Early in 1871 Anna List, his wife, his prop, his anchor, his life’s mainstay, was taken with her first and last illness. Louis was forbidden her room. All was quiet; furtive comings and goings; whispered anxious words. The cloud arose, darkened the world and passed on. One morning, it was told that he, Louis, might see her. He went directly to her room, opened the door, and entered. The white shades were down and all was light within. On the bed he saw extended an object fully covered by a sheet. He advanced, drew aside the sheet, rashly pressed his lips upon the cold forehead, drew back as though stung.

===Chinese===
与此同时，一朵不过手掌大小的云，升起到亨利·李斯特（Henri List）平静生活的地平线上方的晴空里。1871 年初，安娜·李斯特（Anna List）——他的妻子、他的支柱、他的锚、他人生的主要依靠——患上了她此生第一场也是最后一场病。路易斯被禁止进入她的房间。一切都静悄悄的；鬼祟的来来去去；压低嗓音的忧虑话语。云升起来了，把世界染暗，又过去了。一天早晨，有人告诉他，他，路易斯，可以去看她了。他径直走到她的房门前，推开门，走进去。白色的遮帘都放了下来，屋里一片通明。床上他看见平放着一个被床单完全覆盖的东西。他走上前，掀开床单，贸然把嘴唇贴上那冰冷的额头，随即像被蜇了一下似的缩了回来。

===Original===
Standing erect he gazed steadfastly down upon rigid features that seemed of unearthly ivory.

Grandmamma had vanished!

What signified this cold menace he now scanned? This stranger in the house—whence Grandmamma had gone forever?

===Chinese===
他直挺挺地站着，目不转睛地俯视那僵硬的容貌——它仿佛是非人间的象牙。

外祖母不见了！

他此刻端详的这具冰冷的威吓，是什么意思？这个屋里的陌生人——外祖母是从这里永远地去了何处？

===Original===
What meant this effigy, this ivory simulacrum that had come here in her stead?

*It could not see, it could not hear, it could not feel, it could not move, it could not speak, it could not love!*

Grandmamma had vanished!

===Chinese===
这尊偶像、这具顶替她来到此处的象牙拟像，又是什么意思？

*它不能看，它不能听，它不能感觉，它不能动，它不能说话，它不能爱！*

外祖母不见了！

===Original===
She had passed on with a great cloud that had cast its shadow.

And here, now before him lay a counterfeit, where once she was.

An object, a nothing, a something and a nothing, which Louis could not think or name; an ivory mask which repelled, which instantly he rejected, as a ghastly intrusion.

===Chinese===
她已随着那朵投下过阴影的巨云逝去了。

而这里，此刻躺在他面前的，是一件赝品，在她曾经所在的地方。

一件东西，一个虚无，一个某物又兼一个虚无，路易斯既想不清也叫不出；一副象牙的面具，令他憎厌，他当即拒斥了它，当作一场阴森的闯入。

===Original===
And they had said that he would see his Grandmamma!

Ah! then, was this petrified illusion his Grandmamma?

They lied!

===Chinese===
他们还说，他将见到他的外祖母！

啊！这么说，这个石化的幻象就是他的外祖母？

他们撒谎！

===Original===
His true Grandmamma was in his heart and would remain there till his own end should come. Whatever this object before him might be, it was not Gran’ma!

His Grandmamma had vanished!

He replaced the shroud. Dry-eyed, and as one filled with a cold light, he left the room.

===Chinese===
他真正的外祖母在他心里，并将留在那里，直到他自己的末日来临。无论他面前的这件东西是什么，它都不是外祖母！

他的外祖母不见了！

他把裹尸布放了回去。眼睛干干的，像一个盛满了冷光的人，他离开了房间。

===Original===
Never before had Louis seen what Death, the cloud no bigger than a man’s hand, leaves behind it as it passes overhead and vanishes.

===Chinese===
路易斯从未见过死亡——那朵不过手掌大小的云——从头顶掠过并消逝之后，留下的东西。

---

===Original===
An upright white marble slab, in the cemetery, at the point of the promontory that juts into Lake Quannapowitt, says to the stranger wandering therein, that **Anna**, wife of Henri List died 2 April, 1871, aged sixty-six years.

===Chinese===
墓园里，在探入夸纳波威特湖（Lake Quannapowitt）的岬尖上，一块直立的白色大理石碑对在其间漫游的陌生人说：亨利·李斯特之妻**安娜**，卒于 1871 年 4 月 2 日，享年六十六岁。

===Original===
In this laconic statement the cynic hand of Henri List is clearly seen, even as at the funeral service in the “spare room” he was prostrate in an overwhelming flood of hysteria and tears, even as Louis stood by, gazing at him in wonder that a strong man could be so weak; even as Louis, cold and harshly irritated by the Baptist minister, whose sensuous words in praise of human bloodshed, he cursed. Driven to desperation by the whining quartet, he rushed into the open, sat under a tree and damned them all to perdition. Why had he been dragged into this gross orgy of grief? Could he not be left alone and in peace, to revere in memory that grandmamma who still lived on within his heart? The others with their noisy and their mercenary grief would soon forget. He, never. As thus he raged, a peach tree in full bloom in the garden caught his eye. He hastened to it as to a friend, in dire need. Its joyous presence in the garden gave him courage, for spring again was singing her great song. The air was vocal in a choir of resurrection. Here indeed was resurrection and the life. It seemed to him not in the least incongruous that his beloved had vanished into that great life whence she came—whence he had come; and that as Life was within him, so was his beloved within him as life within a life to be treasured evermore.

===Chinese===
这简短刻薄的碑文里，亨利·李斯特那只冷嘲的手清晰可见；正如在「客房」里举行的葬礼上，他瘫倒在一阵铺天盖地的歇斯底里与泪水的洪流里；正如路易斯站在一旁，惊异地望着他，惊异一个强壮的男人竟能软弱至此；也正如路易斯，冷漠而尖利地被那位浸信会牧师激怒——牧师那些称颂人类流血的肉感言辞，他咒骂了。他被那呜呜咽咽的四重唱逼到走投无路，冲到屋外，坐在一棵树下，咒他们统统堕入毁灭。为什么要把他拖进这场粗鄙的哀恸狂欢？难道不能让他独处、让他安宁，在心里敬奉那位仍活在他心中的外祖母吗？别的人带着他们喧哗的、市侩的悲伤，很快就会忘掉。他，永远不会。正当他这样狂怒着，花园里一株盛开的桃树映入他的眼帘。他仿佛身陷急难，把它当作朋友，急忙向它奔去。它在花园里欢欣的临在给了他勇气，因为春天又在唱她的大歌了。空气里洋溢着一部复活的合唱。这里才真正是复活，是生命。在他看来丝毫不见扞格：他的至爱已消隐进她所从来的那伟大的生命——也是他所从来的生命；而正如生命在他之内，他的至爱也在他之内，如生命中之生命，堪为永久珍藏。

===Original===
Thus near the peach tree in full bloom, Louis’s tortured mind was stilled. He accepted death as an evanishment, he accepted Life as the power of powers. It seemed, indeed, as standing near his friend, gazing fondly round about and upward through the invisible firmament, that this great power, Life, in gesture and in utterance through the song of spring, had set its glowing rainbow in the passing cloud as a token of a covenant that the pure of eye might see. And indeed it seemed to him as quite lucid that the cloud with the glowing rainbow in its heart might well stand forever as a symbol of a token of a covenant between Life, and Man’s proud spirit, and the Earth. Thus Louis dreamed. And it seemed as though a small voice coming from afar, said: “If one must dream let the dream be one of happiness.”

===Chinese===
就这样，在那株盛开的桃树旁，路易斯受折磨的心安静了下来。他把死亡当作一场消散来接受，把生命当作万力之力来接受。的确，当他站在他的朋友近旁，深情地环顾四周、透过那看不见的苍穹仰望时，仿佛这伟大的力量——生命——正借着春之歌，以姿态与言说，把一道灼灼的虹安放在那朵行将逝去的云里，作为一纸圣约的信物，唯有目光纯净的人方能看见。而他也确实觉得一清二楚：这朵心里燃着灼灼彩虹的云，尽可以永远矗立着，作为生命、人的骄傲的精神与大地之间一纸圣约之信物的象征。路易斯就这样做着梦。这时仿佛有一个细小的声音自远方传来，说道：「人若非做梦不可，就让这梦是一场幸福的梦吧。」

---

===Original===
For the second time the house of Henri List had collapsed and gone down. This time in fragments. Soon the farm was sold. Julia, she of flaming hair, bewitching fairy tales, and temper of Iseult, cook and companion for nine long years, vanished in turn; Julius, the son, now twenty-five, offered a place, “in Philadelphia,” went there; his father followed.

===Chinese===
亨利·李斯特的家第二次坍塌、沉没了。这一次是碎成了齑粉。不久农庄就卖掉了。朱莉娅——那个火红头发的、会讲迷人童话的、有着伊索尔德（Iseult）般脾气的姑娘，当了九年厨娘和伴侣——也随之消失了；儿子朱利乌斯，如今二十五岁，有人给他在「费城」谋了个位子，他去了那里；他的父亲随后也走了。

===Original===
Louis found welcome and shelter with the next door neighbors, the John A. Tompsons, whose son George for years had been his playmate. And the earth resumed its revolution about its own private axis as before; day following night as usual. Daily, George Tompson went to Tech to pursue his studies in railway engineering. Daily, Louis paid his renewed respects to Moses Woolson. Daily, John A. Tompson returned from Boston at an exact hour, removed his hat, walked to a glass cabinet, took exactly one stiff swig of bourbon straight, smacked his lips, twinkled his eyes, sank into an easy chair which had remained in the same place for exactly how many years no one knows, dozed off for exactly ten minutes, arose, stretched his short muscular body, smiled widely, displaying false teeth, dyed-black side whiskers and moustache, a fine high forehead and dark fine eyes with as merry a twinkle as one could wish; then he went forth to see if each cultivated tree and shrub and bush and vine were exactly where they were in the morning. This man, gifted with extraordinary deftness of hand and a high-spirited intelligence, became a wonder and an inspiration to Louis, who spent the following two years in this charming household where epicureanism prevailed.

===Chinese===
路易斯在隔壁邻居约翰·A·汤普森（John A. Tompson）一家得到了欢迎与栖身之所，他家的儿子乔治（George）多年来一直是他的玩伴。于是地球照旧绕着它自己那根私轴旋转了起来；白昼照旧跟着黑夜。每天，乔治·汤普森去理工学校（Tech）攻读铁路工程。每天，路易斯去续上他对摩西·伍尔森的敬礼。每天，约翰·A·汤普森在准点的时刻从波士顿回来，摘下帽子，走到玻璃柜前，不多不少灌下一大口纯饮的波本威士忌，咂咂嘴，眨眨眼，陷进一张安乐椅——那张椅子在同一个地方放了多少年，没人知道——不多不少打盹十分钟，起身，伸展他矮壮结实的身子，咧嘴一笑，露出假牙、染得乌黑的连鬓胡子和髭须、一个俊美的高额头，和一双任谁看了都巴望有的、乌黑俊秀的眼睛里快活的闪烁；然后他走出去巡看，每一棵栽培的树、每一丛灌木、每一蓬矮篱、每一架藤蔓，是否不多不少还在早晨的位置上。这个人生来一双出奇灵巧的手和一个意气高昂的头脑，成了路易斯眼中的一桩奇迹、一个启发；路易斯随后两年就在这个伊壁鸠鲁主义盛行的迷人家庭里度过。

===Original===
That spring and summer, Louis botanized and mineralized with incessant ardor, and he saw what it signified that each thing should have a name, and what order and classification meant in the way of organized intelligence, and increased power of manipulation of things and thoughts. His insight into the relationship of function and structure deepened rapidly. A thousand things now began to cohere and arrange into groups which hitherto had seemed disparate and wide apart. To be sure, Moses Woolson was the impelling cause and it was up to Louis to do the work and to search and find and see these things objectively and clearly for himself. Thus logical connections began to form a plexus in his growing mind, beside which also upgrew a sense of equal logic and order in action. Now, John A. Tompson had this faculty of order and delicate precision in so marked a degree that Louis kept a close eye on his doings. In the fall Louis returned to the English High School and entered the Second Class under a sub-master named Hale. Mr. Hale was a scholar and a gentleman, a shining light of conscientious, conventional, virtuous routine.

===Chinese===
那年春夏，路易斯以不止息的热忱采植物、寻矿物，他领悟了每样事物各有一个名字意味着什么，领悟了秩序与分类在组织化的智能、在驾驭事物与思想的力量增长方面意味着什么。他对功能与结构之关系的洞见迅速加深。千百样事物如今开始凝聚成群、编列成类，而先前它们显得互不相干、相去悬远。诚然，摩西·伍尔森是那推动的动因，而做工、搜寻、发现、亲身客观而清晰地看见这些东西，则是路易斯自己的分内事。就这样，逻辑的联结开始在他成长的心智里结成一张丛络，在它旁边，还生长起一种行动中同样讲逻辑、同样有秩序的意识。而约翰·A·汤普森身上的这种秩序感与精微的准确，又到了那般显著的程度，以至路易斯密切留意着他的一举一动。秋天，路易斯回到英语中学，进了第二年级，级任是一位名叫黑尔（Hale）的副教习。黑尔先生是一位学者、一位绅士，一盏兢兢业业、循规蹈矩、有德有品的日常事务的明灯。

===Original===
With that clear and ruthless faculty, which boys possess, of spotting the essentials of their elders, Louis at the first session so sized up Hale; and dismay and despair swept through him in an awful wave of depression; it seemed as though the light of life had gone out. What was this tallow dip to the hot sun of Woolson? What could this mannikin accomplish? What could this respectable and approved lay figure do for one who had been trained intensively for a year by Moses Woolson? Let us therefore quickly draw the veil; and forget.

===Chinese===
凭着男孩们独具的那种一眼认出长辈要害的清澈而无情的本领，路易斯在头一堂课上就把黑尔掂量了个透；沮丧与绝望挟着一阵可怕的消沉巨浪扫过他全身；仿佛生命的灯熄灭了。伍尔森那轮烈日底下，这支牛油烛算得了什么？这个小木人能成什么气候？对一个受过摩西·伍尔森一年强化训练的人，这具体面的、众口交赞的人体模型还能有什么用？所以我们还是快快拉上帷幕；并且忘掉。

===Original===
At the end of this school year, George Tompson asked Louis why he did not try for Tech. And Louis replied that he supposed that he must first finish “High.” “Nonsense,” said George. “You can pass easily.” And thus encouraged, Louis passed easily.

It should be mentioned that at the time of the great Chicago fire, Louis received prompt word that the family were safe and sound beyond the reach of its fearsome ravages. And also Louis’s faithful correspondence with those far away must not be overlooked. Thus he now felt safe and strong to face in Tech his first adventure, as prelude to an architectural career.

===Chinese===
学年结束时，乔治·汤普森问路易斯，为什么不去报考理工学校。路易斯回答说，他想必得先把「中学」念完。「胡说，」乔治说，「你轻轻松松就能考上。」就这样受了一番鼓励，路易斯轻轻松松地考上了。

还应一提的是，芝加哥大火事发之时，路易斯及时得到音讯：全家安然无恙，在那场可怖浩劫的肆虐所及之外。同样不可忽略的，是路易斯与远方人们的忠实通信。就这样，他此刻自觉安稳而强壮，可以去理工学校面对他的第一场冒险，作为建筑生涯的序曲。

## VI Boston / 六　波士顿

===Original===
As one in tranquillity gazes into the crystal depths called Memory, in search of sights and sounds and colors long since physically passed out from what is otherwise called memory; when one is intent, not upon recalling but upon reentering, he finds a double motion setting in. While out of the gray surface-obscurity of supposed oblivion, there emerges to his view, as through a thinning haze, a broad vision assuming the color and movement of a life once lived, of a world once seen and felt to be real, so likewise, the intensive soul moves eagerly forward descending through intervening atmospheric depths toward this oncoming solid reality of time and place, a reality growing clearer, more colorful, more vibrant, more alluring, more convincing—filling the eye, the ear with sound and color and movement, with broad expanses, with minute details, with villages and cities, farms and work shops, men and women densely gathered or widely scattered, and children, little children always and everywhere. So moving, the two great illusions, the two dreams of the single dreamer, accelerating, rush onward, and vanish both into a single life which is but a dream;—the dream of the past enfolding and possessing the dreamer of today; the dreamer of today enveloping, entering and possessing the dream-reality of the past; all within the inscrutable stillness of a power unknown, within which we float, with our all, and believe ourselves real. We believe in our reality in our strenuous hours, in our practical doings, in our declamatory moments, and even in our hours of silence.
===Chinese===
当人在静谧中凝望那被唤作记忆（Memory）的水晶深处，去寻找那些早已从寻常所谓记忆中实体地消逝了的景象、声音与色彩；当他一心所求的不是回想，而是重新进入，他便发觉一种双重运动开始了。这时，从那想当然认作遗忘的灰色表层晦暗里，仿佛透过一层渐渐稀薄的雾霭，一片辽阔的景象浮现到眼前，渐次带上了一段曾经活过的生命、一个曾经亲见并感到其真实的世界的色彩与律动；与此同时，那深彻的灵魂也热切地迎向前去，穿越层叠的大气深处，向这迎面而来的、时间与地点的坚实真实沉降而去——这真实越来越清晰，越来越多彩，越来越震颤，越来越魅人，越来越令人信服——把眼睛、把耳朵灌满了声音、色彩与运动，灌满了辽阔的广延与细微的末节，灌满了村庄与城市、农庄与作坊，男男女女或密密攒聚，或零落四散，还有孩子们，小小的孩子们，无时不在，无处不在。就这样行进着，那两场宏大的幻梦，那同一个梦者的两场梦，不断加速，疾奔向前，双双没入一段仅是一场梦的生命；——过去之梦包裹着、占据着今日的梦者；今日的梦者拥抱着、进入着、占据着过去的梦之真实；这一切都笼盖在一种不可究诘的寂静之中，一种未知的力量的寂静之中，我们连同自己的一切漂浮于其间，并且相信我们自身真实不虚。我们相信自己的真实，在我们奋励的时辰里，在我们的实务里，在我们慷慨陈词的时刻里，甚至在我们静默的时辰里。

---

===Original===
In sleep there come images before us, floating by, irretrievable, or steadfastly convincing; and these we speak of casually as dreams. We are willing even to extend the idea of dream to man’s ambition. We say such or such a man had or has dreams of empire, of dominion, of achievement, of fulfilment of this or that sort. And occasionally we acknowledge, upon information, that such dream had taken full possession not of a man we read about, or see in the plenitude of his power, but that the dream arose within a child, in broad daylight—as night-dreams do in their way—and aroused in him a passionate desire.

We do not associate the idea of dream with our strenuous hours of thought and deed in the selfsame broad daylight. Nor do we see the stars at noon—but they are there. So is a dream there, within every human, ever—day and night unceasingly.

We impeach the dream idea, calling certain men “Dreamers.” We do this in derision—much as the pot might call the kettle black. We do not suspect that we could not put one foot forth before the other were we not dreaming; so artificial and sophisticated are we in our practical moments. And it is even so as we forget that each of us was once a child; even as we banish the thought, as crude, that out of that very child we have grown inevitably to be what we are; that the thoughts, the feelings, the emotions, the reactions, the waking dreams of that child have governed and determined us, willy-nilly, through the course of our lives and careers with compelling power—that what the child accepted we accept; that what the child rejected we reject.
===Chinese===
睡眠中，种种影像来到我们眼前，漂浮而过，无从挽回，或者坚牢得令人信服；这些我们随口唤作梦。我们甚至愿意把梦的观念延伸到人的抱负上。我们说，某人曾有过、或正有着帝业之梦、统治之梦、成就之梦、这样那样的圆满之梦。而偶尔，经人点破，我们也承认：这样的梦所全然占据的，并非我们在书里读到、或在其权势鼎盛之际看到的那个男人，而是这梦兴起于一个孩子心中，在光天化日之下——正如夜间的梦以它们自己的方式兴起——并在他心里激起一股炽烈的欲望。

我们不会把梦的观念，与我们在同一片光天化日之下奋励地思考与行动的时辰联系在一起。我们也不会在正午看见星星——但星星就在那里。梦也一样，它就在那里，在每一个人的体内，始终都在——日日夜夜，不曾止息。

我们弹劾梦这个观念，把某些人唤作「梦想家」。我们这样做是出于讥嘲——颇像锅嫌壶黑。我们全然没有想到：倘若不是在做梦，我们连一只脚都迈不到另一只脚前面去；我们在实务时刻竟是如此矫揉造作、如此世故老练。而事情恰恰如此，正如我们忘了我们每个人都曾经是孩子；正如我们把那个念头当作粗陋之物撵走——即我们正是从那个孩子身上，不可避免地长成了今天的我们；那个孩子的种种念头、种种感受、种种情绪、种种反应、种种清醒的梦，不管我们愿不愿意，都以一种不可抗拒的力量，支配并决定了我们一生一世、一世志业的历程——孩子接受的，我们便接受；孩子拒斥的，我们便拒斥。

===Original===
Thus from the abysm of Memory’s stillness, that child comes into being within Life’s dream, within the dream of eternal time and space; and in him we behold what we were and still are. Environment may influence but it cannot alter. For it is the child in multiple and in multiple series that creates the flowing environment of thought and deed that shall continuously mature in its due time. It is the moving child-in-multiple of long ago that created for us the basic environment within which we now live. Thus in a memory-mirror may we rediscover ourselves. Expecting to find therein a true reflection of ourselves as we believe we are, the image dissolves as the features of a long forgotten child confront us. Deny him, we dare not.

Turning about from self-contemplation we find children everywhere. We see the tidal wave of children moving on and on, we partly under their dominion, they partly under ours. But theirs is the new, ours the old; and, as ancients, we move on, unchanged from the children that we were—leaving our thoughts and deeds as a beaten trail behind us.

With this image in view the narrator has laid extended stress upon an authentic study of child life. Maturing years have made it but too clear that only on such foundation, resting deep within the vast-moving and timeless heritage of Instinct and Intellect, might a valid superstructure be reared into the light of our day. Men in their fatuity believe that they cause replicas of themselves to be born of woman; that they create children like themselves for themselves. They are picturesquely unaware, in mass, that they are but instrumental, normally, in bringing forth full grown men and women whom they may never see, but who, it must be so, are in essence of being with them at birth, specifically differing from them. Hence the unceasing flood of child personalities, accepting or rejecting influences in an environment they had no share in making. Historically, and in mass, victims of Fate rather than Masters of Destiny. For Destiny and Fate alike have birth in what is accepted or rejected by the child.
===Chinese===
就这样，从记忆之静默的深渊里，那个孩子在生命之梦中、在永恒时间与空间的梦中显身成形；我们在他身上看见我们曾经如何、至今仍然如何。环境能够影响，却无法改易。因为正是那成群又成列的孩子，创造了思想与行动那流转不居的环境，这环境将在应至之时不断成熟。正是往昔那流转着的、成群的孩子，为我们营造了我们如今生活于其中的基本环境。就这样，我们或许能在一面记忆之镜中重新发现自己。满心指望从中照出一个我们自以为是的确切镜像，那镜像却消散了，迎面浮现的，是一个久被遗忘的孩子的眉目。要否认他，我们不敢。

从自我凝视中转过身来，我们发现处处皆是孩子。我们看见孩子们的浪潮涌动向前，滔滔不绝；我们一部分受他们支配，他们一部分受我们支配。但他们拥有的是新的，我们拥有的是旧的；而作为古人，我们继续前行，同当年的孩子一般未曾改变——把我们的思想和行为当作一条踏平的旧径，留在身后。

眼中带着这幅图景，叙述者便对儿童生活的真实考察再三致意。岁月的成熟使他再清楚不过地看到：唯有在这样的根基之上——深深奠基于本能（Instinct）与理智（Intellect）那浩浩流转、无始无终的遗产之中——才能把一座可靠的上层建筑，树立进我们时代的日光里。人们在愚妄之中相信，是他们让自己的复制品由女人所生；相信他们为自己造出了酷肖自己的孩子。他们成群结队地、煞是好看地浑然不觉：通常，他们不过充当一件工具，助产出一个个他们或许永远见不到的成年男女——而这些人，注定如此，在出生之时便与他们本质同源，又与他们各各有别。于是便有了那不息的儿童人格的洪流，在一个他们未曾参与营造的环境里，接受或拒斥着种种影响。在历史上、在总体上，他们是命运的受害者，而非天命的主宰。因为天命与命运一样，都诞生于孩子的接受与拒斥之中。

---

===Original===
With this digression as a commentary we may now resume in its natural course the story of a growing child well known to us, and proceed to extend that series of rejections and acceptances—beginning in his infancy—into an ever enlarging world of fact and fiction until we may perchance obtain a glimpse of what they really were, and of their significance in determining his onward drift—a drift that as yet has developed no self-defined momentum.
===Chinese===
拿这段题外话权作注脚，我们现在可以循着自然的轨道，继续讲述那个我们早已熟识的成长中孩子的故事，把那一连串始于襁褓的拒斥与接受，延伸进一个不断扩大的事实与虚构的世界，直到我们或许能窥见它们究竟是什么，窥见它们在决定他向前漂流的去向时具有怎样的意义——这漂流至今尚未形成任何自我界定的动量。

===Original===
Shortly after their return to Boston from Newburyport, the father, for reasons of his own, whatever they were, decided to move his family to Halifax, Nova Scotia. They were away six months.
===Chinese===
他们从纽伯里波特回到波士顿后不久，父亲——出于他自己的一番理由，不管那究竟是什么——决定举家迁往新斯科舍（Nova Scotia）的哈利法克斯（Halifax）。他们一去半年。

---

===Original===
A small boy stood on the dock at Eastport, Maine, holding in his hand a huge greengage plum. The same small boy suffered and saw the agonies of those who cross the Bay of Fundy. He saw and lived in a hotel in Halifax, where an Academy was opened. Later he endured in patience the terrible discipline of his father, who in below zero weather walked him for miles along the bleak “Northwest Arm,” to return with white cheeks and nose, only to be told to wash his face in snow—the father doing the like. He saw his gods blasting a deep trench for water pipe through the solid slate ledge, and again he marvelled at what men could do. He saw the great citadel crowning the heights, and from it, he viewed the harbor. Then came calamity. Mamma was taken down with diphtheria; and he saw the great and grand Newfoundland dog, that had welcomed them effusively on their arrival and had adopted them at once, lying day after day, night after night, faithfully guarding her chamber door. Mamma recovered; but her illness was prophetic of change.
===Chinese===
一个小男孩站在缅因州（Maine）伊斯特波特（Eastport）的码头上，手里攥着一颗硕大的青李（greengage plum）。就是这个小男孩，在横渡芬迪湾（Bay of Fundy）时亲身受着、也亲眼看着渡海者的种种苦楚。他在哈利法克斯见过并住过一家旅馆，那里开设着一所学院（Academy）。后来他又耐心熬受了父亲那可怕的操练：零下的天气里，沿着荒凉的「西北臂」（Northwest Arm）步行数英里，回到家时脸颊和鼻子冻得发白，却还被告知要用雪洗脸——父亲自己也照样洗。他看见他的众神在坚实的板岩基石上爆破，为水管炸出一条深沟，于是又一次对人力所能为惊叹不已。他看见宏伟的要塞雄踞高地之巅，又从要塞上眺望了海港。随后灾祸来了。妈妈病倒了，患的是白喉（diphtheria）；他眼看着那条高大威严的纽芬兰犬（Newfoundland dog）——他们初到时它热烈地欢迎过他们，当即把他们认作了自家人——一天又一天、一夜又一夜地卧在那里，忠心耿耿地守着她的房门。妈妈康复了；但她的病预示着变故将至。

===Original===
In the spring they returned to Boston, and Louis was sent to live with his grandparents in South Reading, as before, with the proviso that he was to return to his parents in the fall. He became at once deeply immersed in the miniature activities of the farm, taking the initiative wherever he could, doing small things with large enthusiasm. He did not consider such things work, but joy. He was physically active and mentally active too. He was always excited in his work and always constructive. As Grandpa also worked, they became great pals, and planned and worked together. His natural surroundings became less mystical to him. He held them in affection, but no longer in dreamy wonder. The delicate bloom of early childhood was passing, while the vigor and aggressiveness of budding boyhood were rising as branches from the same deep root. His love of the open remained constant and intense. He was developing pride, ambition, and a sense of growing power over material things. The desire some day to exercise such power to the full became in him a definitive dream, within which, unnoticed, was resident the glow of a deeper power—a power that had suffused a swiftly-moving, vocal springtime, which he had seen and heard and lived in this same spot.

Grandpa did not bother about the child’s education, for, being wise, he knew the child was daily self-acquiring an education exactly suited to his temperament and years. But Grandmamma believed otherwise. She thought her grandson needed polish, and that he should now begin a systematic study of the French language. Louis was willing enough and started in gaily. He liked the sound, and the words in italics looked pretty; all went well for a while. As he got in deeper he began to be oppressed by the inanities of the grammar-book, and the imbecilities of a sort of first reader in which a waxwork father takes his wax children on daily promenades, explaining to them as they go, in terms of unctuous morality, the works of the Creator, and drawing therefrom, as from a spool, an endless thread of pious banalities. Louis rebelled. He declared he was an **American boy**!—that none of his playmates spoke French—why should *he*? Grandmamma, in habitual indulgence, discontinued polishing. She could not enter the child-mind. To her, her grandson was an object of boundless love—and little more; and yet this little more was an impassable gulf, lying as a chasm between old age gently petrifying in the thoughts of her own childhood, and a vigorous young animal with thoughts and an impetuous will of his own. And he in turn held his grandmamma to be the sweetest of mortals—and little more.
===Chinese===
春天他们回到波士顿，路易斯像从前一样被送到南雷丁，住进外祖父外祖母家，但讲定了一个条件：秋天要回到父母身边去。他随即深深沉浸进农庄上那些小小的事务里，凡能领头处便领头，怀着大大的热忱做小小的事情。他不把这些当作劳作，而当作快乐。他身子勤快，脑子也勤快。他干起活来总是兴致勃勃，总是在营建。外祖父既然也劳作，两人便成了莫逆的伙伴，一同筹划，一同动手。周遭的自然于他不再那么神秘。他怀着情意亲近它们，却不再怀着如梦的惊奇。幼年那层娇嫩的粉衣正在褪去，而初萌少年时代的劲气与闯劲，正如枝柯一般从同一条深根上升起。他对旷野的热爱始终炽烈如一。他日渐生出骄傲、抱负，和一种对有形之物日益增长的力量感。有朝一日把这力量施展个淋漓尽致——这愿望在他心里成了一个明确的梦；而在这梦的深处，不知不觉栖居着另一种更深沉的力量的辉光——那力量，曾浸润过一个疾速流转、有声有语的春天，就在这同一个地方，他见过它，听过它，也在其中生活过。

外祖父不为这孩子的教育操心，因为他有智慧，知道这孩子每天都在自行获取一种恰恰合乎他的性情与年龄的教育。外祖母却别有所见。她认为外孙需要调教，认为他现在该开始系统地研习法语了。路易斯十分情愿，欢欢喜喜地开了头。他喜欢那语音，斜体排印的单词看上去也漂亮；有一阵子一切顺遂。随着功课渐深，他开始被语法书的空洞无物压得透不过气，被某本入门读本里的愚不可及搅得心烦——书里一位蜡人父亲每天领着他的蜡孩子们散步，一路用油滑的道德说教向他们讲解造物主的功业，又像从线轴上抽线一般，从中抽出一条没完没了的虔诚的陈词滥调。路易斯造反了。他郑重宣布，他是**美国男孩**（American boy）！——玩伴里没有一个说法语的——凭什么偏要*他*说？外祖母素来纵容，就此搁下了调教。她进不去孩子的心。在她眼里，外孙是无边爱意的对象——此外便没有多少了；然而这一点「此外」，却是一道无法逾越的鸿沟，像一道裂谷，横在一边是正温顺地石化于她自己童年思绪中的迟暮，另一边是一头活力充沛的幼兽，有自己的心思，有一腔急躁的意志。而他呢，也把外祖母看作世上最可爱的人儿——此外便没有多少了。

===Original===
Thus summer passed on broad pinions sweeping, and Louis saw it moving thus. He *saw* such things. Beneath all the overlay the child was a mystic; inarticulate, wondering, believing. These fleeting revelations of Life came and went as interludes within the chosen practicalities of his realistic and material activity. He had rather help build a stone wall than listen to a poem—all except the fairy tales that Julia told—for here was Romance—and romance he could not withstand.
===Chinese===
夏天就这样振着宽阔的翅膀扫掠而过，路易斯眼看着它这样逝去。他*看得见*这些事物。在层层遮覆之下，这孩子是个神秘主义者：讷于言，怀着惊奇，怀着相信。生命的这些转瞬即逝的启示来了又去，如同他那种讲求实际、现实主义而唯物式的活动之间的段段插曲。他宁可去帮忙垒一段石墙，也不愿听人念一首诗——唯有朱莉娅（Julia）讲的童话例外——因为这里有传奇——而传奇，他是抵挡不住的。

===Original===
One morning;—it happened to be September 3rd of that year—Louis Henri Sullivan arose early and sallied forth in pomp and pride. On the Stoneham road he met a farmer friend:
===Chinese===
一天早晨；——那天恰好是那年九月三日——路易斯·亨利·沙利文（Louis Henri Sullivan）早早起了床，威风八面、得意扬扬地出了门。在斯托纳姆路上，他遇见一位农夫朋友：

===Original===
“Hello! Do you know I am eight years old today?”

“No, wall, wall, that’s fine. Heow old did yeh say yeh be?”

“I am Eight! Don’t you think I’m a big boy now? Do you want to feel my muscle?”

“My sakes—but yeh aire strong!”

“Yes I am. I can lift a stone almost as big as my grandfather can; but of course he’s older.”

“How old did yeh say yeh be?”

“I say I am eight years old today and I want you to know it. Do you want to pound my chest?”

“Can’t say’s I do.”

“You may pound my chest as hard as you like and I won’t say a word. Have you noticed my new boots? It’s my first pair. My grandma gave them to me for my birthday.”

“No I hadn’t saw them.”

“Well, look at them now. See; they’re copper-toed and have red tops. Don’t you think they’re fine?”

“Yaas; how old did yeh say yeh be? I think yeh got a mighty fine granny t’give yeh them boots.”
===Chinese===
「喂！你知道我今天满八岁了吗？」

「嗳哟，嗬、嗬，那敢情好。你说你多大来着？」

「我八岁了！你看我现在是不是个大孩子了？想不想摸摸我的肌肉？」

「我的老天——你可真结实！」

「我就是结实。我能扛起一块石头，差不多跟我外祖父扛起的一样大；不过嘛，他年纪当然大些。」

「你说你多大来着？」

「我说我今天满八岁了，我要你知道这个。想不想捶捶我的胸脯？」

「说不上想。」

「你尽管捶我的胸脯，有多狠使多狠，我一声也不吭。你瞧见我的新靴子没有？这是我的头一双。是我外祖母送我的生日礼物。」

「俺还没瞅见过呢。」

「那就现在瞧瞧吧。看；靴头包着铜，靴筒是红的。你说棒不棒？」

「是啊；你说你多大来着？俺寻思，你有个顶顶好的外祖母，才给你这双靴子。」

===Original===
And the Ancient doddered down the road dustily regurgitating the thoughts of his childhood now become decayed and senile; while bounding boyhood clattered on, from house to house, from field to field, wherever might be found man, woman, or child to whom he might sing his own saga in vainglory. For was he not right? Was he not Eight? Was he not heroically aware that that day he was crossing the invisible line between childhood and boyhood? Were not the gaudy boots his plain certificate of valor and of deeds done and to be done? Were they not for him symbols of that manhood toward which he so ardently yearned that his pride might come to the full? He said it was so. In this joyous mood was his saga sung, as of one with a growing faith.
===Chinese===
那位老人家摇摇晃晃、一身尘土地沿着路走远了，嘴里反刍着他童年的心思，如今那些心思已然朽败衰颓；而蹦蹦跳跳的少年郎则嗒嗒地一路向前，挨家挨户，一块田又一块田，凡能找到一个听得他唱的男人、女人或孩子的地方，他就要把属于自己的萨迦（saga）在虚骄之中高唱一通。因为他难道不对吗？他难道不是八岁了吗？他难道不是英武地意识到，这一天他正跨过幼年与少年之间那条看不见的界线吗？那双花哨的靴子，难道不是他堂堂的勇武证书，证明着已成的功业与将成的事业吗？它们在他眼里，难道不正是那成人境界的象征么——他如此炽烈地渴慕着它，好让他的骄傲臻于圆满？他说正是如此。他的萨迦便在这欢畅的心境中唱了出来，像一个信心日增的人所唱的那样。

===Original===
Then came, as it were, a bugle call from the south. He answered the call in person. Boston City swallowed him up.
===Chinese===
随后，仿佛从南方传来了一声军号。他亲身应召而去。波士顿城把他一口吞没了。

===Original===
The effect was immediately disastrous. As one might move a flourishing plant from the open to a dark cellar, and imprison it there, so the miasma of the big city poisoned a small boy acutely sensitive to his surroundings. He mildewed; and the leaves and buds of ambition fell from him. In those about him, already city-poisoned, even in his own kin, he found no solace, and ceased openly to lament. Against the big city his heart swelled in impatient, impotent rebellion. Its many streets, its crooked streets, its filthy streets, lined with stupid houses crowded together shoulder to shoulder like selfish hogs upon these trough-like lanes, irritated him, suffocated him; the crowds of people, and wagons, hurrying here and there so aimlessly—as it appeared to him—confused and overwhelmed him, arousing amazement, nausea and dismay. As he thought of the color, the open beauty of his beloved South Reading, and the great grand doings of Newburyport, where men did things; where there was obvious, purposeful action; an exhibit of sublime power; the city of Boston seemed a thing already in decay. He was so saddened, so bewildered, so grieved, that his sorrow, his bitter disappointment, could find no adequate utterance and relief. Hence he kept it all within himself, and became drugged to the point of lassitude and despair. The prospect of a whole winter to be spent within these confines, shut out from the open world that had been growing so large and splendid for him, filled him at times with a sudden frantic desire to escape. Had not his father at once taken up again the rigorous training of cold baths and outdoor exercise, had he not taken him on long walks to Roxbury, to Dorchester, even to Brookline, where the boy might see a bit of green and an opening-up of things, the boy would surely have carried out his resolution to run away. To run where? Anywhere to liberty and freedom!
===Chinese===
后果立时便是灾难性的。就像有人把一株繁茂的植物从旷野移进黑暗的地窖里囚起来，大城市的瘴气便这样毒害了一个对周遭环境极其敏感的小男孩。他发霉了；雄心的叶与芽纷纷脱落。在周围那些早已中了城市之毒的人身上——连他自己的亲人身上——他都寻不到慰藉，于是不再公开悲叹。对着这座大城市，他的心鼓胀起一场焦灼而无力的反叛。那许许多多的街、弯弯曲曲的街、污秽的街，两旁挤满愚蠢的房屋，肩并肩挤作一团，像一群自私的肥猪挤在这些猪槽般的巷道上——这一切激怒着他，窒息着他；那一群群的人、一辆辆的马车，东奔西窜，那样漫无目的——在他看来如此——搅得他心乱，压得他喘不过气，激起他的惊愕、恶心与沮丧。一想到他心爱的南雷丁的色彩与开阔的美，一想到纽伯里波特那些堂堂的大事业——那里的人在实实在在地做事，那里有明白可见、有的放矢的行动，有一种崇高力量的展演——波士顿城便像是一件已然在朽败中的东西。他那样悲伤，那样惶惑，那样痛心，他的哀恸、他那辛酸的失望，竟寻不到充分的倾诉与纾解。于是他把一切都闷在自己心里，渐渐麻痹到倦怠与绝望的地步。一想到整整一个冬天都要困在这些界墙之内，被关在那个对他正变得日益辽阔、日益壮美的旷朗世界之外，他时时涌起一阵突如其来的、狂乱的出逃之念。若不是父亲当即重新拾起冷水浴与户外锻炼的严格训练，若不是父亲带他长途散步，走到罗克斯伯里（Roxbury），走到多切斯特（Dorchester），甚至走到布鲁克莱恩（Brookline），让孩子看得见一点绿色、一点天地开敞的气象，这孩子肯定早已把出逃的决心付诸行动了。逃去哪里？哪里都行，只要奔向自由与解放！

===Original===
He had partly revived from the first shock, when his ruthless father placed him in the Brimmer School on Common Street. Louis found it vile; unspeakably gloomy; a filthy prison for children. He learned nothing. There was no one to teach him, and what he saw there shall not be recorded here. So passed the winter; Louis looking, ever aimlessly, yearning, for *a teacher*. As a rose springs upward from the muck and puts forth gracious blooming, even so out of the muck of this school a reaction sprang up, a fervent hungered yearning within, for a kindred spirit to arise that might illumine him and in whom he might rejoice; a spirit utterly human that would break down the dam made within him by sanctioned suppressions and routine, that there might pour out of him the gathered cesspool, and the waters of his life again flow on. Of such a nature was the hunger of a well-fed child.
===Chinese===
他刚从最初的重击中缓过一半，那无情的父亲便把他送进了康芒街（Common Street）上的布里默学校（Brimmer School）。路易斯觉得它污糟；阴郁得无以言表；一座给孩子们预备的肮脏监狱。他什么也没有学到。那里没有人教他，而他在那里见到的一切，本篇不予记述。冬天就这样过去了；路易斯始终茫然地、渴慕地张望着，寻找*一位老师*。正如玫瑰从淤泥里向上抽出枝来，绽出温雅的花朵，从这所学校的淤泥里也生出了一股反弹：一种热烈的、饥渴的内心渴望，盼着有一位同道之人奋然而起，能照亮他，也能让他为之欢欣；一位全然通人性的精灵，能推倒那道由得到默许的压制与例行公事在他心里筑起的水坝，好让他体内积成的一潭死水泻将出去，让他生命的水流重新奔涌向前。一个吃得饱饱的孩子的饥渴，便是这样的性质。

===Original===
As the Boston winter of ’64 was groaning on its way to the tomb of all winters, Mamma was again stricken with diphtheria; and again she recovered. The city winter passed, a city springtime passed. With vacation at hand, Louis returned to his grandparents, resumed his activities now enlarged in scope, and in the fall returned to the City, his wounds somewhat healed. He was immediately placed in the newly organized Rice School—temporarily housed in another gloomy structure, but not so foul—at that time situated on the west side of Washington Street and a short distance south of Dover Street. Here he learned nothing at first except in-so-far as there was a sort of mechanical infiltration going on. But, at a nearby book store, Beadle’s Dime Novels appeared in a whirlwind of popularity. Louis Sullivan pounced upon them. He devoured the raw melodramas and cried for more. Here at last was Romance! Here again were great men doing great deeds. Here was action in the open. He could live these scenes. He could visualize these acts even within the deadly philistine air of Washington Street and its Rice School where he was supposed to know that 2 : 4 :: 4 : 8. He did not especially care for the standardized lady in the case who was always ravishingly beautiful and always eighteen; and to the villain he was sometimes lenient, but the hero, that magnificent man-god whose ear had just been grazed by the arrow of a huge red savage—him he took to his bosom. He got a thrill out of every page, which was more than he ever got out of the school. He was to remain at this school for several years, during which time he slowly became citified. His activities naturally spread over an ever widening field; and these years were filled with multifarious details large and small. His geographical ventures extended from South Reading as a center to Stoneham, Woburn, North Reading, Saugus and Ipswich; and from Boston as a center to Rockport, Gloucester, Marblehead, Salem, Lynn and Nahant; and southward into Jamaica Plain. Between Boston and South Reading were dotted, as villages or hamlets, Somerville, Malden, Melrose, Greenville and South Reading Junction. West of the Junction was a small affair called Crystal Lake, with bare and sterile surroundings, including an icehouse on its northern shore. The big pond to the north of South Reading—then a village of possibly two thousand souls—was officially known as Lake Quannapowitt. From the western shore of this lake projected a promontory, and within this promontory was a cemetery.
===Chinese===
当’64 年的波士顿之冬正呻吟着走向一切冬天共同的坟墓时，妈妈又一次染上白喉；又一次康复了。城里的冬天过去了，城里的春天也过去了。假期在即，路易斯回到外祖父外祖母身边，重操起那些如今范围更广的事务，秋天再回到城里时，他的创口已稍稍愈合。他随即被送进新组建的赖斯学校（Rice School）——校舍暂时设在另一栋阴郁的楼房里，只是没那么污糟——当时坐落在华盛顿街（Washington Street）西侧、多佛街（Dover Street）以南不远的地方。起初他在这里什么也没学到，除非把那一直在暗中进行的一番机械渗透算作学问。可是，在附近一家书店里，比德尔十美分小说（Beadle’s Dime Novels）挟着一阵旋风般的声势登场了。路易斯·沙利文扑了上去。他狼吞虎咽地吞下这些生猛的情节剧，还嚷着要更多。这里终于有了传奇！这里又有伟人建立伟业了。这里有露天的行动。这些场景他能亲历。即便在华盛顿街及其赖斯学校那致人死命的庸俗空气里——在那儿，他理应弄懂 2 : 4 :: 4 : 8——他也能把这些壮举历历如绘地想见出来。他对书里那千篇一律的女主角并不特别倾心——她总是美得摄人心魄，又总是十八岁；对恶棍，他有时倒也宽厚，可那位英雄，那位耳朵刚被一个高大的红皮肤蛮子的箭矢擦过的堂堂人间之神——他把他搂进了怀里。每一页都让他心头激灵，这可是学校从未给过他的。他要在这所学校里待上好几年，其间他渐渐染上了城市的习气。他的活动自然铺展到一个越来越宽的天地；这些岁月里填满了大大小小、五花八门的细节。他的地理远征，以南雷丁为中心，伸展到斯托纳姆（Stoneham）、沃本（Woburn）、北雷丁（North Reading）、索格斯（Saugus）和伊普斯威奇（Ipswich）；又以波士顿为中心，伸展到罗克波特（Rockport）、格洛斯特（Gloucester）、马布尔黑德（Marblehead）、塞勒姆（Salem）、林恩（Lynn）和纳汉特（Nahant）；还向南深入牙买加平原（Jamaica Plain）。波士顿与南雷丁之间，星星点点缀着一些村镇：萨默维尔（Somerville）、莫尔登（Malden）、梅尔罗斯（Melrose）、格林维尔（Greenville）和南雷丁枢纽（South Reading Junction）。枢纽以西有一处小小的所在，名叫水晶湖（Crystal Lake），四围光秃贫瘠，北岸还有一座冰库。南雷丁北面的大池塘——当时那还是一座约莫两千口人的村子——官名叫夸纳波威特湖（Lake Quannapowitt）。湖的西岸伸出一道岬角，岬角之内，是一座墓园。

===Original===
During these years, Louis Sullivan, always inquisitive and foolhardily curious, had ferreted out every street, alley and blind court, and dock and wharf from end to end and crosswise within the limits of Boston, and had made partial explorations of Charlestown, Chelsea, and South Boston. Thus there gradually arose within his consciousness a clearing sense of what a city meant objectively as a solid conglomerate of diverse and more or less intricate activities. He began indeed to sense the city as a power—unknown to him before—a power new-risen above his horizon; a power that extended the range and amplified the content of his own child-dream of power as he had seen it manifested in the open within the splendid rhythm of the march of the seasons. Nevertheless, he saw, in his boy-way, and felt it strongly, a great mysterious contrast between the two. In the open all was free, expansive and luminous. In the city all was contraction, density, limitation, and a cruel concentration. He felt that between himself and the city, as such, lay a harsh antagonism that seemed forever insoluble; as though men had made the city when they were mad; and that as it grew under their hands it had mastered and confined them. Yet men, women and children seemed to move about freely enough at certain hours. These waves of doubt and apprehension came and passed at intervals, but each wave left its precipitate, in solution as it were, in the boy’s quizzical mind. He became less and less unfriendly toward the school, as sporadic knowledge crept out of his books and took on a certain segregated appearance of validity, having slight connection, however, with his own world. He ceased to be wholly rebellious, and took his small doses of formal routine education much as he might take a medicine supposedly for his good. Thus far his father had been his only successful teacher.
===Chinese===
在这些年月里，路易斯·沙利文——他永远好奇，而且好奇得有勇无谋——把波士顿地界内的大街、小巷、死胡同，连同码头和货栈，纵的横的都摸了个透，还把查尔斯敦（Charlestown）、切尔西（Chelsea）和南波士顿（South Boston）探了个大概。就这样，他的意识里渐渐升起一种清明之感：一座城市，作为一种由形形色色、或精或粗的活动凝成的坚实混合体，客观上究竟意味着什么。他确实开始把城市感觉为一种力量——他从前不知道的力量——一种在他地平线上新升起来的力量；这力量扩展了他那孩童之力梦的范围，充实了它的内容——那力之梦，他曾在旷野里、在四季行进的光辉节律中亲眼见过它显形。尽管如此，他以他的少年方式看出、并且强烈地感到，两者之间横着一道巨大的神秘反差。在旷野里，一切自由、舒展、明亮。在城市里，一切收缩、稠密、局促，还有一种残酷的凝聚。他感到，他自身与这城市本身之间，横着一种严酷的敌意，似乎永无化解之日；仿佛人是在发疯的时候造出了城市；而城市在他们手下长大的过程中，反倒征服了他们，禁锢了他们。然而男人、女人和孩子们，在某些时辰里走动得倒也够自如。这一波波的疑虑与忧惧时来时去，但每一波都在这孩子好盘根问底的心里留下了它的沉淀，仿佛溶在了溶液里。他对学校越来越不怀敌意了，因为零零星星的知识从书本里渗出来，带上了一种分门别类、煞有介事的有效模样——尽管与他自己的世界关联甚少。他不再全然反叛，把那一剂剂小分量的正规例行教育照单收下，就像服用一剂据说对他有好处的药。迄今为止，父亲是他唯一一位成功的老师。

===Original===
The boy had acquired and was continuing to acquire the education he possessed partly through a series of shocks—frequently humiliating—which inverted his illusions into realities; partly through his own keen powers of observation, and perhaps something in the way of intuition; but mainly and fundamentally through his high sensitiveness to externals which, always with him, took on character, definition and, as it were, a personality. He was now ripe for another shock.
===Chinese===
这男孩已经获得的、并且仍在继续获得的教育，一部分来自一连串的震荡——常常是羞辱性的——那些震荡把他的幻象翻转成了现实；一部分来自他自己敏锐的观察力，或许还有几分直觉；但主要地、根本地，来自他对外物的高度敏感——这些外物，在他这里永远带着性格，带着轮廓，可以说带着一种人格。他如今又熟到了该挨另一次震荡的时候了。

===Original===
One day his father took him on a walk to South Boston, and made him run up a high hill on the top of which was a reservoir. This altitude reached, a great view spread before them. The boy at once became exalted with awe at the living presence and expanding power of Mother Earth. Never—since the long forgotten days of Halifax—had he reached such a peak of observation. His father’s love for “scenery” had taken them there. As the boy gazed in thrilling wonder, his father called attention, one after another, to special points of beauty in the land and waterscape, finally coming around to the Blue Hills, which indeed were blue and enchanting against the far horizon and its haze. After explaining the nature of the haze, father called attention to two outstanding peaks, near together but differing in size, and asked his son a point-blank question: Which of the two hills is the larger? His son walked straight into the trap, saying that of course the larger one was the larger—why did Papa ask? Then the trap fell—knocking Louis senseless—for Papa said, (beyond a doubt maliciously he said it) that *the smaller was the larger*. When Louis came to, he protested vehemently; but Papa said he had been there and knew. Then, relenting, believing he had carried his practical joke far enough, he told his son, seriously, that the effect, the appearance, the illusion was, in fact, due to what he called **perspective**; and the nature of this particular perspective, and perspective in general, he explained with notable skill, simplicity, and with many objective instances. But Louis instead of receiving this information with acclaim and joy, as a new world opening before him, was deeply saddened and perturbed. His father, sincerely believing he was educating his own, came near to destroying him. He was no psychologist, he had indeed but little human sympathy or insight—hence he had no suspicion of what was going on beneath the surface of his own son. For had not that son built up a cherished world all his own, a world made up of dreams, of practicalities, of deep faith, of unalloyed acceptance of externals, only now to find that world trembling and tottering on its foundations, threatening to collapse upon him, or to vanish before this new and awful revelation from the unseen. This ghostly apparition which his father called “perspective” terrorized him. What his father said about it did not help. For behind the perspective that the father saw was a perspective that the child saw—invisible to the father. It was **mystery**—a mystery that lay behind appearances, and within appearances, and in front of appearances, a mystery which if penetrated might explain and clarify all, as his father had explained and clarified a little. Did this mystery reside also in his lovely slender elm tree? Was his great friend the ash tree involved in mystery? Was the sunrise that had glorified him and the earth around him part of this mysterious perspective that lay behind appearances, that lay behind even the clear apparition his father called perspective? Must he lose his faith in what seemed real? Was Boston itself and all within it but a mask and a lie? Was there within it and behind it a perspective, a mystery which if understood might reveal and clarify it, making it intelligible? Could this mystery be penetrated? He was determined it should be, soon or late—and that he would do it. Thus had a father’s playful joke set up in a child a raging fermentation. Such high-pitched emotion could not last. Such vision was bound to fade. Such fear must pass. And so it happened. The turmoil, the chaos lasted but as the span of a daydream. But within that dream, within that turmoil, there awakened a deeper dream that has not passed. Thus Louis Sullivan accepted and rejected; rejected and accepted.
===Chinese===
