一天，父亲带他散步去南波士顿，叫他跑上一座高山，山顶上有一座水库。一登上这个高度，一片宏阔的景色便在他们眼前铺展开来。这男孩顿时肃然起敬，为大地母亲（Mother Earth）活生生的临在与舒展的力量而心魂升腾。自哈利法克斯那些久已忘却的日子以来，他还从未登上过这样一座观照的峰巅。是父亲对「风景」的偏爱把他们带到那里去的。男孩正怀着战栗的惊奇凝望，父亲便一样一样地指给他看，指点陆上水上各处佳景，最后说到蓝山（Blue Hills）——衬着远方的地平线和那里的雾霭，那山确实是蓝色的，确实迷人。讲解完雾霭的本性，父亲又让儿子注意两座拔出的山峰，相去不远而大小有别，随即直截了当地问了他一个问题：这两座山，哪一座更大？儿子径直走进了圈套，回答说大的那座当然更大——爸爸问这个做什么？于是圈套落下——把路易斯霎时打懵了——因为爸爸说（毫无疑问是存心使坏地说），*小的那座才是大的*。路易斯醒过神来，激烈抗议；可爸爸说他到过那里，他知道。随后他心软了，觉得这个恶作剧已经开得够远，便一本正经地告诉儿子：那个效果、那个外观、那个错觉，其实是出自一样他称之为**透视**（perspective）的东西；这种特定透视的道理，连同透视的一般道理，他讲得技巧出众、明白晓畅，还举了许多身外实例。可是路易斯非但没有欢欣鼓舞地接受这一新知，把它当作一个在眼前展开的新世界，反而深深地忧伤、深深地不安起来。他的父亲，真心相信这是在教育自己的骨肉，却险些把他毁掉。他不是心理学家，实际上也极少人间的同情与洞察——因此他全然不曾疑心，自己儿子的表层之下正翻涌着什么。因为那儿子难道不是已经营建起一个全然属于自己、心爱的世界么——一个由梦想、实务、深信、对外物不掺杂质的接受所构成的世界——此刻却发现这个世界正在根基上颤抖、摇晃，眼看就要崩塌到他身上，或者在这来自未见之境的、新奇而可怖的启示面前消散殆尽。这个被父亲唤作「透视」的鬼魅般的幻影恐吓着他。父亲关于它所说的话无济于事。因为在父亲看见的透视背后，还有一层孩子看见的透视——那是父亲看不见的。那是**神秘**（mystery）——一种藏在万象背后、寓于万象之中、又立于万象之前的神秘，一旦参透，或许便能解释一切、阐明一切，正如父亲方才解释、阐明了一小部分那样。这神秘可也栖居在他那株苗条可爱的榆树里么？他的挚友白蜡树，可也牵连在这神秘之中么？那曾经荣耀了他和他四周大地的日出，可也是这藏在万象背后的神秘透视的一部分么——它甚至藏在父亲唤作透视的那道清晰幻影的背后？他必须失去对那些貌似真实之物的信心么？波士顿城本身，连同城中的一切，难道只是一副面具、一个谎么？它之内、它背后，可有一层透视、一层神秘，一旦领会便能揭示它、阐明它，使它变得可以理解么？这神秘能被参透么？他下定决心，迟早要参透它——而且要由他亲手来参透。就这样，父亲一个玩笑的戏言，在一个孩子心里掀起了一场狂暴的发酵。这样高亢的情绪持续不了多久。这样的幻景注定要褪色。这样的恐惧必定会过去。事情也果然如此。那场骚乱、那场混沌，不过延续了一个白日梦的工夫。但在那个梦里，在那场骚乱里，一个更深沉的梦醒来了，而且从未逝去。就这样，路易斯·沙利文有所接受，有所拒斥；又有所拒斥，有所接受。

===Original===
He returned to the school and the streets which were much the same thing to him. At recess he promptly announced that he could lick any boy of his size. Whereupon “his size” knocked him in the eye, and the two “sizes” went at it, according to regulations which consisted in beginning fairly and ending foully—two boys rolling over and over in the middle of the street, in the center of an eager, urging, admiring circle of excited ruffians of varied sizes, who cried at the proper time: “He’s had enough; let him up.” Sometimes Louis’s prophecies were verified. Sometimes they proved unfortunate. But it was all the same, all in the game; and there was established in the school a “Who’s Who” that never reached print. Moreover there was established a Hierarchy in which each “who” was definitely ranked according to the who’s he could lick, and the who’s and sizes who could lick him. And while all this was going on, Louis picked up, in addition to a bit of geography and arithmetic, every form of profanity, every bit of slang, and every particle of verbal garbage he could assimilate. In other words he was one of the gang and a tough. But his honor required that he refrain from licking the good boys just because they were good—which could not be said of some.
===Chinese===
他回到学校，也回到街头——这两者在他看来本是一回事。课间休息时，他立马宣布：凡是跟他一般块头的男孩，他都能揍赢。话音未落，「他的块头」就一拳打在他眼睛上，于是那两个「块头」按章开打——章程规定：起手要正大，收场要下作——两个男孩在街心滚作一团，当中围着一圈兴奋的小泼皮，块头大小不一，一个个又是撺掇又是喝彩，到了合宜的时机便齐声喊：「他够呛了；放他起来吧。」有时路易斯的预言得到应验，有时则被证明倒了大霉。但横竖都一样，全在游戏之中；学校里就此立起了一部从未付印的《名人录》（Who’s Who）。而且还立起了一套等级体制，其中每一个「名人」都按他能揍赢哪些人、以及哪些人和哪些块头能揍赢他，等次分明，各安其位。而在这一切进行的当口，路易斯除了捡到一点地理和算术，还把种种咒骂、各色俚语、一切他消化得动的语言渣滓，一概捡进了肚子。换句话说，他已是帮里的一员，一个小泼皮。但他的荣誉感要求他不得仅仅因为好孩子们乖便去揍他们——这一点，可不是人人都做得到的。

===Original===
He was progressing so well at school, his mother thought—for his teacher so certified for reasons unknown—perhaps to conceal the truth—that she believed it time he learn to play the piano. Louis thought otherwise. Mamma was stern, Louis yielded. Mamma promised it should be half an hour only, every day. She placed her watch in good faith on the piano shelf—fatal error—and the series began. It was not that Louis disliked music; quite the contrary. Had not his parents but recently taken him to Boston Music Hall, there to hear a great Oratorio rendered by the Handel and Haydn Society? Had he not been overwhelmed by the rich volume and splendor of choral harmonies—again a new and revealing world? Had he not thrilled to the call: “Lift up your heads, O ye gates; and be ye lift up, ye everlasting doors; and the King of Glory shall come in.”
===Chinese===
他在学校进步得真好，他母亲这么想——因为他的老师如此出具了证明，缘由不详——也许是想掩盖真相——于是她认定，该让他学弹钢琴了。路易斯另有想法。妈妈态度决绝，路易斯屈服了。妈妈保证每天只弹半小时。她怀着好意把怀表搁在琴架上——致命的错误——这个系列便开始了。这倒不是说路易斯不喜欢音乐；恰恰相反。他父母不是前不久才带他去波士顿音乐厅（Boston Music Hall），在那里听了亨德尔与海顿协会（Handel and Haydn Society）演出的一部大型清唱剧（Oratorio）么？他不是曾被合唱和声那丰沛的音量与壮丽彻底淹没——又一个崭新而启人眼界的世界么？他不是曾为那呼声而战栗么：「众城门哪，你们要抬起头来！永久的门户，你们要被举起！那荣耀的王将要进来。」

===Original===
Was he not always teasing his mother to play for him any one of a group of brilliant five-fingered exercises arranged as stately composition? No; Louis loved his Mamma but hated the piano when annexed to himself. So the series moved on to disaster. The five-finger work bored him, the dinky tunes enraged him; he watched the watch, he kicked the piano, he struck false notes, he became utterly unruly; and at the agonizing end of one especially bad half-hour, Mamma burst into hysterical tears; and Louis, seeing the damage he had done, threw his arms about her neck and cried his heart out with her. Thus the series ended, by mutual understanding and Mamma’s forgiveness—as Mamma’s tears still flowed from bitterly swollen eyes, as she gazed blindly in unspeakable sorrow at her repentant but incorrigible son. But—let it be said in a whisper—Mamma should have known that Louis’s hands were not made for the piano. Louis did not know it; yet there lay all the trouble.
===Chinese===
他不是总在缠着母亲，求她弹那一组灿然生辉的五指练习中的任何一首么——那些练习被编排成俨然堂皇的乐曲。不；路易斯爱他的妈妈，却恨那架一旦归到他名下的钢琴。于是这个系列一路走向灾难。五指练习令他厌倦，那些小里小气的曲子惹他冒火；他盯着那块表，他踢钢琴，他敲出错音，他闹得全无规矩；在一个格外糟糕的半小时的痛苦收尾处，妈妈失声痛哭，歇斯底里；路易斯眼见自己闯下的祸，一把搂住她的脖子，陪她哭了个肝肠寸断。这个系列就这样收了场——以双方的谅解和妈妈的宽恕收场——妈妈的泪水仍从哭肿了的眼睛里涌出，她茫茫然地凝望着，怀着难以言说的悲伤，望着她那个悔而不改的儿子。可是——这话得小声说——妈妈本该知道，路易斯那双手天生不是为钢琴造的。路易斯自己并不知道；然而一切症结恰恰在此。

===Original===
Then the father thought he would teach his son drawing. His son thought otherwise. His son detested drawing. The prospect of copying a lithographic plate setting forth a mangle, a stepladder, a table, a mop and a pail, was not alluring. Louis demurred. Father thought a thrashing would help along some. He started in. A she-wolf glared. He quailed—End of stillborn drawing lesson. No series.
===Chinese===
后来，父亲想教儿子画画。儿子另有想法。儿子深恶画画。把一页石版画临摹下来——画的是一架轧干机、一座折梯、一张桌子、一把拖把和一只提桶——这样的前景实在诱人不起来。路易斯表示不愿。父亲觉得一顿鞭子大约能帮上些忙。他动手了。母狼目光灼灼。他怯了——绘画课就此流产。没有系列。

===Original===
Meanwhile the name of the village of South Reading was, by popular vote, changed to Wakefield. Cyrus Wakefield, rattan magnate, thought it good business to offer a new town hall in exchange for his name. The townspeople thought so too. The deed was done; both deeds were done; and, as if on a magic carpet the farm that Louis had lived on floated from South Reading into Wakefield—meanwhile remaining stationary as of yore. This occurred in the summer of 1868 when Louis was in his twelfth year.
===Chinese===
与此同时，南雷丁村经公众投票，把村名改成了韦克菲尔德（Wakefield）。藤器业大亨赛勒斯·韦克菲尔德（Cyrus Wakefield）认为，捐一座新市政厅来换用自己的名字，是桩好买卖。镇民们也这么认为。买卖做成了；两桩买卖都做成了；于是，仿佛驾着魔毯，路易斯住惯的那座农庄从南雷丁飘进了韦克菲尔德——而与此同时，它一如既往，纹丝未动。此事发生在 1868 年夏天，路易斯那年十二岁。

===Original===
Meanwhile, also, in 1868, a new school building was in course of construction on new made land in the Back Bay district. It was to be up to date in all respects, and was to be called The Rice Grammar School Building.
===Chinese===
同样在这期间，1868 年，后湾区（Back Bay）新填出的土地上，一座新校舍正在兴建。它将样样赶上时代，并将命名为赖斯文法学校（The Rice Grammar School）大楼。

===Original===
In the winter of this year, Mamma, for the fifth time, was stricken with diphtheria and her life despaired of. She pulled through on a perilous margin. Father, now thoroughly frightened, finally got it through his head that the east winds meant death. So in the summer of 1869 he moved his family to Chicago—leaving Louis behind, to live with his grandparents, and continue his education. Louis sobbed on his mother’s shoulder, but was much relieved to say to his father: Goodbye! Now he was free!
===Chinese===
这年冬天，妈妈第五次染上白喉，性命几至不救。她在一道危殆的窄边上挺了过来。父亲这下吓坏了，终于把这道理灌进了脑子：东风就意味着死亡。于是 1869 年夏天，他举家迁往芝加哥（Chicago）——把路易斯留了下来，让他住在外祖父外祖母家，继续学业。路易斯伏在母亲肩头抽泣，可对着父亲道别时，心里却大大松了口气：再见！这下他自由了！

## XI Chicago / 十一　芝加哥

===Original===
Heard and seen by all stands the word **personality**, in solitary and unique grandeur. Heard and seen by all stands the word Personality, eminent, respectable, much admired.

Heard and seen by all in the crowd it calls together, and through which it deftly wanders like a shrewd hunchback, the word personality, now a dwarf, grimaces salaciously.

And now it is a word on fire; a tiger in the jungle; a python hanging from the limb, very still.

===Chinese===
**个性**（personality）这个词，人人得而闻之、得而见之，孑然独立，独具尊崇的气象。个性（Personality）这个词，人人得而闻之、得而见之，声名卓著，体面可敬，广受仰慕。

在它召集起来、又任它像狡黠的驼子般轻巧穿行的人群里，人人都听闻、都眼见这个词——个性，如今成了个侏儒，正淫邪地做着鬼脸。

而此刻，它是一个燃烧着的词；丛林中的一只猛虎；一条蟒蛇，倒挂在树枝上，纹丝不动。

===Original===
How deep, how shallow is that which we call the soul.

How monstrous, how fluent, how vagrant and timorous, how alert are the living things we call words. They are the giants and the fairies, the hobgoblins and the sprites; the warrior and the priest, the lowly and the high; the watchdog and the sheep; the tyrant and the slave﻿—of that wonder-world we call speech.

How like hammers they strike. How like aspens they quiver. How like a crystal pool, a rivulet therefrom, becomes a river moving sinuously between the hills, growing stronger, broader as its affluents pour in their tributary power; and now looms the estuary, and the Ocean of Life.

===Chinese===
我们称之为灵魂的那个东西，何其深，又何其浅。

我们称之为词的那些活物，何其怪异，何其流利，何其漂泊无定而胆怯，又何其警醒。它们是巨人与仙子，是妖精与小鬼；是武士与祭司，是卑贱者与高贵者；是看门狗与绵羊；是暴君与奴隶——皆属于我们称之为言语的那个奇境世界。

它们敲击起来，多么像铁锤。它们颤动起来，多么像白杨。它们又多么像一泓晶亮的池塘、一缕自池中淌出的小溪（rivulet），渐渐化作一条在群丘之间蜿蜒行进的河，随着一条条支流倾注各自的水势，愈流愈强、愈流愈宽；于是河口隐然在望，接着便是生命的大洋。

===Original===
Words are most malignant, the most treacherous possession of mankind. They are saturated with the sorrows of all time. They hold in most unstable equilibrium the vast heritage of man’s folly, his despair, his wrestling with the angel whose name is Fate; his vanity, his pride before a fall, his ever-resurrecting hope﻿—arising as a winged spirit from the grave of disaster, to flit in the sunshine for a while, to return to the dust and arise again as his civilizations, so laboriously built up, have crumbled one by one. And yet all the beauty, all the joy, all the love that man has known, all his kindness, all his yearnings, all his dreams for better things; his passionate desire for peace and an anchorage within a universe that has filled him with fear and mystery and adoration; his daily round of toil, and commonplaces; his assumption of things as they are; his lofty and sublime contemplations, his gorgeous imageries; his valor, his dogged will, his patience in long suffering, his ecstacies, his sacrifices small and great﻿—even to the casting aside of his life for a thought, a compassion, an ambition﻿—all these are held bound up in words; hence words are dangerous when let loose. They may mean man’s destruction, they may signify a way out of the dark. For Light is a word, Courage is a word, and Vision is another. Therefore, it is wise to handle words with caution. Their content is so complex and explosive; and in combinations they may work beautiful or dreadful things.

===Chinese===
词语是人类所有占有物中最恶毒、最阴险的东西。它们浸透了亘古以来的一切忧伤。它们以最不稳固的平衡，托住人类愚蠢的浩大遗产——他的绝望，他与那位名叫「命运」的天使的角力；他的虚荣，他跌倒之前的骄矜，他那死而复生的希望——那希望像一位生翼的精灵从灾难的坟墓里升起，在阳光下飞舞片刻，复归于尘土，又随着他那营建得何其艰辛的文明一座座相继崩塌，再度升起。然而，人类所知的一切美、一切欢乐、一切爱，他的一切仁善，一切渴慕，一切对更美好事物的梦想；他对和平的热切向往，向往在这个令他充满恐惧、神秘与崇敬的宇宙中觅得一处锚地；他日复一日的劳碌与日常琐屑；他对事物现状的默认；他那崇高庄严的沉思，他那绚烂的意象；他的英勇，他的顽韧意志，他在长期苦难中的坚忍，他的狂喜，他或小或大的牺牲——甚至为着一个念头、一念恻隐、一个抱负而抛舍性命——这一切都捆缚收纳在词语之中；因此，词语一经放开，便很危险。它们可能意味着人类的毁灭，也可能昭示一条走出黑暗的路径。因为「光」是一个词，「勇气」是一个词，「远见」（Vision）又是一个。因此，审慎地拿捏词语才是明智。它们的内容如此复杂、如此易爆；一经组合，既能成就美好的事物，也能成就可怕的事物。

===Original===
All these thoughts have flowed from the one word, Personality, with which we began.

===Chinese===
这一切思绪，都是从我们起头所用的那一个词——个性——之中流泻出来的。

===Original===
At Louis’s age upon reaching Chicago, personality meant little as a formal word. He recognized by sight and feeling, by observing action and appearances, many of the phases of the powers of man upon which a word is built for use.

For words in themselves he had come to form a passing aversion, since he had noted their tendency to eclipse the vibrant values of immediate reality. Therefore, he preferred to think and feel and contemplate without the use of words. Indeed, one of his favorite pastimes was deliberately to think and feel and contemplate without the use of words, to create thus a wordless universe, with himself, silent, at the center of it all. Thus came about a widening clarity; an increased sensitiveness to values; a separate isolation of the permanent and the ephemeral; and it seemed, also, as though within his small, self-created silence he listened to the strident noises of the world as coming from without. All this Louis did with buoyant jocularity, for fun, for “practice” as he called it. And yet now and then a word came to him of a sudden, in surprise, a sort of keyword that unlocked, that opened and revealed. Among such was the word self-expression, which gave him a rude shock of hilarity and wonder. He said: “What!”﻿—which expressed quite well what he meant.

===Chinese===
以路易斯抵达芝加哥（Chicago）时的年纪，「个性」作为一个正式的词并没有多大分量。他凭看视与感受，凭观察行动与外表，认得出人的种种力量的诸多面貌——一个词正是围绕这些力量造出来、供人使用的。

对词语本身，他已生出一阵子的反感，因为他注意到它们有一种倾向，会把眼前现实那活泼泼的价值遮蔽掉。因此，他宁愿不用词语去思想、去感受、去沉思。的确，他心爱的消遣之一，便是刻意不用词语去思想、去感受、去沉思，从而营造出一个无声的宇宙，而他自己缄默不语，端坐在这一切的中心。于是有了一种不断拓宽的澄明；对价值的敏感与日俱增；恒久的与短暂的东西各自分离、各自孤立；而且，在他自己营造的这一小片寂静之中，世界的喧嚣仿佛都是从外面传来的。这一切路易斯都做得兴致盎然，为了好玩，为了他所称的「练习」。然而，时不时会突然冒出一个词来，出其不意，像一把开门的钥匙，把门打开、把内里揭示出来。其中就有「自我表现」（self-expression）这个词，它给了他一阵又欢畅又惊异的粗暴冲击。他说：「什么！」——这一声把他心里的意思表达得再贴切不过。

===Original===
For the first week in the strange city, Louis was the prodigal returned; and the fatted calf was offered up in joy. The next week he spent in exploration. As everybody said: “Chicago had risen phoenix-like from its ashes.” But many ashes remained, and the sense of ruin was still blended with ambition of recovery. Louis thought it all magnificent and wild: A crude extravaganza: An intoxicating rawness: A sense of big things to be done. For “Big” was the word. “Biggest” was preferred, and the “biggest in the world” was the braggart phrase on every tongue. Chicago had had the biggest conflagration “in the world.” It was the biggest grain and lumber market “in the world.” It slaughtered more hogs than any city “in the world.” It was the greatest railroad center, the greatest this, and the greatest that. It shouted itself hoarse in *reclame*. The shouters could not well be classed with the proverbial liars of Ecclesiastes, because what they said was true; and had they said, in the din, we are the crudest, rawest, most savagely ambitious dreamers and would-be doers in the world, that also might be true. For with much gloating of self-flattering they bragged: “We are the most heavily mortgaged city in the world.” Louis rather liked all this, for his eye was ever on the boundless prairie and the mighty lake. All this frothing at the mouth amused him at first, but soon he saw the primal power assuming self-expression amid nature’s impelling urge. These men had vision. What they saw was real, they saw it as destiny.

===Chinese===
在这座陌生城市的第一周，路易斯是个归来的浪子；肥牛犊欢天喜地宰了端上。第二周他用来四处踏勘。正如人人所说：「芝加哥已经凤凰涅槃般从灰烬中升起来了。」但灰烬仍所在多有，废墟之感犹与复苏的雄心交织在一处。路易斯觉得这一切壮丽而狂野：一出粗粝的狂想剧：一种醉人的生猛：一种有大事业可干之感。因为「大」正是这里的口头禅。人们更爱说「最大」，而「全世界最大」更是人人挂在嘴边的自夸之辞。芝加哥有过「全世界」最大的一场大火。它是「全世界」最大的粮食与木材市场。它屠宰的猪比「全世界」任何城市都多。它是最大的铁路枢纽，最大的这个，最大的那个。它在自吹自擂的喧腾（*réclame*）里喊哑了嗓子。这些喊话的人，倒不好归入《传道书》（Ecclesiastes）里那句老话所说的说谎者之列，因为他们说的都是实话；倘若他们在这片喧腾里说：我们是全世界最粗粝、最生猛、野心最狂悍的梦想家与准实干家，这话说不定也是真的。因为他们自吹自擂、得意洋洋地夸口道：「我们是全世界抵押负担最重的城市。」路易斯倒相当喜欢这一切，因为他的眼睛始终望着那无边的大草原与浩渺的大湖。这种口沫横飞的做派起初只让他觉得好笑，但很快他便看到，一种原初的力量正在大自然的驱策之中开始自我表现。这些人是有远见的。他们看见的东西是真的，他们把它看成了命运。

===Original===
The elevated wooden sidewalks in the business district, with steps at each street corner, seemed shabby and grotesque; but when Louis learned that this meant that the city had determined to raise itself three feet more out of the mud, his soul declared that this resolve meant high courage; that the idea was big; that there must be big men here. The shabby walks now became a symbol of stout hearts.

The pavements were vile, because hastily laid; they erupted here and there and everywhere in ooze. Most of the buildings, too, were paltry. When Louis came to understand the vast area of disaster, he saw clearly and with applause that this new half-built city was a hasty improvization made in dire need by men who did not falter. And again spread out in thought, the boundless prairie and the mighty lake, and what they meant for men of destiny, even as the city lay stretched out, unseemly as a Caliban.

In spite of the panic, there was stir; an energy that made him tingle to be in the game.

===Chinese===
商业区里架高的木制人行道，每个街角都设着台阶，看上去寒酸而怪诞；但当路易斯得知这意味着全城已下定决心，要把自己再从泥泞里抬高三英尺，他的灵魂宣告：这一决心意味着高度的勇气；这个想法很大；这里必定有大人物。那些寒酸的人行道，如今便成了坚毅之心的象征。

路面很糟糕，因为铺得仓促；这儿那儿、到处渗冒泥浆。大多数房屋也寒碜简陋。当路易斯明白了灾区面积的广袤，他清楚地、带着喝彩地看到：这座半成的新城，是一群不曾迟疑的人在大急需之中仓促而成的即兴之作。于是在思绪里再度铺展开来的，是无边的大草原与浩渺的大湖，以及它们对负有天命之人的意义——而这座城就那样摊开着，丑陋得像一个凯列班（Caliban）。

尽管金融恐慌未消，城里仍有躁动；有一股劲头，令他浑身发痒，恨不得投身局中。

===Original===
So he bethought him he would enter the office of some architect; for a few buildings showed talent in design, and a certain stability. Outstanding among these was the Portland Block, a four-story structure of pressed brick and sandstone at Washington and Dearborn Streets. So he inquired concerning the architect of this structure and was told the name was Jenney: Major Jenney; or in full, Major William Le Baron Jenney. There were still some buildings under way, or arranged for, on the momentum of pre-panic days, though the town was otherwise badly hurt. A great fire, and a panic in finance, certainly made load enough for any community to carry, but Chicago, hard hit, bore up bravely.

Louis learned incidentally that the Portland Block had in fact been designed by a clever draftsman named Cudell. This gave him a shock. For he had supposed that all architects made buildings out of their own heads, not out of the heads of others. His experience in the office of Furness & Hewitt, in Philadelphia, it seems, had given him an erroneous idea. Yet the new knowledge cheered him in this hope: That he might some day make buildings out of his head for architects who did not have any heads of their own for such purpose.

===Chinese===
于是他打定主意，要进某位建筑师的办事处；因为已有寥寥几栋房子显出设计上的才气，与几分安稳。其中出挑的是波特兰大楼（Portland Block），一栋四层的压模砖与砂岩建筑，坐落在华盛顿街与迪尔伯恩街（Dearborn Street）转角。他便打听这栋房子的建筑师，得到的名字是詹尼（Jenney）：詹尼少校；全名，威廉·勒巴隆·詹尼少校（Major William Le Baron Jenney）。城里虽然别处创痕累累，靠着恐慌之前的余势，仍有几栋房子在建造中，或已定妥。一场大火，加上一场金融恐慌，对任何社群都是够重的担子了，但芝加哥虽然受创甚重，却硬朗地扛住了。

路易斯又附带听说，波特兰大楼其实出自一位聪明的绘图员之手，那人名叫卡德尔（Cudell）。这让他吃了一惊。因为他原以为所有建筑师造房子都是出自他们自己的头脑，而不是出自别人的头脑。看来，他在费城（Philadelphia）的富内斯与休伊特事务所（Furness & Hewitt）的那段经历，给了他一个错误的印象。然而这新知识又让他怀着一线欢喜的希望：有朝一日，他或许可以用自己的头脑，替那些自己长不出这种头脑的建筑师造房子。

===Original===
He had once supposed that the genius for creating ugliness was peculiarly a Yankee monopoly; but he later found in New York and Philadelphia that almost all the buildings in these cities were of the same crassness of type; a singularly sordid, vulgar vernacular in architectural speech. So when he found the same thing almost universally in evidence in Chicago, he assumed that this illiteracy was general, and a jargon peculiar to the American people at large. The only difference he could see between the vernacular of the East and that of the West was that one was older and staler; and he cited Fifth Avenue, New York, as an instance. It is true that, scattered through the east were architects of book-attainment in fair number, and a few of marked personality and red blood﻿—particularly one Henry Richardson, he of the strong arm and virile mind﻿—sole giant of his day. In Chicago there were two or three who were bookish and timid, and there were some who were intelligently conscientious in the interest of their clients. Among the latter may be mentioned Major Jenney. The Major was a free-and-easy cultured gentleman, but not an architect except by courtesy of terms. His true profession was that of engineer. He had received his technical training, or education at the École Polytechnique in France, and had served through the Civil War as Major of Engineers. He had been with Sherman on the march to the sea.

===Chinese===
他曾一度以为，创造丑陋的天才是扬基人（Yankee）独有的专利；但后来他在纽约（New York）与费城发现，这两座城的房子几乎清一色是同一种粗陋的类型；是建筑语言中一种出奇寒碜而庸俗的土白。待他发现芝加哥几乎无一处不是同一副景象，他便认定这种无知是普遍性的，是全美国民众特有的一套切口。他看不出东部的土白与西部的土白有什么分别，只除了一个更陈旧、更陈腐；他还举纽约的第五大道（Fifth Avenue）作例子。诚然，东部各地散布着相当一批有书本造诣的建筑师，也有寥寥几位个性鲜明、血气旺盛——尤其是一位亨利·理查森（Henry Richardson），此君臂力强健、心智雄浑——是他那个时代独一无二的巨人。芝加哥则有那么两三位书卷气而怯懦的，也有若干位为业主的利益精明尽责的。后一类里可以举出詹尼少校。少校是一位散淡而有教养的绅士，但说他是建筑师，只是出于措辞上的客气。他真正的本行是工程师。他受技术训练，或者说受教育，是在法国的巴黎综合理工学院（École Polytechnique），南北战争期间以工程兵少校的军衔服役到底。他曾随谢尔曼（Sherman）向大海进军。

===Original===
He spoke French with an accent so atrocious that it jarred Louis’s teeth, while his English speech jerked about as though it had St. Vitus’s dance. He was monstrously pop-eyed, with hanging mobile features, sensuous lips, and he disposed of matters easily in the manner of a war veteran who believed he knew what was what. Louis soon found out that the Major was not, really, in his heart, an engineer at all, but by nature, and *in toto*, a bon vivant, a gourmet. He lived at Riverside, a suburb, and Louis often smiled to see him carry home by their naked feet, with all plumage, a brace or two of choice wild ducks, or other game birds, or a rare and odorous cheese from abroad. And the Major knew his vintages, every one, and his sauces, every one; he also was a master of the chafing dish and the charcoal grille. All in all the Major was effusive; a hale fellow well met, an officer of the Loyal Legion, a welcome guest anywhere, but by preference a host. He was also an excellent raconteur, with a lively sense of humor and a certain piquancy of fancy that seemed Gallic. In his stories or his monologues, his unique vocal mannerisms or gyrations or gymnastics were a rich asset, as he squeaked or blew, or lost his voice, or ran in arpeggio from deep bass to harmonics, or took octaves, or fifths, or sevenths, or ninths in spasmodic splendor. His audience roared, for his stories were choice, and his voice, as one caught bits of it, was plastic, rich and sweet, and these bits, in sequence and collectively had a warming effect. The Major was really and truly funny. Louis thought him funny all the time, and noted with glee how akin were the Major’s thoughts to the vertiginous gyrations of his speech. Thus we have a semblance of the Major’s relations to the justly celebrated art of architecture.

===Chinese===
他说起法文来，口音可怕得硌得路易斯牙齿发酸，而他的英文说起来一颠一跳，仿佛害着圣维特斯舞蹈症（St. Vitus's dance）。他眼球暴突得吓人，五官松垂而多变，双唇肉感，办起事来轻松利落，一副自信什么都门儿清楚的退伍老兵做派。路易斯很快便发现，少校骨子里根本不是工程师，而是天性使然、彻头彻尾（in toto）的一个讲究口腹之欲的享乐家（bon vivant）、一位美食行家。他住在郊区里弗赛德（Riverside），路易斯常常看着他拎着一对上好的野鸭或别的猎禽——拎着它们光溜溜的脚、羽毛一根不拔——或一块海外来的稀罕而气味浓烈的奶酪走回家去，不禁莞尔。少校对葡萄酒的年份个个在行，对酱汁的调制样样精通；他还是火锅与炭烤架的一把好手。总而言之，少校是个热情外露的人；一个见面熟的豪爽汉子，忠诚军团（Loyal Legion）的军官，到哪儿都受欢迎的宾客，但他更乐意做东。他还是一位出色的说故事名家，幽默感活泼，还有一种似乎源自高卢人的奇思妙趣。在他的故事或独白里，他独有的嗓音怪癖、回旋与翻腾是一宗厚实的家底：他时而尖啸，时而喷气，时而失声，时而以琶音从深沉的低音一路跑上泛音，时而痉挛般辉煌地纵身跨越八度、五度、七度、九度。听众哄堂大笑，因为他的故事都是精选，他的嗓音——人们零零碎碎捕捉到的那些——柔韧、丰厚而甜美，这些碎片连缀起来、合拢起来，自有一种暖人的效果。少校是真真正正的滑稽。路易斯无时无刻不觉得他滑稽，并且窃喜地注意到，少校的思想与他的言语那令人眩晕的回旋何其相似。至此，我们对于少校与那门声名素著的建筑艺术之间的关系，也算有了个大概。

===Original===
The Major took Louis in immediately upon application, as he needed more help. And to the fact that Louis had been at Tech he attached the highest importance﻿—as alumni of any school are apt to do; so much for temperamental personality.

There was work enough in the office to keep five men busy and a boy, provided they took intervals of rest, which they did. In the Major’s absences, which were frequent and long, bedlam reigned. John Edelmann would mount a drawing table and make a howling stump speech on greenback currency, or single tax, while at the same time Louis, at the top of his voice, sang selections from the oratorios, beginning with his favorite, “Why Do the Nations so Furiously Rage Together”; and so all the force furiously raged together in joyous deviltry, and bang-bang-bang. For a moment Louis quieted the riot and sang, “Ye people rend your hearts, rend your hearts, but not your garments,” whereupon there followed a clamor of affronts directed toward Elijah the Prophet. The office rat suddenly appears: “Cheese it, Cullies; the Boss!”, which in high English signifies: “Gentlemen, Major William Le Baron Jenney, our esteemed benefactor, approaches!” Sudden silence, sudden industry, intense concentration. The Major enters and announces his pleasure in something less than three octaves. Thus the day’s work comes out fairly even. For “when they work they work; and when they don’t they just don’t.”

===Chinese===
路易斯一申请，少校当即把他收下了，因为正缺人手。而对路易斯上过理工学校（Tech）这一节，他看得无比要紧——任何学校的校友都免不了这样；所谓气质性的个性，由此可见一斑。

办事处里的活儿，足够让五个人加一个学徒忙个不停，只要他们隔三差五歇上一歇——他们也确实这么干了。少校常常出门，一去便是许久；他不在的时候，办事处便成了疯人院。约翰·埃德尔曼（John Edelmann）会蹬上绘图桌，就绿背纸币或单一税问题发表一篇咆哮的街头演说，与此同时路易斯扯开嗓子，唱起清唱剧（oratorio）的选段，头一支便是他的最爱，《万邦为何如此狂怒》；于是全所人马便在欢腾的胡闹中一道狂怒起来，乒乒乓乓。有一回路易斯压下这场骚乱，唱道：「你们这些民哪，要撕裂你们的心肠，撕裂心肠，却不要撕裂衣裳」，随即爆发出一阵冲着先知以利亚去的辱骂喧腾。办事处的眼线忽地现身：「歇菜，哥们儿；老板！」——翻成高雅的英语，意思是：「诸位先生，威廉·勒巴隆·詹尼少校，我们敬爱的恩主，驾到！」霎时鸦雀无声，霎时勤勉起来，全神贯注。少校进门，用不足三个八度的音域宣布了他的欣悦。这样一天的工作下来倒也扯平。因为「干的时候是真干；不干的时候嘛，就真不干」。

===Original===
On the stool next to Louis sat patient Martin Roche, now, and for many years, of Holabird & Roche. There was a tall, fleshy, mild-voiced American-German who had taught school; and a rachitic, sharp-faced, droll, nasal Yankee, who drawled comic cynicisms and did the engineering. “The old man,” he would say, “is some engineer.﻿ ﻿… Like the Almighty, he watches the ‘sparrow’s fall,’ but when it comes to the *tons* he’s a l-e-e-t-l-e shy now and then, and sometimes then and now. You fellows work for glory, but I just work for coin.” And then he rasped in song: “And as I said be-f-o-o-o-r, don’t fall in love with a groceryman what keeps a grocery store,” and thus he cackled on, as he figured strains; this time, he said, on a basis of three sparrows, while Louis hummed: “And as I said before, don’t fall in love with a groceryman what keeps a grocery store.”

===Chinese===
路易斯旁边的凳子上，坐着耐心的马丁·罗奇（Martin Roche）——如今，而且多年来，一直是霍拉伯德与罗奇事务所（Holabird & Roche）的人。还有一位高个子、肉乎乎、嗓音温和、教过书的美籍德裔；另有一个佝偻病似的、尖脸、滑稽、鼻音浓重的扬基佬，拖着腔调说些逗笑的挖苦话，兼做工程计算。老头子，他常说，也算得上是个工程师。……跟全能的上帝一样，「麻雀坠落」他都看在眼里，可一说到*吨*，他时不时就要差那么一丁——点儿，偶尔又时不时地不差。你们这帮家伙为荣耀干活，我只为钱干活。随后他沙着嗓子唱起来：「正像我以——前——说——过的，可别爱上一个开杂货铺的杂货郎」，就这样一边算着应变一边咯咯地念叨；这一回，他说，是按三只麻雀的基数算的；而路易斯则哼着：「正像我以前说过的，可别爱上一个开杂货铺的杂货郎。」

===Original===
John was the foreman. By nature indolent, by vanity and practice very rapid. He laughed to scorn and scattered to dust those that were slow; and would illustrate, in roseate tales, how fast he had done this and that. It speedily became evident that John was a hero-worshipper, as John blandly worshipped John in the presence of all; and Louis casually remarked that John’s unconsciousness of his own personality was remarkable to the point of the fabulous and the legendary, whereupon they became fast friends.

Louis had instantly noted in John a new personality; brawny, twenty-four, bearded, unkempt, careless, his voice rich, sonorous, modulant, his vocabulary an overflowing reservoir. A born orator﻿—he must talk or perish. His inveterate formula was, “I myself”﻿—did﻿—was﻿—said﻿—am﻿—think﻿—know﻿—to the sixteenth decimal and the *n*th power of egoism. It gradually dawned upon Louis that he had run across a **thinker**, a profound thinker, a man of immense range of reading, a brain of extraordinary keenness, strong, vivid, that ranged in its operations from saturnine intelligence concerning men and their motives, to the highest transcendentalisms of German metaphysics. He was as familiar with the great philosophers as with the daily newspapers. As an immediate psychologist, never before or since has Louis met his equal in vitality, in verity, and in perspicacity of thought. He, John, knew all that all the psychologists had written, and much, of his own discernment, that they but recently have begun to unveil. Louis found in John a highly gifted talker, and John found in Louis a practised listener, so their bond of union may be summed up in the token “I myself.”

===Chinese===
约翰是领班。天性懒散，凭着虚荣心与历练却又快手快脚。他把手脚慢的人讥为笑柄、碾成齑粉；还会用一篇篇玫瑰色的故事现身说法，这件那件事他当年干得有多快。很快就清楚了：约翰是一位英雄崇拜者，因为约翰当着众人的面，安详地崇拜着约翰；路易斯随口评了一句：约翰对自己个性的浑然不觉，已到了近乎神话传说的地步——两人从此成了莫逆之交。

路易斯一眼便在约翰身上认出了一种新的个性：筋骨壮硕，二十四岁，蓄着胡须，不修边幅，大大咧咧，嗓音丰厚、洪亮、抑扬有致，词汇像一座满溢的水库。天生的演说家——不开口便活不下去。他雷打不动的句式是「我本人」——做过——当过——说过——是——想——知道——精确到小数点后第十六位，膨胀到自我主义的*第 n 次幂*。路易斯渐渐恍然：他撞见了一位**思想家**，一位深刻的思想家，一位读书浩瀚的人，一副锋利非凡的头脑，强健、鲜活，其运思范围从对世人及其动机的阴郁洞悉，一直上达德国形而上学的至高超越之境。大哲学家们之于他，熟悉得一如日报。而作为一个直觉明快的心理学家，无论在此前还是此后，路易斯再没有遇到过一个能在活力上、在真切上、在思想的明彻上与他比肩的人。他，约翰，读遍了所有心理学家写下的全部东西，还有许多出于他自己眼力的东西——那些东西，心理学家们直到最近才开始揭开幕布。路易斯在约翰身上发现了一位天资极高的谈客，约翰在路易斯身上发现了一位训练有素的听客，于是两人结交的信物可以一言以蔽之：「我本人」。

===Original===
One day John explained his theory of *suppressed functions*; and Louis, startled, saw in a flash that this meant the real clue to the mystery that lay behind the veil of appearances. Louis was peculiarly subject to shock from unexpected explosion of a single word; and when the word “function” was detonated by the word “suppressed,” a new, an immense idea came suddenly into being and lit up his inner and his outer world as one. Thus, with John’s aid, Louis saw the outer and the inner world more clearly, and the world of men began to assume a semblance of form, and of function. But, alas, what he had assumed to be a single vast veil of mystery that might perhaps lift of a sudden, like a cloud, proved in experience to be a series of gossamer hangings that must slowly rise up one by one, in a grand transformation scene, such as he had viewed when, as a small boy, he saw *The Forty Thieves*, where all was transformed into reality by a child’s imagination. Now would it be possible for him, through the reverse power of imagination, to cause the veils of the hidden world to rise and reveal? On this threshold, for a passing moment, he faltered. Then resurging courage came.

Louis soon noticed that while *he himself* had a clear program in life, John had none. That all this talk, while of deep import to him, was for John merely luxurious self-indulgence and a luscious hour with parade of vanity; that he, the elder, regarded the younger with patronage, much as a bright child, but a tyro in the active world; while Louis saw that John was merely drifting. In this regard each kept his thoughts to himself, while encouraging the other.

===Chinese===
有一天，约翰讲解了他的「压抑功能」论（suppressed functions）；路易斯悚然一动，霎时间看出，这正是通往表象之幕背后那个谜底的真正线索。路易斯特别容易受单词意外爆响的冲击；当「功能」这个词被「压抑」一词引爆，一个崭新的、浩大的观念骤然成形，把他的内心世界与外部世界照得浑然一体。就这样，借着约翰之力，路易斯把外部世界与内心世界看得更清楚了，人的世界开始隐然显出形式与功能的模样。然而，唉，他原以为那是一道单一的、浩大的神秘之幕，说不定会像一片云似的骤然掀开；经验却证明，那是一重又一重轻纱似的垂幔，必须在一幕盛大的变换场景里，一重一重缓缓升起——就像他小时候看《四十大盗》（The Forty Thieves）时见到的那样，一切都被一个孩子的想象力化成了真实。那么，他现在能不能借着想象力的反向之力，叫那隐秘世界的重重帷幕也升起来、露出真相呢？在这道门槛上，他踌躇了一瞬。随即，重新涌起的勇气来了。

路易斯不久便察觉，*他自己*对人生有一套清晰的规划，约翰却没有。这一类谈话，于他事关重大，于约翰却不过是一场奢靡的自我沉溺、一段伴着虚荣游行的甘美时光；这位年长者居高临下地看待年幼者，只当他是个伶俐的孩子，一入实世便是个新手；而路易斯看得分明：约翰不过是在随波逐流。在这件事上，两人各怀心思，却又彼此打气。

===Original===
In Philadelphia, one hot summer’s evening, Louis had gone to the Academy of Music to hear a Thomas Concert. During the course of the program he had become listless, when of a sudden came the first bars of a piece so fiery, that, startled, all alert, he listened in amazement to the end. What was this? It was new﻿—brand new. The program now consulted, said: *Vorspiel*, Third Act, *Lohengrin*﻿—Richard Wagner. Who was Richard Wagner? Why had he never heard of him? He must look him up; for one could see at a glance that this piece was a work of genius.

He mentioned this episode to John Edelmann, shortly after they had become acquainted; and John said: “Why, at the North Side Turner Hall, Hans Balatka and his fine orchestra give a concert every Sunday afternoon, and Hans is introducing Wagner to Chicago; let’s go.” They went.

===Chinese===
还在费城的时候，一个炎热的夏夜，路易斯去音乐学院（Academy of Music）听一场托马斯音乐会（Thomas Concert）。节目过半，他已意兴阑珊，忽然响起了某一支曲子的头几个小节，火热逼人，他悚然一惊，全神贯注，惊异莫名地听到了终了。这是什么？这是新的东西——全新的东西。他查了查节目单，上面写着：《罗恩格林》（Lohengrin）第三幕前奏曲（Vorspiel）——理查德·瓦格纳（Richard Wagner）。理查德·瓦格纳是谁？他怎么从来没有听说过？他一定要去查一查；因为谁一眼便看得出，这件作品出自天才之手。

两人相识后不久，他把这段插曲讲给约翰·埃德尔曼听；约翰说：「嗐，北区的特纳厅（Turner Hall）里，汉斯·巴拉特卡（Hans Balatka）和他那支出色的乐队每个星期日下午都开一场音乐会，汉斯正在把瓦格纳介绍给芝加哥；咱们去吧。」他们去了。

===Original===
Louis heard the Pilgrims’ Chorus﻿—and raved. They went every Sunday afternoon until Spring. There followed in course, the *Vorspiel* to *Lohengrin*, to *Die Meistersinger*, to the *Flying Dutchman*, the *Ride of the Walkyrie*, the amazing fabric of the overture to *Tristan und Isolde*, the immense solemnity of Siegfried’s *Tod*, the exquisite shimmering beauty of the *Waldweben*.

Louis needed no interpreter. It was all plain to him. He saw it all. It was all as though addressed to himself alone. And as piece after piece was deployed, before his open mind, he saw arise a Mighty Personality﻿—a great Free Spirit, a Poet, a Master Craftsman, striding in power through a vast domain that was his own, that imagination and will had bodied forth out of himself. Suffice it﻿—as useless to say﻿—Louis became an ardent Wagnerite. Here, indeed, had been lifted a great veil, revealing anew, refreshing as dawn, the enormous power of man to build as a mirage, the fabric of his dreams, and with his wand of toil to make them real. Thus Louis’s heart was stirred, his courage was ten-folded in this raw city by the Great Lake in the West.

===Chinese===
路易斯听到了《朝圣者合唱》——为之如痴如狂。此后每个星期日下午他们都去，直到春天。循序而至的，有《罗恩格林》的前奏曲，有《纽伦堡的名歌手》（Die Meistersinger）的前奏曲，有《漂泊的荷兰人》（Flying Dutchman），有《女武神的骑行》（Ride of the Walkyrie），有《特里斯坦与伊索尔德》（Tristan und Isolde）序曲那惊人的织体，有《齐格弗里德之死》（Siegfried's Tod）那浩大的庄严，还有《森林细语》（Waldweben）那精妙闪烁的美。

路易斯不需要翻译。这一切对他而言明明白白。他全都看见了。这一切仿佛是单对他一个人诉说的。一曲又一曲在他敞开的心智面前次第展开，他看见一位强大的个性昂然升起——一位伟大的自由之灵，一位诗人，一位巨匠，挟着力量阔步穿行于一片属于他自己的辽阔疆域——那疆域是想象与意志从他自身之内孕育成形的。不必多说——说了也是白说——路易斯成了一位热忱的瓦格纳信徒。的确，在这里，一道大幕被掀开了，清新如黎明，重新显豁出人类那浩大的力量：像海市蜃楼一样筑起他梦想的织体，再用他辛劳的魔杖把它们化为真实。就这样，在这西部大湖之滨的生猛城市里，路易斯的心被点燃了，他的勇气增强了十倍。

===Original===
Yet John had the good sense to caution Louis to let the philosophers alone for a while; to let them lie in possession﻿—paraphrasing Siegfried’s Dragon﻿—as each had merely built an elaborate scaffolding, but no edifice within, and each was more concerned with the symmetry of his scaffold than with aught else, unless it be to scorn the flimsy scaffoldings of others. He said that Schopenhauer showed some intelligence, because he was a man of the world, while the others were more like spiders, weaving, in the gloom of obscurantism, festoons of cobwebs in their dens, far from the light of the world of men and things. That Louis had better let the *ding an sich*﻿—the ultimate thing﻿—alone, and keep his eyes on the world as it is; that he would find plenty to interest him there, and that if he had the eyesight he would find a great romance there, also a great tragedy. That﻿—quoting Carlyle﻿—he said: “The eye sees that which it brings the power to see”; which again shocked Louis; for the thought rose up: Maybe the veil is not without, but covers my own eyes; as John went on, preaching of the world of men and their significance, for worth or ill, in the social order, Louis again was shocked at the words “social order.”

===Chinese===
然而约翰有种清醒的头脑，告诫路易斯暂且放过那些哲学家；让他们安卧在自己占着的地盘上——这话是化用齐格弗里德那条龙的说法——因为他们每个人都只是搭起了一座精巧的脚手架，里面却没有房子，而且人人更关心自己脚手架的对称匀停，甚于其他一切，除非是在讥笑别人那单薄脚手架的时候。他说，叔本华（Schopenhauer）还算有些头脑，因为他是个入世之人；其余那些更像蜘蛛，在蒙昧主义的幽暗里，于各自的巢穴中织着一挂挂蛛网帘幕，远离人与事物的世界之光。他说路易斯最好别去碰「自在之物」（ding an sich）——那终极之物——而把眼睛放在世界的本来面目上；他在那里会找到足够多有趣的东西，而且只要有眼力，还会在那里找到一部伟大的罗曼史，还有一部伟大的悲剧。他还说——援引卡莱尔（Carlyle）的话——「眼睛看见的，只是它带来力量去看的东西」；这话又给了路易斯一记冲击；因为一个念头冒了上来：也许帷幕并不在外面，而是蒙在我自己的眼睛上；而当约翰继续布讲人的世界、讲人在社会秩序（social order）之中或善或恶的意义时，路易斯又被「社会秩序」这几个字惊了一下。

===Original===
But their talks were not always so strenuous and disturbing; for John was mercurial﻿—an inventor of self-moods﻿—a poseur, infatuated with the pessimistic attitudinizing he assumed at will, for the sake of the sensation of gazing into the mirror of his thoughts which reflected the image of one he deemed the greatest philosopher and psychic of all time, still unknown to the world. But John had many other moods, as many as he chose to summon, and on the whole, he was jolly bombastic, much alive, and in public, loud of speech in an overweening beggary to attract attention, and thereby feed his hungry vanity. But withal he was Louis’s warm friend, and showed it by a devotion and self-sacrifice singular in one so absorbed in self worship. And be this said here and now: The passing years have isolated and revealed John Edelmann, as unique in personality among fine and brilliant minds. Be assured he will not turn in his grave, unless in bliss, should he hear it said that he was the benefactor and Louis the parasite and profiteer.

===Chinese===
但他们的谈话并非总是这样紧张扰人；因为约翰是水银一般的人物——一位自我心绪的发明家——一个装腔作势的人，沉迷于随兴之所至摆出的悲观姿态，为的是享受凝望自己思想之镜的感觉——镜中映出的那个人像，他认定是有史以来最伟大的哲学家与通灵者，只是世界尚不知晓。不过约翰还有许多别的心绪，要多少有多少，全凭他召唤；大体上说，他快活而浮夸，生龙活虎，人前嗓门洪亮，以一种盛气凌人的乞求姿态招引注意，借此喂养他饥饿的虚荣。但他毕竟是路易斯热忱的朋友，并以一种在如此沉溺自我崇拜之人身上堪称罕见的忠悃与自我牺牲表现出这一点。并且有话在此刻说定：逝去的岁月已把约翰·埃德尔曼分离出来、显豁出来——在众多精美而聪颖的头脑之中，他的个性独一无二。请放心，倘若他有知，听到人们说他才是恩主、路易斯不过是寄生者与沾光者，他在坟墓里也不会翻身——除非是欢喜得翻身。

===Original===
They were both fond of exercise, and frequented the gymnasium. John, though not so very tall, was huge in bulk and over-muscled. He excelled in feats of strength, while Louis was dexterous and nimble in lighter work. As spring approached, John talked more and more about the “Lotus Club,” whose members had boat houses on the bank of the Calumet, near the bridge where the I.C.R.R. crosses. He spoke of a “Great Chief,” one William B. Curtis by name, who had founded the Club, who had beaten Dr. Winship at heavy lifting; was a champion all-round athlete, and had chosen the club name because of a bed of lotus not far down the sluggish stream. He had said briefly, he preferred the Greek word *Lotos* to the Latin *Lotus*.

So in the spring the two went to live in John’s boathouse. There were three other houses, one occupied by said William B. Curtis, who, when asked, said his middle name was Bill﻿—and “Bill” he was called. Louis was simply wild with joy over this new life. He was now actually a member of a real athletic club. He had never been a member of any club. And these young men, all older than he, were heroes in his eyes, if not demigods; they showed such skill in performance, and were so amiable toward a youngster. The mighty “Bill” was thirty-eight, so he said. He was the man of brains who never bragged. He was too cynical to brag, and deadly literal in speech. As a mathematician he had revised Haswell. His brain was hard, his manner human. He knew his anatomy, and had devised special exercises to develop each separate muscle in his body. So when in the sunlight he walked the pier for a plunge, he was a sight for the Greeks, and Louis was enraptured at the play of light and shade. He had won a barrel full of medals and he said he kept them there.

===Chinese===
两人都爱好运动，常去健身房。约翰虽说个子不算很高，却块头庞大、肌肉过剩。他擅长力量项目，路易斯则在轻巧活计上灵活敏捷。春天将近，约翰越来越多地谈起「莲花俱乐部」（Lotus Club），会员们在卡卢梅特河（Calumet）岸边拥有船屋，就在伊利诺伊中央铁路（I.C.R.R.）过河的那座桥附近。他说起一位「大首领」，名叫威廉·B·柯蒂斯（William B. Curtis），是俱乐部的创始人，举重上赢过温希普医生（Dr. Winship），是一位全能冠军运动员；俱乐部这个名字，是他因那条缓流而下不远处的一片莲花而选定的。他曾简短地说过，比起拉丁文的 Lotus，他更喜欢希腊文的 Lotos。

于是到了春天，两人便搬进约翰的船屋去住。另外还有三座船屋，其中一座便住着这位威廉·B·柯蒂斯；有人问起，他说自己的中间名叫比尔（Bill）——于是人人都叫他「比尔」。路易斯对这种新生活简直欢喜疯了。他如今当真成了一个正经体育俱乐部的会员。他从来还没有当过任何俱乐部的会员。这些年富力强的青年个个都比他年长，在他眼里都是英雄，若非半神的话；他们身手如此矫健，对一个少年又如此和蔼可亲。威严的「比尔」三十八岁——他自己说的。他是那种有头脑而从不吹嘘的人。他愤世得懒得吹嘘，说话刻板得吓人。作为数学家，他修订过哈斯韦尔（Haswell）的手册。他的头脑坚硬，为人却有血性。他通晓解剖学，为自己身体的每一块肌肉分别设计了专门的练习。所以每当他在阳光里走上栈桥、准备下水畅游一番，那景象足令希腊人称赏，路易斯更为那光影的流走而心醉神驰。他赢过满满一桶奖牌，他说他都存在那儿。

===Original===
By a strange paradox he detested display. He had no vanity. He had a quizzical sense of humor which he displayed when he said the club was no club, because there were no dues, no entrance fees, no bylaws. All that was needed in an applicant was a sound constitution and a paper shell. And yet he said he had named the Club the Lotus because of his love of flowers, and the nearby presence of the lotus field. His brain was remarkably well stocked with varied information of the so-called higher sort, but he seldom talked of such, except briefly in derision. He was the exact opposite of John, but with an equal egoism which he kept under cover, and which passed as modesty﻿—although he cared not in the least. All this interested Louis, who was beginning to observe men as individuals, and to study personalities; to observe in particular the working of men’s brains; for he had begun to notice, with keen and growing interest, that the thoughts of a man corresponded exactly to his real nature. So Louis discerned in Bill a highly trained mind, self-centered and selfish in its nature. Louis guessed that the man had a past; that at least there was something hidden. So he spoke to John, who said: “You are right. Bill is not in athletics for fun, but for his health. Medals interest him only as tokens of condition. When he was a young man he was attacked by consumption. The doctors gave him up. Bill took to open air exercise. With his scientific brain you can imagine how systematically he went about it. He effected a cure; but now he has only one lung﻿—would you guess it?” Briefly to complete the story of this man, the most remarkable that has ever appeared in the field of amateur athletics﻿—he became editor of *Wilkes Spirit of the Times* and remained such for years. At the age of sixty-three he, with a companion, was making the ascent of Mt. Washington when a blizzard overtook them near the summit. The bodies were found a quarter of a mile apart.

===Chinese===
出奇的是，他偏偏厌恶炫耀。他没有虚荣心。他有一种揶揄式的幽默感，这在他声称本俱乐部根本算不上俱乐部时表露无遗——因为不收会费，不收入门费，也没有章程。申请人所需要的，不过是一副健全的体格，外加一层纸壳。可他又说，他之所以给俱乐部起名「莲花」，是因为他爱花，也因为莲花田就在近旁。他的头脑里囤积着丰富的所谓高等门类的杂学，却绝少谈起，除非简短地讥诮几句。他与约翰恰恰相反，自我主义却不相上下，只是掖着藏着，旁人便当它是谦逊——虽然他毫不在乎。这一切都让路易斯兴味盎然，他正开始把人当作个体来观察，研究种种个性；尤其爱观察人的头脑如何运转；因为他已开始怀着敏锐而与日俱增的兴趣注意到：一个人的思想，与他真实的本性分毫不差地相合。于是路易斯在比尔身上辨出一副训练有素的头脑，本性以自我为中心、自私自利。路易斯猜此人有一段过往；至少有些隐情。他便去问约翰，约翰说：「你说对了。比尔搞体育不是为了好玩，是为了他的健康。奖牌之于他，只是身体状态的凭证。他年轻的时候染上了肺痨。医生放弃了他。比尔开始做户外锻炼。凭他那颗科学的头脑，你可以想象他进行得多么有条不紊。他把自己治好了；可如今他只剩一叶肺——你猜得到吗？」简短补完此人的故事——业余体育领域出现过的最非凡的人物——他后来成了《威尔克斯〈时代精神〉报》（Wilkes Spirit of the Times）的主编，一干多年。六十三岁那年，他偕一位同伴攀登华盛顿山（Mt. Washington），在接近峰顶处遭遇暴风雪。两人的遗体在相距四分之一英里的地方被找到。

===Original===
The effect of Bill Curtis upon Louis was not merely that of a magnificent athlete and man of brains, but primarily, and most valuably that of exemplar in the use of the imagination and the will, doggedly to carry out a program. That a consumptive should have risen to become a great athlete, was enough for him. The living fact profoundly and permanently strengthened Louis’s courage in carrying out his own program. Though Louis did not especially warm up to the man, because their natures were not sufficiently alike, he has never forgotten what he then owed to the force of example of a clear brain. So Louis added “Bill” to his growing collection of personalities.

===Chinese===
比尔·柯蒂斯对路易斯的影响，不只是一位杰出的运动员与有头脑的人，而首先是、也最可贵的是一位榜样：示范了如何运用想象力与意志力，百折不回地执行一套方案。一个痨病之人竟能崛起而成为伟大的运动员，这一件事对他来说已经足够。这个活生生的事实，深切而永久地增强了路易斯执行自己方案的勇气。虽然路易斯对这个人并未特别亲近起来——两人的本性不够相近——他却从未忘记自己当时受惠于一颗清明头脑的榜样之力。就这样，路易斯把他日渐充盈的个性收藏又添了一位：「比尔」。

---

===Original===
In the carrying out of his own program Louis’s thoughts turned definitely towards France; which meant, specifically, the École des Beaux Arts. He wished now to go to the fountain head of theory. Of practice he had enough for present purpose.

===Chinese===
为了执行自己的方案，路易斯的念头明确地转向了法国；具体说，就是巴黎美术学院（École des Beaux-Arts）。他此刻想去理论的源头。实践方面，就眼前的目的而言，他已足够。

===Original===
Thus on 10 July, 1874, he sailed from New York on the steamship *Britannic*, on her maiden trip eastward. Before she left her pier there were grand doings aboard﻿—flowers, speeches, high society. For she was proclaimed “The Pride of the Seas.” She displaced three thousand tons. She was headed for Liverpool.

Prior to leaving, Louis called again upon his friend Stratton in New York, and was given further pointers﻿—first of all, to call at the American Legation.

Louis found the ocean trip disappointing and stupid, with exception of the ship’s great vertical engines and deep stokehole, the various apparatus, and the working of the ship by officers and crew, which he studied carefully, as he had become much interested in engineering.

===Chinese===
就这样，1874 年 7 月 10 日，他从纽约搭乘「不列颠尼克」号（Britannic）汽船启航，那是它首航东行。离泊之前，船上好一阵盛大的排场——鲜花，演说，上流社交。因为它被宣布为「海洋的骄傲」。它的排水量三千吨。它的目的地是利物浦（Liverpool）。

行前，路易斯在纽约再度拜访了他的朋友斯特拉顿（Stratton），又得了几条指点——头一条：先到美国公使馆（American Legation）走一遭。

路易斯觉得这趟海上之旅乏味而愚蠢，唯独例外的是船上巨大的立式发动机与深深的锅炉舱，各式机械装置，以及军官与船员操纵这艘船的种种功夫——这些他都仔细研究了，因为他已对工程学大感兴趣。

===Original===
While Louis was leaning on the railing, watching, with vague emotion, his native land fade in the mist, and sink from sight, as though irrevocably lost, he felt a pang of nostalgia; the sea seemed so lonely﻿—after brisk excitement. Near by at the rail were others also watching the land disappear. As it became dim, a grating voice spoke out: “Thank God we have seen the last of the damned Yankees.” The words were said in savage bitterness and contempt. Another voice agreed. Louis turned to the left and saw two short swarthy men, black bearded, black eyed. For a moment Louis thought it would be nice to throw them both overboard. He looked at them, wide-eyed, with something of the sort in view, and they talked on in lower tones. Louis was puzzled by the speech. Why “damned Yankees” he asked himself. Why this hatred, this anathema? The phrase sounded racial; it stuck in his mind like a burr. It was said with such conviction as to seem impersonal; as though included in something larger. Never had he heard such virulence addressed to his own people. He pondered long over this; were the “Yankees” a hated people? If so, who hated them?

===Chinese===
路易斯倚着栏杆，怀着隐隐的情绪，望着故土在雾中淡去、沉出视野，仿佛一去不返地失落了，他心头掠过一阵乡愁的刺痛；在一番兴奋忙碌之后，海显得如此孤寂。栏杆近旁还有别人，也在望着陆地消失。待它模糊下去，一个刺耳的声音开了口：「谢天谢地，总算把那帮该死的扬基佬看尽了。」这话是带着狂烈的怨毒与轻蔑说出来的。另一个声音附和着。路易斯向左转过身，看见两个矮个子、面色黝黑的男人，黑胡子，黑眼睛。有一瞬间，路易斯觉得把他们两个都扔下海去倒是件美事。他睁大眼睛打量着他们，心里盘算着这一类的事，他们便压低了嗓门继续谈。路易斯被这句话弄糊涂了。为什么是「该死的扬基佬」，他问自己。为什么有这般仇恨，这般咒诅？这说法听着像是种族性的；它像一枚芒刺扎在他心里。说得那样斩钉截铁，倒显得并非针对个人；仿佛隶属于某种更大的东西。他从未听过这样的恶毒，冲着他自己的族人倾泻。他对此思索良久：「扬基佬」是一个被人仇恨的民族么？若是，又是谁恨他们？

===Original===
Louis did not know a soul aboard. He was proud of the ship, proud to be on it, but he was lonesome, and no one paid the slightest heed as he prowled up and down, in and out. The weather was fair all the way. The waves seemed eternally to roll and roll﻿—without crests. A vast expanse of water, dark blue, almost black; the circular horizon always present and only fifteen miles away. Never had his world seemed so small in fact, yet so limitless and grim in suggestion. He seemed to be always at the top of the world, always in the selfsame spot, always in the midst of deadening monotony; day after day not a sail in sight, not a sign of a storm. Day after day confined to a solitary ship moving on through a wilderness of water, the vessel rhythmically rolling and heaving in its course, night and day, night and day; would it never end? Laughter, aboard, had long since ceased. Where was the romance of the high seas? The end came in a total lapse of ten days. The waters turned blue and then green. The boat came to a stop off Queenstown. Enshrouded in heavy mist, Louis saw a coastline of mountains or high hills. This is all he ever saw of Ireland.

===Chinese===
船上路易斯一个熟人也没有。他为这艘船骄傲，也以乘上它为荣，可他寂寞；他上上下下、进进出出地溜达，没有一个人稍加理会。一路天气晴好。波浪仿佛永远翻滚又翻滚——却不翻起浪峰。一片浩瀚的水，深蓝，近乎黑色；圆形的地平线始终在场，而且只在十五英里之外。他的世界事实上从未显得如此之小，在意想中却从未显得如此无边而严峻。他仿佛永远处在世界的顶点，永远在同一个地点，永远身处一种令人麻木的单调之中；日复一日，望不见一片帆，看不见一场风暴的迹象。日复一日，局促在一艘孤船上，穿行于一片水的荒野，船身有节律地侧滚、起伏，夜以继日，夜以继日；它永远到不了头么？船上的笑声早已绝迹。大海的罗曼史在哪里？第十天，一切戛然而止。海水转蓝，又转绿。船在昆士敦（Queenstown）外海停了下来。裹在浓雾之中，路易斯望见一道山峦或高丘构成的海岸。这就是他对爱尔兰（Ireland）的全部所见。

===Original===
The way along St. George’s Channel seemed glorious. The clear, deep waters, and the glimpses of coastline restored his spirits as he felt his normal condition of clarity return. Here at last was an old world, which, as a new world he was to discover. How high his hopes, how buoyant his thoughts, as they swung into the Mersey. England came near to him, and nearer, then slowly nearer, then in contact, as the ship came to dock. Then came all the bustle and the joyous greetings about him, as Louis pressed his foot on English soil. Ah, what fluttering emotion, the overflow of bubbling youth. At last, at last, he had arrived where for years he had dreamed to come, and the broad Atlantic now lay between him and his native land. Now was to come that Great Adventure, which, as a joyous youth *sans peur* he faced with elation, and a confidence known only to pure fools. He stayed but a day or two in Liverpool, for his immediate objective was London. He was at pains to make it a daylight ride, for he wished to give his eyes all the treat of novelty.

===Chinese===
沿圣乔治海峡（St. George's Channel）而行的水路显得壮丽。清澈的深水，海岸线的一瞥一现，让他精神复原，他感到自己惯常的澄明状态回来了。这终于是一个旧世界了——而他要作为一个新世界去发现它。船转入默西河（Mersey）时，他的希望何其高涨，思绪何其轻扬。英格兰向他靠近，更近，缓缓更近，随即相触，船靠了码头。当路易斯一脚踏上英格兰的土地，四周尽是喧忙与道贺的欢声。啊，怎样的悸动心情，怎样沸腾青春的漫溢。终于，终于，他抵达了多年来梦想前来的地方，而浩瀚的大西洋已横在他与故土之间。如今要来的是那场大冒险——作为一个快乐的青年，他*无所畏惧*（sans peur），意气飞扬地迎上前去，怀着唯有纯粹的傻瓜才有的那种自信。他在利物浦只逗留了一两天，因为他眼下的目标是伦敦（London）。他特意把行程安排成白日行车，因为他要让眼睛饱餐一顿新奇。

===Original===
And what he saw was a finished land﻿—something that had ripened through the centuries. This finished land impressed him with a sense of the faraway. It did not seem to vibrate; and, *sub audite*, came to him a stream of ancient tales. He found quiet, unobtrusive charm in the countryside, he noted patches of crops arranged with a precision, an inch by inch economy of space, that gave him a feeling that the people of this Island must be crowded, as each small farm was pressed tight against its neighbors, and each crop pressed tight against the others. This tightness confirmed his impression of a finished land. It was a revelation to him, who had come from the middle west of America with its vast prairies sparsely settled. He noticed, too, the amazing solidity of the roadbed, and the smoothness with which the train flew on at high speed. He saw, too, that there were no grade crossings; that everything was immensely solid in contrast to the flimsiness with which he was familiar. And the country roads were wonderful, so sound, so smooth, as they wound their way; and the charming streams he crossed; the verdure, the lovely groves, the hamlets, the villages, the many church spires rising from masses of green; the rural air everywhere, charmed him with the softness, the velvet, the down of age and tradition. Surely it was a finished land, beautifully finished, sturdy, vigorous, solid, set, and he felt the power of this land, this tight-crowded land, and he thought as an inference it must be true that in such a crowded land its people must be tightly self-conscious and self-centered. But he did not as yet clearly discern the portrait of this Island crowded on all sides by the sea.

===Chinese===
而他所见的，是一片完工的土地——一样历经数百年而熟透了的东西。这片完工的土地给他一种悠远的感触。它仿佛并不震颤；而冥冥听去（sub audite），一脉古老的故事向他流来。他在乡野间找到一种安静而不张扬的魅力；他注意到一片片庄稼排列得毫厘不爽，一寸一寸地省着空间，令他生出一种感觉：这岛上的人必定是拥挤的，因为每一座小农庄都紧紧挤着左邻右舍，每一片庄稼都紧紧挤着另一片。这种紧窄印证了他对一片完工土地的印象。对一个来自人烟稀疏、草原无垠的美国中西部的人，这是一场启示。他还注意到路基惊人的坚实，以及火车高速飞驰其上的平顺。他还看到沿途没有平交道口；样样东西都极其结实，与他熟识的那种单薄恰成对照。乡间公路也妙极了，那样坚实，那样平整，一路蜿蜒；还有他跨过的一条条可爱的溪流；那青翠，那可爱的树丛，那小小村落与村庄，那一座座从团团绿意中升起的教堂尖顶；处处乡野的气息，以其柔软——岁月与传统的天鹅绒与绒毛——迷住了他。这确乎是一片完工的土地，完工得美丽，坚牢，有生气，扎实，板定；他感到了这片土地的力量，这片挤得紧紧的土地的力量；他并由此推断：在这样一片拥挤的土地上，它的人民必定紧紧地自我意识到自我、以自我为中心。但他还不曾清晰辨出这座四面被海水拥挤的海岛的容貌。

===Original===
Arriving in London, he thought the roof of Euston Station would fall down upon him. It was so solid, so oppressively heavy, he was glad to escape to the street. In London he spent two weeks, most of the time joyfully. The weather, it appears, was extra fine. In this strange world called London, he walked many miles every day and examined most carefully everything within reach, and he thrilled to the booming of Big Ben, the like of which he had never heard. It seemed to be an old world sound, a remnant or an aftermath of the Age of Romance. The power of its stroke almost said to him: “I am that I am!”

One evening in his wanderings he found himself in the Haymarket and saw there shoals of wretches. He was rudely shocked, in horror, in pity and dismay. When he finally escaped from the many fingers clutching at his sleeve, he thought: Is *this* also London﻿—does Big Ben boom in pride for these?﻿—and a veil slowly lifted by degrees. And in the shops where he went to make his small purchases, the rudeness, the brutal rawness of the clerks, or “clarks,” amazed him. At the Music Halls, he was equally astonished at the brilliance of the demimonde. London was too much for Louis. He lacked the worldly wisdom to grasp its immensity, the significance of its teeming, struggling population, the cold reserve in certain places. But he noted the manifold variety, the surging crowds, the dismal hardness of so many faces, and a certain ruthlessness; and everywhere, in the jammed highways, the selfish push of those who must live. So he confined himself to the pleasanter aspects, such as Hyde Park, Rotten Row, and the Thames embankment. He was curious at the vast Houses of Parliament, vertical everywhere; and St. Paul’s black with soot; and many structures in which he sensed, in their visages, the solemn weight of age. They did not appeal to him in their historic message so much as in the sense of that which is old. This massive oldness made a new sensation for him. So passed the days.

===Chinese===
到了伦敦，他只觉得尤斯顿车站（Euston Station）的屋顶要塌到身上来。它那么坚实，那么沉得压人，他庆幸自己逃到了街上。在伦敦他盘桓了两周，大半时光过得快活。天气似乎格外晴好。在这个名叫伦敦的陌生世界里，他每天步行许多英里，够得着的东西都细细察看；大本钟（Big Ben）的轰鸣令他震颤——那样的钟声他从未听过。它听来像一种旧世界的声音，罗曼史时代的一件遗存或余绪。那一击的力量几乎在对他说：「我是自有永有的！」

一天傍晚，他在漫游中发现自己走到了干草市场（Haymarket），看见成群结队的落魄之人。他受到粗暴的震撼，惊骇、怜悯而又惶然。当他终于从那一双双抓住他衣袖的手指里脱身出来，他想：这*也是*伦敦么——大本钟也为这些人而骄傲地轰鸣么？——一层帷幕正缓缓地、一分一分地揭开。在他去置办些小件东西的店铺里，店员们——英国人唤作「克拉克」（clarks）——的粗鲁与生暴也让他吃惊。在杂耍音乐厅（Music Halls）里，他又为半上流社会（demi-monde）的珠光宝气而咋舌。伦敦对路易斯来说，是太过了。他还缺那份世故，把握不住它的浩大，把握不住它那沸腾挣扎的人众的意义，把握不住某些地方冷淡的矜持。但他注意到了那万象的繁杂、汹涌的人潮、那么多面孔上凄惨的冷硬，还有一种无情；还有处处拥堵的大道上，那些必须活下去的人自私的推搡。于是他把自己限定在较悦目的一面，诸如海德公园（Hyde Park）、罗滕罗骑马道（Rotten Row）与泰晤士河堤（Thames embankment）。他对那片处处竖直线条的浩大议会大厦（Houses of Parliament）满怀好奇；圣保罗大教堂（St. Paul's）则被煤烟熏得乌黑；还有许多建筑，他从它们的眉目间觉察到岁月庄严的分量。这些打动他的，与其说是它们承载的历史讯息，不如说是那种「古老」本身的意味。这种厚重的古老，给了他一种全新的感受。日子就这样过去了。

===Original===
Louis left England with so many intermingling impressions thrust suddenly upon him, so many seeming contradictions and paradoxes, that time was needed for the turbid mixture to settle, to clarify, and to reveal a dominant idea.

Thus Louis reached the shores of France much puzzled as to England.

He had sailed from Dover to Dieppe.

===Chinese===
路易斯离开英格兰时，心头猛然堆压了这么多交织的印象，这么多看似矛盾与悖谬的东西，这团浑浊的混合物需要时间来沉淀、澄清，而后显出一个主导的观念。

就这样，路易斯怀着对英格兰的满腹困惑，抵达了法兰西的海岸。

他是从多佛尔（Dover）渡海到迪耶普（Dieppe）的。

===Original===
In the course of the passage, all the transcendental curves, known and unknown to mathematics, were revealed to him by the packet, which distorted and twirled the very heavens, in its cancan with the sea.

As they moved into the little harbor of Dieppe, what was left of Louis gazed at the quaint city with acceptance and delight. How different from England. What a change in physiognomy. How cheerful the aspect﻿—a delicate suggestion not so much of age as of medievalism; he had read about it in many books﻿—a surviving fragrance of romance. But on the way through Normandy, Louis was equally startled, at the rigid spacing of trees, at the dinky châteaux, new-made, stuck here and there as though forming the heads of pins. All was clean, all was stiff. But the farms and the cattle were a revelation, especially the cattle﻿—never had he seen such.

As the train passed through Rouen, twilight was under way, and the spire of the Cathedral seemed to float in the air as though there were no earth.

===Chinese===
渡海途中，这条邮船在与大海跳康康舞的当口，把天上的一切都扭曲拧转，把数学所已知晓和未知晓的一切超越曲线，都演示给他看了。

船驶进迪耶普那座小港时，路易斯身上剩下的那点精气神，带着领受与欢喜，凝望这座古雅的城市。与英格兰多么不同。面目何等一变。景象何其明朗——那微妙的意思，与其说是古老，不如说是中世纪风味；他在许多书里读到过——一缕幸存下来的罗曼史余香。但在取道诺曼底（Normandy）的途中，路易斯又同样吃了一惊：树木排列的间距刻板如尺量，新造的小巧城堡东一座西一座，像是在给一枚枚图钉当钉头。一切都干净，一切都僵硬。但农庄和牛群是一场启示，尤其是牛——他从未见过这样的牛。

火车经过鲁昂（Rouen）时，暮色正在降临，大教堂的尖塔仿佛浮在空中，仿佛底下根本没有大地。

===Original===
Arrived in Paris after nightfall, Louis saw the streets aglow. He boarded a fiacre, and shouted to the cocher:

“Hotel Saint Honoré!”

===Chinese===
夜幕降临后抵达巴黎（Paris），路易斯看见满街灯火。他跳上一辆菲亚克出租马车（fiacre），冲车夫（cocher）喊道：

圣奥诺雷旅馆（Hotel Saint Honoré）！

## XIV Face to Face / 十四　面对面

===Original===
If with open mind one reads and observes industriously and long; if in so doing one covers a wide field and so covering reflects in terms of realism, he is likely, soon or late, to be brought to a sudden consciousness that Man is an unknown quantity and his existence unsuspected.

One will be equally amazed to note that the philosophers, the theologians, of all times turned their backs upon Man; that, from the depths of introspection, fixing their gaze in all directions save the real one, they have uniformly evolved a phantasm, or a series of phantoms, and have declared such to be man in his reality—and such reality to be depraved. A small feature, however, was overlooked by them in the neglect to observe that their man, in his depravity, had created the gods. Their insistent view of man—a further product of their fantasy—lay in the dogma, protean in form, that man is creature.

===Chinese===
倘若一个人怀着开放的心智，勤勉而长久地阅读与观察；倘若在此过程中他涉猎广博的领域，并在涉猎之时以写实的方式反思，那么他多半迟早会被带入一个突然的领悟：人（Man）是一个未知量，而人的存在竟从未被猜疑。

他同样会惊讶地注意到：历代的哲人与神学家都背对着人；他们自内省的深处出发，把目光投向除真实方向之外的四面八方，千篇一律地演化出一个幻影，或一系列幻影，并宣称那便是真实的人——而这样的真实是堕落的。然而他们漏看了一个小小的细节：竟没有注意到，他们笔下那个人，在堕落之中，创造了诸神。他们那固执的人观——又一件他们幻想的产物——凝结为一条形态千变的教义：人是受造物。

===Original===
Meanwhile the real man was always at their elbow, or moving in groups or multitudes about them, or even looking them in the eyes and holding converse with them. But they did not see him; he was too near, too commonplace—too transparent. The gods were far away and could be understood.

The mighty man of war also turned his back. Yet the wise man, the warrior and the priest differed in no valid sense from the multitude enfolding them as in a genesis; for man in his state of depravity as creature, created these also, as his demigods.

===Chinese===
与此同时，真实的人始终就在他们肘边，或成群结队、浩浩荡荡地在他们四周走动，甚至直视他们的双眼，与他们交谈。但他们看不见他；他太近了，太寻常了——太透明了。诸神在远处，反倒可以理解。

威风凛凛的武士也背过了身。然而智者、武士与祭司，同那如创世般包裹着他们的民众之间，并无任何有效的分别；因为人既处于受造物的堕落状态，这些人也是他造出来的，造出来充作他的半神。

===Original===
Thus man, not knowing himself, and none else knowing him, lived as a mirage, within a world of mirage which he fancied real. It was real for him; for such is the habit of man’s imagination in playing tricks with him in his credulity.

The careful reader and observer again may be astonished to note that to the multitudes imagination, as such, is unknown—that the multitudes are unconscious of this power within themselves. Hence the reader, the observer, who is not so completely unconscious of himself, becomes aware of the imposing phenomenon that the huge and varied superstructures of the civilizations of all times have rested for support on so tenuous a foundation as the fabric of the radiant dream of the multitudes. That in such dream he will clearly see Imagination playing its clandestine role. The mass imagination of the multitudes is thus seen to be the prime impelling and sustaining power in the origins and growth of the civilizations. Let the mass imagination withdraw its consent, withhold its nourishing acquiescence and faith, then the civilization founded thereon begins to wither at the top, emaciates, atrophies and dies. One will further note that such changes in the mass imagination, in the mass dream, are of highly varied origins; but once under way, are beyond recall.

===Chinese===
于是，人既不认识自己，也无别人认识他，便像一道幻景，活在一个他自以为真实、其实满是幻景的世界里。这世界于他是真实的；因为人的想象力惯于在他轻信之际耍弄他，向来如此。

细心的读者与观察者还可能惊讶地发现：对民众（the multitudes）而言，想象力（imagination）本身是无从知晓的——民众并不意识自身之内的这种力量。于是，那位对自己不至于如此浑然无觉的读者、观察者，便觉察到一桩巍然的现象：历代文明那些庞大而纷繁的上层建筑，竟是以民众那绚丽之梦的织体这样一层稀薄的根基来支撑的。在这样的梦里，他将清楚地看见想象力正扮演着它隐秘的角色。民众的群体想象力，由此显形为文明发源与生长之中首要的推动与维系之力。一旦群体想象力收回它的同意，扣住它那滋养性的默许与信念，那么建立在其上的文明便开始自顶端枯萎，消瘦、萎缩而死去。人们还会进一步注意到：群体想象力、群体之梦中的这类变动，其来源千差万别；然而一经启动，便无可挽回。

===Original===
One also minutely notes that the tricks of imagination are universal and beyond numbering in variety, permeating all phases of the social fabric. Hence man’s vagaries and follies and cruelties are beyond computation; yet all these betrayals and cajolings and trickeries flow from the same single source, namely the individual, unconscious that his imagination is incessantly at work. Because he is not acquainted with its nature, and unaware that he is its puppet, his waking hours are a continuing dream of inverted Self.

It is the mass dream of inverted self, populous with fears overt and secret, that forms the continuous but gossamer thread upon which are strung as phantom beads all civilizations from the remotest past of record to that of the present day and hour. As we follow back upon this thread—one end of which is delicately attached to our own inverted secret thoughts, we find it unchanging from end to end, regardless of environment; the civilizations it passes through and upholds on its way are but local manifestations and exhibits.

===Chinese===
人们还会细致地注意到：想象力的把戏无所不在，花样多得数不胜数，渗透于社会织体的各个层面。因此人的异想、愚行与残虐便无从计量；然而这一切背叛、哄诱与欺诈都出自同一个单一的源头，即个人——他浑然不知自己的想象力正无休无止地运转着。因为他不熟悉这力量的本性，又不知自己正是它的傀儡，他清醒的时辰便成了一场关于倒置的自我（inverted Self）的连绵不绝的梦。

正是这倒置的自我的群体之梦——其中熙熙攘攘，住满了公开的与隐秘的恐惧——构成了那根连绵不绝却轻若游丝的线；自有文字记录的最远过去直至今日此时的一切文明，皆如幻影的珠子串在这根线上。当我们沿此线回溯——它的一端正纤巧地系在我们自己倒置的隐秘念头上——我们会发现它从头到尾一成不变，无关环境；它沿途穿过并支撑起的那些文明，不过是些局部的显现与陈列。

===Original===
This intense and continuing preoccupation with inverted self makes it clear why man has turned his back on man, and why man is still unknown to himself—and unsuspected.

So long as imagination slyly tricked him into self deprecation, self debasement and the slavery of the creature conviction, or into the opposite, megalomania, with its unquenchable thirst for blood, for plunder, and dominion; or with siren song beguiled him through the portals of a closed world of abstraction—he could not know himself, and the neighbor must remain a stranger to be feared, despised, or placated.

===Chinese===
这种对倒置的自我强烈而持续的沉溺，说清楚了人为何背对着人，人为何至今不为自己所知——且从未被猜疑。

只要想象力还在狡猾地诱他陷入自贬、自辱与受造物信念的奴役，或诱他落入相反的自大狂——带着那对鲜血、劫掠与统治的无从遏止的渴；或用海妖之歌诱他穿过一道道门扉，进入一个封闭的抽象世界——他便无从认识自己，邻人便始终是一个陌生者，只能令人惧怕、鄙夷或安抚。

===Original===
Indeed, until we come as pioneers, to seek out and know imagination as such, to view it clearly defined as an erratic and dangerous power, to be controlled; until we have observed with realistic clarity its multifarious doings from black magic upward to mighty deeds of hand and head and heart, we shall remain remote from man’s reality, and from the splendor of his native powers.

===Chinese===
诚然，除非我们像拓荒者一样前来，把想象力本身探寻出来、认识清楚，把它明明白白地看作一种需要加以约束的飘忽而危险的力量；除非我们以写实的明澈，观察过它种种五花八门的作为——从黑巫术一路向上，直到手、头脑与心灵的伟业——我们将始终远离人的实在，远离人天赋力量的光辉。

---

===Original===
One who has made the rough pilgrimage through the jungled infirmities of philosophy, of theology, and through the wilderness of turbid dream-words uttered by the practical man who deals in cold, hard facts; one who as pioneer worked his troubled way through the undergrowth of culture with its acceptances, its preconceptions and precious finalities; one who, led on by a faith unfaltering, at last arrives at the rendezvous with Life, here testifies the natural man as sound to the core and kindly, yet innocent of himself as the seat of genius, as container of limitless creative powers of beneficence.

===Chinese===
凡曾作过那番崎岖朝圣、穿过哲学与神学丛生的痼疾，穿过务实之人——那位经营冷硬事实的人——吐出的浑浊梦语之荒原者；凡曾作为拓荒者，带着文化那层层下木里的种种接受、成见与宝贵的定论，一路艰辛地开出一条路者；凡被一份从不动摇的信念引领着，终于抵达与生命（Life）的约会之地者——在此见证：自然之人核心健全而仁善，只是他对自己浑然不觉——不知自己是天才的所在，是一只容器，盛着无限创造的仁善之力。

===Original===
Solely on the strength of this faith was begun the story of a child-dream of power.

===Chinese===
正是单凭着这份信念的力量，才开始了那个孩童之力之梦的故事。

---

===Original===
Wherefore we may now inquire: What are these powers, and what is the reality we affirm to be man?

He is none other than ourselves divested of our wrappings. If we in imagination divest ourselves of our wrappings we may see that he is ourselves. If we remove our blinders we shall see more clearly. If we look out between the bars of our self-imprisonment, we may note him nearby, walking familiarly in the Garden of Life. Undoubtedly he is ourselves, he is our youth, he is our spirit, he is that within us which has yearned for frank utterance—how long—and still yearns.

===Chinese===
因此我们现在可以追问：这些力量究竟是什么？我们所断言为「人」的那个实在（reality）又是什么？

他不是别的，正是卸下了层层包裹的我们自己。倘若我们在想象中卸下自己的层层包裹，便会看到他就是我们自身。倘若摘下我们的眼罩，我们会看得更清楚。倘若从自我囚禁的栅栏缝隙间向外张望，我们也许会注意到他就在近旁，熟稔地漫步在生命的花园里。毫无疑问，他就是我们自身；他是我们的青春，他是我们的精神，他是我们之内那个渴望坦率吐露的东西——渴望多久了啊——而且仍在渴望。

===Original===
It is appalling to think he is ourselves; to wake from our dreams and see him. Yet will it not be inspiriting to find him at our elbow—no longer a stranger—no longer to be feared? To know that he is like us all? To feel the widening sense, as we regard him, that he stands not only as our explanation, but as our self-revelation. True, he is not at all what we had supposed and what we have affirmed. Yet will he be grimly recognized as he comes into view—to our amaze, for he is precisely that which we have denied.

===Chinese===
一想到他就是我们自己，便令人惊骇；一想到从梦中醒来看见他，更是如此。然而，若发现他就在我们肘边——不再是陌生者——不再可惧，岂不令人振奋？若知道他同我们大家一样，岂不令人振奋？当我们凝视他时，若感到那不断拓宽的感知——他不仅立在那里作为我们的解释，更作为我们的自我启示——岂不令人振奋？诚然，他全然不是我们原先设想的模样，也不是我们一向断言的那个样子。然而当他进入视野，他将被严峻地认出来——令我们惊愕，因为他恰恰是我们向来否认的那个东西。

===Original===
We may be shocked at first, retreat, and disclaim; for denial of the power of life is our habit of old. We have other habits of old woven into weird grotesqueries. These are among our wrappings.

===Chinese===
起初我们或许会震惊、退缩、抵赖；因为否认生命之力是我们积习已久的老习惯。我们还有别的积习，编织成种种怪诞的奇形。这些也都在我们的层层包裹之列。

---

===Original===
Inasmuch as man has been affirmed herein as sound and kindly, let us examine him. Rest assured we shall find naught in him that is not truly in ourselves and was not there in latency at birth.

===Chinese===
既然我们在此已断言人是健全而仁善的，那就让我们来检视他。请放心，我们在他身上将找不出任何东西，不是真真切切在我们自己之内、不是与生俱来便以潜伏状态存在那里的。

===Original===
To begin: He is a *Worker* and a *Wanderer* in varied ways. With his bodily powers he may go here and there, he may move objects about, he may change the order of things. Here at the onset we find a portentous power—the power to change situations; he can make *new situations*. With his ten fingers he can do wonderful things, make things he needs, make accessory things to extend his muscular powers. Thus he *manipulates*—he further changes situations. He changes his own situations, he creates an environment of his own. One sees here the Adventurer, the Craftsman, the Doer—ever growing in power. Thus man’s first collective power within himself is the power to aspire, to work—to wander—to go from place to place near and far—to return to his home.

===Chinese===
先从头说起：他是一个*劳作者*（Worker），一个*漫游者*（Wanderer），方式多种多样。凭着身体之力，他可以四处走动，可以挪动器物，可以改变事物的秩序。一开篇我们便在这里发现一种预示重大之力——改变处境的力量；他能造出*新的处境*。凭着十根手指，他能做出奇妙的事情，造出他需要的东西，造出延伸其筋肉之力的辅助之物。于是他*操持*——他进一步改变处境。他改变自身的处境，他创造出属于自己的环境。在这里，人们看到了冒险者、工匠、实干者——力量永远在增长。于是，人自身之内的第一种集合力量，便是渴慕之力、劳作之力——漫游之力——往返远近各处之力——重返家园之力。

===Original===
Now comes into view that power we call Curiosity—and coupled with it the power to *inquire*. Man’s power to inquire we call a mental power, to distinguish it from his somatic power. It may have had a beginning, it can have no end. The result of inquiry we call knowledge; its high objective we call science. The objective of science is more knowledge, more power; more inquiry, more power.

===Chinese===
现在进入视野的，是我们称为好奇（Curiosity）的那种力量——与它相连的，是*探究*之力。人的探究之力，我们称之为心智之力，以区别于他的身体之力。它或许曾有一个开端，却不可能有终结。探究的结果，我们称为知识；它的高远目标，我们称为科学。科学的目标是更多的知识、更大的力量；更多的探究、更大的力量。

===Original===
Now, if to the power to do we added the power to inquire, Man, the worker, grows visibly more compact in power, more potent to change situations and to make new situations for himself. The situation may be a deep gorge in a wilderness; the new situation shows a bridge spanning the chasm in one great leap. Thus it is that man himself, as it were, leaps the chasm, through the adventurous coordination of his power to inquire and his power to do. And thus the natural man ever enlarges his range of beneficence. His life experiences are real. He reverses the dictum “I think: Therefore I am.” It becomes in him, *I am: Therefore I inquire and do!*

===Chinese===
如今，倘若在实干之力上再添探究之力，人这劳作者便明显地长得力量更为凝练，更有能力改变处境、为自己造出新处境。那处境可以是荒野中的一道深壑；新处境则显示出一座桥梁，以一记宏大的腾跃跨过深渊。如此，人自身便可以说是亲自腾过了那道深渊——凭着探究之力与实干之力的冒险协同。于是，自然之人不断扩大他仁善的范围。他的生命经验是真实的。他把那句格言「我思：故我在」反转了过来。在他身上，它变成了：*我在：故我探究、故我实干！*

===Original===
It is this affirmative “**I am**” that is man’s reality.

Wherefore warrior, philosopher and priest turned their backs. This “I am” they could not see, could not suspect, even as it stood at their elbow regarding them with ordinary human eyes. For it had been settled long ago on abundant evidence that man is creature and depraved.

===Chinese===
正是这肯定式的「**我在**」，才是人的实在。

所以武士、哲人与祭司才背过了身。这「我在」，他们看不见，也猜疑不到，哪怕它就立在他们肘边，以寻常人的眼睛注视着他们。因为早在多少世代之前，凭着充分的证据，事情便已定谳：人是受造物，是堕落的。

===Original===
In the history of mankind there are recorded two great **inversions**. The first, set forth by the Nazarene to the effect that love is a greater power and more real than vengeance. The second, proclaimed the earth to be a sphere revolving in its course around the sun. These affirmations were made in the face of all evidence sacred to the contrary. Who could feel the earth revolving? Who could fail to see the sun rise and set? What but blood could satisfy, or an eye for an eye?

===Chinese===
在人类的历史上，记载着两次伟大的**倒转**（inversions）。第一次由拿撒勒人（the Nazarene）提出，大意是：爱是一种比复仇更伟大、更真实的力量。第二次宣告：地球是一个球体，在其轨道上环绕太阳运转。这些断言，都是在一切神圣的相反证据面前做出的。谁能感觉到地球在转动？谁能看不见太阳的升起与沉落？除了血，还有什么能满足？除了以眼还眼？

===Original===
Hence man’s powers were not seen as himself, nor himself as his powers. Such recognition would involve a reversal and inversion both of sacred lore and common sense.

In reactive consequence of age-long self-repression and self-beguilement the world of mankind is now preparing its way for a Third Inversion. The world of heart and head is becoming dimly sentient that man in his power is Free spirit—Creator. The long dream of inverted self is nearing its end. Emerging from the heritage of mystical unconsciousness and fantasy, the world of mankind is stirring. Man’s deeds are about to become conscious deeds in the open. The beauty, the passion, the glory of the past shall merge into a new beauty, a new passion, a new glory as man approaches man, and recognizing him, rejoices in him and with him, as born in power.

===Chinese===
因此，人的力量不曾被看作他自身，他自身也不曾被看作他的力量。这样的承认，将意味着对神圣典籍与常识的双重反转与倒转。

作为亘古自我压抑与自我蒙骗的反激后果，人类的世界此刻正在为第三次大倒转（Third Inversion）铺路。心与头脑的世界正隐隐约约地觉出：人在其力量之中，是自由精神——是创造者。倒置的自我的长梦已近尾声。从神秘的无意识与幻想的遗产中浮出，人类的世界正在骚动。人的行迹即将成为公开的、有意识的作为。当人走向人，认出了他，便为他欢欣、与他同欢——同为力量所生——过去的美、过去的激情、过去的荣光，将汇入一种新的美、新的激情、新的荣光。

---

===Original===
Never in man’s time has there been such sound warrant for an attitude of Optimism as in our own, the very present day. Yet to him who in myopic fear looks but at the troubled surface, there appears equal warrant in the fantasy of Pessimism. What a price man shall have paid for freedom! For freedom from the thrall of his parlous imagination! For freedom from the strangle hold of his own phantasmal self!

===Chinese===
自有人的时代以来，还从不曾有过像我们自己的时代、像今日此时这样，对一种乐观主义（Optimism）的姿态有如此扎实的凭据。然而，对于那怀着近视的恐惧、只看扰攘表面的人，悲观主义（Pessimism）的幻想中似乎也有同等的凭据。人为了自由，将付出何等的代价！为了挣脱他那凶险想象力的奴役！为了挣脱他自己那幻影自我的扼杀之握！

---

===Original===
He who has lived, alive, during the past fifty years has viewed an extraordinary drama. He who starting young, shall live through the coming fifty years will move within the action and scene shifting of a greater drama.

The gravitation of world thought and dream is shifting. Out of the serial collapses of age-long feudalism is arising a new view of man. For man’s powers in certitude, approach the infinite. They are bewildering—amazing in diversity. They unfold their intimate complexity to our view as an equally amazing solidarity, as we hold, steadfast, to the realistic concept of man as free spirit—as creator—even as the vast complexity in the outworking of the feudal thought simplifies into a basic concept of self-delusion and self-fear.

===Chinese===
在过去五十年里真正活过、生活过的人，已经看过一出非凡的戏剧。而从年轻时就启程、将活过未来五十年的人，将置身于一出更伟大的戏剧的行动与换景之中。

世界思想与梦想的引力重心正在转移。从亘古封建制度（feudalism）一次次接连的崩塌之中，一种新的人观正在升起。因为人的力量，就其确凿而言，已逼近无限。它们令人目眩——其纷繁多样令人惊叹。当我们坚定不移地持守那个写实的人之概念——人是自由精神，是创造者——它们便将其内在的繁复向我们展开为一种同样惊人的团结一致；而与此同时，封建思想运作中那浩大的繁复，也正简化为一个自我蒙骗与自我恐惧的基本概念。

---

===Original===
Our portrayal is not yet wholly clear. Let us go on. There lies another power in man. That power is **moral**: Its name is **choice**! Within this one word, Choice, lies the story of man’s world. It stands for the secret poise within him. It reveals as a flashlight all his imagings, his fantasies, his wilful thoughts, his deeds, from the greatest to the least, even in this gliding hour we call today. *This one word, Choice, stands for the sole and single power; it is the name of the mystery that lies behind the veil of all human appearances.* A word that dissolves the enigma of men’s deeds. A word, a light that not only illuminates all his obvious works, all the inner springs and motives of his civilizations, but a light whose rays reach within the sanctuary of the secret thought of each and all, thus revealing the man of the past and the man of today, starkly in personal status as a social factor of beneficence or woe. Need we know man’s thoughts? View his works, his deeds; they tell his choice.

===Chinese===
我们的写照尚未全然清晰。让我们继续下去。人的身上还潜藏着另一种力量。那力量是**道德的**：它的名字叫**抉择**（choice）！在抉择这一个词里，藏着人的世界的故事。它代表着他之内那隐秘的沉稳。它像一支手电，照亮他一切的想象、一切的幻想、一切任性的念头、一切的作为，从最大到最小，甚至就在我们称之为「今天」的这个滑行的时辰里。*抉择这一个词，代表着那独一无二的力量；它是隐在人类一切表象的帷幕之后的那个奥秘的名字。*一个能消解人的作为之谜的词。一个词，一束光，不仅照亮他一切显见的作品、他的文明一切内在的源泉与动机，而且其光芒直透入每一个人、所有人的隐秘思想的圣所（sanctuary），从而揭示出过去的人与今天的人，赤裸裸地以其个人身份，作为仁善或灾祸的社会因子。我们需要知道人的思想吗？看他的作品、他的作为；它们道出了他的抉择。

===Original===
Implicit in true freedom of spirit lies a proud and virile will. Such glorious power of free will to choose, envisages beneficent social responsibility as manifest and welcome. Here now stands in full light Man erect and conscious as a moral power. The will to choose aright lifts him to the peak of social vision whence he may forecast new and true situations.

===Chinese===
在真正的精神自由之中，隐含着一种骄傲而雄健的意志。这样一种自由意志去抉择的光荣力量，把仁善的社会责任设想为显见的、受欢迎的。此刻，人挺立着，自觉为一个道德力量，全然立在亮光之中。抉择得当的意志，把他提升到社会视野的峰顶，从那里他可以预告新的、真实的处境。

---

===Original===
The Free Spirit is the spirit of Joy. It delights to create in beauty. It is unafraid, it knows not fear. It declares the Earth to be its home, and the fragrance of Earth to be its inspiration. It is strong, it is mighty in beneficence. It views its powers with emotions of adventure. Humility it knows not. It dreams a civilization like unto itself. It would create such a world for mankind. It has the strength. It sees the strength of the fertile earth, the strength of the mountains, the valleys, the far spreading plains, the vast seas, the rivers and the rivulets, the great sky as a wondrous dome, the sun in its rising, its zenith, and its setting, and the night. It glories in these powers of earth and sky as in its own. It affirms itself integral with them all. It sees Life at work everywhere—Life, the mysterious, the companionable, the ineffable, the immensest and gentlest of powers, clothing the earth in a pattern of radiant sublimity, of tenderness, of fairy delicacy—ceaselessly at work. Thus the free spirit feels itself to be likewise clothed as with a flowing shoulder-garment, symbol of power akin to the fluent mystery and fecundity of Life. Thus it moves in the open with vision clear. Thus is man the wonder-worker bound up in friendship with the wonder-worker—Life.

===Chinese===
自由精神（Free Spirit）是欢乐的精神。它乐于在美之中创造。它无所畏惧，它不知恐惧为何物。它宣告大地（Earth）是它的家，大地的芬芳是它的灵感。它强壮，它在仁善中浩大有力。它怀着冒险的激情看待自己的种种力量。谦卑，它一无所知。它梦想一个与它自身相像的文明。它要为人类创造这样一个世界。它有这份力量。它看见丰饶大地的力量，群山的力量，山谷的力量，漫远铺展的平原的力量，浩瀚海洋的力量，江河与小溪（rivulet）的力量，伟大的天空如一座奇妙的穹顶，太阳的升起、中天与沉落，还有夜晚。它以这些天地之力为荣，如以其自身之力为荣。它确证自己与它们全体浑然一体。它看见生命在处处劳作——生命，那神秘的、可亲的、不可言说的力量，诸力之中最浩大者也最温柔者，为大地披上一袭绚丽崇高、温情脉脉、仙灵精致的织纹——无休无止地劳作着。于是，自由精神觉得自己也同样披上了一袭流动的肩衣，那是力量的象征，与生命那流变的神秘和丰饶同源相似。于是它视野澄澈，在旷朗之中行动。于是，人这奇迹工人，与那奇迹工人——生命——结成了友谊。

---

===Original===
Now the real man begins to shape within our vision. Consider his primary powers: He, the *worker*, the *inquirer*, the *chooser*. Add to these the wealth of his emotions—also powers. Think how manifold they are, how colorful; how with them he may dramatize his works, his thoughts, his choosings; how he may beautify his choice. Think of his power to *receive*; to receive through the channels of his senses, to receive through his mystic power of sympathy which brings understanding to illumine Knowledge. Think of what eyesight means as a power, the sense of touch, the power to hear, to listen; and the power of contemplation. Add these to his cumulating interblending power; then think again of his enlarging power to act. Deep down within him lies that power we call Imagination, the power instantly or slowly to picture forth, the power to act in advance of action; the power that knows no limitations, no boundaries, that renders vivid both giving and receiving; the inscrutable dynamic power that energizes all other powers. Think of man as Imagination! Then think of him as Will! Now enrich the story of his prior-mentioned powers with the flow of imagination and the steadiness of will. Think anew of his power to act; of the quantity and quality of this power.

===Chinese===
如今，真实的人开始在我们的视野中显出形貌。细想他的种种基本力量：他，*劳作者*，*探究者*，*抉择者*。再给这些添上他情感的丰饶——情感也是力量。想想它们何其纷繁，何其多彩；有了它们，他能把他的作品、他的思想、他的种种抉择敷演成戏；他能美化他的抉择。想想他*领受*的力量；经由感官的渠道去领受，经由他那神秘的同情之力去领受——那同情带来理解，为知识（Knowledge）照明。想想视力作为一种力量意味着什么，触觉意味着什么，听与倾听的力量；还有沉思的力量。把这些加到他不断累积、相互交融的力量之上；然后再想想他那不断扩张的行动之力。在他深处的深处，潜藏着我们称为想象力（Imagination）的那种力量——即时或缓缓地将图景呈现出来的力量，先于行动而行动的力量；那不知限制、不知边界的力量，它令给予与领受都变得鲜明；那不可测度的动力性力量，为其余一切力量供能。把人想象成想象力吧！再把人想象成意志（Will）吧！现在，用想象力的流注与意志的稳定，把他前述种种力量的故事再加以充实。重新想想他的行动之力；想想这力量的数量与质量。

===Original===
Now think of the freedom such power brings!

===Chinese===
现在，想想这样的力量所带来的自由吧！

===Original===
Think of the power we call Vision; that inner sight which encompasses the larger meanings of its outer world, which sees humanity in the broad, which beholds the powers without itself, which unifies its inner and its outer world, which sees far beyond where the eye leaves off seeing, and as sympathetic insight finds its goal in the real.

===Chinese===
想想我们称为远见（Vision）的那种力量；那内在的视看，包揽它外部世界的更大意义，在宏阔处看见人性，瞻望自身之外的种种力量，统一它的内在世界与外在世界，看见远远超出目光止步之处的地方，并以同情的洞察，在实在之中找到它的目标。

===Original===
Now see Man go forth to work, inspired by his vision of the outer world, himself made eager by the passion to live and worthily to do!

See him go forth in certitude as seer, as prophet, as evangelist, proclaiming his faith—in certitude as worker, to build a new home.

===Chinese===
现在看人出发去劳作吧，被他对外部世界的远见所鼓舞，他自身也因生之激情、因值得去做的激情而热切起来！

看他满怀确信地出发，作为先见者，作为先知，作为布道者，宣讲他的信念——又满怀确信地作为劳作者，去建造一个新的家园。

===Original===
See him, as poet, as troubadour, as he goes forth, singing the new song, the refreshing song—calling in carols: Awake! ye dreamers all, lift up your heads, and be your hearts lifted up that Life in splendor may come in: Ye who dream in the shadows and are sore perplexed.

===Chinese===
看他作为诗人、作为游吟诗人出发，唱着那支新歌，那支令人清爽的歌——以颂声呼唤：醒来吧！你们这些做梦的人，抬起你们的头，让你们的心也昂扬起来，好让荣光中的生命进来：你们这些在暗影里做梦、苦苦困惑的人。

===Original===
Thus the multitudes vibrate, as they dream—at the sound of a song in their dream.

It is the richness of the soul-life of the multitudes that inspires and at times appalls the observer.
