For the multitudes are compact of human beings—a vast ceaseless flow of individuals, each a dreamer, each latent in power, the mass moving noiselessly through time—slowly changing in its constancy of renewal.

===Chinese===
于是民众震颤起来，一边做梦——一边被梦中一支歌的声音所震动。

正是民众灵魂生活的丰厚，激励着观察者，有时也令他骇然。因为民众是由一个个人密密聚合而成——一道浩瀚不息的个人之流，每一个都是做梦者，每一个都潜藏着力量，这群体无声地穿越时间移动——在它绵延不绝的更新之中缓缓变化。

---

===Original===
Thus though Man now appears before us in glamor as a maze of powers, we have not yet made his image clear in full, and in diversity.

While it is plain, when all wrappings are removed, we shall find all men to be alike in native possession of essential powers, we are at once confronted by this paradox: That all men obviously are different; that no two are alike. In plain words we find each human being unique. When we say unique, we mean *the only one*. Thus each one is the only one. If we have mused long upon the immense fecundity and industry of Life, the paradox vanishes: The only one and the all coalesce. The individual and the mass become one, in a new phase of power whose stupendous potency of creative art in civilization stuns the sense of possibility.

===Chinese===
这样，尽管人如今已光彩熠熠地出现在我们面前，如一座力量的迷宫，我们尚未把他的形象完整地、并在其多样性之中廓清。

虽然很明白，一旦卸下所有包裹，我们会发现所有人在天赋地拥有本质力量这一点上一模一样，我们却又立刻面对这样一个悖论：所有人显然又各不相同；没有两个人是相像的。说白了，我们发现每一个人都是独一无二的。当我们说独一无二，我们指的就是*唯一者*（the only one）。于是，每一个人都是唯一者。倘若我们对生命那浩大的丰饶与勤勉沉思得足够久，悖论便消散了：唯一者与全体合而为一。个人与群体化为一体，进入力量的一个新相位，其在文明之中创造艺术的惊人伟力，令可能性之感为之震撼。

===Original===
*Now opens to our view the Democratic Vista!*

Now see unfold the power of the *only one* in multiple, and the *One* become a vast complex of unique powers inspired of its free spirit and its power of beneficence—its works now solidly founded on the full emergence of courage—the evanishment of fear!

===Chinese===
*此刻，民主远景（Democratic Vista）向我们敞开！*

现在看吧，*唯一者*的力量成倍地展开，而那*一*（One）化作一个独特力量的浩大复合体，受其自由精神与其仁善之力的感发——它的作品如今坚实奠立在勇气的完全涌现之上——恐惧的消散！

---

===Original===
Alas, the world has never known a sound social fabric, a fabric sound and clean to the core and kindly. For it has ever turned its back on Man. Through time immemorial it has, in overt and secret fear of self, been impotent to recognize the only one, the unique. Hence wars and more wars, pestilence, famine and desolation; the rise and crumbling of immense fabrics.

===Chinese===
可叹啊，这个世界从来不曾有过一个健全的社会织体，一个核心健全、洁净而仁善的织体。因为它永远背对着人。自太古以来，它便怀着对自我的公开与隐秘的恐惧，无力认出那唯一者，那独一无二的。于是战争连着战争，瘟疫、饥荒与荒芜；庞大织体的崛起与崩碎。

===Original===
The feudal concept of self-preservation is poisoned at the core by the virulent assumption of master and man, of potentate and slave, of external and internal suppression of the life urge of the only one—of its faith in human sacrifice as a means of salvation.

===Chinese===
封建的自我保存概念，其核心早已被一剂毒液浸透：主人与仆役、君侯与奴隶的设定，对唯一者的生命冲动的内外压制——以及对人牲献祭作为一种救赎手段的信仰。

===Original===
The *only one* is Ego—the “I am”—the unique—the most precious of man’s powers, their source and summation in diversity. Without Ego, which is Life, man vanishes. Ego signifies Identity. It is the free spirit. It is not a tenant, it is the all in all. It is present everywhere throughout man’s wondrous being. It is what we call the spiritual, a term now becoming interchangeable with the physical. It is the sign and symbol of man’s immense Integrity—the “I am that I am.” To it the Earth, the world of humanity, the multitudes, the universe—become an Egocosm.

===Chinese===
*唯一者*就是自我（Ego）——「我在」——那独一无二的——人的一切力量中最珍贵的，是它们在多样之中的源头与总括。没有了自我——自我即生命——人便消散了。自我意味着同一性（Identity）。它就是自由精神。它不是一位房客，它是万有中的万有。它在人这奇妙存在的全身上下无处不在。它就是我们称之为「灵」的东西——这个词如今正渐渐变得可与「物」互换。它是人那浩大的完整性（Integrity）的记号与象征——「我即我所是」。对它而言，大地，人类的世界，民众，宇宙——化为一个自我宇宙（Egocosm）。

===Original===
Thus to the eye of the earnest watcher, the dual man of legend and of present mythical belief fades, incorporeal as a ghost. Departing it leads the ghostly feudal scapegoat with its burden of sin.

===Chinese===
于是，在恳切的注视者眼中，传说里的、当今神话信仰中的那个二元的人，像鬼魂一样无形无质，渐渐淡去。它退场时，还牵着那鬼影般的封建替罪羊，连同它一身的罪担。

===Original===
It is man’s manifest integrity that reveals him valid—sound to the core. It is this spiritual integrity that defines him human, that points true to his high moral power—the power of valid choice.

This new vision of man is the true vision of man.

===Chinese===
正是人那显然可见的完整性，揭示出他是站得住的——核心健全。正是这精神的完整性，把他定义为人，真切地指向他那崇高的道德力量——有效抉择的力量。

这新的人观，才是真正的人观。

===Original===
Toward this new truth, this inversion, the world of mankind slowly turning, vaguely conscious, strives to articulate that which is as yet too deep, too remote, too new for its words. But it is not too deep, too remote or too new for its aspirations.

===Chinese===
朝着这个新的真理，这新的倒转，人类的世界缓缓转身，意识蒙眬，竭力要言说那对它的话语而言还太深、太远、太新的东西。但对它的渴慕而言，它并不太深、不太远、也不太新。

---

===Original===
Thus in portrayal stands *Man the Reality*: Container of self-powers: A moving center of radiant energy: Awaiting his time to create anew in his proper image.

===Chinese===
这便是写照中挺立的*实在之人*（Man the Reality）：自具其力的容器：一个流动的、辐射能量的中心：静候他的时辰，好按他本真的形象重新创造。

===Original===
Are then the multitudes infertile? Is genius rare? Has our traditional education and culture left us wholly blind? Have we forgotten the children—Egos at our elbow? The springtide of genius there! Shall we continue to destroy? What is our *Choice*? How have we exercised it? How shall we exercise it? Is our moral power asleep? Are we without faith in our own?

===Chinese===
那么，民众是贫瘠的吗？天才是罕见的吗？我们传统的教育与文化已把我们变得全然盲目了吗？我们忘记了孩子们——那些就在我们肘边的诸自我（Ego）——吗？天才的春潮正在那里！我们还要继续毁掉它吗？我们的*抉择*（Choice）是什么？我们一直怎样行使它？我们将要怎样行使它？我们的道德力量睡着了吗？我们对自己的力量竟没有信心吗？

===Original===
Whence, then, this story of a child’s dream of power?

What shall *our* dream be?

===Chinese===
那么，这个孩童之力之梦的故事，是从哪里来的？

*我们*的梦，又该是什么？

---

===Original===
Our dream shall be of a civilization founded upon ideas thrillingly sane, a civilization, a social fabric squarely resting on man’s quality of virtue as a human being; created by man, the real, in the image of his fruitful powers of beneficence; created in the likeness of his aspirant emotions, in response to the power and glory of his true imagination, the power of his intelligence, his ability to inquire, to do, to make new situations befitting his needs. A civilization that shall reflect man sound to the core and kindly in the exercise of his will to choose aright. A civilization that shall be the living voice, the spring song, the saga of the power of his Ego to banish fear and fate, and in the courage of adventure and of mastership to shape his destiny.

===Chinese===
我们的梦，将是一个奠立在令人心颤地健全的观念之上的文明；一个文明，一个方方正正安放在人之为人 的德性品质之上的社会织体；由真实的人创造，依照他那丰饶多产的仁善之力的形象；依照他那些渴慕向上的情感的模样创造，回应他真想象力的力量与荣光，回应他智性的力量，回应他探究、实干、创造合乎自身所需之新处境的能力。一个映照出核心健全而仁善之人的文明，在他抉择得当的意志的行使之中。一个将成为活的声音、春之歌的文明，将成为他的自我之力——驱逐恐惧与命运之力——的萨迦（saga），并凭着冒险的勇气与主掌的勇气，塑造他的命运。

===Original===
Such dream is the vigorous daylight dream of man’s abounding power, that he may establish in beauty and in joy, on the earth, a dwelling place devoid of fear. That in the so doing he shall establish an anchorage within his universe, in courage, in the mighty spirit of adventure, of masterful craftsmanship, as he rises to the heights of the new art of all arts—the art of upbuilding for the race a new, a stable home.

===Chinese===
这样的梦，是人那丰沛力量的朝气蓬勃的白日之梦：他可以在美之中、在欢乐之中，在大地之上，建立一个没有恐惧的居所。而在这样做的过程中，他将在自己的宇宙中确立一处锚地（anchorage）——以勇气，以冒险的雄伟精神，以精湛的匠艺——当他升上众艺之艺的那门新艺术的高处：为种族建起一个崭新的、安稳家园的艺术。

---

===Original===
Plainly the outworking of so sublime a conception as that of rearing the fabric of a worthwhile civilization upon the basic truth of man’s reality as a sure foundation, implies the inversion of a host of fixed ideas “consecrated by the wisdom of the ages.” The time has come to place the wisdom of the ages in the balance of inquiry; to ascertain, when weighed, wherein it may be found wanting in the human sense. One sure test is sanity, for to be unkind is to be dangerously unbalanced.

===Chinese===
显然，要把一个值得拥有的文明的织体，营造在人的实在这一基本真理这块可靠的基石之上——如此崇高的构想，其付诸实施意味着把一大群「被历代智慧祝圣」的固定观念统统倒转过来。把历代智慧放上探究的天平的时候到了；称一称分量，查明它在何处可能被发现在人的意义上有所亏欠。有一个可靠的检验，就是神志健全：因为不仁，便是一种危险的失衡。

===Original===
It is also time to test out the folly of the ages, the multifarious corruption involved in abstract and concrete irresponsibility, the abuse of power, the abuse of the useful, the successive collapses and ruin, the ever present sense of instability, the all-pervading fear, the lack of anchorage.

So testing, we shall find that alike the wisdom and folly of the ages rest in utter insecurity upon a false concept of the nature of man. For both “wisdom” and folly have committed and still commit the double folly of turning away from man in contempt.

===Chinese===
也到了检验历代愚行的时候了：抽象的与具体的无责任之中所牵连的种种腐坏，权力的滥用，对有用之物的滥用，接二连三的崩塌与倾覆，无时不在的不稳之感，无处不在的恐惧，以及锚地的缺失。

这样一检验，我们便会发现：历代的智慧与愚行一样，都全然不牢靠地栖身于一个关于人之本性的错误概念之上。因为「智慧」与愚行都已犯下、且仍在犯着那双重的愚行：带着轻蔑背过身去，不理会人。

===Original===
Glancing at our modern civilization we find on the surface crust essentially the same idea at work that has prevailed throughout the past. Yet if we search beneath the surface we discern a new power of the multitudes everywhere at work. It is the power of a changing dream, of a changing choice; of Life urging upward to the open the free spirit of man—so long self-suppressed under the dead weight of the “consecrated wisdom of the ages” and its follies.

===Chinese===
瞥一眼我们的现代文明，我们会发现表层地壳上起作用的，基本上还是贯穿过去的同一个观念。然而，倘若我们在表面之下探查，便辨认出民众的一种新力量正在处处运作。那是一个变动中的梦、一次变动中的抉择的力量；是生命把人的自由精神向上推向开阔——那精神在「历代祝圣的智慧」及其愚行的死重之下，自行压抑了如此之久。

---

===Original===
The fabricating of a virile, a proud and kindly civilization, rich in its faith in man, is surely to constitute the absorbing interest of the coming generations. It will begin to take on its functional form out of the resolve of choice, and the liberation of those instincts within us which are akin to the dreams of childhood, and which, continuing on through the children and the children of the children, shall be a guide evermore. For who shall say the child is not the unsullied wellspring of power!

===Chinese===
锻造一个雄强的、骄傲而仁善、富于对人之信心的文明，必将成为未来世代念兹在兹的头等大事。它将从抉择的决意之中，从我们之内那些与童年之梦同源的本能的解放之中，开始取得它的功能形式（functional form）；这些本能经由孩子们、孩子们的孩子们一路延续下去，将永远是一位向导。因为谁敢说，孩子不是那未曾玷污的力量之源！

===Original===
The chief business now is to pave the way for the child, that it may grow wholesome, proud and stalwart in its native powers.

So doing we shall uncover to our view the amazing world of instinct in the child whence arises genius with its swift grasp of the real.

===Chinese===
现在的头等大事，是为孩子铺路，好让它在天赋力量之中长得健全、骄傲而壮硕。

这样做，我们便会向自己的视野揭开孩子身上那令人惊叹的本能世界——天才正是从那里升起，带着它对实在的迅捷把握。

===Original===
The great creative art of upbuilding a chosen and stable civilization with its unique culture, implies orderly concentration and organization of man’s powers toward this sole end, consciously applied in each and every one of his socially constructive activities in the clear light of his understanding that the actualities of good and evil are resident in man’s choice—and not elsewhere. Thus will arise a new *Morale* in its might!

===Chinese===
建树一个经抉择的、安稳的文明及其独特文化的伟大创造艺术，意味着把人的力量朝着这唯一的目的作有序的集中与组织——在他清明的理解之光中，自觉地运用于他每一项社会性的建设活动，因为他明白：善与恶的实在，居于人的抉择之中——而不在别处。于是一种新的*士气*（Morale），将以其雄力兴起！

===Original===
And let it be well understood that such creative energy cannot arise from a welter of pallid abstractions as a soil, nor can it thrive within the tyranny of any cut and dried system of economics or politics. It must and will arise out of the heart, to be nurtured in common honesty by the intelligence, and by that sense of artistry which does not interfere with the growth of a living thing but encourages it to seek and find its own befitting form. Thus the living idea of man, the free spirit, master of his powers, shall find its form-image in a civilization which shall set forth the highest craftsmanship, the artistry of living joyously in stable equilibrium.

===Chinese===
还必须让人透彻明白：这样的创造能量，不可能从一滩苍白抽象物充当的土壤里生出来，也不可能在任何一套干燥僵化的经济或政治体系的暴虐之中茁壮。它必定、也将会从心里升起，在寻常的诚实之中由智性来培育，由那样一种艺术感来培育——那艺术感不干预一个活物的生长，而是鼓励它去寻找、去找到它自己合宜的形式。于是，人这活的理念，这自由精神，这力量的主宰，将在一个文明之中找到它的形式意象——那文明将彰显最高的匠艺，彰显在安稳平衡之中欢乐生活的艺术。

===Original===
*Thus widens the Democratic Vista!*

===Chinese===
*民主远景，就这样开阔起来！*

---

===Original===
The historic Feudal thought sought and found its form in a series of civilizations resting upon a denial of man by the multitudes themselves, who sought cohesion in mutual fear of life, and out of the culture of fear they created their tyrants. Their unsafe anchorage lay in the idea of force, in its convincing outward show of domination, splendor and glory.

===Chinese===
历史上的封建思想，在一系列文明中寻找并找到了它的形式；这些文明奠基于民众自身对人的否认——民众在对生命的相互恐惧中求取凝聚，又从恐惧的文化里造出了他们的暴君。他们那不安全的锚地，在于力量的观念，在于力量那令人信服的外表排场：统治、辉赫与荣光。

===Original===
In terror of the unknown, in appeal for mediation, the multitudes passed their immense unconscious power to those they raised aloft—gods or men, and as value received they created and accepted the status of servitude. Those thus raised aloft became enormously parasitic, capping and sapping the strength of the multitudes. As the latter grew in self-sacrifice and poverty, they became luxurious in that they gave their all in the name of glory that their children, the great, might flourish. They staggered beneath the weight of the mighty they upheld aloft and who came to know them not—other than as beasts to toil or fight. Thus has the feudal super-power ever undermined its own foundation, ever, in recurring cycle, collapsing and renewing—renewing and collapsing. Times, places, names, local colors, mechanisms, countenances, change. The idea, the thought, the fear, persists through the ages.

===Chinese===
出于对未知的恐惧，出于祈求调停，民众把他们浩大的无意识力量移交给他们高高捧起的那些存在——诸神或众人；而作为收到的回报，他们创造并接受了奴仆的身份。那些被高高捧起的存在变得寄生成性，高踞其上、吮吸着民众的气力。民众愈是自我牺牲、愈是贫乏，那些存在便愈是奢靡——因为民众以荣光之名献出自己的一切，好让他们的孩子——那些大人物——得以昌盛。他们被自己高举在上的强者压得踉跄，而那些强者渐渐不再认识他们——只把他们当作劳作或打仗的牲口。就这样，封建强权从来都在掏空自己的根基，永远在一轮轮循环里崩塌又重建——重建又崩塌。时代、地点、名字、地方色彩、机制、面孔，都在变。观念、思想、恐惧，却亘古长存。

---

===Original===
For us the chief impress of the self-revealing story of mankind lies in the perception that all sanctioning power comes from below. From the vast human plenum we have called the multitudes, it arises gently, massively, step by step, stage by stage, height upon height; all of which but signifies the peoples’ dreams of glory taking shape vicariously in their times and places. The spectacular and imposing groups and summits of the feudal superstructure have no other base, no other sanction. Like towering cumulus clouds they float upon thin air.

===Chinese===
对我们而言，人类这部自我揭示的故事给人的主要印刻，在于一个洞见：一切授予权力的力量都来自下面。它从我们称之为民众的那片浩瀚的人类充盈之中升起，轻柔地、庞然地，一步一步，一阶一阶，一高度又一高度；而这一切只意味着：各时各地的民众，把他们的荣光之梦借他人之身铸成形状。封建上层建筑那煊赫夺目、巍巍峨峨的群峰与峰顶，再没有别的基座，再没有别的授权。它们像高耸的积云，漂浮在稀薄的空气之上。

===Original===
As there are truths that lie within truths, so are there dreams that lie within dreams. The most ancient of dreams lies indeed within the feudal dream. This dream is none other than the dream of the reality of man.

As truths one by one appear above the surface, ever more powerful, farther reaching as they come from greater depths of life, so the great deep dream of man’s free spirit has been moving upward through the feudal dream. The flair of his powers is now sensing in the thought of the man of today.

===Chinese===
正如真理之中还嵌着真理，梦之中也还嵌着梦。最古老的那个梦，确确实实就嵌在封建之梦的里面。这个梦不是别的，正是人之实在的梦。

随着真理一个接一个浮出表面——来自生命越深的层次，便越有力、越及远——人这自由精神的浩大深梦，也一直在穿过封建之梦向上移动。他的力量的那种直觉锐敏，如今正在今日之人的思想中感应着。

---

===Original===
With the great inversion of the Earth and the Sun, brought definitely about by so small an object as a telescope which man in his curiosity invented—created—to extend his power of eyesight and the daring thought—the dream—it stood for; with this shock of inversion definitely began the greatest of man’s adventures upon his Earth.

===Chinese===
地球与太阳的那场大倒转，由望远镜这样一件小小的物件促成——那是人在好奇之中发明——创造——出来的，为的是延展他目视之力，以及它所代表的那大胆的思想——那梦；随着这倒转震撼的一记，人在他的大地上最伟大的冒险，确凿地开始了。

===Original===
We in present sense and in retrospect call it the **modern**.

The feudal flow poured on, the germ of the modern growing in embryo apace and inexterminable. Inquiry upon inquiry followed; invention upon invention, discovery upon discovery; and wars and more wars, tremors, and the downfall of mighty superstitions; cunning and betrayal raged in abuses of delegated power, institutions rocked, dogma came forth in the open, knife and torch in hand the feudal flow went on in stealth, the modern power grew and ramified; there was calm and there was turbulence; onward flowed the feudal stream with its new arrangements, its new collapses, its new horrors, its new deaths, its new resurrections, as the power of man’s self-determination, the assertion of his free spirit, none too articulate as yet, none too sane, clarified in growing strength, its inventions seized upon, its uses turned to abuse, yet goading the feudal power into titanic writhings, fears and dreads, desperations, ruses and stratagems, wars and more wars—the dread phantom of awakening multitudes—the resolve to foster hate.

===Chinese===
我们以当下的感觉、以事后的回望，把它称为**现代**（the modern）。

封建的洪流继续倾泻，而现代的胚芽在胚胎中急速生长，无可扑灭。探究接着探究而来；发明接着发明，发现接着发现；还有战争连着战争，震动，以及强大迷信的倾覆；狡诈与背弃在对委托之权的滥用中肆虐，诸般制度摇撼，教义公然出笼，封建的洪流手持刀与火把潜行不辍，现代的力量生长蔓衍；有平静，也有动荡；封建的水流带着它的新安排、新崩塌、新恐怖、新死亡、新复活一路奔流向前，而与此同时，人的自我决断之力，他自由精神的伸张，虽尚欠明晰、尚欠健全，却在增长的力量中渐次澄清；它的发明被人攫取，它的用途被人转为滥用，却依然刺得封建权力巨灵般翻滚扭动——恐惧与惊惶，绝望，诡计与权谋，战争连着战争——对觉醒民众的可惧幻影——培植仇恨的决意。

===Original===
Yet man the worker, the inquirer, ever pushed onward in hope. Came the printing press, the mariner’s compass, the power of steam, railroads, great ships, the discovery and development of new vast hidden riches of earth, the harnessing of the mystical power of electricity, the land telegraph, the ocean cable, the telephone, the growth of libraries, the daily papers, the public schools, the technical schools, the automobile, vast systems of transportation of all kinds, the radio, the aeroplane, the mastery of the air, the mastery of the seas, the mastery of the earth, the increasing mastery of ideas. The immense growth in power of constructive imagination and of the will to do. And all to what end? What may tomorrow and tomorrow bring forth out of bloodstained yesterday and the flowing yesterdays since History’s dawn?

===Chinese===
然而，人这劳作者、探究者，始终怀着希望向前推进。于是来了印刷机，来了航海罗盘，来了蒸汽之力，铁路，巨轮，大地隐秘巨富的新发现与开发，电力那神秘之力的驯驭，陆上电报，海底电缆，电话，图书馆的滋长，日报，公立学校，技术学校，汽车，各类交通运输的庞大系统，无线电，飞行器，对天空的掌握，对海洋的掌握，对大地的掌握，以及对观念日益增进的掌握。建设性想象力与实干意志的力量浩荡增长。而这一切为了什么目的？从血迹斑斑的昨天，从历史破晓以来川流不息的各个昨天，明天又明天，可能生出什么来？

===Original===
The great drama we herein have called the Modern, unique in the story of mankind, beginning with a small telescope, advancing to the radio, to the measurement of the stars, to the searching out of the utterly minute in Life’s infinitude of variety, to enormous strides in developments of utility, we may say is in character so eye-opening as to constitute the first act in the drama of the universal education of mankind through a series of imposing object lessons, changing situations, shifting scenes. Also, in that act begins the lifting of veils revealing object lessons coming closer up, and closer, from beneath the surface of feudal repression, and of the savage inertia of superstitions born of the habit of fear, and of unawareness, of dread of the reality of man; object lessons—ever object lessons—crowding upon us.

===Chinese===
我们在此称为「现代」的那出伟大戏剧，在人类的故事中独一无二：始于一具小小的望远镜，进展到无线电，到星辰的测量，到在生命无穷品类中对至微至细之物的搜求，到实用进展上的巨大飞跃——我们可以说，其品格之开启眼界，足以构成人类普及教育之戏剧的第一幕：通过一连串气势逼人的实物示教课（object lessons）、变动中的处境、转换中的场景。也正是在那一幕里，帷幕开始掀动，露出越来越近、越来越迫近的示教课——它们从封建压抑的表层之下浮上来，从恐惧习惯所生迷信的蛮荒惰性之下浮上来，从浑然无觉、从对人之实在的畏忌之下浮上来；示教课——永远是示教课——向我们蜂拥而来。

===Original===
Among the most startling of these object lessons we are coming to apperceive the significance of choice—its dire or its joyous man-made results. Slowly in consequence comes forth from the hitherto invisible, and shapes before us, a presence no gesture can debar, no noise of words deter—the sublime, the warning, the prophetic image of man as Moral Power.

Thus clarifies in the dawning light of our modern day the fuller meaning, the effulgence of the Democratic Vista; the super-power of Democratic Man.

===Chinese===
在这些最令人吃惊的示教课之中，我们开始统觉到抉择的意义——它那惨烈或欢畅的、人为的结果。于是，慢慢地，从迄今不可见者之中走出一个存在，在我们面前成形——任何手势都拦不住它，任何词句的喧嚣都阻不住它——人作为道德力量（Moral Power）的那个崇高的、警示的、预言的形象。

于是，在我们现代之日的曙光里，民主远景更充分的意义、它的辉光渐渐澄清；那是民主之人的超级力量。

===Original===
Moral Power, in the intensity of its choice, in the full exercise of its purpose to create a world of sanity, of beauty and of joy, alone can cause to dissolve and fade into thin air as though it had never been, the baleful feudal superstition of dominion and blood-sacrifice.

This moral power residing in the multitudes and awakening to voice, is what Democracy means.

===Chinese===
唯有道德力量，凭着它抉择的强烈，凭着它创造一个健全、美与欢乐之世界的意图的全然行使，才能令那统治与血祭的凶邪封建迷信消解散灭、化为乌有，仿佛它从未存在过。

这居于民众之内、正醒觉发声的道德力量，就是民主（Democracy）的意义所在。

===Original===
To envisage Democracy as a mechanical, political system merely, to place faith in it as such—or in any abstraction, is to foster an hallucination, to join in the Dance of Death; to confuse the hand of Esau with the voice of Jacob. The lifting of the eyelids of the World is what Democracy means.

===Chinese===
把民主仅仅设想为一套机械的政治制度，对它——或对任何抽象物——抱持信心，便是豢养一场幻觉，便是加入死亡之舞；便是把以扫的手与雅各的声音混为一谈。世界抬起它的眼睑——这才是民主的意义。

---

===Original===
The implications of the Democratic Idea branch into endless ramification of science, of art, of all industrial and social activities of human well-being, through which shall flow the wholesome sap of its urge of self-preservation through beneficence, drawn up from roots running ever deeper and spreading ever finer within the rich soil of human kindness and intelligence. For kindness is the sanest of powers, and by its fruits shall Democracy be known. It is of the antitheses that Feudalism has prepared the way for kindness. Kindness, seemingly so weak, is in fact the name of a great adventure which mankind thus far has lacked the courage, the intelligence, the grit to undertake. Its manly, its heroic aspect has been unknown, by reasons of inverted notions of reality. This form of myopia is of the feudal view.

===Chinese===
民主理念的蕴含，分杈进入科学、艺术、人类福祉的一切产业与社会活动的无穷分支；通过这些分支，将流涌它那经由仁善而自我保存的冲动的健全树液——那树液自根系中抽吸上来，根在人类仁厚与智性的沃土中越扎越深、越铺越细。因为仁厚是最健全的力量，民主将凭它的果实为人所知。而正相反相成的是：恰是封建主义为仁厚备好了路。仁厚，看似如此柔弱，实则是一场伟大冒险的名字——这场冒险，人类至今还欠缺勇气、欠缺智性、欠缺坚毅去从事。它那刚健的、英雄的一面所以无人知晓，是因为实在观念被倒置了。这一种近视，正属于封建之见。

===Original===
In place of myopic ideas, democratic modern thought uses clear vision. Clear vision leads to straight thinking, sound thinking to sane action, sane action to beneficent results that shall endure.

In this sense of sound thinking and clean action all sciences, all arts, all activities, become sentimentally, emotionally, dramatically and constructively imbued with the stirring, the self-propelling impulse of the democratic idea. Therefore they will all hold in common a thought whose inexhaustible power will shape a common end which shall signify in the solidity of its logic fruitful peace and joy on earth, as equally the romance of goodwill toward men.

===Chinese===
民主的现代思想不用近视的观念，而用清晰的远见。清晰的远见引出端直的思考，健全的思考引出神志健全的行动，健全的行动引出能够持久的仁善之果。

本着这种健全思考与洁净行动的意识，一切科学、一切艺术、一切活动，都在情感上、情绪上、戏剧性地、建设性地浸透了民主理念那激荡的、自驱的冲动。因此，它们将共同持有一个思想，其不竭之力将塑成一个共同的目的——这目的将以其逻辑的坚实，意味着大地上丰饶的和平与欢乐，也同样意味着对世人善意的那份浪漫。

---

===Original===
Now that we have a clarifying idea of the nature of man and his powers; now that we behold in him that which lies deepest and surest in ourselves, we may suggest the nature of a democratic education.

These things it shall do:

===Chinese===
既然我们已对人的本性及其力量有了一个澄明的观念；既然我们在他身上看见了我们自己之内最深、最可靠的东西，我们便可以设想一种民主教育的性质了。

它将做这些事情：

===Original===
It shall regard the child body, the child mind, the child heart, as a trust.

It shall watch for the first symptom of surviving feudal fear and dissolve it with gentle ridicule while it teaches prudence and the obvious consequences of acts. No child that can toddle bravely is too young to know what choice means, when presented objectively and humanly. Thus it shall teach the nature of choice at the beginning.

===Chinese===
它将把孩子的身体、孩子的心智、孩子的心，看作一份受托之物。

它将警惕残存封建恐惧的最初征候，用温和的嘲笑将它化解，同时又教导审慎与行为的显然后果。凡能勇敢迈出蹒跚步子的孩子，只要客观而合乎人情地呈现给他，没有谁小到不能懂得抉择意味着什么。因此，它将在一开始就教导抉择的本性。

===Original===
It shall allow the child to dream, to give vent to its wondrous imagination, its deep creative instinct, its romance.

It shall recognize that every child is the seat of genius; for genius is the highest form of play with Life’s forces.

===Chinese===
它将容许孩子做梦，容许他倾泻那奇妙的想象力、那深沉的创造本能、他的浪漫奇想。

它将承认，每一个孩子都是天才的所在；因为天才，是与生命诸力游戏的最高形式。

===Original===
It shall allow the precious being to grow in its wholesome atmosphere of activities, giving only that cultivation which a careful gardener gives—the children shall be the garden.

It shall utilize the fact that the child mind, in its own way, can grasp an understanding of things and ideas, supposed now in our pride of feudal thought to be beyond its reach.

===Chinese===
它将容许这珍贵的存在在其健全的活动氛围中生长，只给予一位细心的园丁所给予的那种栽培——孩子们就将是那座花园。

它将利用这样一个事实：孩子的心智，以其自己的方式，能够领会对事物与观念的理解——尽管在我们封建思想的自负里，那些还被认为超出了它的所及。

===Original===
It shall recognize that the child, undisturbed, feels in its own way the sense of power within it, and about it. That by intuition the child is mystic—close to nature’s heart, close to the strength of Earth.

The child thus warded will be a wholesome, happy child. It will forecast the pathway to its maturity.

===Chinese===
它将承认：孩子不受搅扰时，会以自己的方式感觉到他之内、他四周的力量之感。凭着直觉，孩子是神秘的——贴近自然的心，贴近大地的力量。

这样被护佑着的孩子，将是一个健全、快乐的孩子。这将预示出通往他成熟的那条路径。

===Original===
As from tender age the child grows into robust demonstrative vigor, and ebullition of wanton spirits, the technic of warding will pass by degrees into the technic of training or discipline—bodily, mentally, emotionally; the imagination, the intellect, organized to work together; the process of coordination stressed. The idea of the child’s natural powers will be suggested a little at a time and shown objectively.

===Chinese===
随着孩子从幼龄长入茁壮外露的元气，长入恣肆精力的沸涌，护佑之术将渐次转为训练或规训之术——身体的、心智的、情感的；想象力与智性被组织起来协同工作；着重协调的过程。孩子天赋力量的观念，将一点一点地加以提示，并客观地展示出来。

===Original===
The child by this time is passing out of its reveries; life is glowing, very real, very tangible. So shall its awakening powers be trained in the glowing real, the tangible, the three *R*’s, made glowing and real to it as a part of its world. It is here the difference between welcome work and a task comes into play; the difference between a manikin and a teacher.

===Chinese===
孩子此时正走出他的种种冥想；生活正焕发着光，非常真实，非常具体。于是他那些正在苏醒的力量，将在焕发的实在、具体可触之物、以及三个*R*（读、写、算）之中得到训练——这三R对他将被造就得焕发光彩、真实可信，成为他世界的一部分。正是在这里，受欢迎的工作与硬派的功课之间的分别开始起作用；一具人偶与一位教师之间的分别，也是如此。

===Original===
Now arrives the stage of pre-adolescence—unromantic urge of hastening vegetative growth; the period of the literal, the bovine, disturbed at times by prophetic reverie. This is the time for literal instruction.

Now comes the stage of adolescence, when the whole being tends to deliquesce into instability, vague idealisms, emotions hitherto unknown or despised, bashfulness, false pride, false courage, introspection, impulsiveness, inhibitions, awkward consciousness of self, yet with an eye clairvoyant to that beauty which it seeks, a stirring in the soul of glory, of adventure, of romance. The plastic age of impressionability, of enthusiasms. Also the Danger Age; the age of extreme susceptibility under cover of indifference in self-protection: The age when thoughts and musings are most secret. The age that makes or breaks.

===Chinese===
现在到来的是青春期前的阶段——毫无浪漫可言的、植物性生长加速的冲动；一个拘于字面、如牛般浑噩的时期，不时被先知式的冥想搅动。这是进行确切讲授的时期。

现在到来的是青春期的阶段：整个存在都有消融为不稳定的倾向——朦胧的理想主义，前所未有或一向鄙夷的情绪，羞怯，虚假的骄傲，虚假的勇气，内省，冲动，压抑，笨拙的自我意识——然而却有一只千里眼，望得见他所追寻的那种美；灵魂里有荣光、有冒险、有浪漫在搅动。这是可塑的年纪，易感的年纪，热情迸发的年纪。也是危险的年纪；在自我保护的无所谓之掩护下极端易感的年纪：思想与冥想最为隐秘的年纪。成也在此、毁也在此的年纪。

===Original===
This is the crisis where democratic education, recognizing it as such, shall attain to its first main objective in fixing sound character, in alert intensive training of the native power to feel straight, to think straight, to act straight, to encourage pride in well-doing, to make so clear the moral nature of choice that the individual may visualize the responsibilities involved in the consequences of choice. To train the imagination in constructive foresight, in the feeling for real things, in the uses of sentiment, of emotion, in the physical and the spiritual joy of living; to stabilize the gregarious into the social sense; to set forth the dignity of the ego and all egos.

===Chinese===
正是在这一危机处，民主教育认清了它的所在，将达到它的第一个主要目标：固定健全的品格；以警觉而密集的训练，训练那感受端直、思考端直、行动端直的天赋之力；鼓励以善为荣；把抉择的道德性质讲得如此透彻，让个人能够把抉择后果中所牵连的责任观想出来。训练想象力，练其建设性的先见，练其对真实事物的感受，练其情感与情绪的运用，练其身体与精神的生之欢乐；把群居天性稳化为社会感；昭示自我与一切自我的尊严。

===Original===
This is the time to put on the heavy work, to utilize to the full this suddenly evolving power, the recrudescent power of instinct, to direct this power into worthwhile channels, to prepare adolescents to become worthwhile adults, free in spirit, clean in pride, with footing on the solid earth, with social vision clear and true.

===Chinese===
这是承接繁重功课的时候，是充分利用这骤然演化的力量——本能复萌之力——的时候，是把这力量导入值得的渠道的时候，好让少年长成值得的成年人：精神自由，骄傲洁净，脚踏坚实的大地，社会视野清朗真确。

===Original===
The later technical trainings shall be imbued of the same spirit. The varied kinds shall all be set forth as Specialized yet Unified social activities. Science shall be thus understood and utilized, the fine arts shall be thus understood and utilized, the industrial arts, the arts of applied science, and most urgently the science and the art of education, all shall thus be understood and utilized as social functions, ministering to the all-inclusive art of creating out of the cruel feudal chaos of cross purposes, a civilization, in equilibrium, for freemen conscious of their powers, and with these powers under moral control.

===Chinese===
后来的专业技术训练，也应浸透同一种精神。各种门类都应被呈现为专门化而又是统一的社会活动。科学将如此被理解与运用，美术将如此被理解与运用，产业技艺、应用科学诸艺，而最紧迫的是教育这门科学与艺术，都将如此被理解与运用为社会职能，共同效力于那门包罗一切的艺术：从各怀异心的残酷封建混乱之中，创造出一个平衡的文明，给意识到自身力量、并将这些力量置于道德掌控之下的自由人。

===Original===
Such civilization shall endure, and even grow in culture, for it shall have a valid moral foundation, understandable to all. It will possess a vigor hitherto undreamed of, a versatility, a virtuosity, a plasticity as yet unknown, for all work will be done with a living purpose, and the powers of mankind shall be utilized to the full, hence there shall be no waste.

No dream, no aspiration, no prophecy can be saner.

===Chinese===
这样的文明将长久存续，甚至在文化中滋长，因为它将有一个站得住的道德根基，人人都能理解。它将拥有一种做梦也不曾想到的活力，一种多才多艺，一种精湛娴熟，一种迄今未闻的可塑性；因为一切工作都将带着活的目的去做，人的力量将被用到极致，因而将没有浪费。

没有任何梦、任何渴慕、任何预言能比这更健全。

===Original===
Man shall find his anchorage in self-recognition.

*Thus broadens and deepens to our comprehension the power and the glory of the Democratic Vista!*

===Chinese===
人将在自我认取之中找到他的锚地。

*民主远景的力量与荣光，就这样向我们的领悟开阔起来、深湛起来！*

## X Farewell to Boston / 十　告别波士顿

===Original===
During the two years Louis dwelt in the home of the John A. Tompsons, in Wakefield, he was very busy in thought and deed. A certain materialistic clarification of intellect was proceeding within a new light which enabled him to see things superficially and to share in that state of illusion concerning realities which was the common property of the educated and refined. The dreams of childhood—that form of mystical illumination which enables the little one to see that upon which the eyes of its elders seldom focus—were thereby eclipsed; and, in one less romantic and willful by nature, would have vanished permanently from active consciousness in the usual and customary way. For this very period of imaginative childhood is by most adults relegated to obscurity; and if referred to at all, dismissed as inconsequential and “childish.” But childhood, thus banished, remains sequestered within us unchanged. It may be obscured by an overlay of our sophistication, our pride and our disdain; we, the while, unaware that to disdain our fertile childhood is precisely equivalent to disdain of our maturity. Hence the illusion that we are no longer the child; the delusion that we are any other than grown children. For where lives the man who does not firmly believe in magic and in fairy tales; who does not worship something with a childlike faith, who does not dream his dreams, however sordid or destructive, however high, however nobly altruistic? And Louis thus dared to disdain and eclipse his own childhood. For was he not rising now like a toy balloon into the rarefied atmosphere of intellect? And what had intellect to do with childhood? Intellect, indeed, was the cachet of manhood, in whose borderland he was now wandering, making ready to cross the frontier, some day to enter what men called “real life.” This mood began when Louis was well settled in the Massachusetts Institute of Technology—familiarly known as “Tech”—pursuing his special course in Architecture.
===Chinese===
在路易斯借居韦克菲尔德（Wakefield）约翰·A·汤普森（John A. Tompson）府上的那两年里，他在思想上与行动上都忙得很。一种唯物的理智澄清正在一道新光里进行，这道光使他得以浅表地观看事物，得以分享那种对现实的幻见状态——它是受教育者与文雅之士的共同财产。童年诸梦——那种形态的神秘启明，能让孩子看见长辈的眼目甚少聚落之处——就此被遮蚀；而换上一个天性较少浪漫、较少任性的孩子，这些梦早就会循着惯常而寻常的路径，从活跃的意识中一去不返了。因为恰恰这段富于想象的童年，被大多数成年人打发进了晦暗；即便偶尔提及，也被当作无足轻重的「孩子气」而挥斥一边。然而童年虽遭这般放逐，却依然幽居在我们体内，未尝改变。它也许会被我们的世故、我们的骄傲与我们的轻蔑所遮蔽；而我们在此期间浑然不觉：轻蔑我们丰饶的童年，恰恰等同于轻蔑我们的成熟。于是便有了这错觉：我们已不再是孩子；这迷误：我们竟会是长成了的孩子之外的什么东西。试问世上哪里住着一个男人，不坚信魔法与童话；不用孩子般的信仰崇奉着什么；不做着他自己的梦——无论那梦何等卑污、何等毁伤，何等高远，何等高尚地利他？而路易斯竟也这样，敢于轻蔑并遮蚀他自己的童年。因为他此刻不正像一只玩具气球，冉冉升入理智的稀薄大气中去么？而理智与童年又有何相干？理智，确确实实，是成年的印记，而他此刻正游荡在它的边地，准备着有朝一日越过边界，进入人们所谓的「现实生活」。这种心绪，始于路易斯在麻省理工学院（Massachusetts Institute of Technology）——俗称「理工学校」（Tech）——安顿下来、修习建筑专课之时。

===Original===
To John A. Tompson’s tutelage Louis owed many pirouettes, particularly some knowledge and some understanding and misunderstanding of the great oratorios. Under the sway of their beauty, the sensuous allure of the sacred music, Louis would return again and again to his childhood’s sensibilities and faith. But there came a telling change when he had acquired from John A. some knowledge of their structure, some definition and labelling of the wondrous chords and modulations that had exalted him to an agony, and had borne him along in a great resplendent stream of song, which became a stream of wonder upon wonder, that men had made these things—had made them all out of their heads. And in this maze of hero-worship he had dreamed again and again his natal dream of power, of that power within man of which no one had told him; for he had heard only of the power of God. And in this special dream he had in truth and noble faith seen man as magician bringing forth from nothingness, from depths of silence of a huge world of sleep, as though, by waving of some unknown unseen wand, he had evoked this sublime, this amazing fabric; which equally would pass away and vanish with the sound of the last note, even as the bare thought of such passing left a haunt within.

It was then John A. Tompson, he of the precise, the articulate, the exact, the meticulous, the hard intelligence—who bit by bit led Louis on. He dispelled for him the music-world of enchantment wherein simple faith had seen the true substance and value of results; he substituting therefor a world of fact and technique. It was all subtly done, bit by bit. The first effect of this was to arouse in Louis a new interest—an interest in technique—in the how. John A. Tompson, himself, indeed loved these oratorios, with a fanaticism peculiarly his own, somewhat as though he were impersonating a machinist’s vise. He clung to them indeed as though imagining he was a shipwrecked mariner and they a saving raft; yet he was quiet and gleeful amid the dangers of the open sea of sound.
===Chinese===
对于约翰·A·汤普森的耳提面命，路易斯欠下过许多个旋转舞步（pirouettes），尤其是关于那些伟大清唱剧的一点知识、一点理解与误解。在清唱剧之美、圣乐的感官魅惑的笼罩下，路易斯会一次又一次回到他童年的感受力与信仰里去。但当他从约翰·A. 那里得到关于其结构的一些知识、对那些奇妙和弦与转调的一些界定和命名之后，一个意味深长的变化来了——正是那些和弦与转调，曾把他高举到一种近乎痛楚的狂喜里，曾载着他顺流而下，漂在一条煌煌壮丽的歌之巨川里；那巨川变成了一川复一川的惊奇：这些东西竟是人造出来的——全是从人的头脑里造出来的。而在这英雄崇拜的迷宫里，他一次又一次做着他那个与生俱来的梦，力量之梦——那种人身内藏着的、从没有人对他讲起过的力量；因为他听说过的，唯有上帝的大能。而在这个特别的梦里，他怀着真诚而高贵的信仰，看见人是魔法师，从虚无中、从一个沉睡大世界的静默深处，仿佛挥动着一支无人知晓、无人得见的魔杖，便唤出了这座崇高而惊人的宏构；而它同样将随着末一个音符的声响消逝而去、湮没无踪——哪怕仅只是想到这样的消逝，也在他心里留下了一缕萦回不去的阴影。

正是这位约翰·A·汤普森——那精确、明晰、一丝不苟而硬邦邦的智性——一点一点地引领着路易斯。他为他驱散了那音乐魔力的世界，在那世界里，朴素的信仰曾看见成果的真实实质与价值；他拿一个事实与技艺的世界取而代之。这一切都做得细腻入微，一点一点。其第一个效果，是在路易斯心中唤起一种新的兴趣——对技艺的兴趣——对「怎么做」的兴趣。约翰·A·汤普森本人确实热爱这些清唱剧，怀着一种他独有的狂热，那样子颇像在扮演一台钳工的老虎钳。他紧抱着它们，真仿佛自以为是遭遇海难的水手，而它们是救生的木筏；然而在声音的汪洋大海的种种险境之中，他却又安静又欢快。

===Original===
He used to grit his teeth when he was pleased and he frequently was pleased when on shore he was giving Louis a hypodermic of technique. Louis’s utter innocence of music’s artful structure, form and content was John A.’s joy, his secret delight. Thus Louis learned, concerning chords, that the one in particular that had overwhelmed him with a sort of gorgeous sorrow was called the dominant seventh, and another that seemed eerie and that gave him a peculiar nervous thrill and chill was named the augmented fifth. Louis had been very curious concerning these two chords; and furthermore he was insistent to know why certain parts of the music filled him with joyful, inspiriting and triumphant pleasure, while other parts made him sad even to melancholy and despair. He was told that these opposites were known as the major and the minor modes and he was much concerned too, regarding what he later learned were the diatonic and the chromatic scales—and further concerning that strange swaying and turning of surging harmonies—that it was a movement technically known as a modulation from one key into another. Now Louis became avariciously curious concerning all the remaining technicalities and names, and amassed them as one might collect precious curios. It seemed to him that in giving names to all these sounds and movements he had heard and felt, it was much like giving names to the flowers and shrubs and trees he had loved so well. But this difference he marked: That while his plants and trees in spite of names lived on in mystery, and slept their winter sleep, to be again awakened by the call of Spring, giving names to music had dispelled the mystery, and had caused its sweet enchantments one by one to pass in defile into a group of words, which might mean much or nothing according as one first had felt the living power without their aid. That the danger was that music might become enslaved to the intellect and might nevermore be free. For as he began to see the full bulk of the mechanics, the mechanisms, and the tyranny of rules he became alarmed that music might die. For he could not yet see that here also, spite of names, the mystery, the enchantment would live on even though it be in winter sleep, and, at imagination’s rousing call, again and again would renew its onward flow of rejuvenescence, and thus retain its magic power to stir the heart.
===Chinese===
他高兴的时候惯常咬紧牙关；而当他上了岸、给路易斯注射一剂技艺的时候，他往往是高兴的。路易斯对音乐的精巧结构、形式与内容一无所知，这正是约翰·A. 的乐事、他私下的欢喜。就这样，关于和弦，路易斯得知：其中那曾以一种绚烂哀愁将他淹没的，名叫属七和弦（dominant seventh）；另一个显得诡谲、给他一阵奇特的神经战栗与寒意的，名叫增五和弦（augmented fifth）。路易斯对这两个和弦本来好奇得很；此外他还执意要知道，为什么音乐的某些部分令他满心欢喜、奋发、胜利般的愉悦，另一些部分却令他悲伤，直悲到忧郁与绝望。人家告诉他，这两极名为大调（major）与小调（minor）；他还很挂心那两样他后来才学会叫自然音阶（diatonic）与半音音阶（chromatic）的东西——还挂心那奔涌和声的奇异摇曳与回转——那是一种技法上称作由一个调转入另一个调的运动，也就是转调（modulation）。这一来，路易斯对其余所有的技法名目与名称都起了贪馋的好奇，把它们当珍奇的古玩一般积攒起来。在他看来，给他听过、感受过的这一切声音与运动命名，很像给他深爱的花草树木命名。只是他记下了这一个分别：他的花草树木虽然有了名字，依旧活在神秘里，睡着它们的冬眠之眠，等春天的呼唤再来唤醒；而给音乐命名却把神秘驱散了，使它那甜蜜的魅惑一个个鱼贯而行，走成一串词语，这串词语或许多有所指、或许毫无所指，全看人先前是否曾不靠它们而感受到那活生生的力量。危险在于，音乐可能沦为理智的奴婢，从此再不得自由。因为当他开始看清那套机械装置、那些机关、那规则的专制的全貌时，他惊慌起来，生怕音乐要死了。因为他还看不见：在这里，纵然有了名字，那神秘、那魅惑也依旧会活下去，哪怕沉入冬眠；而一闻想象力的唤醒呼声，它便会一次又一次重续它那青春焕发的前流，从而保住它激动人心的魔力。

===Original===
Thus Louis learned a modicum concerning music. A very trifle, to be sure. For he lived in Puritan New England where large utterances of joy and faith in the Earth, of faith in Life, of faith in Man, were few and far between.

Nevertheless he had now definitely entered the cultural world, within which were the blest, without which were the damned. The world of intellectual dissection, surgery and therapeutics; the world of *theory*, of conjecture, of analysis and synthesis; the world of Idea, of Abstraction, of tenuity, of minute distinctions and nuances, filled with its specific belief in magic, its own superstitions, its aberrations, its taboos, denials and negations, and yet equally a world of vast horizons, of eagle-eyed range, of immense powers of ethereal flight to the far and the near, seeking the stars to know them, seeking the most minute to know it, searching the invisible to inquire what may be there, ever roaming, ever inquiring, inquisitive, acquisitive, accumulating a vast fund of the how and why, wherewith to record, to construct, to upbuild; and yet, withal, in giant service to the willful power of Imagination without whose vitalizing spark it could not stir; while in the fullness of its strength it can no more than carry on the heart’s desire.
===Chinese===
就这样，路易斯学到了有关音乐的一点点。确实只是一星半点。因为他生活在清教徒的新英格兰，在那里，对大地、对生命、对人怀有喜乐与信仰的宏大言辞，寥寥无几，旷日难得。

尽管如此，他如今确凿地进入了那个文化世界——居其内者为蒙福者，居其外者为受谴者。那是一个理智的解剖、外科与疗治的世界；*理论*、臆测、分析与综合的世界；观念、抽象、稀薄、毫厘之别与幽微之致的世界，充满了它自己特有的魔法信仰、它自己的迷信、它的乖谬、它的禁忌、否认与否定；然而它又同样是一个地平线辽阔的世界，一个鹰隼般目力的世界，一种以太般飞行的伟力的世界，飞向远与近，寻索群星以求识之，寻索至微以求知之，探察不可见者以究其中所有，永远游弋，永远究问，好奇而喜得，积攒起一笔「如何」与「为何」的浩大库存，用以记录，用以营造，用以建树；然而，归根结底，它是在为那任性的伟力——想象力（Imagination）——做着巨人般的效劳，没有它的赋活火花，理智便无由动弹；而当理智倾尽全力之时，它所能做的，也无非是把心底的愿望奉行到底罢了。

===Original===
The living relationship of Intellect and Instinct has far too long been overlooked. For Intellect is recent, and neuter, and unstable in itself, while Instinct is primordial and procreant: It is a power so vast, so fathomless, so omnipresent, that we ignore it; for it is the vast power of all time that sleeps and dreams; it is that power within whose dream we dream—even as in our practical aspect, our hard headed, cold-blooded, shrewd, calculating suspicious caution we are most obviously dreamers of turbid dreams, for we have pinned our faith to Intellect; we gaze in lethal adoration upon a reed shaken by the wind.
===Chinese===
理智与本能（Instinct）之间这活的关联，被忽视得太久了。因为理智晚出、中性、自身不定；而本能太初而有化育之力：它是一种如此浩大、如此不可测度、如此无处不在的力量，以致我们对它视而不见；因为它是那个睡着并且梦着的、历一切时间而常在的浩大力量；我们正是在它的梦里做我们的梦——正如在我们的实务面相里，在我们那头脑冷静、冷血、精明、工于算计而多疑审慎的模样里，我们分明是浑浊之梦的梦者，因为我们已把信仰针在理智上了；我们怀着致命的崇拜，凝望一根随风摇动的芦苇。

===Original===
About this time flamboyantly arose Patrick Gilmore with his band and his World Jubilee. Then Louis discovered there had been in existence music quite other than oratorio, hymn, sentimental songs of the hoi polloi and burnt-cork minstrels, or the classic grindings of the hurdy-gurdy.

He found it refreshing and gay, melodious above all. When he heard full bosomed Parepa sing in *coloratura*, he could scarcely keep his seat; never was such soprano heard in oratorio, and when the elder Strauss like a little he-wren mounted the conductor’s stand, violin in hand, and dancing, led the orchestra through the lively cadence of the Blue Danube, Louis thought him the biggest little man on earth; and when it came to the “sextette” from Lucia, Louis roared his approval and listened just as eagerly to the inevitable encore. And the “Anvil Chorus”—oh, the Anvil Chorus! And so on, day by day, night by night from glorious beginning to glorious end. He had heard the finest voices in the world, great orchestral outpourings, immense choruses. But he was, above all, amazed at the power of the single voice, when trained to perfection of control. He felt again with delight its unique quality, its range, its fluency, its flexibility, its emotional gamut, its direct personal intimate appeal; he felt a soul, a being, in the single voice, the heartful, the perfect instrument whereby to interpret and convey every state of feeling and of thought; and he was glad indeed.
===Chinese===
大约就在这时，帕特里克·吉尔摩（Patrick Gilmore）带着他的乐队和他的世界庆典音乐节（World Jubilee）轰轰烈烈地登场了。路易斯由此发现，世上还存在着一种音乐，与清唱剧、圣歌、平民大众的感伤小曲、涂黑脸的吟游艺人全然两样，也与手摇风琴磨出来的古典调子全然两样。

他觉出它清爽而欢快，尤其是旋律出众。当他听见丰腴的帕雷帕（Parepa）唱起*花腔*（coloratura）时，几乎按捺不住；清唱剧里何曾听见过这样的女高音；而当老施特劳斯（the elder Strauss）像一只小小的雄鹪鹩，手执提琴登上指挥台，且舞且奏，引领乐队奏完《蓝色多瑙河》（Blue Danube）那活泼的节律时，路易斯觉得他是世上最了不起的小个子；待到《拉美莫尔的露琪亚》（Lucia）的「六重唱」，路易斯高声喝彩，对那必不可少的返场又同样热切地听了下去。还有《铁砧大合唱》（Anvil Chorus）——啊，铁砧大合唱！就这样，日复一日，夜复一夜，从辉煌的开端到辉煌的终局。他听过了世上最美的歌喉、管弦乐的滔滔倾泻、浩大的合唱。而最令他惊异的，还是人的独唱之声被训练到控制臻于完美之境时的力量。他又一次满怀欣喜地感受到它独一的资质、它的音域、它的流畅、它的柔韧、它的情感全域、它那种直抵人心的亲切诉求；他在独唱之声里感受到一个灵魂、一个生命，这颗衷心，这件完美的乐器，足以诠释并传递感受与思想的每一种状态；他实在是欢喜。

===Original===
This blossoming of music exotic to all he had known hitherto, made him glad, made him gay, relaxed his sobriety, refreshed his outlook on life. It filled him with a new consciousness of beauty; of a beauty that seemed free and debonair, like a swan in the pool, like rain on the roof, like roses on a garden wall, with groves, and a turquoise sky; like bold and joyous horses, saying ha! ha!—and like unto furtive gentle creatures of wood and stream, and like curling breakers when close by, or the tossing of trees in a hearty gale.
===Chinese===
这丛音乐之花，于他此前所知的一切都是异域的；它令他欢喜，令他轻快，松开了他的持重，焕新了他对人生的展望。它使他满怀着一种新的美的意识；那美仿佛自由而洒脱，像池中的天鹅，像屋上的雨，像花园墙头的玫瑰，还有小树林，还有绿松色的天空；像雄壮欢腾的骏马，喊着哈！哈！——又像林间溪畔那些隐秘温存的小兽，像近旁翻卷而来的浪花，像劲风里树木的欢腾摇掷。

---

===Original===
More excitement: Came the great conflagration of 9 and 10 November, 1872. Louis saw this terror from its trifling beginning—a small flame curling from the wooden cornice of a building on the north side of Summer street. There were perhaps a half dozen persons present at the time. The street was night-still. It was early. No fire engine came. Horses were sick, “epizootic” was raging. Engines must be drawn by hand. All was quiet as the small flame grew into a whorl and sparks shot upward from a glow behind; the windows became lighted from within. A few more people gathered, but no engine came. Then began a gentle purring roar. The few became a crowd but no engine came. Glass crackled and crashed, flames burst forth madly from all windows, and the lambent dark flames behind them soared high, casting multitudes of sparks and embers abroad, as they cracked and wheezed. The roof fell, the floors collapsed. A hand-drawn engine came, but too late. The front wall tottered, swayed and crumbled to the pavement, exposing to view a roaring furnace. It was too late. The city seemed doomed. With this prelude began the great historic fire. Louis followed its ravages all night long.
===Chinese===
更多的兴奋事：1872 年 11 月 9 日与 10 日的大火灾来了。路易斯从它微不足道的开端便目睹了这场恐怖——一星小火苗，从萨默街（Summer Street）北侧一座建筑的木檐口里蜷曲着探出。当时在场的约莫只有五六个人。街上静得像深夜。时候还早。没有消防车来。马匹病了，「马瘟」（epizootic）正肆虐。消防车须由人力拖曳。一切都静悄悄的，只见那小火苗长成一团旋涡，后头的火光里蹿出火星；窗子从里面亮了。又聚拢了几个人，消防车还是没有来。随后响起一阵低低的呼呼轰鸣。寥寥数人变成了一群人，消防车还是没有来。玻璃噼啪炸裂，火焰从所有的窗口疯狂喷出，它们背后幽幽的暗焰高高腾起，一面噼啪、一面喘息，把成群的火星与余烬抛洒四方。屋顶塌了，楼板陷了。一辆人力拖曳的消防车来了，但为时已迟。临街的前墙晃了晃、倾侧、崩碎在人行道上，露出一座轰鸣的熔炉。太迟了。全城似乎命定难逃。这场历史性的大火便以这一段前奏开了场。路易斯追踪着它的肆虐，彻夜不休。

---

===Original===
It was a magnificent but terrible pageant of wrathful fire before whose onslaught row after row of regimented buildings melted away. As far as the eye could reach all was consuming fire, and dire devastation; an inferno, terrible and wonderful to look upon. Louis went here and there; retreating as the holocaust advanced ever northward. All the city seemed doomed, but it was not. All hope seemed lost, but it was not. The end came at last; courageous, weary and worn men triumphed, after agonies of hope and despair. What a terror, what a holocaust, what ruin of men, what downfall, what instant collapses of fortune, what a heavy load to meet and bear, what a trial and a test. Yet a proud spirit, the eternal spirit of man rose to the height of the call of calamity. The city was rebuilt. For Louis it was a terrifying experience; so sudden, so overwhelming, so fatalistic, so cruel.

When the ruins cooled Louis found it difficult to locate the streets. They seemed labyrinthine, lost in a maze of wreckage and debris; bit by bit he found his strange way about. At night he was put on guard duty as a member of the M.I.T. battalion. Clad in full uniform with Springfield rifle and fixed bayonet at right shoulder, he walked his beat from Tremont street to Pleasant street as far as opposite the tower of the Providence Depot, and return. For hours in the night, all alone, he walked his beat and saw not a soul. At first it was novel and exciting, but as nothing happened, he became weary from loss of sleep, bored by the monotonous to and fro, and glad to be relieved. He had two nights of this. Then came a show of order throughout the city and the great work of clearing and upbuilding, in due time began. He returned to his studies in Tech.
===Chinese===
那是一场壮丽而恐怖的怒火盛典，在它的攻击面前，一列又一列排列成行的楼宇熔化了。目力所及，全是噬人的烈火与惨烈的毁弃；一座地狱，可怖而又壮观。路易斯东奔西走；随着那场浩劫不断向北推进，他步步后撤。全城似乎在劫难逃，却并没有。一切希望似乎都已断绝，却并没有。结局终于到来；勇敢的、精疲力竭的人们胜利了，在希望与绝望的阵阵绞痛之后。何等的恐怖，何等的浩劫，何等的人员毁灭，何等的倾覆，何等的财富瞬息崩塌，何等一副要迎要扛的重担，何等的试炼与考验。然而一种骄傲的精神，人类那不朽的精神，奋起应和了灾难的召唤。城市重建了。对路易斯来说，这是一段骇人的经历；如此骤然，如此排山倒海，如此听凭命运，如此残酷。

废墟冷却之后，路易斯发现要认出街道很不容易。它们像迷宫一般，迷失在残骸与瓦砾的乱阵里；他一点一点地摸出了自己怪异的路径。夜里，他作为麻省理工学院学生营的一员被派去执勤。他一身戎装，斯普林菲尔德步枪上了刺刀，枪托倚在右肩，从特里蒙特街（Tremont Street）走到普莱森特街（Pleasant Street），一直走到普罗维登斯车站（Providence Depot）的塔楼对面，再走回来。夜里的几个钟头，他独自一人巡逻，见不到一个人影。起初觉得新鲜刺激，但因无事发生，他因缺觉而困顿，为单调的往返而无聊，换班时只觉解脱。这样的夜他值了两班。随后全城恢复了秩序的气象，清理与重建的伟大工作适时开始了。他回到理工学校继续学业。

===Original===
He had liked military drill; he had had two years of it at “High.” He liked the exercise, the sense of order and precision, the neat evolutions and the compact team work of the many cadets. But he considered it as discipline in play. He had no thought of war other than to loathe it, as the wild dream of madmen who stood safely behind the evil. For Louis long since had begun to sense and to discern what lay behind the veil of appearances. Social strata had become visible and clear, as also that hypocrisy of caste and cant and “eminence” against which his mother, time and time again, had spoken so clearly, so vehemently in anger and contempt. Her ideal she averred was a righteous man, sound of head, clean of heart, a truthful man too natural to lie or to evade. These outbursts of his mother sank deep into the being of her son; and in looking back adown the years, he has reason justly to appraise in reverence and love a nature so transparent, so pure, so vehement, so sound, so filled with a yearning for the joy of life, so innocent-ecstatic in contemplation of beauty anywhere, as was that of the one who bore him forth, truly in fidelity, to be and to remain life of her life. Thus the curtain of memory ever lifts and falls and lifts again, on one to whom this prayer is addressed. If Louis is not his mother’s spirit in the flesh, then words fail, and memory is vain.
===Chinese===
他喜欢军训；在「英语中学」他操练过两年。他喜欢那运动，那秩序感与精确感，喜欢众学员利落的队形变换与紧凑的协同。但他把它看作游戏中的操练。对于战争，他除憎恶之外别无念头——憎恶它是狂人们的狂想，而那些狂人安然躲藏在邪恶的后面。因为路易斯早已开始感觉、开始辨察表象之幕后面的东西了。社会的层次已变得分明可见，同样分明可见的，还有种姓、伪善与「名位」的虚伪——对此，他的母亲曾一次又一次，怀着愤怒与轻蔑，说得那样清楚，那样激烈。她声称她的理想是一个正直的人：头脑健全，心地干净，一个真实得撒不来谎、躲闪不来的人。母亲的这些激愤之言深深沉入了她儿子的生命；在回望来路之时，他有理由怀着敬意与爱，公正地珍视这样一种天性——如此透明，如此纯净，如此激烈，如此健全，如此满怀着对生命之欢愉的渴望，在凝望无论何处的美时都那样天真而入迷——这正是那个生下他的人的天性；她以真正的忠贞，把他生出来，让他做、并且永远做她生命中的生命。记忆之幕就这样在一个被这篇祷告所祈念的人身上，揭起又落下，落下又揭起。倘若路易斯不是他母亲血肉中的精神，那么言语无能，记忆徒然。

---

===Original===
Upon his entry into Tech Louis felt a marked change in atmosphere from that of “High.” It was now an atmosphere of laissez faire, of a new sort of freedom. Tuition paid, the rest he found was up to him. There was no special regularity of hours or of attendance. He might exert himself or not as he saw fit. He might learn as much or little as he chose. There was no discipline further than this: That one was expected to conduct himself with decorum and with a reasonable degree of application. It was broadly assumed that the student was there in his own interest and would apply himself accordingly.

The school was housed in Rogers Hall, adjoining, on the south, the Museum of Natural History, at Boylston and Berkeley streets. The quarters were pleasant and airy, the long drafting-room or atelier facing broadside to the south. There was also a Library and a Lecture Room. At this date the school was comparatively new, having been opened in 1865. Louis therefore was among its early students. This one building housed the Institute entire.
===Chinese===
一进理工学校，路易斯便感到气氛与「英语中学」截然不同了。现在是一种自由放任（laissez faire）的气氛，一种新式的自由。学费缴了，其余的事他发现全在自己。作息与出勤并无特别的规矩。用功与否，悉听自便。学多学少，随他自选。纪律仅止于此：期望每个人举止得体，用功程度合乎情理。大家泛泛地认定：学生是为了自己的利益而来，自会相应地用功。

校舍设在罗杰斯楼（Rogers Hall），南面毗邻博伊尔斯顿街与伯克利街（Boylston and Berkeley streets）转角的自然历史博物馆（Museum of Natural History）。这些房舍宜人而通透，长长的绘图室——或者说画室（atelier）——整个宽面朝南。还有一间图书馆和一间讲演室。这时学校还相当新，1865 年才开办。路易斯因此算得上它的早期学生。这一座楼，装下了整个学院。

===Original===
The School of Architecture was presided over by Professor William R. Ware, of the Boston architectural firm of Ware & Van Brunt. Among the important works of this firm were the Memorial Building at Harvard, and the large Railway Station at Worcester. Professor Ware was a gentleman of the old school; a bachelor, of good height, slender, bearded in the English fashion, and turning gray. He had his small affectations, harmless enough. His voice was somewhat husky, his polite bearing impeccable and kind. He had a precious sense of quiet humor, and common sense seemed to have a strong hold on him. Withal he was worthy of personal respect and affection. His attainments were moderate in scope and soundly cultural as of the day; his judgments were clear and just. The words amiability and quiet common sense sum up his personality; he was not imaginative enough to be ardent.

His assistant, Eugene Letang, was a *diplomé* of the École National des Beaux Arts, Paris, and specifically an *ancien* of the *atelier libre* of Emil Vaudremer, *architect*, a winner of the Grand Prix de Rome.
===Chinese===
建筑系（School of Architecture）由威廉·R·韦尔教授（Professor William R. Ware）主持，他来自波士顿的建筑事务所韦尔与范·布伦特事务所（Ware & Van Brunt）。这家事务所的重要作品中有哈佛的纪念堂和伍斯特（Worcester）的大火车站。韦尔教授是一位老派绅士；独身，身量颇高，清瘦，胡子按英国式样蓄着，已然花白。他有他小小的做作，无害得很。他的嗓音略带沙哑，礼貌的仪态无可挑剔而和蔼。他有一种可贵的静默的幽默感，常识似乎对他握有强大的支配力。他终究值得个人敬重与爱戴。他的造诣范围适中，就当日标准而言文化教养扎实；他的判断清晰而公正。「和蔼可亲」与「安静的常识」两个词足以概括他的人格；他还不够多想象力，成不了炽热之人。

他的助手欧仁·勒唐（Eugene Letang）是巴黎国立美术学院（École National des Beaux Arts）的*毕业生*（diplomé），说得确切些，是建筑师（architect）埃米尔·沃德雷默（Emil Vaudremer）那间*自由画室*（atelier libre）的*老生*（ancien）；沃德雷默是罗马大奖（Grand Prix de Rome）的得主。

===Original===
This man Letang was sallow earnestness itself; long and lean of face with a scanty student beard. Let us say he was thirty. He had no professional air; he was a student escaped from the Beaux Arts, a transplanted *massier* as it were of the *atelier*, where the *anciens*, the older students, help the *nouveaux*, the younger set, along. He was admirably patient, and seemed to believe in the real value of the work he so candidly was doing; and at times he would say: “From discussion comes the light.” So here was a student absorbed in teaching students, while Professor Ware conserved the worldly pose and poise of the cultural Boston of the time—creating and maintaining thus an air of the legitimate and approved.
===Chinese===
这位勒唐，蜡黄的脸就是「认真」二字的化身；脸又长又瘦，蓄着一撇稀疏的学生胡。姑且说他三十岁吧。他没有职业的架子；他是个从美术学院逃出来的学生，一株移栽过来的画室*社长*（massier）——在画室里，*老生*（anciens）们，年长的学生，帮带*新生*（nouveaux）们，年轻的一辈。他耐心得令人叹服，而且似乎真相信他如此坦率做着的工作具有真实的价值；他时不时会说一句：「光明出自讨论。」于是，这边是一个潜心教学生的学生，那边是韦尔教授端着当日有文化的波士顿那种入世的姿态与镇定——由此营造并维系着一种正当而获得认可的气息。

===Original===
There were perhaps not over thirty students, all told, in the architectural course, and Louis found them agreeable companions. Some of them were University graduates and therefore older than he and much more worldly wise, in their outlook. And there were as well a few advanced students. A few were there as rich men’s sons, to whom the architectural profession seemed to have advantages of tone. Arthur Roche was one of these. A few were there as poor men’s sons. They worked hard to become breadwinners. Among these was William Roche Ware, nephew of the Professor, and George Ferry of Milwaukee. What certain others were there for, including Louis, is a somewhat dubious surmise. But Louis began to like companionship for the first time. Hitherto he had been entirely neglectful of his school comrades, caring neither who nor what they were as persons. Here, however, there was space, freedom of movement and continued personal informal intercourse. So Louis began to put on a bit of swagger, to wear smart clothes, to shave away the down and to agitate a propaganda for inch-long side whiskers. A photograph of that date shows him as a clean-cut young man, with a rather intelligent expression, a heavy mop of black hair neatly parted for the occasion, a pearl stud set in immaculate white, and a suit up to the minute in material and cut. But inasmuch as in this photograph he neither moves nor speaks, we are free to infer that, being young, there may be either something or nothing of real value there. Louis, however, knew more about that picture than the picture knew or could convey of him. For memory, reviving, he knew all his past; and this does not in the least appear in the picture, nor what was of abiding significance in that past. So Louis posed a bit, sensing the reflected prestige and social value of a student at Tech. But he did not altogether make a nuisance of himself, not a complete nuisance, for he was toppy rather than vain.
===Chinese===
建筑课程里的学生，总共大约不超过三十人，路易斯觉得他们是些令人愉快的同伴。其中一些是大学毕业的，因此比他年长，眼界上也比他老练得多。还有几位高年级学生。有几位是富人之子，在他们看来，建筑师这一行似乎别有一种格调上的好处。阿瑟·罗奇（Arthur Roche）便是其中之一。有几位是穷人之子。他们发奋用功，为的是挣得饭碗。这些人里有教授的侄子威廉·罗奇·韦尔（William Roche Ware），还有密尔沃基（Milwaukee）来的乔治·费里（George Ferry）。至于另外某些人——包括路易斯——究竟为何而来，则是个颇可怀疑的猜度了。但路易斯生平第一次开始喜欢起同伴之情。此前他对学校同窗一向全然不顾，既不在乎他们是谁，也不在乎他们为人如何。而在这里，有空间，有行动的自由，有持续而不拘形迹的个人交往。于是路易斯开始摆出一点派头，穿起时髦衣裳，刮去脸上的绒毛，还为倡导一寸长的连鬓胡奔走鼓吹。那个年月的一张照片里，他是一个眉目清爽的青年，神情颇为聪慧，一头浓密的乌发为拍照梳得分缝齐整，雪白的衬衫上缀一粒珍珠纽扣，一身衣料与剪裁都最入时的西装。但既然在这张照片里他既不动、也不说话，我们大可推断：他既然年轻，那里头或许真有些货色，也或许什么都没有。然而路易斯对那张照片的了解，远多于照片对他的了解、或所能传达的他。因为记忆一旦苏醒，他知道自己的全部过往；而这在照片里丝毫看不出，那过往中具有恒久意义的东西也丝毫看不出。于是路易斯摆了一点姿势做态，体味着理工学校学生那份折射而来的声望与社会价值。但他还不至于招人厌，不至于完全招人厌，因为他是自负其才，而非虚荣自恋。

===Original===
Louis had gone at his studies faithfully enough. He learned not only to draw but to draw very well. He traced the “Five Orders of Architecture” in a manner quite resembling copper plate, and he learned about diameters, modules, minutes, entablatures, columns, pediments and so forth and so forth, with the associated minute measurements and copious vocabulary, all of which items he supposed at the time were intended to be received in unquestioning faith, as eternal verities. And he was told that these “Orders” were “Classic,” which implied an arrival at the goal of Platonic perfection of idea.

But Louis by nature was not given to that kind of faith. His faith ever lay in the oft-seen creative power and glory of man. His faith lay indeed in freedom. The song of Spring was the song in his heart. These rigid “Orders” seemed to say, “The book is closed; Art shall die.” Then it occurred to him: Why five orders? Why not one? Each of the five plainly tells a different story. Which one of them shall be sacrosanct? And if one be sacrosanct the remaining four become invalid. Now it would appear by the testimony of the world of scholarship and learning that the Greek is sacrosanct; and of all the Greek, the Parthenon is super-sacrosanct. Therefore there was and has been in all time but the unique Parthenon; all else is invalid. Art is dead. And it should not be forgot that the unique Parthenon was builded by the ancient Greeks, by living men. It was physically upreared in an exact spot on the Acropolis at Athens, a timely demonstration of Greek thought concerning ideas.
===Chinese===
路易斯对待学业算得上勤勉。他不仅学会了制图，而且学得极好。他描摹「建筑五柱式」（Five Orders of Architecture），手法极似铜版蚀刻；他学习了直径、模数、分度、檐部、柱身、山花等等等等，连同相关的琐细尺寸和浩繁的语汇——这一切名目，他当时以为，都是要人不加疑问地凭信仰接受的，当作永恒的真理。人家还告诉他，这些「柱式」是「古典的」，这就意味着观念已达柏拉图式圆满之境的目标。

但路易斯的天性给不出那种信仰。他的信仰始终寄托在人类那屡见不爽的创造伟力与光荣上。他的信仰，实在说，寄托在自由上。春天之歌是他心里的歌。这些僵硬的「柱式」仿佛在说：「书已合上；艺术当死。」于是他想到：为什么是五种柱式？为什么不是一种？五种之中的每一种分明讲着一个不同的故事。它们中间哪一种该奉为神圣？倘若一种是神圣的，其余四种便都成了无效。而照学术界与学问界的证词，似乎希腊的那种是神圣的；在一切希腊的东西里，帕特农神庙（Parthenon）又是神圣中的神圣。因此，古往今来便只有那独一无二的帕特农；其余一切概属无效。艺术已死。但不该忘记的是，那独一无二的帕特农，是古希腊人、是活生生的人建造起来的。它实实在在立在雅典（Athens）卫城（Acropolis）上一个确切的地点，是希腊人关于观念之思想的一次应时昭示。

===Original===
Now after centuries of ruin the Parthenon is dead; therefore all is invalid, Art is dead. This line of reasoning amused Louis quaintly. It seemed to him romantic; much like a fairy tale. And this is all that he gathered from the “Orders”—that they really were fairy tales of the long ago, now by the learned made rigid, mechanical and inane in the books he was pursuing, wherein they were stultified, for lack of common sense and human feeling. Hence he spent much time in the library, looking at pictures of buildings of the past that did not have pediments and columns. He found quite a few and became acquainted with “styles” and learned that styles were not considered sacrosanct, but merely human. That there was a difference in the intellectual and therefore social scale, between a style and an order. Professor Ware did not press matters thus; he did not go so far as to apotheosize the cognoscenti and the intelligentsia. He himself was quite human and in a measure detached. The misfortune was that in his lectures on the history of architecture he never looked his pupils in the eye, but by preference addressed an audience in his beard, in a low and confidential tone, ignoring a game of spitball underway. Yet a word or a phrase reached the open now and then concerning styles, construction and so forth, and at times he went to the blackboard and drew this and that very neatly. Louis picked up something of all this melange, but his thought was mostly on the tower of the New Brattle Street Church, conceived and brought to light by the mighty Richardson, undoubtedly for Louis’s special delight; for was not here a fairy tale indeed! Meanwhile there were *projets* to be done and Eugene Letang surely earned his pay in the sweat of his brow. Prof. William Ware did the higher criticism and frequently announced he had no use for “gimcrack” roofs.

Thus passed the days, the weeks, the months in a sort of mishmash of architectural theology, and Louis came to see that it was not upon the spirit but upon the word that stress was laid, even though it were a weighty matter of sprinkling or immersion. He began to feel a vacancy in himself, the need of something more nutritious to the mind than a play of marionettes. He felt the need and the lack of a red-blooded explanation, of a valiant idea that should bring life to arouse his cemetery of orders and of styles, or at least to bring about a danse macabre to explain why the occupants had lived and died.
===Chinese===
如今，历经数世纪的倾颓，帕特农死了；于是一切皆无效，艺术死了。这套推理让路易斯觉出一种古雅的趣味。在他看来它颇浪漫；很像一篇童话。而这就是他从「柱式」中收获的一切——它们其实是很久很久以前的童话，如今被学问家们弄成僵硬、机械而空洞的东西，印在他正啃着的书里；在这书里，因为缺了常识与人的情感，它们被糟蹋成了呆物。于是他把大量时间花在图书馆里，翻看往昔那些没有山花与柱式的建筑图片。他找到不少，结识了「风格」，并且得知：风格并不被奉为神圣，而只是人的东西。风格与柱式之间，在智识上、因而也在社会阶梯上，是有高下之别的。韦尔教授没有把道理逼到这一步；他还不至于把鉴赏家与知识界捧上神坛。他自己相当通人情，也有几分超然。不幸的是，他在建筑史讲座上从不正眼看学生，而是偏爱对着自己的胡子演讲，语调低低的、推心置腹，对正在进行的一场纸团大战浑然不顾。但关于风格、构造等等，偶尔也有一词半语漏到光天化日下来；有时他走到黑板前，把这样那样画得非常整洁。这一锅杂烩路易斯多少都捡起了一些，但他的心思多半在新布拉特尔街教堂（New Brattle Street Church）的塔楼上——那塔楼出自伟大的理查森（Richardson）的构想与手笔，无疑正是为了给路易斯特别的欢愉；因为这里可不真是一篇童话么！与此同时还有*设计方案*（projets）要做，欧仁·勒唐的薪水真真是眉头上的汗水挣来的。威廉·韦尔教授做的是高等考据，还时常宣布，他可不要那种「花哨货」的屋顶。

日子、星期、月份，就这样在一种建筑神学的杂拌里过去了。路易斯渐渐看出，受强调的不是精神，而是字句——哪怕那字句不过是洒水礼还是浸礼这样的重大事项。他开始感到自己心里有一处空虚，需要某种比提线木偶戏更有滋养的东西。他感到需要、也缺少一种血气充沛的解释，一个勇毅的观念，能把生命带进来，唤醒他那一座柱式与风格的公墓；或者至少来上一场死亡之舞，好解释那些墓中人为何生、又为何死。

===Original===
Moreover, as time passed he began to discover that this school was but a pale reflection of the École des Beaux Arts; and he thought it high time that he go to headquarters to learn if what was preached *there* as a gospel, really signified glad tidings. For Louis felt in his heart that what he had learned at Tech was after all but a polite introduction to the architectural Art—as much as to say, “I am glad to meet you.” He reflected with a sort of despair that neither immaculate Professor Ware nor sweaty, sallow, earnest Eugene Letang was a Moses Woolson. Ah, if but Moses Woolson had been versed in the story of architecture as he was in that of English Literature, and had held the professorship; ah, what glowing flame would have come forth to cast its radiance like a rising sun and illuminate the past. But why dream such foolish dreams?
===Chinese===
况且，随着时间过去，他开始发现，这所学校不过是巴黎美术学院（École des Beaux-Arts）的一抹苍白的映影；他觉得该到总部去一趟了，去看看那边当福音书布讲的东西，究竟是不是真的佳音。因为路易斯心里觉得，他在理工学校学到的，归根结底不过是与建筑艺术的一次客气介绍——不外乎说一声：「幸会。」他带着几分绝望想道：一尘不染的韦尔教授也好，汗涔涔、蜡黄而热诚的欧仁·勒唐也好，都不是摩西·伍尔森（Moses Woolson）。啊，倘若摩西·伍尔森对建筑的故事能像他对英国文学那样烂熟，又执了这教授之席；啊，那时将会有怎样一团炽焰喷薄而出，如朝阳放射光辉，照亮整个过往啊。可是，为什么要做这样的傻梦呢？

===Original===
Louis made up his mind that he would leave Tech at the end of the school year, for he could see no future there. He was progressive, aggressive and impatient. He wished to live in the stream of life. He wished to be impelled by the power of living. He knew what he wanted very well. It behooved him he thought before going to the Beaux Arts, to see what architecture might be like in practice. He thought it might be advisable to spend a year in the office of some architect of standing, that he might see concrete preparations and results; how, in effect, an actual building was brought about. So he said a warm goodbye to Boston, to Wakefield (to his dear South Reading of the past), to all his friends, and made straightway for Philadelphia where he was to find his uncle and his grandpa. On the way he stopped over in New York City for a few days. Richard M. Hunt was the architectural lion there, and the dean of the profession. Louis called upon him in his den, told him his plans and was patted on the back and encouraged as an enterprising youngster. He listened to the mighty man’s tale of his life in Paris with Lefuel, and was then turned over to an assistant named Stratton, a recent arrival from the École to whom he repeated the tale of his projects.

Friend Stratton was most amiable in greeting, and gave Louis much time, receiving him in the fraternal spirit of an older student toward a younger. He sketched the life in Paris and the School—and in closing asked Louis to keep in touch with him and be sure to call on him on the way abroad. Thus Louis, proud and inflated, went on his joyous way to face the world. He arrived in Philadelphia in due time, as they say. He had noticed in New York a sharper form of speech, an increase of energetic action over that he had left behind, and also a rougher and more arrogant type of life Stratton had mentioned that Louis, on his arrival in Philadelphia, should look up the firm of Furness & Hewitt, architects, and try to find a place with them. But this was not Louis’s way of doing. Once settled down in the large quiet village, he began to roam the streets, looking quizzically at buildings as he wandered. On the west side of South Broad street a residence, almost completed, caught his eye like a flower by the roadside. He approached, examined it with curious care, without and within. Here was something fresh and fair to him, a human note, as though someone were talking. He inquired as to the architect and was told: Furness & Hewitt. Now, he saw plainly enough that this was not the work of two men but of one, for he had an instinctive sense of physiognomy, and all buildings thus made their direct appeal to him, pleasant or unpleasant.
===Chinese===
路易斯拿定了主意：学年一到尽头就离开理工学校，因为他在那里看不到前程。他是进取的、莽撞的、性急的。他愿活在生命的川流里。他愿被活的力量推动着。他非常清楚自己要什么。他想，去巴黎美术学院之前，理应先看看建筑在实干中是什么模样。他心想，到某位有声望的建筑师事务所里待上一年或许是明智的，好看见具体的筹备与成果；看看一栋真实的建筑究竟是怎样诞生的。于是他向波士顿、向韦克菲尔德（向他亲爱的、旧日的南雷丁）、向所有的朋友热忱地道了别，径直奔费城（Philadelphia）而去，他的舅舅和外祖父正在那里。途中他在纽约（New York）停留了几天。理查德·M·亨特（Richard M. Hunt）是那里的建筑界雄狮，也是这一行的泰斗。路易斯到他的巢穴里拜见他，讲述了自己的计划，作为一个有闯劲的小伙子被拍了拍背、受了鼓励。他听这位大人物讲了他当年在巴黎随勒菲埃尔（Lefuel）求学的经历，随后被交给一位名叫斯特拉顿（Stratton）的助手——一个刚从美术学院来的人；路易斯对他又把那套志向的宏论讲了一遍。

斯特拉顿友人迎候得极其和蔼，给了路易斯许多时间，以老学生对小学生的兄弟情谊接待他。他略画了画巴黎的生活和那所学校——临别时叮嘱路易斯与他保持联系，出国途中务必再来看他。就这样，路易斯骄傲而飘飘然，欢欢喜喜地上了路，去面对世界。他如期抵达费城，如人们所说。他在纽约已注意到一种更尖利的言谈，比起他离开的地方更多了几分劲健的活力，还有一种更粗粝、更倨傲的生活样式。斯特拉顿曾提到，路易斯一到费城，就该去寻访富内斯与休伊特事务所（Furness & Hewitt）这两位建筑师，设法在那里谋个位置。但这不是路易斯行事的路数。在这座安静的大村落里一经安顿下来，他便开始满街游荡，一路用探究的眼光打量着房屋建筑。在南布罗德街（South Broad Street）西侧，一座接近完工的宅邸像路旁的一朵花，攫住了他的目光。他走上前去，里里外外怀着好奇细细端详。这里有某种他觉得清新而美好的东西，一个人的音调，仿佛有人在说话。他打听建筑师是谁，人家告诉他：富内斯与休伊特。而他看得分明，这不是两个人的作品，而是一个人的——因为他有一种对「面相」本能的辨识力，一切建筑都这样直接向他发出诉求，或悦或不悦。

===Original===
He made up his mind that next day he would enter the employ of said Furness & Hewitt, they to have no voice in the matter, for his mind was made up. So next day he presented himself to Frank Furness and informed him he had come to enter his employ. Frank Furness was a curious character. He affected the English in fashion. He wore loud plaids, and a scowl, and from his face depended fan-like a marvelous red beard, beautiful in tone with each separate hair delicately crinkled from beginning to end. Moreover, his face was snarled and homely as an English bulldog’s. Louis’s eyes were riveted, in infatuation, to this beard, as he listened to a string of oaths yards long. For it seems that after he had delivered his initial fiat, Furness looked at him half blankly, half enraged, as at another kind of dog that had slipped in through the door. His first question had been as to Louis’s experience, to which Louis replied, modestly enough, that he had just come from the Massachusetts Institute of Technology in Boston. This answer was the detonator that set off the mine which blew up in fragments all the schools in the land and scattered the professors headless and limbless to the four quarters of earth and hell. Louis, he said, was a fool. He said Louis was an idiot to have wasted his time in a place where one was filled with sawdust, like a doll, and became a prig, a snob, and an ass.

As the smoke blew away he said: “Of course you don’t know anything and are full of damnable conceit.”
===Chinese===
他拿定主意：第二天就进上述富内斯与休伊特事务所做雇员，由不得他们置喙，因为他主意已定。于是第二天他便站到弗兰克·富内斯（Frank Furness）面前，告知对方：他已前来受雇。弗兰克·富内斯是个奇特的人物。他凡事仿效英国气派做派。他穿花哨的格呢衣裳，一脸怒容，面孔上垂下来一面扇子似的奇妙红胡子，色泽之美，每一根都从头到尾精致地打着卷儿。何况他的脸皱眉瞪眼、其貌不扬，活像一条英国斗牛犬。路易斯的目光痴痴地钉在那把胡子上，听着一长串好几码长的咒骂。因为似乎在他发布完开场白之后，富内斯半是茫然、半是震怒地看着他，像看一条不知怎么从门缝里溜进来的异种狗。富内斯的第一个问题问的是路易斯的经历，路易斯的回答足够谦逊：他刚从波士顿的麻省理工学院出来。这个回答便是引爆雷管的撞针，轰然炸开了地雷，把全国的学校炸得粉碎，把诸位教授炸得缺头少肢，四散炸飞到大地上和地狱的四面八方。他说，路易斯是个傻瓜。他说路易斯是个白痴，竟把光阴虚掷在一个把人塞满锯末、变成玩偶、又变成自命不凡的道学先生、势利眼和蠢驴的地方。

硝烟散去后他说：「当然咯，你什么都不懂，还满肚子该死的自负。」

===Original===
Louis agreed to the ignorance; demurred as to conceit; and added that he belonged to that rare class who were capable of learning, and desired to learn. This answer mollified the dog-man, and he seemed intrigued that Louis stared at him so pertinaciously. At last he asked Louis what in hell had brought him there, anyway? This was the opening for which Louis had sagaciously been waiting through the storm. He told Frank Furness all about his unaided discovery of the dwelling on Broad street, how he had followed, so to speak from the nugget to the solid vein; that here he was and here he would remain; he had made up his mind as to that, and he looked Frank Furness in the eye. Then he sang a song of praise like a youthful bard of old to his liege lord, steering clear of too gross adulation, placing all on a high plane of accomplishment. It was here, Louis said, one could really learn. Frank Furness admitted as true a part of what Louis had said, waving the rest away as one pleasantly overpraised, and said: “Only the Greeks knew how to build.”

“Of course, you don’t want any pay,” he said, to which Louis replied that ten dollars a week would be a necessary honorarium.
===Chinese===
无知这一条，路易斯认了；自负这一条，他抗辩了几句；并补充说，他属于那种罕见的、有能力学习并且渴望学习的人。这个回答让这位犬人平息了下来，而路易斯如此死死盯着他看，似乎倒引起了他的兴味。末了他问路易斯：究竟是什么鬼把他引到这儿来的？这正是路易斯在风暴中明智等候着的破口。他对弗兰克·富内斯从头到尾讲了讲他如何独自发现了布罗德街上那座宅邸，他如何——不妨这样说——从天然金块追到了坚实的矿脉；他人在这里，人也将留在这里；这一点他主意已定，而且他直望着弗兰克·富内斯的眼睛。随后他唱起了一支颂歌，像古时年轻的吟游诗人对着他的君王，避开过于露骨的谀词，把一切都安放在成就的高境上。正是在这里，路易斯说，人才能真正学到东西。弗兰克·富内斯承认路易斯所说有一部分属实，其余的则像受誉过甚的人那样挥手拂去，然后说：「只有希腊人才懂得怎样盖房子。」

「当然咯，你是不要工钱的，」他说。路易斯答道：每星期十美元，是一份必不可少的酬金。

===Original===
“All right,” said he of the glorious beard, with something scraggy on his face, that might have been a smile. “Come tomorrow morning for a trial, but I prophesy you won’t outlast a week.” So Louis came. At the end of that week Furness said, “You may stay another week,” and at the end of that week Furness said, “You may stay as long as you like.” Oh what a joy! Louis’s first task was to retrace a set of plans complete for a Savings Institution to be erected on Chestnut street. This he did so systematically and in so short a time that he won his spurs at once. In doing this work he was but carrying out the impulsion of Moses Woolson’s training in accuracy and speed; and Moses Woolson followed him thereafter everywhere.
===Chinese===
「行，」那位光荣的胡子说，脸上掠过一样皱巴巴的东西，也许算是微笑。「明早来试用吧。不过我预言，你撑不过一个星期。」于是路易斯来了。一个星期结束时，富内斯说：「你可以再留一星期。」那一星期结束时，富内斯说：「你爱留多久留多久。」啊，何等的欢喜！路易斯的头一件差事，是把一套要在栗树街（Chestnut Street）兴建储蓄机构（Savings Institution）的完整图纸重新描一遍。他办得井井有条、又那么快，当即赢得了他的马刺。做这件工作时，他不过是把摩西·伍尔森那准确与神速的训练所赋予的冲动付诸实行；而摩西·伍尔森从此便跟着他，走到哪里跟到哪里。

===Original===
The other members of the firm was George Hewitt, a slender, moustached person, pale and reserved, who seldom relaxed from pose. It was he who did the Victorian Gothic in its pantalettes, when a church building or something of that sort was on the boards. With precision, as though he held his elements by pincers, he worked out these decorous sublimities of inanity, as per the English current magazines and other English sources. He was a clean draftsman, and believed implicitly that all that was good was English. Louis regarded him with admiration as a draftsman, and with mild contempt as a man who kept his nose in books. Frank Furness “made buildings out of his head.” That suited Louis better. And Furness as a freehand draftsman was extraordinary. He had Louis hypnotized, especially when he drew and swore at the same time.

But George Hewitt had a younger brother named John, and John was foreman of the shop. He was a husky, smooth-faced fellow under thirty. Every feature in his clean cut, rather elongated face, bespoke intelligence and kindness, in fact a big heart. He had taken a fancy to Louis from the start. He was the “practical man” and Louis ran to him for advice whenever he found himself in a tight place. John was patience itself and made everything clear with dainty sketches and explanatory notes. These drawings were beautiful and Louis frankly told him so. He begged John to teach him “touch” and how to make such sketches, and especially how to “indicate” so crisply. This John did. In fact, it was not long before he had made of Louis a draftsman of the upper Crust, and Louis’s heart went out to lovable John in sheer gratitude.
===Chinese===
事务所的另一位成员是乔治·休伊特（George Hewitt），一个清瘦的、蓄着髭须的人，苍白而缄默，难得从架子里松懈下来。每逢教堂建筑或诸如此类的东西上了图板，做那穿灯笼裤的维多利亚哥特式的，便是他。他以精准的手法——仿佛用钳子夹着他的各种元素——加工出这些端庄崇高的空洞之物，一律遵照英国时新杂志和其他英国来源。他是个洁净的制图员，并且不折不扣地相信：凡是好的，都是英国的。路易斯作为制图员敬服他，作为一个把鼻子埋在书里的人则对他有温和的轻蔑。弗兰克·富内斯「凭脑袋造房子」。这更合路易斯的胃口。而富内斯作为徒手制图员是非凡的。他把路易斯催眠了，尤其是当他一边画图一边咒骂的时候。

但乔治·休伊特有个弟弟叫约翰（John），约翰是制图间的领班。他是个三十岁不到、脸庞光洁的壮实汉子。他那清秀而略长的脸上，每一处眉目都透着聪慧与和善，实际上是一颗宽厚的心。他从一开始就喜欢上了路易斯。他是那位「实干的人」，路易斯一发现自己陷入窘境，就跑去向他讨教。约翰就是耐心本身，用精巧的小速写和说明性注解把一切都讲得明明白白。这些图漂亮得很，路易斯也坦率地这么告诉他。他恳求约翰教他「笔触」（touch），教他怎样画这样的速写，尤其是怎样如此爽利地「点到即止」（indicate）。约翰教了。事实上，没过多久，他就把路易斯调教成了一个头一等的制图员，而路易斯的心也向着可爱的约翰倾倒过去，满怀纯粹的感激。

===Original===
In looking back upon that time Louis Sullivan gives thanks that it was his great good fortune to have made his entry into the practical world in an office where standards were so high—where talent was so manifestly taken for granted, and the atmosphere the free and easy one of a true work shop savoring of the guild where craftsmanship was paramount and personal. And again he goes back to the day of Moses Woolson and his discipline. We may say in truth that Moses Woolson put him there. For without that elastic alertness and courage, that grimness Moses Woolson imparted, it is sure that Louis would not have broken through the barrier of contempt in that first interview.

Louis worked very hard day and night. At first he had lived with his grandpa and uncle in West Philadelphia. But soon he decided to move into town to be nearer the office and to be freer to study into the small hours. His relaxation on Sundays was Fair-mount Park and a walk up the rough road of the Wissahickon valley, a narrow beauteous wilderness such as Louis had never seen, and with which he was completely charmed. He loved the solitude through which the Wissahickon purled its way. The companionship of the wild was soothing to him. The isolation gave him comfort and surcease. Thus passed a hot summer.
===Chinese===
在回顾那段岁月时，路易斯·沙利文心怀感谢：他的大运气，是走进实务世界的头一步，便跨进了这样一间标准高企的事务所——在那里，才干被如此显豁地视为理所当然，气氛则是一个真正作坊的自由自在，带着行会的风味，手工技艺至上，而且全然关乎个人。他又回到了摩西·伍尔森和他的训练的那些日子。我们可以确切地说：是摩西·伍尔森把他放到那里去的。因为若没有那种富有弹性的机敏与勇气，那种摩西·伍尔森灌输给他的冷峻，路易斯定然闯不过头一次面谈中那道轻蔑的壁垒。

路易斯日夜苦干。起初他同外祖父和舅舅住在西费城（West Philadelphia）。但不久他便决定搬进城去，好离事务所近些，也好多几分自由读书到深夜。星期日的消遣，是费尔芒特公园（Fair-mount Park）和沿着维萨希肯谷（Wissahickon）的崎岖小路走上一程——那样一条狭窄而美丽的荒野溪谷，路易斯从未见过，也为之心醉神驰。他爱那片幽寂，维萨希肯溪一路淙淙流过其间。荒野的陪伴使他平静。那份孤寂给他安慰与止息。一个炎热的夏天就这样过去了。

---

===Original===
The offices of Furness & Hewitt occupied the entire top floor of a new, brick, four-story building at the northeast corner of Third street and Chestnut.

One day in September, it was very warm, all windows were open for air, the force was wearily at work. As they worked, there came through the open windows a murmur, barely noticed at first; then this murmur became a roar, with wild shouting. Then, all to the windows. Louis saw, far below, not pavement and sidewalks, but a solid black mass of frantic men, crowded, jammed from wall to wall. The offices of Jay Cooke & Co. were but a short distance south on Third street. Word came up that Jay Cooke & Co. had just closed its doors. Louis saw it all, as he could see down both Chestnut street and Third. Chestnut westward from Third also was a solid mass. The run on the banks had begun. The devastating panic of 1873 was on, in its mad career. Louis was shocked, appalled at the sight. He was too young, too inexperienced, to understand what it really meant, even when told it was a panic in finance, that credit had crumbled to dust, that men were ruined, and insane with despair; that this panic would spread like wildfire over the land leaving ruin in its wake everywhere. And still he could not understand what had brought it about.
===Chinese===
富内斯与休伊特事务所，占据了第三街（Third Street）与栗树街转角东北侧一座新建四层砖楼的整个顶层。

九月里的一天，天气很热，所有窗子都开着透气，职员们在困倦地工作。干着干着，开着的窗外传来一阵低语，起初几乎无人注意；随后低语变成了吼声，夹着狂野的呼喊。于是所有人都涌向窗口。路易斯看见，在下面很远的地方，不是路面和人行道，而是一大块密实的黑色人团，全是发了狂的男人，挤得满坑满谷、从一堵墙到另一堵墙。杰伊·库克公司（Jay Cooke & Co.）的办公处就在南边第三街上不远处。消息传上来：杰伊·库克公司刚刚关上了大门。路易斯把这一切尽收眼底，因为他顺着栗树街和第三街两条街都能望下去。栗树街从第三街往西，也是黑压压实心的一片。挤兑开始了。毁灭性的 1873 年大恐慌正发着疯，横冲直撞。路易斯震惊了，被眼前的景象吓呆了。他太年轻、太没有阅历，即使听人说这是金融恐慌、信用已碎为齑粉、多少人倾家荡产、绝望得发了疯、这恐慌将如燎原之火烧遍全国、所过之处处处留下废墟，他仍然不明白这一切究竟意味着什么。而他仍旧无法明白，这一切是何以酿成的。

===Original===
The office held steady for a while; there was work on hand which had progressed so far that it must be completed.

One day in November Frank Furness said: “Sullivan, I’m sorry, the jig is up. There’ll be no more building. The office now is running dry. You’ve done well, mighty well. I like you. I wish you might stay. But as you were the last to come it is only just you should be first to go.” With that he slipped a bill into Louis’s hand, and wished him farewell and better days.
===Chinese===
事务所撑住了一阵子；手头有的工作已经进行到这个地步，非完成不可。

十一月里的一天，弗兰克·富内斯说：「沙利文，很抱歉，戏收场了。不会再有房子盖了。事务所这就要干涸了。你干得很好，好得很。我喜欢你。我本希望你留下。可是你来得最晚，你最先走，这才公平。」说着，他把一张钞票塞进路易斯手里，祝他前程似锦、来日更好。

===Original===
Within a week Louis took the Pennsylvania train for Chicago. He saw the great valley of the Susquehanna; surmounted the huge Alleghenies; passed along the great descending Horseshoe Curve, the marvel of the day; and then night fell. He was aroused and broadened by what he had seen. It was all new. His map was enlarged. So was his breadth of view; his inner wealth.

Next morning he was utterly amazed and bewildered at the sight of the prairies of northern Indiana. They were startling in novelty. How could such things be! Stretching like a floor to the far horizon—not a tree except by a watercourse or on a solitary “island.” It was amazing. Here was power—power greater than the mountains.
