Soon Louis caught glimpses of a great lake, spreading also like a floor to the far horizon, superbly beautiful in color, under a lucent sky. Here again was power, naked power, naked as the prairies, greater than the mountains. And over all spanned the dome of the sky, resting on the rim of the horizon far away on all sides, eternally calm overhead, holding an atmosphere pellucid and serene. And here again was a power, a vast open power, a power greater than the tiny mountains. Here, in full view, was the light of the world, companion of the earth, a power greater than the lake and the prairie below, but not greater than man in his power: So Louis thought.
===Chinese===
不出一个星期，路易斯便登上了开往芝加哥（Chicago）的宾夕法尼亚火车。他看见了萨斯奎哈纳河（Susquehanna）的大河谷；翻越了巍峨的阿勒格尼山脉（Alleghenies）；沿着那了不起的下坡「马蹄弯」（Horseshoe Curve）——当时的工程奇迹——一路而下；随后夜幕降临。他所见到的一切使他振奋，也使他开阔。一切都是新的。他的地图扩大了。他的眼界也扩大了；还有他内心的财富。

第二天早晨，北印第安纳（Indiana）的草原在他眼前铺开，他全然惊呆了、迷乱了。它们新颖得吓人。这样的事物怎么可能存在！像一方地板铺展到遥远的地平线——除水边或孤零零一座「岛屿」之外，不见一棵树。真是惊人。这里是力量——比群山更伟大的力量。不久路易斯瞥见了一片大湖，也像地板一样铺展到遥远的地平线，在澄澈的天空下色彩壮丽之极。这里又是力量，赤裸的力量，像草原一样赤裸，比群山更伟大。而在这一切之上，穹顶般的天穹笼罩四野，远远安放在四面八方的地平线边缘上，头顶永远宁静，涵着一片澄明安详的大气。这里又是一种力量，一种浩大而坦荡的力量，一种比那小小的群山更伟大的力量。这里，尽显眼前的，是世界之光，大地的伴侣，一种比脚下湖泊与草原更伟大的力量，却不比力量之中的人更伟大：路易斯如是想。

===Original===
The train neared the city; it broke into the city; it plowed its way through miles of shanties disheartening and dirty gray. It reached its terminal at an open shed. Louis tramped the platform, stopped, looked toward the city, ruins around him; looked at the sky; and as one alone, stamped his foot, raised his hand and cried in full voice:
===Chinese===
火车逼近城市；它闯进了城市；它在绵延数英里、令人灰心的灰扑扑的棚户区里犁出一条路来。它抵达终点，一座敞棚。路易斯踏上月台，停下脚步，朝城市望去，四下尽是废墟；又望了望天空；而后像一个孤身立于天地之间的人，顿了顿脚，举起手来，放声高喊：

===Original===
> **“This is the place for me!”**
===Chinese===
> **「这正是我要来的地方！」**

===Original===
That day was the day before Thanksgiving in the year Eighteen Hundred Seventy-three.
===Chinese===
那一天，是 1873 年感恩节的前一天。

## II / 二

===Original===
> “There was a child went forth every day.”
> — Whitman
===Chinese===
> 「有一个孩子天天向前走去。」
> ——惠特曼

===Original===
Thus after traversing a long orbit inversely to the prehistoric of the family genealogy, and tracing, on the backward swing, the curve of a little one’s experience in contact with the outer world and his individual impulsive responses thereto, we again take the train for South Reading.
===Chinese===
就这样，我们朝着与家族谱系的太古洪荒相逆的方向，绕行了一条长长的轨道；又在回摆之际，描画出一个幼儿接触外部世界的经验曲线，以及他对此一连串出于本性的冲动回应。如今，我们再度登上开往南雷丁的火车。

===Original===
Arriving at the station a man descends, asks directions, and follows the first dirt road to the left, leading over an almost treeless flat, and heading for a somewhat distant hill. Part way up the hill he notices a house on the right. Here lived a man named Whittemore, who having lost a leg, proceeded in due consideration of the remaining one, to invent, perfect and manufacture a new type of crutch, which has remained the standard to this day. The workshop stood some distance back of the house, just at the beginning of the pine woods that covered part of the hill. The road here takes a curve to the right, traverses the back of the hillside with a heavy growth of pines on the right ascension, and a neat valley to the left with scattering woods and meadow. The road then straightens, becomes of easy grade, and begins to emerge from the wilderness, so to speak. An orchard comes into view on the left, a field of herds-grass on the smoothly rounded hilltop at the right. Straight ahead, running at the right angles and terminating the road thus far traversed, was the main road from South Reading to Stoneham. The land here was level for a moment or two. At the left-hand corner of the intersection stood a rather modern house, clapboarded, painted white with green shutters, and in front of it on the Stoneham Road were two stately elms. Here lived the Tompsons. The person who made this trip had no sooner reached the intersection and made a mental note or two of the surroundings than he saw a middle-aged or elderly couple, quite near, slowly approaching from the left on the road running toward South Reading. They were leading between them a chubby child who was screaming at the top of his angry voice, crying savagely, declaring vindictively he would not go, he *would not* go to school. The traveler must have worn the tarnhelm of legend, for they saw him not. To our thinking he was a phantasm of years to come. The child was absurdly dressed. Under an immense straw hat, curving broadly upward at the brim and tied on with a ribbon, appeared his upturned face, red, bloated, distorted; angry eyes, terribly bright, running with tears in a stream; a mouth hideously twisted out of shape. Below this raging hell was a sort of white jacket and a big bow tie. Below this, *white pantalettes*, gathered in at the ankle and more or less flounced or frizzled. These pantalettes were the source of his fear, of his rage and his protest. He had already on account of them, he said, been regularly insulted by the neighbors’ children who had formed a circle around him and danced, sneered, pointed the index of scorn, and made merry. Was that not enough? Must he now face a schoolful of tormentors? He would not go, he would *not* go! He bawled and screamed that he would not *go*! The child was on the verge of hysterics; it seemed less agonizing to face death than ridicule. The elders consulted quietly, turned back, the child still between them, and disappeared at the entranceway of a house a hundred yards or so beyond the Tompsons on the Stoneham Road. Next day, the child appeared in conventional garb. His name was Louis, or, as his Grandmother pronounced it, Louie. It was a joyous day for him, a sad day for her. For in her heart she knew that with the laying away of the pantalettes there was laid away a child﻿—a child gone forever﻿—a child soon to be but a sweet memory﻿—a child soon to metamorphose into a tousle-headed, freckled, more or less toothless, unclean selfish urchin in jeans; and that he would continue to grow bigger, stronger, rougher, and gradually grow away from her﻿—ever more masculine, ever more selfish. But this apprehension, this heart’s foreboding was not to come wholly true, for she held his love﻿—she held it to the end. The child was not an *enfant terrible*; he was, rather, an independent, isolated compound of fury, curiosity and tenderness. Subtle indeed were the currents flowing and mingling within him, embryonic passions arising and shaping, ambitions vaguely stirring; while his sharp eyes saw everything. Spring was on the wane. The birds were full-throated in glorification of the number of bugs and worms eaten, or the intensive discussion of domestic affairs. High up in one of the Tompson elms﻿—the one to the east﻿—hung the purse-like nest of the selfsame golden orioles that came there year by year, while from a nearby meadow floated the tinkle of a solitary bob-o-link winging its way rejoicing. The day was beauteous; full sunshine flooded and enfolded all. The child, after much thought﻿—of its kind﻿—suddenly announced he was ready. His curiosity had been insidiously at work. He would see the school; he would meet new children; he had become eager; he would be a big boy in the world’s opinion. So, on this same cheerful morning, hand in hand with Grandma, who alone habitually assumed responsibilities, he began the pilgrimage of learning that hath no end. They took the dusty road that led eastward, directly toward the north end of the village. They leisurely mounted a gentle grade until the crest was reached. At this exact point, just behind the stone wall to the right of the road﻿—marvel of marvels﻿—stood a gigantic, solitary ash tree. On account of a certain chipmunk, various flowers, pebbles, and other things, the child had not noticed it during the approach. But of a sudden, there it stood, grand, overwhelming, with its immense trunk, its broad branches nearly sweeping the grass, its towering dome of dense dark green; opposite it, across the road was a farm house; back of it an open pasture. From the vantage of the road spread out a view of things below. The grandmother was for going on. The child stood transfixed, appalled. A strange faraway storm, as of distant thundering, was arising within his wonderself. He had seen many trees, yes; but this tree﻿—*this tree*! He trembled strangely, he wished to cry; with gentle scolding he was dragged away. From this point the road was bare and shaggy. Halfway down, to the left, and set well back, was found not the little red schoolhouse of romance, but a rather large white one, clapboarded, green blinds, gabled, a bell, a well with force-pump, trampled playground, and so on. He was duly presented to the teacher. Her face and form, alas, like many another face and form, have passed into memory’s oblivion. All details settled, he was to come the next morning, which he did, after successfully passing the magnet tree, while saluting it affectionately in a calmer mood. Day after day he passed the tree. It became *his* tree﻿—his Great Friend.
===Chinese===
到了站，一个男人走下车来，问明路径，顺着左手第一条土路走去；这条路越过一片几乎不见树木的平地，通向一座稍远的山丘。上到半坡，他注意到右手边有一户人家。这里住着一个姓惠特莫尔（Whittemore）的人；此人失了一条腿，出于对仅存那条腿的郑重考虑，着手发明、改良并制造出一种新型拐杖，至今仍是通行的标准式样。作坊坐落在屋子后面一段路的地方，恰好在一带松林的起点，那松林覆住了山丘的一部分。道路在这里向右一弯，绕行山坡的背面——右侧的上坡地带松林茂密，左边则是一道齐整的山谷，点缀着疏林与草甸。随后道路转直，坡度平缓下来，可以说，开始渐渐走出荒野。左边现出一座果园，右边浑圆的山顶上是平平整整一片猫尾草（herds-grass）田。正前方，一条与来路成直角相交的大道截住了去路，那便是从南雷丁通往斯托纳姆的大道。这一带的地面稍稍平坦了一小段。交叉口的左手拐角上立着一座相当时新的房子，鱼鳞板外墙，白漆，绿色百叶窗；房子前面的斯托纳姆路（Stoneham Road）边，挺立着两棵气宇轩昂的榆树。这里住着汤普森一家（the Tompsons）。作此一行的人刚到路口，心里刚把周遭景致记下一两笔，便看见一对中年或已上年纪的夫妇，就在近旁，从左边通往南雷丁的路上缓缓走来。夫妇俩中间牵着的一个胖乎乎的孩子，正扯着愤怒的嗓门尖声大叫，撒野地哭嚎，恨恨地宣告他不去，他*偏不*去上学。这位旅人想必是戴上了传说里的隐身盔（Tarnhelm），因为他们压根儿没有看见他。在我们看来，他是从未来岁月里来的一个幻影。这孩子穿得一副滑稽相。一顶硕大无朋的草帽，帽檐向四外宽宽上翘，用一根缎带系在头上；帽檐底下露出他仰起的小脸：通红，涨鼓鼓，走了形；一双怒眼亮得吓人，眼泪成串地淌；一张嘴可怕地扭曲变形。在这座怒火熊熊的地狱之下，是一件白色的短外衣，一个大大的蝶形领结。再往下，是*白色的灯笼裤*（pantalettes），裤脚在脚踝处收拢，缀着或多或少的花边褶襞。这灯笼裤正是他恐惧、暴怒与抗议的根源。他说，就为了这个，他已经正正经经地受过邻家孩子们的侮辱：他们围着他站成一圈，跳舞，讥笑，伸出轻蔑的食指指指点点，乐不可支。这难道还不够？难道如今还要他去面对满满一学堂折磨人的家伙？他不去，他*就是不去*！他嚎着、叫着，说他*不*去！这孩子眼看就要歇斯底里了；在他看来，直面死亡似乎都比受人嘲弄好受些。两位长者低声商量了一下，转身往回走，孩子仍被牵在两人中间，随后在斯托纳姆路上汤普森家再往前一百来码的一户人家门口消失了。第二天，这孩子便以一身寻常装束露面了。他的名字叫路易斯，或者，照他外祖母的念法，是路易。于他这是欢天喜地的一天，于她却是伤心的一天。因为她心里知道，随着那灯笼裤被收起，一个孩子也被一并收起了——一个一去不返的孩子——一个不久便只剩一缕甜蜜回忆的孩子——一个很快就要变成蓬头散发、满脸雀斑、缺这少那几颗牙、不讲干净、自私自利、穿粗布裤子的顽童的孩子；而且他还会越长越大、越长越壮、越来越粗野，渐渐离她远去——越来越男子气，越来越自私。不过这份忧惧、这心头的不祥预感，终究没有全然应验，因为她攥住了他的爱——她把它攥到了最后。这孩子并不是什么*可怕的顽童*（enfant terrible）；毋宁说，他是一个独立的、孤零零的混合体，由暴怒、好奇与温柔化合而成。在他体内流转交融的暗流着实微妙，胚芽般的种种激情正升起成形，种种抱负在依稀悸动；而他那双锐利的眼睛，什么都看得见。春天正在消逝。鸟儿们引吭高歌，颂扬着吞下的虫子的数目，或是热烈讨论着家务事。汤普森家的一棵榆树上——靠东的那一棵——高高悬着那只钱袋似的鸟巢，巢主正是年年飞回来的那对金黄鹂（golden orioles）；附近一片草甸上，则飘来一只刺歌雀（bob-o-link）独自飞过时叮咚如铃的欢畅啼鸣。这一天明媚可人；饱满的阳光漫涌而来，把万物拥进怀里。这孩子经过了——以他那个年纪的方式——一番深思，忽然宣布他准备好了。他的好奇心早已在悄悄使劲。他要去看学校，他要见见新的孩子们；他已急切起来；在世人的眼光里，他要当个大男孩了。于是，就在这个同样晴朗的早晨，他牵着外祖母的手——家里惯于担起责任的，唯有她一人——踏上了那条永无止境的学习的朝圣之路。两人走上那条朝东去的尘土路，直通村子的北端，又从容地登上一道缓坡，直到坡顶。恰恰在这一点上，路右边石墙的后头——奇迹中的奇迹——立着一棵巨大的、孤零零的白蜡树。因为一路上惦记着一只花栗鼠（chipmunk）、各样花草、卵石和别的种种，孩子来时竟不曾留意到它。可是冷不丁地，它就立在了那里：魁伟，摄人，树身巨大，宽宽的枝柯几乎扫着草地，浓密深绿的树冠高高隆起如穹顶；路的对面正对着它的是一座农舍，它背后是一片开阔的牧场。从路边这块制高点望下去，低处的一切铺展开来。外祖母只顾赶路。孩子却呆立当场，心惊魄骇。一场奇异而邈远的风暴，仿佛远方的雷鸣，正在他那惊奇的小自我深处酝酿升起。他见过的树不少，是的；可是这棵树——*这棵树*！他奇怪地颤抖着，直想哭；在外祖母温言的嗔怪里，他被拽走了。从这一点往前，路面荒秃粗粝。下到半坡，在左边、离路稍退后的一处，见到的不是传奇故事里的小红校舍，而是一座相当大的白校舍：鱼鳞板墙，绿色百叶窗，人字屋顶，一口钟，一口带压力泵（force-pump）的水井，一片踩得瓷实的操场，等等。他被郑重其事地引见给老师。她的容貌身形，唉，同许多别的容貌身形一样，都已沉入记忆的忘川。一应细节谈妥，约定次日早晨来上学；他果真来了——先是顺利走过了那棵磁石般的树，还怀着平静了些的心情向它亲亲热热地致意。一天又一天，他从树下走过。它成了*他的*树——他的伟大的朋友。

===Original===
He was to spend many days at this barren hillside school. He became acquainted with the boys and girls there, for it was coeducational. What these children did during the recess hour would scandalize the wholly good. But to the casual sinner, scrutinizing the depths of his own past, reason might be found and a certain tolerance engendered whereby these vagaries of small animals, if not exactly condoned, might at least be minimized as the native output or byproduct of inquisitiveness and emulation. The child was as yet too young to fight. But according to the rules and regulations of the gang his time was but deferred, for each new boy must establish his fistic status.
===Chinese===
他注定要在这所荒山坡上的学校里度过许许多多日子。他同那里的男孩女孩们都熟识起来，因为这是一所男女同校（coeducational）的学校。课间时辰里这些孩子的所作所为，足以令十足的好人骇然失色。但对于一个寻常的罪人，若肯细察自己往昔的幽深处，倒也未尝找不出缘由、生不出几分宽容：凭着这份宽容，这些小动物的反复无常，纵然谈不上宽赦，至少也可以淡化看待，视之为好奇心与争胜心的天然产物或副产品。这孩子还太小，不到打架的年纪。但按照那帮孩子帮的规章制度，他的时辰不过是延期而已，因为每个新来的男孩都必须确立自己拳头上的地位。

===Original===
The schoolroom was large and bare with two wooden posts supporting the roof. The teacher sat at her desk on a raised platform at the wall opposite the entrance. The children sat at rows of desks (a row per grade) at right angles to the rear wall; in front of them an open space for recitation by class; blackboard on the wall and so forth. There were five grades in the single room. Teacher sat at her desk, ruler in hand to rap with or admonish. All the children studied their lessons aloud, or mumbled them. The room vibrated with a ceaseless hum, within which individual voices could be heard. Everything was free and easy; discipline rare. There was however a certain order of procedure. Came time for a class to recite. They flocked to the wall and stood in a row; neither foot nor head at first. Questions and answers concerning the lesson of the day. Teacher’s questions specific; pupils’ answers must be definite, categorical. Teacher was mild, patient; the answers were sometimes intelligent, more often hesitant, bashful, dull, or hopelessly stupid. Each answer was followed by a monotonous “go to the foot,” “go to the head;” and all the time the hum went on, the unceasing murmur, a thin piping voice here, a deeper one there, a rasping out yonder, as they pored over their primers, first readers, geographies, arithmetics; while now and again Teacher’s voice rose high, questioning the class on the rack, the children answering as best they could. This babel merged or deliquesced into a monotone; there seemed to be a diapason, resonant, thick, the conjoined utterance of many small souls trying to learn, entering the path of knowledge that would prove short for most of them. The children were all barefoot and rather carelessly clad; notably so in the matter of omissions. One thing is certain and the rest is lies: This school was of, for, and by the people.
===Chinese===
教室宽大而空荡，两根木柱支撑着屋顶。老师的讲桌设在门口对面墙边一座垫高的台子上，她就坐在那里。孩子们坐在一排排课桌前——一个年级一排——课桌与后墙成直角；孩子们前面留出一块空地，供各班背诵之用；墙上挂着黑板，等等。一间屋子里共有五个年级。老师坐在讲桌后，手执戒尺，以敲以诫。所有的孩子都出声念功课，或者含在嘴里咕哝。满屋子震颤着一股不息的嗡嗡声，其中还分辨得出一个个单独的嗓音。一切都自由散漫；纪律是稀罕物。不过倒也有某种固定的程序。到了某个班背诵的时辰。他们涌到墙边，站成一排；起初不分排尾排头。问的答的，都是当天的功课。老师发问，明确具体；学生作答，必须确切、干脆。老师温和而有耐心；回答有时聪明，更多的时候是迟疑、羞怯、木然，或者蠢得无可救药。每答一问，便跟着一句单调的「退到排尾去」或「升到排头去」；而那嗡嗡声始终不停，那绵绵的低吟，这边一条细而尖的嗓音，那边一条低些的，远处又有一条沙哑的，孩子们埋头苦读着启蒙课本、初级读本、地理书、算术书；时不时地，老师的声音陡然拔高，考问着那个如上刑架的班级，孩子们便竭尽所能地作答。这一片巴别塔式的喧声渐渐融汇、消解成一片单调的嗡鸣；仿佛有一股浑厚鸣响的底音，洪亮而浓稠，是许多奋力求学的小小灵魂齐声的吐诉——他们正踏上求知之路，而对其中多数人来说，这条路注定不长。孩子们全都光着脚，衣着相当马虎，缺这少那之处尤其明显。有一件事确凿无疑，其余皆是谎言：这所学校，乃是民有、民治、民享的。

===Original===
The child was given his proper place in the lowest grade, or class, or whatever it was called. He took hold rather blithely. He seemed to feel the importance of his entry into this new world, so different from home. Little by little he seemed to feel that he belonged there; but he never succeeded in feeling that the school belonged to him except as to its externals. Somehow he did not fit into the curriculum or the procedure. He was of a pronounced, independent nature. He quickly became listless as to his own lessons. He seemed to be nothing but a pair of eyes and ears not intended for books, but for the world little and big about him. In this immediate sense he was almost devoid of self-consciousness. His normal place was at the foot of his class. But one day he awakened to the fact that unawares he had become interested, not in books, but in procedure; said procedure consisting in the oral examinations and recitations of the grades above his own, as they, in accordance with the arrangement of the schoolroom, stood directly in front of him, drawn up in line, undergoing the routine torture. He began to notice their irregular mass-effect and their separate persons. He followed their fortunes in going to the foot and going to the head. He transferred himself to them. He noticed, too, which girls were the prettiest and which boys were the gawkiest. He learned the names of all. He became solicitous of their personal fortunes, in their struggle for knowledge or their attempts to escape it. For him, it became a sort of drama, a sort of stage performance, and he began to note with growing interest what they said and what teacher said, which answers were correct, which were failures. Over and over he saw and heard this until he came to know the groundwork of what all the grades above him were struggling with. But as to his own lessons﻿—Alas! Yet he followed the upper grades so intently that he became critical: What was this about the four men who built so many perches of stone wall in three days, and two other men who were to build some wall in six days? What did it amount to anyway? The real question was *where* was the wall to be built? For *whom* was it to be built? What was his name? What were the names of the men who were building the wall, (for it was becoming a real wall)? Were they Irish or Scotch? Where did they get the stone to build the wall? Did they get it from the rough quarry across the road from the schoolhouse? Did they gather up boulders from the fields? Was not this matter of four men and two men irrelevant? The information was too sparse, too unconvincing. He could not *place* the wall, and what good was any wall he could not see? And thus he went on, unaffected by the abstract, concerned only with the concrete, the actual, the human.
===Chinese===
这孩子在最低的年级——或者说班级，或者说随便什么名目——里得到了他应有的位置。他倒是相当欢快地上了手。他似乎感到了自己跨进这个新天地——这个与家如此不同的天地——的分量。渐渐地，他似乎觉得自己属于这里；可除了表面那一层，他始终不曾觉得这学校也属于他。不知怎么，他就是合不进课程表和那套程序。他天性鲜明，独立不羁。对自己的功课，他很快就没了心思。他似乎整个人只剩了一双眼睛、一对耳朵，而生就它们并非为了书本，而是为了他身边那个或小或大的世界。就这层直接的意义而言，他几乎没有自我意识。他的常态位置，就在班里的排尾。可有一天他醒悟过来：不知不觉间，他发生了兴趣——不在书本，而在程序；所谓程序，便是高于他的各年级的口试与背诵，因为按照教室的格局，那些人恰恰站到他跟前，排成一行，受着例行公事的煎熬。他开始留意他们整体的参差效果，也留意他们一个个单独的人。他追踪着他们退到排尾、升到排头的命运。他把自己整个儿转移到了他们身上。他还留意到哪些女孩最标致，哪些男孩最笨拙。他把所有人的名字都记熟了。他开始牵挂起他们各自的命运——牵挂他们求知的挣扎，或者逃避求知的努力。在他，这一切成了某种戏剧，某种舞台表演；他开始怀着越来越浓的兴趣留意他们说什么、老师说什么，哪些回答对了，哪些砸了。一遍又一遍，他看着听着，直到把高于他的各年级苦苦挣扎的那些底细摸得一清二楚。至于他自己的功课——呜呼！然而他追得如此专注，以致挑剔起来：四个男人三天里砌了多少杆（perch，砌墙计量单位）石墙、另外两个男人要在六天里砌多少墙，这是讲的什么名堂？这到底有什么意思？真正的问题是，墙要砌在*哪里*？是替*谁*砌的？他叫什么名字？砌墙的那几个男人都叫什么名字？（那墙在他心里正渐渐变成一堵真的墙。）他们是爱尔兰人还是苏格兰人？他们砌墙的石头是从哪儿弄来的？是从校舍路对面那座粗粝的采石场弄来的吗？还是从田里捡拾的漂石？这四个男人两个男人的账，难道不是全然离题？给出的信息太稀薄，太不能令人信服。他没法把这堵墙*安放到*一个实处，而一堵他看不见的墙，要它何用？他就这样走下去：抽象之物动他不得，他只关心具体的、实在的、人情的那一面。

===Original===
One evening when all were at home, a letter arrived addressed to Grandpa. He opened the envelope and read the letter aloud. It was from Teacher, and set forth with deep regret and concern that his grandson was a dull boy, that he was inattentive, would not study his lessons, was always at the foot of his class, but he was a nice boy. Could not Mr. List bring influence to bear to induce Louis to reform his ways? Would not a kindly word from him, concerning the need of education, have a moral effect? She had used all her powers of persuasion, and so forth and so on. At the end of the reading Grandpa dropped the letter on the floor, burst into volcanic laughter, roaring until the lid of the heater rattled, rocking forward and backward on his chair, clapping himself on the knee, in a series of subsiding outbursts, ending in a long drawn spasmodic chuckle, expressive of his cynical sense of humor, his infinite contempt for those who had eyes and yet saw not. To call his sharp-eyed grandson a dullard! Why, he said, one might as well call Sirius a flapjack, and other joking words to that effect, for he was fond of teasing his grandson, whom he had so long watched out of the corner of his eye. But Grandma, more conservative, took the matter seriously. With her grandson standing at her knees, a bit abashed, a bit afraid, after giving her six propitiatory kisses, his arms about her neck and cheek to cheek, she found it, oh, so hard, to scold him. Instead she told him gently how necessary it was to acquire an education; how necessary to that end that little boys, particularly her own grandson, for the family’s pride, should attend industriously to lessons. Could he not do better, would he not do better? He said he could and would; and all was peace.

Next day, at school, he pitched in, and the next day and the next; shutting out all else. Oh, it was so easy to head this class; so easy for one who knew what the upper grades knew, or thought they knew for a moment or perhaps a day. They knew not that it was all, save a bare remnant, fated to fade away forever. Tired of heading the class, which was so easy, he occasionally, and indeed with increasing frequency fell to zero, because of a lapse, because, perhaps, of a twitching squirrel in a tree nearby the window, or a beautiful white cloud curiously changing shape as it slowly drifted through a beautiful blue sky. And what did it all amount to? What signified it to be at the head of a row of dull-wits? He was becoming arrogant. For Grandma’s sake, he kept on, after a fashion. He was becoming bored.
===Chinese===
一天晚上，一家人都在家，一封信送到了，收信人是外祖父。他拆开信封，大声念了出来。信是老师写来的，以深切的遗憾与忧虑陈述道：他的外孙是个愚钝的孩子，上课不用心，不肯用功，总是坐在班里的排尾，不过他是个好孩子。李斯特先生（Mr. List）能否施加影响，促使路易斯改弦更张？由他就教育之必要讲几句温言，岂不自有道德的效力？她已用尽了一切劝说之力，云云，等等。念到末尾，外祖父把信往地板上一丢，爆出一阵火山般的狂笑，直吼得炉盖咯咯作响，连人带椅前仰后合，一巴掌一巴掌拍着自己的膝盖，一阵接一阵地发作又平息，最后收作一声悠长而抽搐的窃笑——这笑声里，是他那愤世的幽默感，是他对那些有眼却看不见的人的无限轻蔑。竟把他那眼尖得不得了的外孙唤作笨蛋！嗐，他说，这就好比管天狼星（Sirius）叫煎饼——外加诸如此类的打趣话；他就爱逗他的外孙，这孩子，他打眼角里已经端详了好久了。外祖母却较为守旧，把这事当了真。外孙站在她膝前，有点讪讪的，有点怕怕的，在给了她六个求和的亲吻之后——小胳膊搂着她的脖颈，脸颊贴着脸颊——她发觉，唉，要责骂他，实在难。她反而温言告诉他：受教育是何等必要的事；为此，小男孩们——尤其是她自己的外孙，为了家族的体面——该何等勤勉地用功于功课。他能不能学好些？肯不肯学好些？他说能，也肯；于是一切太平。

第二天，在学校里，他埋头猛干起来，第二天又一天，天天如此；把旁的一切统统关在了门外。噢，坐上这班的第一把交椅，何其容易；对一个已经知道高年级之所知——或他们自以为知道那么一会儿、也许一天——的人来说，何其容易。他们不知道，这些东西，除了一星半点的残余，都注定要永远褪色消散。老占着头名——既然这么容易——他也腻了；他不时地、而且越来越频繁地一落千丈跌回零分：因为一阵失神，因为，或许，窗外附近树上一只抽动尾巴的松鼠，或是一朵好看的白云，正缓缓飘过一片好看的蓝天，奇妙地变换着形状。而这一切又算得了什么？当一排呆头脑的头名，又有什么意味？他渐渐变得倨傲起来。看在外祖母的份上，他照旧应付着往前走。他渐渐觉得无聊了。

===Original===
Summer was waning. The third of September was at hand. Six candles in the cake announced an anniversary. He was overjoyed. He was actually six.
===Chinese===
夏天渐逝。九月三日近在眼前。蛋糕上的六支蜡烛宣告了一个周年。他大喜过望。他实实在在是六岁了。

===Original===
The winter of 1862﻿–﻿3 passed along with its usual train of winter sports and hardships. Louis joined heartily according to his height and weight in all the sports. Of hardships he knew nothing. What fun it was to be drawn on a sled over the snow by his Uncle Julius. To be drawn on the same sled over the dark sheer ice of the pond by Uncle on newly sharpened skates. What thrill of courage it required not to cry out as he shuddered at the darkness below, and wondered whether the pace were not too swift. But Uncle, some fifteen years older than he, was to him a big man; and what could not a big man do? So he had faith in Uncle, if not entire confidence, as they flew here and there among the gay crowd of skaters. How they went way to the end of the pond and then swung back past the ice houses where men were beginning to work! And later on how thrilled and stilled he was by the thunderous boom and tear of an ice crack ripping its way from shore to shore! And many such booms he heard on similar trips in zero weather. And then the men at work cutting ice! How exciting it was to watch men at work. They used large hand saws to cut ice into square blocks and there was one strange saw drawn by a horse. Then men with poles shoved and dragged the ice-blocks through the clear water to the bottom of the runway, and then it was hauled up the runway by a horse that walked away with a rope that ran through a pulley and then back to the ice cake. The ice seemed very thick and clear.
===Chinese===
1862 至 1863 年间的冬天，连同它惯常的一串冬季游戏与苦楚，照常过去了。路易斯以他身高体重所许可的额度，满腔热忱地投入了所有的游戏。苦楚嘛，他一无所知。让朱利乌斯舅舅拉着雪橇在雪地上跑，多么有趣。让舅舅穿着新磨快的冰刀，拖着同一架雪橇，从池塘乌黑陡直的冰面上掠过，多么有趣。望着脚下的幽暗他直打寒颤，心里嘀咕这速度怕是太快了，可他硬是憋着不喊出声——这需要怎样一股勇气，那勇气也是一阵战栗。好在舅舅比他大着约莫十五岁，在他眼里已是条大汉；而一个大汉还有什么干不了的？于是在满场欢腾的滑冰人群里飞来飞去时，他对舅舅有信仰——纵然还谈不上全然的信托。他们一路滑到池塘的尽头，又荡回来，掠过那些储冰屋——屋里的人们已经开工了！再后来，一声冰裂的轰然巨响与撕裂声，从这岸直劈到那岸，他听得又激动又屏息！零下的天气里，一次次这样的远足中，多少声这样的轰响他都听过了。还有那些割冰的人们！看人劳作，多么令人兴奋。他们用大手锯把冰切成方砖似的冰块，还有一把古怪的锯子由马拉着。然后人们用长杆把冰块在清澈的水里又推又拽，送到滑道的底端，再由一匹马沿着滑道把它拖上来——那马拖曳着一条绳索走开去，绳索穿过一个滑轮，再折回拴到冰块上。那冰看上去又厚又清。

===Original===
And then came splendid snowstorms, decorating the trees, forming great drifts through which he struggled in exultation, every now and then stumbling and falling with his face in the snow. How he rolled over and over in glee in the snow of a white world, a beautiful world even when the gray skies lowered. And why not? Had he not warm woolen mittens knitted by Grandma, and hood and stockings by the same faithful hands, and “artics”? Was he not all bundled up?

And the sleigh rides. Oh, the sleigh rides in the cutter with the horse looming so high, and the row of bells around the horse’s collar, jangling and tinkling in jerky time. And he so warm under the buffalo robe. And they met so many other sleighs in the village when they went to the post-office or the grocery store, and he noticed so many men walking about clad in buffalo coats. And he made snowballs and did all the minor incidentals. It was his first experience within the pulchritude of a winter in the open. His mother came frequently to see him and caress him. He could hardly understand why she loved him so; he had so many other personal interests and distractions. But he hailed her comings and deplored her departures.
===Chinese===
接着来了几场壮观的大雪，把树木装点起来，堆出一座座大雪堆；他在雪堆间欢腾地奋力跋涉，时不时绊上一跤，一头栽进雪里。在一个白银世界的雪里，他乐得打起滚来，一圈又一圈——纵然灰云低垂，这也是个美丽的世界。为什么不呢？他不是有外祖母织的羊毛连指手套，有同样一双忠实的手织的风帽和长袜，还有那双「北极」套靴（artics）吗？他不是从头到脚裹得严严实实吗？

还有坐雪橇出游。噢，坐着双人雪橇（cutter）出游：马显得那么高峻，颈圈上一串铃铛，随着一颠一颠的节奏叮当乱响。而他裹在野牛皮毯（buffalo robe）底下，那么暖和。去邮局或杂货铺的路上，他们在村里遇见那么多别的雪橇；他还注意到那么多男人穿着野牛皮大衣走来走去。他团雪球，把一切零七八碎的小差事都做了个遍。这是他头一回置身于户外冬天的那份华美之中。母亲常来看他，爱抚他。他几乎不明白她何以这样爱他；他自己另有许多个人的兴趣与分心之事。但她的到来他欢呼，她的离去他哀叹。

===Original===
While his name was Louis, he had other names﻿—interesting ones, too. He had not been christened or baptized. The question had called for a family council. The father, a nominal Freemason, not sure whether he was a Catholic or an Orangeman or anything in particular, expressed no serious interest; he would leave it to the rest. Grandpa, as usual, vented his view in scornful laughter. Grandma, a Mennonite, was opposed to baptism. But Mother in her excited way was rampant. What! Would she permit any man to say aloud over the body of her pure and precious infant that he was born in sin and ask for sponsors? Never! That settled it and they named him Louis Henri Sullivan. It has been declared and denied that the name was given in order to heap honors upon Napoleon III. Be that as it may. The name Henri, obviously, was to deify Grandpa. The Sullivan could not be helped. It was scorned by all but its owner. They detested the Irish, whose peaceful penetration of Boston had made certain sections thereof turn green. Even his wife could not stand for it, much less for Patrick. So sometimes she gallicized the name; which wasn’t so bad, when she used it in the third person, nominative, singular. Then she had an inspiration, an illumination one might say, and invented the word Tulive, whatever that may have meant, as a general cover-name, and thus secured a happy, lifelong escape. But later on, say about the age of twelve, the scion asked his father about this name Sullivan, which seemed to coincide with shanty-Irish. So his father told him this tale: Long ago in Ireland, in the good fighting days, there were four tribes or clans of the O’Sullivans: The O’Sullivan-Moors, the O’Sullivan Macs, and two others. That *We* were descended from the O’Sullivan-Moors, and that all four tribes were descended from a Spanish marauder, who ravished the west Irish coast and settled there. His name it appears was O’Soulyevoyne or something like that, which, translated, meant, “The Prince with One Eye.” Now, however great was the glory of this pirate chief, his descendant, Louis Henri Sullivan O’Sullivan-Moore-O’Soulyevoyne, had this specific advantage over him of the high-seas. The prince had but one eye that must have seen much; the youngster of six had two eyes that saw everything, without desire to plunder.
===Chinese===
他名叫路易斯，可他还有别的名字——而且都挺有意思。他没有受过洗礼，也没行过命名礼。这个问题曾需要召开家庭会议。父亲呢，名义上是个共济会（Freemason）会员，自己也拿不准究竟算天主教徒、奥兰治会（Orangeman）会员还是别的什么名目，未表示任何郑重的兴趣；他愿意把这事留给其余的人。外祖父照例以一阵轻蔑的大笑发泄了他的意见。外祖母是门诺会（Mennonite）教徒，反对洗礼。可母亲以她那激切的方式气势汹汹。什么！她能容许任何人当着她那纯洁无瑕、千金不换的婴孩的身体，高声宣说他生而有罪、还要索要代父代母吗？休想！一句话定了案，他们给他取名路易斯·亨利·沙利文（Louis Henri Sullivan）。有人说，取这名字是为了把荣光堆到拿破仑三世（Napoleon III）头上；也有人矢口否认。是也罢，非也罢。亨利这个名字，显然是要把外祖父奉若神明。沙利文这个姓，却是无法可想的。除了它的主人，一家上下人人都鄙夷它。他们憎恶爱尔兰人——爱尔兰人对波士顿的和平渗透，已使那座城市的某些地段泛出了绿色。就连他的妻子也容忍不了这个姓，至于「帕特里克」（Patrick）之流，就更不必说了。于是她有时把这姓氏法语化一下；当她用第三人称单数主格提到它时，倒还不算太难听。后来她灵机一动——简直可以说是一场天启——发明了「图利夫」（Tulive）这个词，天知道它究竟是什么意思，权作一个笼而统之的代姓，就这样稳稳当当、终生受用地脱了身。可到后来，约莫十二岁上，这位家族的苗裔问起父亲这个沙利文姓——它似乎正与「棚屋爱尔兰」（shanty-Irish）对得上号。于是父亲给他讲了这么一段故事：很久以前，在爱尔兰，在那些精彩纷呈的打斗年代，奥沙利文家（O'Sullivans）有四大部族：奥沙利文-穆尔（O'Sullivan-Moors）、奥沙利文-马克（O'Sullivan Macs），还有另外两支。说*我们*就是奥沙利文-穆尔一支的后裔；还说四支部族全都出自一个西班牙海上劫掠者，此人蹂躏了爱尔兰西海岸，便在那里落了脚。他的名字，据说，是奥苏利沃因（O'Soulyevoyne）还是怎么的，翻译过来，意思是「独眼王子」。不过，这位海盗酋长的威名纵然再大，他的后裔——路易斯·亨利·沙利文·奥沙利文-穆尔-奥苏利沃因——比起这位纵横公海的老祖宗来，却自有一样独到的长处。那位王子只有一只眼，想必见得不少；而这六岁的小家伙有两只眼，什么都看得见，且全无掳掠之心。

===Original===
> These became part of that child who went forth every day, and who now goes, and will always go forth every day.
 

 And these become part of him or her that peruses them here.
> — Whitman
===Chinese===
> 这些成了那个天天向前走的孩子的一部分；他如今还在走，并且将永远天天向前走。

而这些，也成了此时在此细细捧读它们的他、或她的一部分。
> ——惠特曼

## VIII Louis Goeth on a Journey / 八　路易斯踏上旅程

===Original===
Early in the summer of ’70, Henri List felt an impelling desire to visit his second daughter, Jennie, whom he had not seen in a number of years. In 1862, she was married to a certain Walter Whittlesey, a contracting railway engineer, and they lived on 300-acre farm at Lyons Falls, N.Y. On the 29th day of February, 1864, she added to the world’s population a daughter, in due time named Anna, under Presbyterian auspices. Mrs. Whittlesey at the time we are considering, was 34 years of age﻿—one year younger than her sister, Andrienne, greatly beloved mother of Louis Sullivan. When Henri List’s desire had ripened into a resolve and was so announced, there was “the devil to pay,” as was said at times in those days. Louis became frantic. He must go too. He, also, had not seen his Tante Jennie in many years. He must see where she lived and how she lived. He must see his dear little cousin Anna, and Uncle Walter too. He must see the farm, and the river and the great waterfall.

===Chinese===
1870 年夏初，亨利·李斯特（Henri List）心头涌起一股按捺不住的愿望：去看看他已有多年不见的二女儿珍妮（Jennie）。1862 年，她嫁给了沃尔特·惠特尔西（Walter Whittlesey），一位承包铁路工程的工程师，两人住在纽约州莱昂斯福尔斯一座300 英亩的农场上。1864 年2 月29 日，她在长老会（Presbyterian）的操办下给世间添了一个女儿，适时取名为安娜（Anna）。在我们此刻所讲的这个时节，惠特尔西太太年方34——比她的姐姐、路易斯·沙利文深爱的母亲安德丽安（Andrienne）小一岁。待到亨利·李斯特的愿望酿成决心并如此宣布之后，用那时人们偶尔的说法，家里登时「可要闹翻天了」。路易斯简直发了狂。他也非去不可。他也已有多年没见过他的姨妈珍妮（Tante Jennie）了。他要看看她住在哪里、过得怎样。他要看看亲爱的小表妹安娜，还有沃尔特姨父。他要看看那农庄、那条河，和那道大瀑布。

===Original===
“Grandpa, I have never seen a waterfall, only in pictures, and in pictures they don’t move and they don’t roar; I want to live with a *real* waterfall; and I want to see the Berkshire Hills; and the Hudson; you know, Grandpa, pictures don’t give you any real idea; why Grandpa, a picture of a tree isn’t anything at all when you see a real tree, like our great Ash at Cowdry’s; and to think, Grandpa, I’ve never been farther away than Newburyport; take me with you, Grandpa. I want to see something big; everything in Boston and Wakefield has grown so small; we are so shut in; my geography says there are big things as you go west, that outdoors gets bigger and bigger; I want to go Grandpa; now is the time; I may never have another chance.”

===Chinese===
「外祖父，我从来没有见过瀑布，只在画上见过；画里的瀑布不会动，也不会吼。我想同一道*真的*瀑布住在一起。我还想看看伯克希尔山，看看哈得孙河。您知道的，外祖父，画片给不了人半点真切的概念；嗐，外祖父，一棵树的画，等你见了真的树——比如考德雷家（Cowdry's）我们那棵大白蜡树——就什么都不是了；再说您想想，外祖父，我到过最远的地方不过纽伯里波特。带我一起去吧，外祖父。我想看看大的东西；波士顿和韦克菲尔德的一切都变得那么小；我们给关得太死了；我的地理书上说，一路往西去尽是大的东西，说屋外的天地越走越开阔。我要去，外祖父；就是现在了；错过这回，兴许再没有别的机会了。」

===Original===
Grandpa, at first was angry and obdurate. He thought only of what a pest, of what a continuous nuisance his growing grandson would be, and the thought became a nightmare; for Henri List, conforming to custom, was growing older, was acquiring nerves; his easygoing humor showed occasional thin spots of temper. He roared at the “dear little Cousin Anna” business, but the possible significance of the pleadings concerning a “shut-in life” and “big things as you go west” dawned upon him, grew stronger, and he came finally to believe that what he had heard was not altogether boyish nonsense but a rising cry for expansion, a defining hunger for larger vision, bigger things; that his grandson, as it were, was outgrowing his cocoon. Upon second and third thoughts he agreed; whereupon the few remaining sane ones also agreed that Louis needed kennel, collar and chain.

===Chinese===
外祖父起初又气又硬。他满脑子想的只是：这个一天天长大的外孙一路上会是个多大的祸害、多大的没完没了的麻烦，这念头简直成了梦魇；因为亨利·李斯特循着世人的惯例正在变老，神经也渐渐添了上来；他那随和的幽默上，偶尔也磨出几处露出脾气的薄痕。听到「亲爱的小表妹安娜」那一节，他吼了起来；可是那番关于「关得太死的日子」与「一路往西尽是大的东西」的恳求里可能藏着的深意，渐渐在他心里亮了起来，愈来愈亮，他终于相信，方才听到的不全是男孩子家的傻话，而是一声正在升起的、要求扩张的呐喊，一种轮廓分明的饥渴——渴求更宏大的视野、更宏大的事物；他的外孙，不妨说，正在撑破他的茧。再三再四地想过之后，他答应了；于是家里剩下几位神志还清醒的人也一致同意：路易斯需要的是狗窝、项圈加锁链。

===Original===
The day came. They departed via the Boston and Albany Railway in the evening. Sleepless, restive, Louis awaited, as best he might, the coming of the Berkshire Hills into his growing world. He knew he would see them near dawn. The *hour* came; he entered the foothills and began winding among them, as with labored breath the engines, like heavy draft horses, began a steady pull, the train dragging reluctantly into steadiness as succeeding hills grew taller﻿—with Louis eagerly watching. The true thrill of action began with the uprearing of imposing masses as Louis clung to the solid train now purring in the solitudes in ever-lengthening swings﻿—deep valleys below﻿—until, amid mists and pale moon gleaming, arose the mighty Berkshires, their summits faint and far, their immensities solemn, calm, seeming eternal in the ghostly fog in the mild shimmer, clad in forests, uttering great words, runic words revealing and withholding their secret to a young soul moving as a solitary visitant, even as a wraith among them, the engines crying: “We will!” the mountains replying: “We will!” to an expanding soul listening within its own mists, its own shimmering dream, to the power without and within, amid the same echoes within and without, bereft of words to reply, a bare hush of being, as though through mists of mind and shimmer of hope, **sublimity**, in revelation, had come to *one* wholly unprepared, had come to *one* as a knock on the door, had come to *one* who had known mountains only in books. And Louis again, in wonder, felt the power of man. The thought struck deep, that what was bearing him along was solely the power of man; the living power to wish, to will, to do. That man, in his power, with broad stride, had entered the regioned sanctity of these towering hills and like a giant of Elfinland had held them in the hollow of his hand. He had made a path, laid the rails, builded the engines that others might pass. Many saw engines and rails, and pathway, and *one* saw what lay behind them. In the murky mist and shimmer of moon and dawn, a veil was lifted in the solitude of the Berkshires. Louis slept, his nerves becalmed, amid the whistle’s sonorous warnings, the silence of the engine, the long, shrill song of the brakes, with mingling echoes, as the train, with steady pace, wound slowly downward toward the Hudson, leaving the Berkshires to their silence and their solitude﻿—and Louis slept on, under the wand of the power of man.

===Chinese===
这一天真来了。傍晚，他们搭乘波士顿与奥尔巴尼铁路（Boston and Albany Railway）的火车启程。路易斯睡不着，也安顿不下，只得尽其所能地等着，等伯克希尔山走进他那个日渐长大的世界。他知道，将近黎明时便会见到它们。那个*时辰*到了；火车进了山前的丘地，开始在丘峦之间盘旋绕行；机车像几匹拉重载的驮马，喘着粗气，开始沉稳地使劲；山丘一座高过一座，列车拖着老大不情愿，渐渐稳住了步子——路易斯眼巴巴地望着。真正撼人的激荡，是随着一座座巍峨山体拔地而起开始的；此刻路易斯紧贴着这列结实的火车——它正在旷寂之中，以愈拉愈长的摆动轻轻哼鸣——脚底下是深深的谷地——直到雾霭之中、淡月的微光之下，巍巍伯克希尔山耸然而起：峰顶隐约而邈远，山势浩瀚、庄严、沉静，在幽幽的雾里、柔柔的微光中恍若亘古永恒；周身披着森林，吐出洪大的言语，如尼符文般的言语，向一个年轻的灵魂既昭示、又隐匿着它们的秘密——这灵魂只身行在其间，宛如他们中间的一个幽灵；机车喊着：「我们要！」群山回答：「我们要！」——对一个正在扩张的灵魂说着；那灵魂在自己心头的雾里、在自己闪烁的梦里，听着身外与身内的力量，同样的回声在里在外激荡，它却寻不出言语作答，只剩一派赤裸静穆的存在——仿佛穿过心智的雾、希望的微光，**崇高**（sublimity）携着启示，降临于*一个*全无准备的人，降临于*一个*人，如同一记敲在门上的叩击，降临于*一个*只在书里识得山的人。路易斯又一次在惊叹中感到了人的力量。这念头扎得很深：此刻载着他向前的，唯独是人的力量；是那要去愿望、要去决意、要去实行的活生生的力量。人，凭着他的力量，已经迈着阔步，走进了这些巍巍群山各自成域的圣所，像精灵之国（Elfinland）的巨人一般，把它们托在了掌心里。他开出了一条路，铺下了铁轨，造出了机车，好让后来的人得以通行。许多人看见的是机车与铁轨，是那条路；而有*一个*人，看见了它们背后的事物。在昏沉的雾里，在月色与曙光的微芒中，伯克希尔山的寂寥里，一幅帷幕掀开了。路易斯睡着了，神经平静了下来——汽笛鸣着浑厚的警示，机车归于沉默，制动闸唱着悠长尖啸的歌，回声交叠；列车迈着沉稳的步子，朝哈得孙河方向缓缓盘旋而下，把伯克希尔山留给它的寂静与孤独——路易斯继续睡着，睡在人的力量那根魔杖的护佑之下。

===Original===
They reached Albany in broad daylight. The Hudson, to Louis’s dismay, did not impress him as greatly as he had hoped and believed it would. Its course was straight instead of broadly curving, and the clutter of buildings along its western flank seemed to belittle it. It appeared to him as a wide waterway, not unpleasant of its kind. It seemed to lack what Louis had come to believe the character of a river. The bridge crossing it, with its numberless short spans and lack of bigness, beauty and romance he gazed upon in instant disdain. It appeared to creep, cringing and apologetic, across the wide waters which felt the humiliation of its presence.

===Chinese===
他们抵达奥尔巴尼时，天已大亮。令路易斯失望的是，哈得孙河并不像他希望并相信的那样令他震撼。河道笔直，不见壮阔的弯转；西岸一串杂沓的房舍，仿佛把它衬小了。在他眼里，它成了一条宽阔的水道，就其本身而言倒也不惹人厌。它似乎缺少一点什么——缺少路易斯已然认定一条河流应有的品格。至于横跨河上的那座桥：桥孔又多又短，既无宏大，又无美，也无浪漫，他一眼扫去便生鄙夷。它像是在爬，畏畏缩缩、赔着不是，爬过宽阔的水面；河水也因它在场而觉出了羞辱。

===Original===
Yet he received a shock of elation as the train had moved slowly along the bridge, carrying him with it; and as he gazed downward upon flowing waters, again he marvelled at what men could do; at the power of men to build; to build a bridge so strong it would carry the weight of a great train, even with his own precious and conscious weight added thereto. And Louis mused about the bridge; why was it so mean, so ugly, so servile, so low-lived? Why could not a bridge perform its task with pride? Why was not a proud bridge built here? Was not New York a great state? Was it not called in his geography “The Empire State”? Was not Albany the Capital City of that state? Then why so shabby an approach? Was not the broad Hudson figuratively a great aqueous frontier between Massachusetts and New York, each state proud in sovereignty? And was not this bridge a presumptive greeting between sovereign states? For surely, the railroad train came straight from proud Boston to exalted Albany? And a veil lifted as there came to his mind a striking verse he had read:

===Chinese===
然而，当列车载着他缓缓驶过桥上时，他心头却掠过一阵昂扬的震颤；他俯望奔流的河水，再一次惊叹于人所能为之事，惊叹于人的建造之力——竟能造出一座如此坚实的桥，扛得起一列重载火车的分量，甚至连他自己那份金贵的、有自觉的分量加在上面也扛得起。于是路易斯对这座桥沉思起来：它为何这般小家子气，这般丑陋，这般奴颜婢膝，这般下作？一座桥为何不能昂然当行地履行职分？这里为何不造一座骄傲的桥？纽约难道不是一个大州？他的地理书上不是称它作「帝国州」（The Empire State）吗？奥尔巴尼难道不是该州的首府？那为何偏是这么寒碜的一副门面？打个比方，宽阔的哈得孙河岂不是马萨诸塞与纽约之间一道壮阔的水上疆界，两州各自矜于主权？这座桥岂不算是主权州之间一记应有的致意？要知道，这列车可是径直从骄傲的波士顿开到尊贵的奥尔巴尼来的呀！于是一幅帷幕掀开了，他想起自己读到过的几句铿锵的诗：

===Original===
> “Why were they proud?
 

 Again I cry aloud
 

 Why in the name of glory
 

 Were they proud?”

===Chinese===
> 「他们为何骄傲？
 

 我再一次高声呼喊
 

 究竟为了何等荣名
 

 他们竟骄傲？」

===Original===
And there came up also to him the saying: “By their fruits ye shall know them,” as, lost in imagery, he visioned forth the great Bay State, saluting the great Empire State, saying solemnly: “The Sovereign State of Massachusetts greets the Sovereign State of New York. Let this noble bridge we herewith present you be a sign and a bond of everlasting amity between us, even as Almighty God proclaimed unto Noah of old and his sons: ‘I do set my bow in the cloud, and it shall be for a token of a covenant between me and the earth.’ ” Thus Louis, ruminating rather fiercely, wished to know what was behind the pestiferous bridge. He keenly felt that man’s amazing power to do, should, in all decency and all reason, be coupled with Romance in the deed. And even more keenly he felt, as his eyesight cleared, that this venomous bridge was a betrayal of all that was best in himself, a denial of all that was best in mankind.

===Chinese===
随后又浮上他心头的是那句话：「凭着他们的果子，就可以认出他们来。」他沉酣在意象里，心中映现出伟大的海湾州（Bay State）向伟大的帝国州致敬的一幕，庄严地说道：「马萨诸塞主权州谨向纽约主权州致意。谨以此桥相赠，愿它作我们之间永恒和睦的凭证与盟约，一如全能的上帝昔日晓谕挪亚（Noah）及其众子：『我把虹放在云彩中，这就可作我与地立约的记号。』」路易斯就这样颇为激烈地反刍着，一心想知道这座可憎的桥背后究竟是什么。他痛切地感到：人那惊人的行事之力，无论按体面还是按情理，都应当在作为之中与浪漫相偕。而随着他的目光愈见清明，他更痛切地感到：这座恶毒的桥，是对他自身至善的一切的背叛，是对人类至善的一切的否定。

===Original===
That day they took the New York Central train for Utica. After traversing the roughage, the Mohawk Valley opened to them its placid beauty as in welcome to a new land. And to Louis it was in verity a new land, known to him up to this very present hour as a geographical name﻿—an abstraction﻿—unknown to him and wholly unimagined, in its wealth of open rarity, its beauteous immensity of atmosphere. Here was freedom; here was expanse! Louis ranged with his eyes from near to far, following the sweep of the valley floor from the Mohawk to the distant low-flowing hills, and to and fro caressingly; and as mile after mile of valley passed by, and again mile upon mile Louis’s peaceful mind passed into wonder that such an open world could be; and now he marvelled, not at man’s power and his works, but at the earth itself, and a reverential mood claimed him for its own, as he began in part to see with his own calm eyes what Mother Earth, in her power, had done in her varied moods, and to surmise as best he could what more she had done that he knew not of. And all this while the Mohawk wound its limpid way, gentle as all else; and Louis, softening into an exquisite sympathy, cast his burden upon the valley, and there he found rest; rest from overintrospection, rest from overconcentration; freed from suppression and taboo.

===Chinese===
当天，他们登上开往尤蒂卡的纽约中央铁路（New York Central）列车。驶过那段嶙峋的硌石地段之后，莫霍克河谷向他们敞开了它恬静的美，仿佛在欢迎来客进入一方新的土地。而对路易斯来说，这确乎是一方新天地：直到此时此刻，它于他还只是地理书上的一个名字——一个抽象概念——他不曾认识它，也全然无从想象它那份旷达的珍稀、那份天宇浩瀚的美。这里是自由；这里是开阔！路易斯的目光由近及远地巡视，顺着谷底的漫展从莫霍克河一路扫到远处低伏的丘峦，又爱抚般地来回流连；一英里又一英里的河谷向后退去，再一英里又一英里，路易斯安宁的心渐渐化入惊叹——惊叹世上竟会有这般开阔的天地；此刻他惊叹的不再是人的力量与人的造物，而是大地本身；一种敬畏的心绪把他认作了自己的人，因为他开始用自己平静的眼睛，部分地看见大地母亲凭她的力量、在她多变的情绪里成就了怎样的作为，并竭尽所能地揣想她还有哪些他不曾知晓的作为。这一路，莫霍克河蜿蜒着它澄澈的水道，与其余的一切一样温存；路易斯的心软化成一种精微的同情，他把肩上的重担卸给了这道山谷，就在这里，他寻得了安息；从过度自省中得安息，从过度专注中得安息；从压抑与禁忌中得了释放。

===Original===
Thus Louis became freshened with new growth as a tree in spring, and a new resilience came to take the place of the old. He was cleansed as by a storm, and purified as by fire; but there was no storm, no fire, no whirlwind﻿—there arose from the valley a still, small voice, and Louis heard the voice and recognized it as his own returning to him, and he was overjoyed and strengthened in his faith and became as one translated into the fresh, free joy of living; for in this valley, this wilderness of light and earth he had found surcease.

===Chinese===
就这样，路易斯像春日的树木一般焕发出新的生机，一股新的韧劲接替了旧的。他如经风暴般得了洗净，如经烈火般得了纯化；然而没有风暴，没有烈火，没有旋风——从山谷里升起来的是一个微小的声音；路易斯听见了这声音，认出那正是他自己的声音回到了他身上；他喜出望外，信念愈发坚固，整个人如同被移换了境界，进入了清新自由的生之欢愉；因为在这道山谷里，在这光与大地的旷野中，他寻得了止息。

===Original===
Louis turned to Grandpa, whom he found dozing. The hills were coming together; a lurch of the train awakened Grandpa; he regarded Louis with a lazy smile and asked him if he had found the “big things,” and how about the “shut-in life.” Louis at once overflowed concerning the Berkshires, the Hudson, and the Bridge, but said not a word about the valley﻿—*that* was sacred. When he had finished, Grandpa’s face spread into one of those grimaces that Louis knew but too well as a preliminary to speech; and Grandpa said: “As to your bridge, young man, I know nothing; as to the Hudson, you know nothing; as to your Berkshires, they are an impertinence.”

===Chinese===
路易斯转向外祖父，见他正在打盹。丘峦渐渐合拢过来；车身一个趔趄，把外祖父晃醒了；他带着慵懒的笑意端详路易斯，问他可找到了「大的东西」，还有那「关得太死的日子」怎么样了。路易斯登时滔滔不绝地讲起伯克希尔山、哈得孙河和那座桥，对那道山谷却只字不提——*那*是神圣的。他讲完了，外祖父的脸慢慢咧成路易斯再熟悉不过的一种怪相——那是开口之前的预备动作；外祖父说道：「至于你那座桥嘛，年轻人，我一无所知；至于哈得孙河，你一无所知；至于你那些伯克希尔山，它们纯属放肆。」

===Original===
Grandpa was an incorrigible tease. With inward chuckle, with sweet, succulent sinfulness, he gazed his fill upon a crestfallen face, knowing the while how quickly and how well he could restore its color; then, having gloated long enough, he, as always, relented﻿—but slowly, for effect, he began: “Louis, what good does the study of your stupid geography do you? Suppose you *can* bound all the states, you haven’t an idea of what the states are. You see a crooked black line on your map and it is marked such or such a river; what do you know about that river? Have your teachers ever told you anything of value about a river? Any river? Have they ever told you that there are rivers and rivers each with its special character? Have they ever given you a word-picture of a river, so that you might at least summon up an image of it, however short of reality? They have not. They can not. They are not inspired. They are victims of routine, wearied on the daily treadmill until they can no longer see into the heart of a child. Now, I have watched you since you were a babe in arms, and I have mostly let you alone for fear of meddling with nature’s work; for you were started right by my daughter, the mother who carried you and yearned for you. She is sound to the core. She alone of my children might fittingly wear the red cap of liberty. Yet you do not know your own mother. *I* know *you*. I know your abominable selfishness﻿—come from your father; and your generosity and courage﻿—come from my proud daughter. You have a God-given eye and a dull heart. You are at one and the same time incredibly industrious and practical, and a dreamer of morbid dreams, of mystic dreams, sometimes clean, brilliant dreams, but these are too rare.

===Chinese===
外祖父是个无可救药的促狭鬼。他心里咯咯直笑，怀着一种甜滋滋、水淋淋的罪愆，把一张垂头丧气的脸看了个够，同时心里有数：只要他愿意，多快好省地便能把血色召回到那张脸上去；随后，把这份得意受用够了，他照例软下心来——不过慢慢地，为的是效果——他开口了：「路易斯，研习你那本愚蠢的地理书，于你有什么益处？就算你*能*画出所有各州的边界，你也全然不知各州究竟是什么。你在地图上看见一条弯弯曲曲的黑线，标注着某某河；对那条河，你知道些什么？你的老师们可曾就一条河、任何一条河，讲过一句有价值的话？他们可曾告诉过你，河与河各有不同，每一条都有它独特的品格？他们可曾给你描画过一条河的文字图像，好让你至少能唤起它的一个影像，哪怕远远不及真实？他们没有。他们也不能。他们没有灵感。他们是例行公事的牺牲品，在日复一日的踏车上磨得疲惫不堪，直到再也看不进一个孩子的心。我呢，打你还在襁褓里就一直看着你，而且多半放任你自流，唯恐搅扰了天性自己的功课；因为你起头起得端正——是我女儿，那个怀了你、盼望着你的母亲，把你带好的。她通体健全。我的儿女里，唯独她配得上戴自由的红帽（red cap of liberty）。可你并不认识你自己的母亲。*我*可认识*你*。我认得你那可憎的自私——得自你父亲；也认得你的慷慨与勇气——得自我那骄傲的女儿。你有一双上帝赋予的眼睛，和一颗迟钝的心。你同时是不可思议地勤勉而务实，又是一个做着病态之梦、神秘之梦的梦者——偶尔也做些干净明亮的梦，可惜太少了。

===Original===
“What you have said, from time to time, concerning man’s power to do, has astounded and frightened me, coming from you. *That idea* you never got from any of us. *There* shines the light of the seer, of the prophet, leading where?﻿—to salvation or destruction? I dare not think how that flame may grow into conflagration, or mellow into a world-glow of wisdom. But I know, worst of all, that adolescence is at hand; that you are in grave danger of a shakeup. Hard work and clear straight thinking may pull you through; that is my sincere hope. I regret, now, having spoken harshly: I did not intend to, but one thought led to another as a river flows. Now let us return to earth and I will tell you about the Hudson.”

===Chinese===
「你不时说起的那些关于人之行力的话，出自你之口，叫我震惊，也叫我不安。*那个念头*，你绝不是从我们哪个人这里得来的。*那里面*闪耀着先见者与预言者的光——它引向何方？——引向得救，还是毁灭？那团火焰会烧成怎样一场燎原大火，抑或会温润成一片智慧的世界之光，我不敢往下想。但我知道，顶要紧的是：青春期已在门口；你正处在一场天翻地覆的大险之中。苦干，加上清明直截的思考，或许能渡你过关；这是我诚挚的期望。方才话说重了，我后悔了：本不打算这样的，只是思绪牵着思绪，像河水一样流了下去。现在让我们回到地上来，我给你讲讲哈得孙河。」

===Original===
Then Grandpa, aroused to eloquence, made a splendid, flowing, word-picture of the Hudson, from Albany to the sea, that brought out all the rare qualities of his fine mind, and so aroused Louis that he made the journey with him﻿—lost to all else. Just then the train slowed up and came to a full stop. Louis looked out of the window. They were in a ravine, with high walls of rock, jagged and wild, and through this gorge came dashing, plunging, swirling, sparkling, roaring over the ledges in cascade after cascade, laughing and shouting in joy, the same Mohawk River that had flowed as gently as the footstep of a veiled nun, through the long, quiet valley they had traversed. Louis was exultant, he leaped from the train, waved his hat, and in spirit sang with the waters the song of joy. The bell clanged its warning note, Louis was aboard with a swing, and as the train moved on, from the rear platform he waved his farewell to Little Falls.

===Chinese===
于是外祖父兴起，舌灿莲花，从奥尔巴尼一直讲到海口，把哈得孙河描画成一幅酣畅流转的文字画卷，把他那颗精美头脑里一切罕有的品质都使了出来；路易斯听得心驰神往，随他一同把这趟旅程走了一遍——此外万事皆忘。恰在此时，列车放慢下来，停了个稳稳当当。路易斯望向窗外。他们正处在一道沟谷之中，两壁是高峻、嶙峋而荒野的岩崖；就在这峡谷中间，奔涌而来、俯冲而下、回旋激荡、水光四溅，越过一道道石台轰鸣成一级接一级的瀑布，欢笑着、呐喊着——正是那条莫霍克河，方才还在他们穿行的那条悠长安静的山谷里，流得像蒙纱修女的足音那样轻柔。路易斯欣喜若狂，纵身跳下火车，挥舞着帽子，魂魄里与流水齐声唱起了欢歌。车铃铛然示警，路易斯一个箭步甩身上了车；列车开动时，他从尾部的平台向着利特尔福尔斯挥手作别。

===Original===
They soon arived at Utica; and Grandpa, who had begun to feel the fatigue of the journey; announced that they would spend the night there in order to be fresh on the morrow. Louis, still restless, took a long, evening stroll. Utica had not impressed him. It seemed staid and somnolent, giving out an air of old and settled complacence, differing however, in kind and quality, from that of New England. So he strolled; his thoughts reverting to Grandpa and his extraordinary monologue; and for the first time, since he had begun think such thoughts, he asked himself, what lies hidden behind Grandpa?

===Chinese===
不久他们便到了尤蒂卡；外祖父已开始觉出旅途的劳顿，宣布两人当晚在此过夜，好教明日精神焕发。路易斯仍安顿不下来，趁着黄昏出去遛了一大圈。尤蒂卡没有给他留下什么印象。这座城显得沉滞而昏昏欲睡，散发出一种年深日久、安之若素的自得之气——只是其种类与质地，与新英格兰的那一套不同。他就这样信步走着；心思回到外祖父和他那番异乎寻常的长篇独白上；而且，自从他开始想这一类念头以来，他头一次问自己：外祖父的背后，究竟藏着什么？

---

===Original===
The Black River at that time flowed irregularly northward, as presumably it does today. Originating in hills not far northeast of Utica, it finally, after much argument with the lay of the land, debouched into Lake Ontario, not far beyond Watertown. About midway in its course it picked up the Moose River, and a short distance beyond their junction broke into a rough and tumble waterfall of perhaps forty feet descent, beyond which, its surface at first much ruffled, it went smoothly on its way as far as the eye could comfortably follow. The water-tumult was named Lyons Falls. Near the falls, on a narrow flat, close to the west bank of the river, sat dismally, in true American style, in the prevailing genius of ugliness, a hamlet or village, also called Lyons Falls. It was occupied at the time by what were then known as human beings and was the terminus of a canal, already in decay, that had somehow found its way from the city of Rome. At a level higher than the village flat, ran, substantially north and south, a railway, named, if memory serves, the Rome, Carthage & Watertown. During what time the village had served as the terminus of the railway, it flourished; when the line moved on, the village drooped and withered into what has eminently been set forth as a state of innocuous desuetude. At the station was a dirt road at right angles to the railroad, that quickly fell around a curve down to the village. To the westward, however, it ran straight as a section line over the hills and vanished.

===Chinese===
那时候，黑河曲折不羁地北流，想来今日也是如此。它发源于尤蒂卡东北不远的山间，与地势反复争执之后，终于在沃特敦（Watertown）以外不远处注入安大略湖（Lake Ontario）。在流程大约一半的地方，它收纳了穆斯河；两河汇口再往下不远，便跌成一场翻腾喧豗的瀑布，落差约有四十英尺；过了瀑布，河面先是揉皱了一阵，随后便一路平顺地流下去，流到目力从容可及的远处，安然无语。这场水的喧闹，名叫莱昂斯福尔斯。瀑布近旁，河西岸一片窄窄的平地上，以地道的美国做派、以当道盛行的丑之天才，凄凄惶惶蹲着一座小村——也叫莱昂斯福尔斯。当时村里住着一批彼时所谓的人类；它还是一条运河的终点——运河早已衰败，不知怎么竟从罗马城（Rome）一路摸到了这里。比村中平地高出一层，大体南北走向，跑着一条铁路——如果我没记错，名叫罗马、迦太基与沃特敦铁路（Rome, Carthage & Watertown）。村子充任铁路终点的那段岁月里，它也兴旺过；待线路延伸而去，村子便垂头萎顿，凋敝成了一种被极精当地表述为「无害的荒废」的状态。车站处有一条与铁路成直角的土路，拐个弯便俯身落到村里。往西去，它却笔直如一条勘测线，翻过丘峦，消失在远方。

===Original===
From the railway station the ascent was gradual for a space, and at a distance, say of a hundred yards from the railway, and close to the northern side of the dirt road, rested the home of Walter Whittlesey, a rather modern structure for that day, surrounded by spruce trees that looked as though they had been dragged there and chained. Across the road from the family residence was the ice house, secreted in a lovely and refreshing glen of wildwood; at a decorous distance northeast of the “Mansion” was a big barn with its outbuildings, all in a state of dilapidation, and adjacent thereto was a worn and weary apple orchard, lichen-covered and rheumatic with age. Beyond this orchard was sheer stubble over a vast acreage.

===Chinese===
从车站起，坡道缓缓上升了一截；离铁路大约一百来码、紧贴土路北侧，坐落着沃尔特·惠特尔西的家——在当年算得上一座相当时新的房子——四周围着云杉，那模样像是被人拖到那里、又拿链子锁住的。住宅隔路相望处是储冰屋，藏在一道可爱沁凉的野林幽谷里；「府邸」东北方一段得体的距离外，是一座大谷仓连着些附属棚舍，一概破败不堪；紧挨着它的，是一片疲态尽显的苹果园，地衣满面，老得风湿驼背。果园之外，是一望无际、满目麦茬的大片田地。

===Original===
Not very far west of the house, however, was a charming valley, quite incidentally berthed between the looming earth-billows. Throughout the length of this ever-to-be-hallowed spot busily ran a rivulet to the encouragement of a swath of herbage, and of thankful shrubbery clinging to its edges. Part way up the western slope was a long horizontal outcropping of limestone ledge, along which, in comparative safety, grew a slender grove of tall, hardwood trees, with inviting undergrowth. One cannot drive a plow through a limestone ledge, and it is too much trouble to drain a low spot where there are plenty of hills. The grove-land paid its rent in firewood, the rivulet paid no rent at all﻿—thus were they tolerated in their beauty. Hay was the general crop.

===Chinese===
然而，房子往西没多远，有一道宜人的山谷，纯属偶然地泊在隆起的土浪之间。在这处注定永蒙祝福的地方，一条小溪沿着谷长忙碌地奔流，滋养出一带青草，也滋养着攀住溪岸、知恩图报的灌丛。西坡半腰有一长列水平的石灰岩台露头，沿着它，在相对的安全里，长着一小片修长的硬木高树，林下的灌木诱人可亲。石灰岩台上驾不得犁，而丘陵遍地的地方，去垦一片洼地又太费事。这片林地以柴薪缴租，小溪分文不缴——它们的美，就这样被容忍了下来。干草是这里普遍的作物。

===Original===
The Black River was crossed by a wagon bridge at a point between the Moose River and the falls. The road continued on to Lyonsdale. This same Black River gave an impression of performing a bold, high-handed deed. It split its territory sharply in halves. From its left bank rose wave upon wave of smooth hills mounting to a high plateau, while, as sharply from its right bank spread a huge, somber, primeval hemlock forest, mounting in turn upon its hills beyond the range of vision. Out of this forest rushed the Moose River, its waters icy and dark. Into this forest ran no road for long. The Black River appeared to have done this big, high-handed act; but the recurrence of the name Lyons, and the presence of a baronial seat at Lyonsdale, just within the edge of the forest, might have offered a diverging explanation to one intent upon what lies beyond appearances. However, such was the lay of the land.

===Chinese===
黑河在穆斯河与瀑布之间的一处，由一座马车道木桥横跨而过。路继续通向莱昂斯代尔（Lyonsdale）。正是这条黑河，给人干下了一桩大胆专断之事的印象：它把它的一方疆土干脆利落地劈成两半。左岸涌起一浪又一浪浑圆的山丘，层层登上高处的高原；同样干脆地，从右岸铺展开一大片幽暗的原生铁杉森林，又攀上它自己的丘峦，直到目力之外。穆斯河便从这片林中奔涌而出，水色冰寒而黝黑。这片林子里，很久都无路可通。这一切看似是黑河干下的这桩专断大手笔；不过，「莱昂斯」之名的反复出现，加上莱昂斯代尔——恰在林缘之内——那座男爵气派的宅邸，对一个执意要追问表象之下的人，或许另备有一条分岔的解释。然而，此地的地势就是这样。

===Original===
The train bearing Grandpa and Louis, after the preliminary whistle and bell clanging of ceremony, slowed up at the station. Grandpa, clean shaven, erect, aglow, descended with dignity; Louis, somewhat begrimed because of his fixed belief that the place for his head lay outside the car window, jumped after him, already excited by the Black River. He wanted to investigate everything at once or immediately; oh﻿—yes﻿—he must kiss Tante Jennie.

===Chinese===
载着外祖父和路易斯的列车，行过鸣笛撞钟的开场礼数之后，在车站放慢下来。外祖父刮净了脸，身板笔挺，容光焕发，庄严地下了车；路易斯因为坚信自己的脑袋理应待在车窗外面，弄得有些灰头土脸，跟着跳了下来，早已被黑河撩拨得心头发热。他恨不得立刻马上把一切查探个遍；哦——对了——他还得亲一亲姨妈珍妮。

===Original===
They were greeted at the station by Walter Whittlesey, a sizeable man, swarthy, grave, full bearded﻿—black sprinkled with gray, wearing the wide felt hat of a landowner who knew horses. He had given instructions, and had so notified Grandpa, that all baggage and luggage would be cared for, extraneously, by menials. He was a calm, courteous man, whose bearing suggested a lineage of colonels on horseback, blue grass, bourbon, blooded stock, beauteous women, and blacks.

===Chinese===
在车站迎候他们的是沃尔特·惠特尔西：一条魁伟的汉子，肤色黝黑，神情凝重，一部络腮全须——黑须间星星点点染了霜灰；头上那顶宽檐毡帽，是识马的地主戴的那一种。他早已吩咐妥当，并预先知会过外祖父：一切箱笼行李，自有仆役在外料理。他是个沉静有礼的人，那气派令人想见一脉马背上的上校世家——蓝草、波本酒、纯种牲口、姣好的女子，还有黑人。

===Original===
The three walked leisurely up the road, to the white house with green blinds where Tante Jennie, otherwise Mrs. Jenny List Whittlesey, awaited them with the reserve of a gentlewoman whom long practice had enabled to speak with delicate precision in a voice scarcely audible, and to inhale her smiles.

===Chinese===
三人沿着路从容上行，走向那座绿百叶窗的白房子；姨妈珍妮——也就是珍妮·李斯特·惠特尔西太太——正在那里等着他们，带着一位大家闺秀的矜持：长年的修习，使她能以细若几不可闻的嗓音说得精微确当，也使她能把她的微笑吸进体内，不令外泄。

===Original===
As the trio mounted the steps leading to the veranda, Louis in his rough and ready way casually noticed, not far from the doorway, a young lady reclining in an easy chair, quietly rocking, deeply absorbed in a book. Scarcely had he entered the open door but she had affirmed: “I’m going like that boy.”

===Chinese===
三人登上通向游廊的台阶时，路易斯以他那毛毛躁躁、随随便便的做派，顺带瞥见门边不远处一位年轻小姐斜倚在安乐椅里，轻轻地摇着，整个人浸在一本书里。他前脚刚跨进敞开的门，她便已然断言：「我要像那个男孩一样向前走去。」

===Original===
Within the “spare room” of the house, Grandpa folded his daughter in fervent arms, kissed her with the profound affection of an ageing father, and wept. Auntie did not weep; she amiably returned her father’s greeting, and said something in very pure French that seemed to satisfy. Louis went through the performance, awkwardly, and as hastily as possible. Auntie gave him the dry kiss of superculture and assured him in very pure English of her gratification at his arrival within her home.

===Chinese===
在屋内的「客房」里，外祖父把女儿搂进热烈的臂弯，以一位渐老的父亲深挚的爱怜吻她，落了泪。姨妈没有落泪；她和悦地回应了父亲的问候，又用十分纯正的法语说了几句什么，听上去皆大欢喜。路易斯把这过场走了一遍，笨手笨脚，只求尽快了结。姨妈给了他一个上流教养那干巴巴的吻，并用十分纯正的英语向他保证，他来到她的家中，她深感欣慰。

===Original===
Louis at the earliest moment escaped to the veranda. He had forgotten all about the young lady, and was startled and abashed to find her still there, gently rocking, absorbed in her book. Before he could retreat she arose in greeting with a smile known otherwhere only in Paradise; she said in glee: “My name is Minnie! I am eighteen, and a ‘young lady’ now. Oh Louis! I have waited for you so impatiently, and here you are at last. I am sure we shall like each other; don’t you think we will? I’m in society in Utica and I’m going to tell you lots of things. See, I wear long skirts and do up my hair, but I can’t climb trees any more; isn’t that a shame? But I’ll run races with you and we’ll have lots of fun; and I’ll tell you all about the books I’ve read and all about society. Here I’ve been for a month reading French books and speaking French with Aunt Jenny and have grown weary of myself; now you and I are to be chums! Don’t you think you’ll like me?”

===Chinese===
路易斯一得空便溜到了游廊上。那位年轻小姐，他已忘了个干净，撞见她还在那里，轻轻地摇着，浸在她的书里，不由得又惊又窘。他还来不及退走，她已含笑起身相迎——那种笑，别处只在天堂里见识得到；她喜滋滋地说：「我叫明妮（Minnie）！我十八岁了，如今是个『年轻小姐』啦。哦，路易斯！我等你知道等得多不耐烦，你可算来了。我敢说我们准会彼此喜欢；你说是不是？我在尤蒂卡已经进了社交界，我有好些事要讲给你听。瞧，我穿长裙子了，头发也挽起来了，就是再不能爬树了；你说气人不气人？可我还能跟你赛跑，咱们会有说不完的乐子；我还要把我读过的书、把社交界的事统统讲给你听。我在这儿待了一个月，天天读法文书、跟珍妮姨妈讲法语，早把自己腻烦透了；往后你和我就是好伙伴啦！你说你会不会喜欢我？」

===Original===
And Louis, taken thus unawares, and thus caressed with words, dared at last to look into gray Scotch eyes that seemed endowed with an endless fund of merriment, of badinage, of joy, of appeal, of kindness, and saturated with an inscrutable depth beyond all of these. He gazed steadily at a tender face, narrow, tapering, slender, and very pale, delicately freckled; at nostrils trembling; at a wide thin-lipped supersensitive mouth; at large ears; at thin, vagrant, dark, sandy hair; at a sprightly medium figure, all alive. She was clad in dark blue silk. He found in her not beauty but irresistible pervading charm. As he was thus absorbed Minnie said: “Sit down beside me, Louis dear, and watch me die. Sit very still and watch.” Whereupon, leaning back in her easy chair, she closed her eyes, deepened her pallor, closed her nostrils, made a thin line of her mouth, elongated her face, and lay deathly still, as though in veritable rigor mortis, until Louis’s nerves were on edge. Then, still dead and rigid, the fine line indicating her closed lips slowly widened across her face, the thin lips parting slightly as of themselves, cadaverously, the teeth also, a little later; after a seemingly endless wait, from this baleful rictus there came out moans, wails, gurgles, the ears began to crawl as of themselves. Then of a sudden the corpse sat bolt upright, with wide glaring eyes, grasped Louis by the shoulders and in fierce, frothy words forecast for him the direst of misfortunes by sea and land. Then she patted Louis’s pale cheek, fell back into her chair and giggled softly, casting at Louis the funniest, merriest, glances.

===Chinese===
路易斯就这样被攻了个措手不及，又被这番言语爱抚了一通，终于敢抬起眼来，望进一双灰色的苏格兰眼睛：那双眼睛仿佛天生怀着一座无尽的储备——打趣、欢愉、恳求与和善，而在这一切之外，又饱浸着一泓深不可测。他定定端详一张纤柔的脸：窄窄的，尖尖的，瘦瘦的，十分苍白，点缀着细细的雀斑；颤动的鼻翼；一张宽而唇薄、敏感过人的嘴；一双大耳朵；一头稀薄的、飘忽的深沙色头发；还有一个伶俐匀称的中等身段，浑身是活气。她穿一身深蓝色的绸衣。他在她身上发现的不是美貌，而是一种无可抗拒、无处不在的魅力。他正看得出神，明妮说话了：「坐到我旁边来，亲爱的路易斯，看我死一回。坐着别动，好好看着。」于是她往安乐椅里一仰，阖上双眼，把苍白加深一层，闭住鼻息，把嘴抿成一条细线，把脸拉长，随后直挺挺躺着，死了一般，俨然真进了尸僵，直到路易斯的神经绷到了边儿上。接着，人还死着、僵着，那条标示双唇闭拢的细线在她脸上缓缓加宽，薄唇自动地、尸腔尸调地微微开启，稍后牙齿也露了出来；挨过一段仿佛没有尽头的等待之后，从这道凶恶的死相里发出了呻吟、哀号与咕噜声，两只耳朵也开始自行蠕动起来。然后，死尸猛地直挺挺坐起，双眼暴睁，一把攥住路易斯的双肩，用凶狠而口沫四溅的话，为他占出陆上海上种种最凶的凶灾。随后她拍了拍路易斯发白的脸颊，倒回椅中，轻轻地咯咯直笑，朝路易斯抛去一个又一个顶滑稽、顶快活的眼风。

===Original===
“How old are you, Louis?”

“Fourteen.”

“Oh, I knew that. I asked your auntie. But isn’t it lovely, fourteen and eighteen; fourteen and eighteen!﻿—and to think that I have died for you, and have come back to you!

===Chinese===
「你几岁了，路易斯？」

「十四。」

「哦，这个我知道。我问过你姨妈了。可这不是妙极了吗，十四和十八；十四和十八！——想想看，我为你死过一遭，又为你还了魂！

===Original===
“Tomorrow we’ll go to church. The new minister’s a rather nice chap. I like to hear him pray, he’s so genteel about it; and he’s sound in doctrine, so your auntie says, and you know she’s a blue Presbyterian.” And Minnie immediately took Louis under her wing.

===Chinese===
「明天咱们上教堂去。新来的牧师是个挺不赖的小伙子。我爱听他祷告，他祷告起来那么文雅；教义上也纯正——你姨妈是这么说的，你知道的，她可是位铁杆的长老会信徒。」就这样，明妮当即把路易斯收拢到了自己的翼下。

===Original===
Next day she *took* him to church, leading him by a string, as it were, set him down beside her in the family pew, and their whisperings mingled with other whisperings in the repressive silence. Then the minister appeared in the pulpit, a fairly young man with mien and countenance betoking earnestness, piety and poverty. Louis thought he prayed well, as with quiet fervor he set forth his belief that God was within his temple, and assuredly within the hearts of his flock. When it came to the sermon, Louis sat up straight and took eager notice, for the good man had just read from the big Bible this text: “And the Lord went before them by day in a pillar of cloud, to lead them on the way, and by night in a pillar of fire, to give them light to go by day and night.” Louis needed no sermon; in a flash he knew that all his life he had been led in by a pillar of gleaming cloud, and a pillar of fire; and his far-reaching instant vision forecast it would be thus until the end. Yet he took much heart in listening to the youngish man in the pulpit grasp the totality of this simple story, transmute it into a great symbol, and in impassioned voice lift it to the heights of idealism and of moral grandeur, refashioning it into a spiritual pillar of cloud and a pillar of fire ever present in the hearts, the minds, the souls of all humans, as he urgently, yea, piteously, besought the blind to see.

===Chinese===
第二天，她果真把他*带*去了教堂，仿佛用一根线牵着他，把他安置在自家的教堂专席里，坐在自己身旁；两人的窃窃私语，混进了那压抑的寂静中别的窃窃私语里。随后牧师登上讲坛：一个相当年轻的人，神情面容透着恳切、虔诚与清寒。路易斯觉得他祷告得很好——他以安静的炽热申明自己的信念：上帝在他的殿中，也确然在他的群羊心里。到了讲道的时候，路易斯坐直了身子，热切地留意起来；因为这位好人刚从那本大圣经里念了这段经文：「日间，耶和华在云柱中领他们的路程；夜间，在火柱中光照他们，使他们日夜都可以行走。」路易斯不需要讲道；电光石火之间他就知道了：他这一生，一直被一柱闪亮的云、一柱火引领着前行；他那望得极远的顿悟还预见到，直到终局都将如此。然而，听那讲坛上的年轻人如何把握住这个简单故事的全局，把它点化成一个宏大的象征，再以炽烈的声音把它举到理想主义与道德崇高的高处，重新锻造成一柱云、一柱火，永驻于所有人的心、脑、灵魂之中——他又深受鼓舞：那人是那样急切，是的，甚至是那样哀恳地，央求盲者们睁开眼睛。

===Original===
As they walked home Minnie remarked that it was an extra-fine sermon, but as Louis did not reply she scented danger and tactfully chatted about little things, until her joyous sweetness detached him from his pillar of ravishing cloud and the pillar of wondrous fire. Soon she had him laughing as gaily as herself and plucking wayside flowers for her. For Minnie was intuitive to a degree. She knew that Louis had been deeply stirred, that he had been dreaming somberly as they left the church: and this she would not countenance. She believed that if one must dream it should be of happiness, and the dreamer wide awake to the joy of living. They sat for a while by the falls, but Louis was not content. There seemed to be something purposeless in this clumsy tumbling about of dark waters, losing their balance, falling helplessly over ledges and worn boulders, lost in their way among them, and reeling absurdly off at the bottom. It all seemed to lack order and singleness of purpose. Near the falls was a small wooden mill afflicted with the rickets, and this alone seemed in tune with the falls.

===Chinese===
回家的路上，明妮评论说，这真是一篇格外精彩的讲道；可路易斯没有答话，她嗅出了危险，便乖觉地扯起些鸡毛蒜皮，直到她那欢快的甜，把他从那柱令人迷醉的云、那柱奇异的火上卸了下来。不多时，她已逗得他同她自己一样开怀大笑，还教他给她采摘路旁的花。因为明妮的直觉到了家了。她知道路易斯受了深深的撼动，知道他们走出教堂时，他一直在做阴郁的梦：而这是她断断不能容许的。她相信，人若非做梦不可，就该做幸福的梦，而做梦的人对生之欢愉要保持全然的清醒。他们在瀑布旁坐了一会儿，但路易斯心有不惬。这些黑沉沉的水笨拙地翻滚，似乎全无目的：失了平衡，无助地跌过一道道石台与磨圆的巨砾，在乱石间迷了路，又在底下荒唐地踉跄而去。这一切似乎缺少秩序，缺少目标的专一。瀑布近旁有一座害着佝偻病的小木磨坊，唯独它，看来与这瀑布气味相投。

===Original===
So they trudged home and Aunt Jenny said the blessing. Grandpa had just returned from a long walk, his favorite pastime﻿—fifteen or twenty miles﻿—nothing for him. It became his daily habit. He always went barehead, always got lost and always found his way back.

===Chinese===
于是两人徒步回家，珍妮姨妈做了谢饭祷告。外祖父刚散步回来——长途步行是他的心头好——十五二十英里，在他不算一回事。这成了他每日的功课。他总是光着头出门，总是迷路，又总能找回回来的路。

===Original===
Next day Minnie told Louis, in confidence, she knew of a charming spot not very far away, where there were ledges of rock and tall trees, and a darling rivulet with green along its banks. She *took* him there, and would not even let him help her over the lichen and moss covered rocks. With Louis in tow she found a shady spot, with ferns and undergrowth forming a nook, and the wide-branching trees a canopy. She had taken books with her, and on a large, ancient stone which she called her pulpit, she perched with her slave. Below them ran the rivulet, and above the opposite crest there showed a bit of the roof of the dwelling. Minnie clapped her hands with joy. “Louis, don’t you think I’m good to bring you here? It is the solitary oasis in this desert of hayland. There is hay, hay, hay, for miles.”

===Chinese===
第二天，明妮悄悄告诉路易斯，说她知道不远处一个迷人的去处：那里有岩石的台地和高树，还有一条可爱的小溪，两岸铺着青翠。她果真把他*带*了去，甚至不许他扶她跨过那些覆满地衣与青苔的石头。她拽着路易斯，寻到一处浓荫之地：蕨草与灌木围成一个小小的角落，枝柯张开的大树作帐为盖。她随身带了书来；在一块她唤作自己讲坛的大古石上，她带着她的奴隶栖了上去。他们脚下小溪流淌，对面山脊的上方露出住宅的一角屋顶。明妮乐得直拍手：「路易斯，你说我带你来这儿，待你多好？这是这片干草荒漠里独一无二的绿洲。放眼望去，只有干草、干草、干草，绵延数英里。」

===Original===
Presently she opened a book and read from Tennyson, making her selections carefully varied, feeling her way through Louis’s responses to see where she could reach his heart, how she could bare it, and then keep her secret. She read from Byron, recited many other poems with a skill unknown to elocutionists, and a stealthy, comfortable look came into her eyes now turned green, her face wreathed in a Mona Lisa smile, as she said: “Louis, this is a great, beautiful, good world if but we knew it, and to this very spot I have often come in thankful mood, and from this very pulpit prayed to these trees to make me pure in heart.” And then she told Louis about the many books she had read, largely French novels﻿—for practice, she said; and then Louis told her he had read all of Captain Mayne Reid’s books, all the Leather Stocking Tales, some by Maryatt, and some wonderful and beautiful stories in the Bible; and he recited for her, verbatim, the story of Elijah, the whirlwind, and the still small voice.

===Chinese===
不一会儿，她翻开一本书，诵读起丁尼生（Tennyson），选篇刻意搭配得错落有致，一面循着路易斯的反应探路，看从哪里能够抵达他的心，怎样把那颗心剥露出来，然后再守住她的秘密。她念了拜伦（Byron），又以职业朗诵家们闻所未闻的技巧，背了许多别的诗；这时一种悄悄的、餍足的神色，爬进她那双转成了绿色的眼睛，脸上笼起蒙娜丽莎式的微笑，她说：「路易斯，这世界伟大、美丽、良善，只要我们知道就好；我常怀着一颗感恩的心来到这个去处，就在这座讲坛上，向这些树木祈祷，求它们使我内心纯净。」随后她对路易斯讲起自己读过的许多书，大半是法国小说——她说是为了练语言；接着路易斯告诉她，梅恩·里德船长（Captain Mayne Reid）的书他读了个遍，《皮袜子故事集》（Leather Stocking Tales）全读了，马里亚特（Maryatt）的读了几本，圣经里一些奇妙而美丽的故事也读了；还一字不差地给她背诵了以利亚（Elijah）的故事——旋风，与那微小的声音。

===Original===
The smile on Minnie’s pale face became luxurious, her gleaming eyes about to close, as she said half-warningly: “Louis, Louis, you are in danger!” and refused to explain. Then suddenly coming to herself she cried: “We must go back to the house at once; if we are late at supper, your auntie will give me just one look, and I will know exactly what that one look means; but *you* won’t.” And she took Louis by the hand, her books under the other arm, resumed her jaunty mood and led him to the house, delivering to his Auntie a human package not merely stirred, but churned into butter and whey.

===Chinese===
明妮苍白脸上的笑变得奢靡起来，她那双亮晶晶的眼睛阖了一半，半是告诫地说：「路易斯，路易斯，你有危险！」却拒绝解释。随后她猛然回过神来，喊道：「咱们得马上回屋里去；晚饭要是迟了，你姨妈只要看我一眼，我马上就知道那一眼是什么意思；可*你*就不会知道。」她牵起路易斯的手，另一只胳膊夹着书，重新摆出她轻快活泼的神气，把他领回屋里，交给他的姨妈——一件不只被搅动过、而是被搅成了黄油与乳浆的人形包裹。

===Original===
Auntie again said grace; the thoughts of all bowed heads but hers were on supper. The evening was spent by the family on the dark veranda singing old-fashioned hymns; after which the peace of night came over all﻿—but one.

===Chinese===
姨妈又做了谢饭祷告；一桌低垂的头颅里，除了她的，所思所想都在晚饭上。入夜后，一家人在昏暗的游廊上唱着老式的赞美诗消磨时光；唱罢，夜的安宁降临到每个人身上——只有一个人例外。

===Original===
Next day, Minnie, repentant of her wickedness, appeared as a fresh blown morning glory, gave hearty, cheerful greetings to all, and to Louis talked as might an ordinarily affectionate sister. Her eyes were crystalline, her carriage buoyant. Then, at the appointed time, she began her hour of French with Auntie; and as Louis, nearby, listened, he framed a desire and a resolve to learn the language which Minnie seemed to read and speak as easily as Auntie. The lesson over, Minnie came to Louis, took a place beside him and as one wooing, said, “Dear protégé: the hour is at hand. I have much to say. The woods are calling, the birds are waiting. Let us now repair to the pulpit and be two sensible humans.” To the pulpit they repaired, that day and many a day. Once seated on the great stone, Minnie put Louis at his ease and began rapid-fire questions, about Louis’s home and school life. She wished every detail; and Louis answered faithfully. He told her not only the story of his life, but the story of everyone and everything therewith connected: Minnie saying: “Fine, fine, how well you tell it,” in running comment. He even told Minnie one of Julia’s fairy tales, the tale of the “Good People,” and Minnie cried: “Oh, what a lovely brogue, isn’t it sweet?” and Louis said yes, it was, and added that Julia had taught him some real Gaelic words, but he had forgotten the meaning of most of them. “That gives me a bright idea, Louis; you don’t know French, so I will give you a password, in French, that is better than any Gaelic. Say to me, once every day, *Je t’aime*”; and Louis said to her once every day *Je t’aime*﻿—deeming it a secret. And Minnie would gravely say each time, in approval, that he pronounced it beautifully.

===Chinese===
第二天，明妮为自己的顽劣悔过了，露面时像一朵初绽的朝颜，向众人一一送上诚挚爽朗的问候，对路易斯说起话来，就像一个寻常亲昵的姐姐。她的眼睛清亮如水晶，步态轻盈欢畅。到了约定的时辰，她开始跟姨妈上一小时法文课；路易斯在一旁听着，心里立起一个愿望、一个决心：要学会这门明妮似乎同姨妈一样读来说来毫不费力的语言。课上完了，明妮走到路易斯跟前，在他身旁坐下，像个求爱者似的说道：「亲爱的小门生（protégé）：时辰到了。我有许多话要说。树林在呼唤，鸟儿在等候。咱们这就上讲坛去，做两个明白事理的人。」他们便上了讲坛——那一天，以及往后许多天。在大石头上坐定之后，明妮让路易斯自在下来，便开始连珠炮似的发问，问的是路易斯的家居与学校生活。每一处细节她都要；路易斯也一一如实作答。他不仅把自己一生的故事讲给她听，还把与之相连的每一个人、每一件事都讲了个遍：明妮一路点评着：「好，好，你讲得真好。」他甚至把朱莉娅的童话讲了一则给明妮听，就是那则《善良的人们》；明妮叫了起来：「哦，多好听的爱尔兰土腔，是不是甜极了？」路易斯说是，甜极了，又说朱莉娅还教过他几句真正的盖尔语，只是大多数的意思他已经忘了。「这倒给了我一个妙主意，路易斯；你不懂法文，那我就给你一句口令，法文的，比什么盖尔语都强。每天对我说一次：*Je t'aime*（我爱你）。」于是路易斯每天对她说一次 *Je t'aime*——只当那是一个秘密。而明妮每次都一本正经地表示赞许，说他发得美极了。

===Original===
She told him conversationally about herself and her home. She described in detail her finishing school, and mimicked its follies. She raved over her adored brother Ed, fresh from Yale. Told of her coming out, of Utica society, and her set, and of the landed aristocracy, the old families, the exclusive, best people; said her father was a big grain forwarder, and had plenty of money, as far as her simple needs were concerned, and described minutely her trip to Europe. She travelled this ground to and fro with many a mimicry, flippancy, wise saw, and splendid enthusiasms.

===Chinese===
她又像闲谈似的对他讲起自己和她的家。她细细描摹了她念的精修学校（finishing school），把学校的种种蠢态模仿了个够。她大谈特谈她崇拜的哥哥埃德（Ed），刚从耶鲁（Yale）回来。她讲了自己的初入社交界，讲尤蒂卡的上流社会和她那个小圈子，讲有产的贵族、旧家世族、排他的最上等人；说她父亲是个大宗粮食转运商（grain forwarder），钱是不缺的——就她那点简单的需要而言；还把她的欧洲之行一五一十讲了个详尽。这片地界她来来回回犁了又犁，夹带着一茬又一茬的模仿、轻浮话、格言警句，和一阵又一阵的兴高采烈。

===Original===
So Louis began to see that people were graded. He was pained at many things Minnie casually described. She was revealing too much. She was unconscious of lifting many veils, as Louis was unconscious of repeating world-truth when he said, every day, *Je t’aime*. He was not lifting any veil for Minnie; this selfsame Minnie having one small devil peeping through each eye. Their talk, throughout that livelong day, was gossip.

When Minnie came, through questioning, to a full sense of the depth of Louis’s ignorance of the world, of social organization both in its ephemeral and its momentous inert and stratified aspects, that he was provincial, that he was honest, frank, and unsuspecting, she became alarmed at the new danger, and determined to prepare him; and in so doing, she lifted at least a corner of a sinister and heavy veil that lay behind appearances. This she did with skill, and a little at a time, proving her case in each instance, by direct illustration and remarks none too complimentary. But Minnie could not be serious for long at a time; she preferred frivolity, nonsense and high spirits﻿—never for a moment neglecting to keep Louis dazed in her land of enchantment.

===Chinese===
于是路易斯开始看出：人是分着等级的。明妮随口描述的许多事情都令他痛苦。她揭示得太多了。她意识不到自己掀开了多少重帷幕，正如路易斯意识不到，他每天说的那句 *Je t'aime*，是在重复一条世界性的真理。他并没有为明妮掀开任何帷幕；倒是这位明妮本人，每只眼睛里都藏着一个小魔鬼，正向外窥探。他们那一整天的谈话，全是闲言碎语。

等明妮通过盘问，充分体察到路易斯对世界的无知有多深——对社会组织转瞬即逝的一面，以及它沉滞板结、等级层叠的另一面，都一无所知——体察到他是乡气的、诚实的、坦率的、不疑有诈的，她为这新的危险惊觉起来，决意替他做些准备；而这样做的时候，她至少掀开了那幅罩在表象之后的阴沉厚重的帷幕的一角。她做这件事很有手艺，一次掀开一点，每一回都举出直接的例证和绝称不上恭维的评语，把她的论断坐实。只是明妮严肃不了多久；她偏爱轻浮、胡闹和兴高采烈——同时一刻也不曾疏忽，始终把路易斯留在她的迷醉之国里，叫他眼花缭乱。

===Original===
Minnie became Louis’s precious teacher. She made him feel he was not being taught, but entertained with gossip. She knew that what she said in persiflage, would later sink in deep, and she knew why it would do so.

Minnie was both worldly and unworldly. With nature she was dreamy; but when it came to people, she became a living microscope, her sharp brain void of all illusion, for her true world was of the world of people﻿—there she lived﻿—as Louis’s world had been a world of the wide open﻿—of romance. Hence, with Louis she was ever gentle, even though she dangled him as though he were a toy balloon.

An aching in her guarded heart was soothed by him; and he became for her a luxury﻿—a something to remain awhile a precious memory. Thus Minnie filled the air with laughter, and with debonnaire delight﻿—meanwhile feeding honey drop by drop﻿—just to see upon a human face the rare, the precious witching aspect of idolatry.

===Chinese===
明妮成了路易斯珍贵的老师。她让他觉得自己不是在受教，而是在听闲话取乐。她知道，她以打趣说出的话，日后会深深地沉进他心里；她也知道，为什么会那样沉法。

明妮既世故又不世故。面对自然，她如梦如幻；可一涉及到人，她便成了一架活的显微镜，那颗锋利的头脑里空无幻象，因为她真正的世界是人的世界——她就活在那里——正如路易斯的世界一直是一片辽阔的旷野——一个浪漫的世界。因此，她对路易斯永远温存，尽管她拎着他晃来晃去，活像拎着一只玩具气球。

她那颗设防的心里的一处隐痛，被他熨平了；他成了她的一件奢侈品——一样能暂且留驻、化作珍贵记忆的事物。就这样，明妮让满空气都是笑声，都是温文尔雅的欢愉——同时把蜂蜜一滴一滴地喂过去——只为在一张人脸上，看见崇拜之光那稀有、珍贵而醉人的模样。

---

===Original===
So came a day when Minnie, on the pulpit, talked of things pertaining to herself. Among other words she said the young men of her set were grossly stupid; incapable of thought above the level of the sty. Their outlook upon life she said was vapid, coarse and vain. That they held women to be property, their appendage, their vehicle of display. They were all rich, she said, and this made matters worse. To be anchored to such brutes, scarcely decent in their evening clothes, she said, was horror. She would be owned, she said, by no man rich or poor. She must be free, she said; free as air. Knowing all this now, she had marked her course in life, and she said that never would she marry﻿—the risk of sorrow was too great. All this she said as lightly as a swallow on the wing.

===Chinese===
于是有这么一天，明妮在讲坛上谈起了与她自身相关的事。在诸般言语之间，她说她那个圈子里的年轻男子蠢得可以，思维能力高不过猪圈的水准。她说他们对人生的看法乏味、粗鄙而虚荣。他们把女人当财产，当附属品，当他们炫耀用的摆设。她说他们个个有钱，而这只会更糟。她说，被拴在那样一批畜生身上——他们穿起晚礼服也才勉强像样——简直是恐怖。她说，无论富的穷的，她绝不让任何一个男人占有她。她说她必须自由；像空气一样自由。如今这一切她都已看得分明，人生的航线她已标定，她说她永不结婚——招致悲伤的风险太大了。这一切，她说得轻巧，如一羽掠空的燕子。

===Original===
At these last words, something fell away in Louis’s solar plexus, sometimes known as the sensorium, and Minnie said: “Never mind, never mind, you’ll outgrow it, Louis, you are fourteen, I’m eighteen. While it lasts, let us be dear friends together; the dearest comrades ever known. Your heart’s in mine and mine in yours, I know. Let these great oaks, as witnesses, betrothe us in such way, and prophesy a lovely memory.”

===Chinese===
听到最后这几句，路易斯的太阳神经丛（solar plexus）——那地方有时也被称作感觉中枢（sensorium）——里，有什么东西直坠了下去。明妮说：「不要紧，不要紧，你长大就会把它长过去的，路易斯，你十四，我十八。趁它还在，咱们就做亲亲爱爱的朋友吧；做天底下从未有过的最亲的伙伴。你的心装在我心里，我的心装在你心里，这我知道。就让这些大橡树作见证，以这样的方式为我们定情，预言一段可爱的回忆吧。」

===Original===
Louis with unheard-of stoicism held back his tears. And Minnie said: “Come now, let’s be going; don’t refer to this again. Let’s be as we’ve always been, together, carefree﻿—and let laughter ring again.”

===Chinese===
路易斯以闻所未闻的斯多噶式的坚忍，忍住了他的眼泪。明妮便说：「好了，咱们走吧；这事不要再提了。咱们还像从前那样，在一起，无忧无虑——让笑声重新响起来。」

===Original===
Such was Minnie’s way of doing and of saying. She was Louis’s loyal friend. She mothered him in sprightly malice and in tenderness alike. All her vagaries and sweetness came from one constant nature. She was ever thoughful of the needs of others. She was exquisitely human. To Louis, long adapted to the elderly, she was held by him as in a shrine, to be the only truly human he had ever known; and her kindness in adopting him, and making him her own, not for a day, but for all the glad summer long, made him feel as though his life, before her floating into it, had been but a blank. How could he ever repay! She had come, it seemed to him, out of the invisible that lies behind all things, all dreams, to be his faerie queen.

===Chinese===
明妮的行事与言语，就是这样。她是路易斯忠实的朋友。她以活泼的促狭待他，也以温柔体贴待他，两样都带着母亲的情分。她一切的奇想与甜美，都出自一副始终如一的天性。她对别人的需要，永远体贴入微。她通人性通到了精微的境地。对早已习惯了长者的路易斯来说，她被他供在神龛里，当作他平生认识的唯一一个真正的人；而她收养他、把他纳为自己人的那份善意——不是一天两天，而是整整一个欢快的夏天——使他觉得，在她飘进他的生活之前，他这一生不过是一片空白。他怎样才能报答！在他看来，她是万物、万梦背后那无形之境里来的，来做他的仙后（faerie queen）。

===Original===
And now it seems as though a half a century had stood still.

===Chinese===
而现在，仿佛有半个世纪一直伫立未动。

## V Newburyport / 五　纽伯里波特

===Original===

The train now well under way for Newburyport, our poet, he of the dream-life, crawled forth from his cave of gloom and began to take notice. Soon he was all notice and no gloom. His prior and only trip in a railway train was now over two years back in ancient history, which signified oblivion. Hence all was now new and novel. He began at once, at the very beginning of the beginning, that intolerable, interminable series of questions which all children ask and no mother can for long stand the strain of answering.
