Each individual rejoiced in envious admiration, and all rejoiced in the thought that these great men, these mighty men, had, with few and negligible exceptions, risen from the ranks of the common people: That this one began as a telegraph operator at a lonely way-station, and this one was boss of a section gang on such and such a railroad; another started in life as a brakeman; that one was clerk in a country store; this one came to our hospitable shores as a penniless immigrant; that one was a farmer boy; and their hymn arose and rang shimmering as a paean to their mighty ones, and their cry went up to their God, even as a mighty anthem, lifting up its head to proclaim to all the world that this, their Country, was vastly more than the land of the free and the home of the brave; it was the noble land of equal opportunity for all; the true democracy for which mankind has been waiting through the centuries in blood and tears, in hope deferred. This, they cried, as one voice, is the Hospitable Land that welcomes the stranger at its gates. This is the great Democracy where all men are equal and free. All this they sang gladly as they moved up the runways.

===Chinese===
人民欢腾了。每个人怀着艳羡的钦慕而欢腾；而全体又怀着这样的念头欢腾：这些伟人、这些强人，除寥寥可忽略的例外，都是从平民的行列里升起来的：这一位起家时，是僻静小站上的电报员；那一位是某条铁路上养路班的工头；另一位人生起步，是个司闸员；那一位是乡村杂货店的伙计；这一位是身无分文、来到我们好客海岸的移民；那一位是农家子弟。他们的颂歌升起来了，铿然如一首献给强者的凯歌；他们的呼声上达他们的神，俨然一支雄壮的圣歌，昂起头来向全世界宣告：他们的这个国家，远不只是自由之土与勇士之乡；它是人人机会均等的高贵之邦；是人类千百年来在血泪之中、在延宕的希望里等待着的真民主。这个，他们异口同声地喊着，就是那在城门口欢迎生客的好客之邦。这就是人人平等自由的伟大民主。这一切，他们都欢欢喜喜地唱着——一路走上通道。

===Original===
Thus the Land was stirring and quivering in impulses, wave upon wave. The stream of immigration was enormous, spreading over vast areas, burrowing in the mines, or clinging to the cities. Chicago had passed St. Louis in population and was proud. Its system of building had become known as the “Chicago Construction.” It was pushing its structures higher and higher, until the Masonic Temple by John Root had raised its head far into the air, and the word “skyscraper” came into use. Chicago was booming. It had become a powerful magnet. Its people had one dream in common: That their city should become the world’s metropolis. There was great enthusiasm and public spirit. So things stood, in the years 1890, 1891 and 1892. John Root had said to Louis: “You take your art too seriously.” Burnham had said to Louis: “It is not good policy to go much above the general level of intelligence.” Burnham had also said: “See! Louis, how beautiful the moon is, now, overhead, how tender. Something in her beauty suggests tears to me.”

===Chinese===
于是大地在一波接一波的冲动中翻搅震颤。移民的人流浩浩荡荡，漫过广袤的地域，钻进矿井，或附着在城市上。芝加哥的人口已超过圣路易斯，颇为自豪。它的建造体系已得名「芝加哥建造法」。它的楼越盖越高，直到约翰·鲁特的共济会圣殿把头远远探进空中，「摩天楼」一词遂告通行。芝加哥正轰轰烈烈。它已成一块强大的磁石。它的人民共有一个梦：他们的城，要成为世界的都会。热忱与公益精神高涨。局面便是如此，在 1890、1891 与 1892 年。约翰·鲁特对路易斯说过：「你把你的艺术看得太重了。」伯纳姆对路易斯说过：「比一般智力水平高出太多，不是好策略。」伯纳姆还说过：「看哪，路易斯，此刻头顶上的月亮多美，多么温柔。她美里有什么东西，让我眼里含泪。」

===Original===
And Chicago rolled on and roared by day and night except only in its stillest hours toward dawn. There seemed to reside in its dreams before the dawn during these years something not wholly material, something in the underlying thoughts of men that aspired to reach above the general level of intelligence and the raucous hue and cry. At least Louis thought so. Then, as now, was the great Lake with its far horizon, the sweeping curve of its southern shore, its many moods, which every day he viewed from his tower windows. And there was the thought, the seeming presence of the prairies and the far-flung hinterland. In such momentary trance his childhood would return to him with its vivid dream of power, a dream which had now grown to encompass the world; from such reverie he would perchance awaken to some gossip of Adler, standing by, concerning the inside story of some of the city’s great men, all of which was grist for Louis’s mill, for Adler was quite literal when he told these anecdotes, and Louis listened keenly to them, and learned. The two frequently lunched together. Shop talk was taboo. But they did not talk about the coming World’s Fair, as authorized by Act of Congress in 1890. It was deemed fitting by all the people that the four hundredth anniversary of the discovery of America by one Christopher Columbus, should be celebrated by a great World Exposition, which should spaciously reveal to the last word the cultural status of the peoples of the Earth; and that the setting for such display should be one of splendor, worthy of its subject.

===Chinese===
芝加哥就这样昼夜滚动、轰鸣，只在破晓前最静的时辰稍歇。那些年里，它黎明前的梦寐之中，仿佛居着某种并非全然物质的东西，居在人们企望超出一般智力水平与喧嚣鼓噪之上的底层念头里。至少路易斯这样想。那时同现在一样，有大湖与它的远天水线，有南岸那道开阔的弧线，有它多变的脾气，他每天从塔楼的窗里眺望。还有那个念头：草原与迢遥腹地似在眼前。在这样的片刻出神里，他的童年会带着那个鲜明的力之梦，回到他身上——那梦如今已长大到拥抱世界；从这样的遐想里，他或许会醒过来，听见立在一旁的阿德勒讲些本城大人物内幕的闲话——这一切都成了路易斯磨坊里的粮，因为阿德勒讲这些轶事，句句按字面直说，路易斯听得入神，也学到了东西。两人时常一起午餐。谈公事是禁忌。但他们不曾谈起那届即将到来的世界博览会——1890 年国会法案批准举办。全体人民都认为理当如此：美洲因一位克里斯托弗·哥伦布而被发现，满四百周年，理应以一场盛大的世界博览会来庆祝，把地球各民族的文化状态展示到极致；而这场陈列的布景，理当壮丽，配得上它的主题。

===Original===
Chicago was ripe and ready for such an undertaking. It had the required enthusiasm and the will. It won out in a contest between the cities. The prize was now in hand. It was to be the city’s crowning glory. A superb site on the lake adjoined the southern section of the city. This site was so to be transformed and embellished by the magic of American prowess, particularly in its architectural aspects, as to set forth the genius of the land in that great creative art. It was to be a dream city, where one might revel in beauty. It was to be called The White City by the Lake.

===Chinese===
芝加哥对这样一桩事业，已然熟透，跃跃欲试。它有必需的热忱与意志。它在一番城市间的竞逐中获胜。奖品到手了。这将是本城至高的荣耀。湖上一处绝佳的地段，紧挨着城南。这地段将要借美国伟力的魔法——尤其在建筑一面——来改造与装点，以彰显这片土地在那门伟大创造艺术中的天才。这将是一座梦之城，供人在美中纵情。它将被唤作湖畔白城（The White City by the Lake）。

===Original===
Now arose above the horizon the small white cloud. It came from eastward. It came borne upon the winds of predestination. Who could fancy that a harmless white cloud might cast a white shadow? Who could forecast the shape of that shadow? It was here that one man’s unbalanced mind spread a gauze-like pall of fatality. That one man’s unconscious stupor in bigness, and in the droll fantasy of hero-worship, did his best and his worst, according to his lights, which were dim except the one projector by the harsh light of which he saw all things illuminated and grown bombastically big in Chauvinistic outlines. Here was to be the test of American culture, and here it failed. Dreamers may dream; but of what avail the dream if it be but a dream of misinterpretation? If the dream, in such a case, rise not in vision far above the general level of intelligence, and prophesy through the medium of clear thinking, true interpretation﻿—why dream at all? Why not rest content as children of Barnum, easy in the faith that one of “them” is born every minute. Such in effect was the method adopted in practice while the phrase-makers tossed their slogans to and fro.

===Chinese===
这时，地平线上方升起了那朵小白云。它自东方来。它乘着前定的风而来。谁能想到，一朵无害的白云，也会投下白色的阴影？谁能料到那阴影的形状？正是在这里，一个人失衡的心智，铺开了一层薄纱般的宿命之幕。这一个人对「大」的无意识昏迷，对英雄崇拜的滑稽幻想，尽其明暗，做了他最好也最坏的事——照着他的光，那光是昏暗的，唯独那一台放映机除外，借它刺目的光，他看见万物被照亮、被吹胀成沙文主义轮廓的庞然大物。这里本该是美国文化的一场考验，而它在此考砸了。做梦的人尽可以做去；可这梦若只是一场误读之梦，又有何益？这梦若不能在远见中，升到一般智力水平之上高得多处、不能借清晰的思考，预言出真实的诠释——那又何必做梦？何不安心做巴纳姆的孩子们，笃信「他们」那种人每分钟都出世一个。实情之中，被采用的正是这套办法，而口号的匠人们，把标语抛来抛去。

===Original===
At the beginning it was tentatively assumed that the firm of Burnham & Root might undertake the work in its entirety. The idea was sound in principle﻿—one hand, one great work﻿—a superb revelation of America’s potency﻿—an oration, a portrayal, to arouse that which was hidden, to call it forth into the light. But the work of ten years cannot be done in two. It would require two years to grasp and analyze the problem and effect a synthesis. Less than three years were available for the initiation and completion of the work entire, ready for the installation of exhibits. The idea was in consequence dismissed. As a matter of fact there was not an architect in the land equal to the undertaking. No veteran mind seasoned to the strategy and tactics involved in a wholly successful issue. Otherwise there might have arisen a gorgeous Garden City, reflex of one mind, truly interpreting the aspirations and the heart’s desire of the many, every detail carefully considered, every function given its due form, with the sense of humanity at its best, a suffusing atmosphere; and within the Garden City might be built another city to remain and endure as a memorial, within the parkland by the blue waters, oriented toward the rising sun, a token of a covenant of things to be, a symbol of the city’s basic significance as offspring of the prairie, the lake and the portage.

===Chinese===
起初曾试探性地假定，伯纳姆与鲁特事务所可以承担全部工程。这想法原则上不差——一只手，一件大工程——美国力量的一场绝佳显示——一篇演说，一幅画卷，去唤醒那隐匿的，唤它出到光里。但十年的功，做不进两年。单是把问题抓住、分析透、做出综合，就需要两年。而从启动到全部完工、待展品进场，可用的还不到三年。这想法于是作罢。其实，全国也没有一位建筑师当得起这桩事业。没有一个老成的头脑，熟于这场全胜所需的战略战术。否则，本可能升起一座壮丽的花园城市，一颗心灵的映像，真切地诠释众人的渴望与心中所愿，每一细部都经深思熟虑，每一功能都得其应得之形式，带着人性至善之感、一片氤氲的大气；而花园城市之内，本可再筑一座留驻长存的城，以为纪念，在蔚蓝水畔的园林之间，面向初升的太阳，作一个将临之事之约的信物，作这座城基本意义的一个象征——大草原、大湖与那道水陆转运口的子孙。

===Original===
But “hustle” was the word. Make it big, make it stunning, knock ’em down! The cry was well meant as things go.

So in the fall of 1890 John Root was officially appointed consulting architect, and Daniel Burnham, Chief of Construction.

===Chinese===
可当时的口号是「快干」。搞大，搞炫，把他们撂倒！以世事而论，这呐喊用心不坏。

于是 1890 年秋，约翰·鲁特被正式任命为顾问建筑师，丹尼尔·伯纳姆任工程总管。

===Original===
Later, with the kindly assistance of Edward T. Jefferey, Chairman of the Committee on Buildings and Grounds, Burnham selected five architects from the East and five from the West, ten in all. Burnham and Jefferey loved each other dearly. The thought of one was the thought of both, as it were﻿—sometimes. Burnham had believed that he might best serve his country by placing all of the work exclusively with Eastern architects; solely, he averred, on account of their surpassing culture. With exquisite delicacy and tact, Jefferey, at a meeting of the Committee, persuaded Daniel, come to Judgment, to add the Western men to the list of his nominations.

===Chinese===
其后，蒙建筑与场地委员会主席爱德华·T·杰弗里（Edward T. Jefferey）的好意相助，伯纳姆从东部选了五位建筑师，从西部选了五位，共十人。伯纳姆与杰弗里彼此深爱。一人的想法便是两人的想法，可谓——有时候。伯纳姆曾认为，把全部工程独交东部建筑师，才是报效祖国的上策；他声称，唯一缘故，是他们那超群的文化。杰弗里以精妙入微的分寸与手腕，在委员会的一次会议上，说服了俨然升堂断案的丹尼尔，把西部的同行补进了他的提名名单。

===Original===
A gathering of these architects took place in February, 1891. After an examination of the site, which by this time was dreary enough in its state of raw upheaval, the company retired for active conference. John Root was not there. In faith he could not come. He had made his rendezvous the month before. Grace-land was now his home. Soon above him would be reared a Celtic cross. Louis missed him sadly. Who now would take up the foils he had dropped on his way, from hands that were once so strong? There was none! The shadow of the white cloud had already fallen.

===Chinese===
这批建筑师于 1891 年 2 月聚齐。察看过场地——此时它在生翻腾掘的粗陋状态，委实够凄凉——一行人退下来，进入实质的会谈。约翰·鲁特不在。他实在来不了。一个月前，他已赴了他的约会。格雷斯兰（Graceland）如今是他的家。不久，他上将竖起一具凯尔特式十字架。路易斯深深想念他。如今谁来接起他在路上撂下的那些花剑——从那双曾经何等有力的手里？没有了！白云的阴影，已经落下来了。

===Original===
The meeting came to order. Richard Hunt, acknowledged dean of his profession, in the chair, Louis Sullivan acting as secretary. Burnham arose to make his address of welcome. He was not facile on his feet, but it soon became noticeable that he was progressively and grossly apologizing to the Eastern men for the presence of their benighted brethren of the West.

===Chinese===
会议开张。公认的行业泰斗理查德·亨特（Richard Hunt）任主席，路易斯·沙利文任记录。伯纳姆起身致欢迎辞。他并不善于站着讲话，但很快便可以听出，他正在没完没了、变本加厉地为西部那些蒙昧兄弟的在场，向东部诸公赔罪。

===Original===
Dick Hunt interrupted: “Hell, we haven’t come out here on a missionary expedition. Let’s get to work.” Everyone agreed. Burnham came out of his somnambulistic vagary and joined in. He was keen enough to understand that “Uncle Dick” had done him a needed favor. For Burnham learned slowly but surely, within the limits of his understanding.

A layout was submitted to the Board as a basis for discussion. It was rearranged on two axes at right angles. The buildings were disposed accordingly. By an amicable arrangement each architect was given such building as he preferred, after consultation. The meeting then adjourned.

===Chinese===
迪克·亨特打断了他：「见鬼，我们不是出来传教的。干活吧。」人人赞同。伯纳姆从他那梦游式的出神里醒了过来，也加入了进来。他够精明，懂得「迪克大叔」帮了他一个必要的忙。因为伯纳姆学得慢，但学得扎实——在他理解力的限度之内。

一份总平面图提交给委员会，作为讨论的基础。它被重新排布在两条正交的轴线上。各建筑依此布置。经商定，每位建筑师在磋商之后，各得其所选的建筑。会议随即散场。

===Original===
The story of the building of the Fair is foreign to the purpose of this narrative, which is to deal with its more serious aspects, implications and results. Suffice it that Burnham performed in a masterful way, displaying remarkable executive capacity. He became open-minded, just, magnanimous. He did his great share.

The work completed, the gates thrown open 1 May, 1893, the crowds flowed in from every quarter, continued to flow throughout a fair-weather summer and a serenely beautiful October. Then came the end. The gates were closed.

===Chinese===
建造博览会的故事，与本叙事的宗旨无关——这里要谈的，是它更严肃的方面、含义与后果。只需说一句：伯纳姆干得出色，显示出非凡的执行才干。他变得开明、公正、宽宏。他尽了他重大的一份。

工程告成，1893 年 5 月 1 日大门敞开，人流从四面八方涌来，流过一个大晴的夏天，又流过十月的一片澄美。然后到了尽头。大门关上了。

===Original===
These crowds were astonished. They beheld what was for them an amazing revelation of the architectural art, of which previously they in comparison had known nothing. To them it was a veritable Apocalypse, a message inspired from on high. Upon it their imagination shaped new ideals. They went away, spreading again over the land, returning to their homes, each one of them carrying in the soul the shadow of the white cloud, each of them permeated by the most subtle and slow-acting of poisons; an imperceptible miasm within the white shadow of a higher culture. A vast multitude, exposed, unprepared, they had not had time nor occasion to become immune to forms of sophistication not their own, to a higher and more dexterously insidious plausibility. Thus they departed joyously, carriers of contagion, unaware that what they had beheld and believed to be truth was to prove, in historic fact, an appalling calamity. For what they saw was not at all what they believed they saw, but an imposition of the spurious upon their eyesight, a naked exhibitionism of charlatanry in the higher feudal and domineering culture, conjoined with expert salesmanship of the materials of decay. Adventitiously, to make the stage setting complete, it happened by way of apparent but unreal contrast that the structure representing the United States Government was of an incredible vulgarity, while the building at the peak of the north axis, stationed there as a symbol of “The Great State of Illinois” matched it as a lewd exhibit of drooling imbecility and political debauchery. The distribution at the northern end of the grounds of many state and foreign headquarters relieved the sense of stark immensity. South of them, and placed on the border of a small lake, stood the Palace of the Arts, the most vitriolic of them all﻿—the most impudently thievish. The landscape work, in its genial distribution of lagoons, wooded islands, lawns, shrubbery and plantings, did much to soften an otherwise mechanical display; while far in the southeast corner, floating in a small lagoon or harbor, were replicas of the three caravels of Columbus, and on an adjacent artificial mound a representation of the Convent of La Rabida. Otherwhere there was no evidence of Columbus and his daring deed, his sufferings, and his melancholy end. No keynote, no dramatic setting forth of that deed which, recently, has aroused some discussion as to whether the discovery of America had proven to be a blessing or a curse to the world of mankind.

===Chinese===
这些人群惊呆了。他们看见的，对他们而言，是建筑艺术的一场惊人启示；相形之下，他们此前对它可谓一无所知。在他们眼里，这是一部真正的《启示录》（Apocalypse），一道自天而降的默示。在它之上，他们的想象力塑造出新的理想。他们四散而去，重新散布到大地上，各自还家，每个人灵魂里都携着白云的影子，每个人都被那最隐微、最缓性的毒浸透；一种在更高文化之白色阴影里、无从察觉的瘴气。一大群暴露于外、毫无准备的人，他们既无时间也无因由，对不属于他们的世故形态、对一种更高明更灵巧阴损的貌似有理，养成免疫。他们就这样欢天喜地地散去，人人都是带菌者，浑然不知他们目睹并信以为真的东西，在历史的事实上，将被证明是一场骇人的灾祸。因为他们看见的，根本不是他们以为自己看见的，而是一件赝品对视力的强加，是更高封建统治文化里江湖骗术的一场赤裸裸的炫示，外加对腐朽材料的老练推销。又凑巧，为把舞台布景凑齐，出现了一层貌似对比、其实不然的对照：代表合众国政府的那座建筑，俗得叫人难以置信；而立在北轴顶端、作为「伟大的伊利诺伊州」之象征的那座建筑，则以流涎的白痴相与政治堕落，与之旗鼓相当，成了一件下流的展品。场区北端散布的各州与各国馆部，冲淡了那种赤裸裸的庞大感。在它们南面、依着一泓小湖的边上，立着艺术宫（Palace of the Arts）——其中最刻毒的一座——最无耻偷艺的一座。园林景观以它和悦布置的潟湖、林木葱茏的岛屿、草坪、灌木与花木，大大柔化了这场本会机械呆板的陈列；而远远的东南角，浮在一泓小潟湖或者说小港里，是哥伦布三艘卡拉维尔帆船的复制品，近旁一座人工土丘上，是拉比达修道院（Convent of La Rabida）的模型。除此之外，再无哥伦布与他那大胆功业、他的苦难与忧郁下场的踪影。没有主旨音，没有对那桩功业的戏剧性铺陈——那桩功业，近来正引起一场讨论：美洲的发现，对人类世界究竟算是福，还是祸。

===Original===
Following the white cloud, even as a companion in iniquity, came the gray cloud. It overwhelmed the land with a pall of desolation. It dropped its blinding bolt. Its hurricane swept away the pyramided paper structures of speculation. Its downpour washed away fancied gains; its raindrops, loaded with a lethal toxin, fell alike upon the unjust and the just, as in retribution, demanding an atonement in human sacrifice. The thunder ceased to roll, the rain became a mist and cleared, the storm subsided, all was still. Overhead hung the gray cloud of panic from horizon to horizon. Slowly it thinned, in time it became translucent, vanished, revealing the white cloud which, in platoons, unseen, had overrun the blue. Now again shone the sun. “Prosperity” awakened from its torpor, rubbed its eyes and prepared for further follies.

===Chinese===
跟着白云，俨然一个为恶的同伙，来了灰云。它以一片荒凉之幕，覆盖了大地。它掷下它炫目的霹雳。它的飓风，扫荡了投机那纸糊的金字塔。它的骤雨，冲走了幻想的收益；它的雨点，载着致命的毒素，落在不义者身上与义人身上一般无二，俨然报应，索要一场以人为牺牲的赎罪。雷声止了，雨化作雾、又散了，风暴平息，万籁俱寂。头顶上，灰云自天际压到天际。它慢慢变薄，渐渐半透明，终于消隐，露出白云——它已成一列列纵队，无声无息地漫过了蓝天。太阳重又照耀。「繁荣」从麻木中醒来，揉揉眼睛，准备下一轮的蠢行。

===Original===
It is said that history repeats itself. This is not so. What is mistaken for repetition is the recurrent feudal rhythm of exaltation and despair. Its progressive wavelike movement in action is implicit in the feudal thought, and inevitable, and so long as the feudal thought holds dominion in the minds of men, just so long and no longer will calamity follow upon the appearance of prosperity. The end is insanity, the crumbling and the passing of the race, for life is ever saying to Man: “If you wish to be destroyed I will destroy you.” The white cloud is the feudal idea. The gray cloud, the nemesis contained within that idea. The feudal idea is dual, it holds to the concept of good and evil. The democratic idea is single, integral. It holds to the good alone. Its faith lies in the beneficence of its power, in its direct appeal to life. Its vision reveals an inspiring vista of accomplishment. Its common sense recognizes man as by nature sound to the core, and kindly. It as clearly sees, in the feudal scheme, a continuous warfare﻿—as well in so-called times of peace as in sanguinary battle. It views all this as lunacy, for its own word is kindness. It bases its faith upon the heart in preference to the intellect, though knowing well the power of the latter when controlled. It knows that the intellect, alone, runs amuck, and performs unspeakable cruelties; that the heart alone is divine. For it is the heart that welcomes Life and would cherish it, would shield it against the cannibalism of the intellect.

===Chinese===
人们说，历史会重演。并非如此。被误当作重演的，是那振奋与绝望交替复现的封建节奏。它波浪式递进的动势，隐含在封建思想之内，且不可避免；只要封建思想还在人的头脑中称霸，灾难随繁荣而至的日子就还有多久，便也只到多久为止。结局是疯狂，是这族群的碎裂与消亡；因为生命一直在对人说：「你若愿意被毁灭，我就毁灭你。」白云是封建观念。灰云，是该观念之内包藏的报应。封建观念是二元的，它抱着善与恶的概念不放。民主观念是单一而完整的。它只认善。它的信念，寄于其力量的仁善，寄于它对生命的直接吁求。它的远见，展开一片令人振奋的功业远景。它的常识认定，人天性核心健全而仁善。它同样清楚地看见，封建的图式里是一场不停的战争——在所谓和平年代，同在血腥战斗里一样。它视这一切为疯癫，因为它自己的言词是仁慈。它宁把信念安在心上，而不安在理智上，虽然它深知理智受控时的力量。它知道，理智独自撒起野来，会做出说不出口的残虐；唯独心是神圣的。因为是心欢迎生命、愿意珍爱生命，愿意护住生命，使它免遭理智的同类相食。

===Original===
From the height of its Columbian Ecstacy, Chicago drooped and subsided with the rest, in a common sickness, the nausea of overstimulation. This in turn passed, toward the end of the decade, and the old game began again with intensified fury, to come to a sudden halt in 1907. There are those who say this panic was artificial and deliberate, that the battle of the saber-toothed tigers and the mastodons was on.

===Chinese===
芝加哥从它哥伦比亚迷狂的高处，同其余各地一道萎顿、消沉下去，害着一场共同的病：过度刺激的恶心。这阵病到九十年代末也过去了，老戏重开，火气更凶，到 1907 年又戛然而止。有人说，那场恐慌是人为的、蓄意的，说剑齿虎与乳齿象之战，开打了。

===Original===
Meanwhile the virus of the World’s Fair, after a period of incubation in the architectural profession and in the population at large, especially the influential, began to show unmistakable signs of the nature of the contagion. There came a violent outbreak of the Classic and the Renaissance in the East, which slowly spread westward, contaminating all that it touched, both at its source and outward. The selling campaign of the bogus antique was remarkably well managed through skillful publicity and propaganda, by those who were first to see its commercial possibilities. The market was ripe, made so through the hebetude of the populace, big business men, and eminent educators alike. By the time the market had been saturated, all sense of reality was gone. In its place had come deep-seated illusions, hallucinations, absence of pupillary reaction to light, absence of knee-reaction﻿—symptoms all of progressive cerebral meningitis: The blanketing of the brain. Thus Architecture died in the land of the free and the home of the brave﻿—in a land declaring its fervid democracy, its inventiveness, its resourcefulness, its unique daring, enterprise and progress. Thus did the virus of a culture, snobbish and alien to the land, perform its work of disintegration; and thus ever works the pallid academic mind, denying the real, exalting the fictitious and the false, incapable of adjusting itself to the flow of living things, to the reality and the pathos of man’s follies, to the valiant hope that ever causes him to aspire, and again to aspire; that never lifts a hand in aid because it cannot; that turns its back upon man because that is its tradition; a culture lost in ghostly mésalliance with abstractions, when what the world needs is courage, common sense and human sympathy, and a moral standard that is plain, valid and livable.

===Chinese===
与此同时，世界博览会的病毒，在建筑业界与广大民众、尤其有影响的人士中间潜伏了一阵之后，开始显出此传染不容误认的本性。东部爆发了一场猛烈的古典主义与文艺复兴热，缓缓西渐，所触之处无不污染，源头内外皆然。假古董的推销战，经熟练的造势与宣传，经营得异常出色，操盘者正是头一批看出其中商机的人。市场熟了——是民众、大商人兼显赫教育家们共同的迟钝，把它催熟的。待到市场饱和，现实感已荡然无存。取而代之的，是根深蒂固的错觉、幻觉，是对光瞳孔反应的消失、膝反射的消失——全是进行性脑膜炎的症候：大脑的被蒙覆。建筑，就这样死在了自由之土、勇士之乡——死在一片高唱其炽烈民主、其创造发明、其足智多谋、其独一无二的胆魄、进取与进步的土地上。一种势利的、与这片土地格格不入的文化病毒，就这样完成了它瓦解的功课；那苍白的学院心智，素来如此运作：否认实在，抬高虚构与虚假，无力调整自己以适应活物的流变，适应人的愚行之实在与哀婉，适应那令他一而再、再而三奋起向上的英勇希望；它从不伸手援助，因为它不能；它背对着人，因为那是它的传统；一种迷失在与抽象之物幽灵般错配联姻中的文化——而这世界需要的，是勇气、常识与人的同情，以及一套明白、有效、可依之而活的道德标准。

===Original===
The damage wrought by the World’s Fair will last for half a century from its date, if not longer. It has penetrated deep into the constitution of the American mind, effecting there lesions significant of dementia.

===Chinese===
世界博览会造成的损害，自其开幕之日起，将持续半个世纪，甚至更久。它已深深透入美国心智的体质，在那里造成了意味痴呆的病灶。

===Original===
Meanwhile the architectural generation immediately succeeding the Classic and Renaissance merchants, are seeking to secure a special immunity from the inroads of common sense, through a process of vaccination with the lymph of every known European style, period and accident, and to this all-around process, when it breaks out, is to be added the benediction of good taste. Thus we have now the abounding freedom of Eclecticism, the winning smile of taste, but no architecture. For Architecture, be it known, is dead. Let us therefore lightly dance upon its grave, strewing roses as we glide. Indeed let us gather, in procession, in the night, in the rain, and make soulful, fluent, epicene orations to the living dead we neuters eulogize.

===Chinese===
与此同时，紧接古典主义与文艺复兴商贩之后的那一代建筑师，正谋求以一套接种法，确保对常识入侵的特殊免疫：用每一种已知的欧洲风格、时期与变故的淋巴液接种；待这全方位的接种发作起来，还要再加上品味的祝福。于是，我们如今有了折衷主义（Eclecticism）横流的自由、品味动人的微笑，唯独没有建筑。因为建筑——须知——已经死了。那就让我们在它坟上轻轻起舞，滑行之际撒下玫瑰。真的，让我们列队聚来，在夜里，在雨中，向我们这些中性人所歌颂的活死人，发表深情、流畅、不男不女的悼词。

===Original===
Surely the profession has made marvelous improvements in trade methods, over the old-fashioned way. There is now a dazzling display of merchandise, all imported, excepting to be sure our own cherished colonial, which maintains our Anglo-Saxon tradition in its purity. We have Tudor for colleges and residences; Roman for banks, and railway stations and libraries﻿—or Greek if you like﻿—some customers prefer the Ionic to the Doric. We have French, English and Italian Gothic, Classic and Renaissance for churches. In fact we are prepared to satisfy, in any manner of taste. Residences we offer in Italian or Louis Quinze. We make a small charge for alterations and adaptations. Our service we guarantee as exceptional and exclusive. Our importations are direct. We have our own agents abroad. We maintain also a commercial department, in which a selective taste is not so necessary. Its province is to solve engineering problems of all kinds, matters of cost, income, maintenance, taxes, renewals, depreciation, obsolescence; and as well maintenance of contact, sales pressure, sales resistance, flotations, and further matters of the sort. We maintain also an industrial department in which leading critics unite in saying we have made most significant departures in design. These structures however, are apart from our fashionable trade. Our business is founded and maintained on an ideal service, and a part of that service we believe to consist in an elevation of the public taste, a setting forth of the true standards of design, in pure form, a system of education by example, the gradual formation of a background of culture for the masses. In this endeavor we have the generous support of the architectural schools, of the colleges and universities, of men of wealth, and of those whose perspicacity has carried them to the pinnacle of eminence in finance, industry, commerce, education and statesmanship. Therefore we feel that we are in thorough accord with the spirit of our times as expressed in its activities, in its broad democratic tolerance, and its ever-youthful enthusiasms. It is this sense of solidity, solidarity and security that makes us bold, inspires us with the high courage to continue in our self-imposed task. We look for our reward solely in the conviction of duty done; our profound belief that we are preparing the way for the coming generation through the power of our example, our counsel and our teachings, to the end that they may express, better than we ourselves have done, the deep, the sincere, the wholesome aspirations of our people and of our land, as yet not fully articulated by the higher culture, in spite of our best efforts toward that end. This task we are quite aware we must eventually leave to the young who are crowding upon us, and we wish them joy in their great adventure when we relinquish our all.

===Chinese===
诚然，本行业在生意手法上，有了长足的改进，远胜旧式的一套。如今是琳琅满目的商品陈设，一概进口——自然，除了我们自家珍爱的殖民地式，它以纯粹，维系着我们盎格鲁-撒克逊的传统。大学与宅邸，我们有都铎式；银行、车站与图书馆，我们有罗马式——您若乐意，也可以希腊式——有些主顾偏爱爱奥尼，胜过多立克。教堂嘛，法式、英式、意大利式哥特，古典式与文艺复兴式，应有尽有。事实上，无论何种品味，我们都备妥了让您满意。宅邸，我们供意大利式或路易十五式（Louis Quinze）。改制与变通，只收薄费。我们的服务，保证非凡而独到。我们的进口是第一手的。我们在国外自有代理。我们还设有一个商业部，那厢对品味的选择，就不那么苛求了。它的职分，是解决各类工程问题，以及造价、收益、维护、税负、翻新、折旧、报废诸事；还有客户维系、销售压力、销售阻力、证券发行，以及进一步的同类事项。我们还设有一个工业部，领头的批评家们异口同声说，我们在设计上做出了最意义重大的创新。不过这类建筑，不在我们时髦生意之列。我们这门生意，建立在并维持于一种理想的服务之上；这服务的一部分，我们相信，在于提升公众的品味，以纯粹的形式昭示设计的真标准，一套以身作则的教育制度，为大众渐次培养出一层文化的底子。在这番事业上，我们得到建筑院校、各大学院与大学、富有之士，以及那些以洞见登临金融、工业、商业、教育与治国之术荣誉之巅者的慷慨支持。因此，我们自觉与时代的精神深相契合——契合于它的种种活动，它宽泛的民主宽容，它常青的热情。正是这种坚实、团结与安稳之感，使我们胆壮，激发我们高昂的勇气，去继续这自任的任务。我们的报偿，只求之于尽责的信念；求之于我们深沉的信仰：我们正以我们榜样的力量、我们的进言与我们的教诲，为下一代开路，愿他们把我们人民与我们土地的深沉、真挚而健全的渴望，表达得比我们自己更好——这些渴望，尽管我们已朝此目标竭尽全力，至今尚未被更高的文化充分道出。这项任务，我们心知肚明，终须交卸给正拥上来的年轻人；当我们撒手交出我们的一切时，祝他们在这场大冒险里尽兴。

===Original===
In the better aspects of eclecticism and taste, that is to say, in those aspects which reveal a certain depth of artistic feeling and a physical sense of materials, rather than mere scene-painting or archaeology, however clever, there is to be discovered a hope and a forecast. For it is within the range of possibilities, one may even go so far as to say probabilities, that out of the very richness and multiplicity of the architectural phenomena called “styles” there may arise within the architectural mind a perception growing slowly, perhaps suddenly, into clearness, that architecture in its material nature and in its animating essence is a *plastic art*. This truth, so long resisted because of the limited intellectual boundaries and deficient sympathy of academic training, must eventually prevail because founded upon a culture of common sense and human recognition. Its power is as gentle and as irresistible as that of the Springtime﻿—to which it may be likened, or to sunrise following the night and its stars, and herein lies beneath the surface and even on the surface the inspiration of our High Optimism, with its unceasing faith in man as free spirit! as creator, possessed of a physical sense indistinguishable from the spiritual, and of innate plastic powers whose fecundity and beneficence surpass our present scope of imagination. Dogma and rule of the dead are passing. The Great *Modern* Inversion, for which the world of mankind has been preparing purblindly through the ages, is now under way in its worldwide awakening. The thought of the multitudes is changing, withdrawing its consent, its acquiescence; the dream of the multitudes is metamorphosing, philosophy is becoming human and immersing itself in the flow of life; science is pushing the spectres back into the invisible whence they came. The world is in travail, smeared with blood, amid the glint of bayonets; the feudal idea has reached the pitch of its insanity, yet by the way of compensation the veils are lifting rapidly, all the veils of hypocrisy and sinister intent, all the veils of plausible, insidious speech, of propaganda, of perfidy, of betrayal. It requires courage to remain steadfast in faith in the presence of such pollution. Yet it is precisely such courage that marks man in his power as free spirit. For beneath this corruption the enlightened one perceives the everlasting aspirations of mankind, the ever-yearning heart in its search for kindness, peace and a safe anchorage within its world, and to such, the compassionate one gives out words of encouragement and prophecy, even as the gray clouds hover from horizon to horizon; a prophecy that this cloud shall melt away, and reveal aloft a shining white cloud, in the blue, announcing the new man and the new culture of faith.

===Chinese===
而在折衷主义与品味较好的一面——就是说，在那些显出几分艺术情感的深度、几分对材料的身体之感，而不只是布景画或考古学（再灵巧也算不得数）的一面——之中，可以寻到一份希望与一个预告。因为这在可能性的范围之内——甚至不妨说，在或然性的范围之内：从「诸风格」这一建筑现象的丰饶与纷繁之中，建筑的心智里可能会生出一种识见，慢慢生长，或者突然，臻于澄明：建筑在其物质性质与其赋予生机的本质上，是一门*造型艺术*（plastic art）。这条真理，因学院训练的智识边界狭隘、同情不足，而被长期抵制，终将获胜，因为它建立在一种以常识与对人的认取为本的文化上。它的力量，温柔而不可抗拒，一如春光——不妨如此比方，或如夜与群星之后的日出；而就在这底下，甚或就在这表面，躺着我们高扬的乐观主义的灵感——它对人作为自由精神（free spirit），怀有不止息的信念！作为创造者，拥有一种与精神之感无从分辨的身体之感，拥有天赋的造型之力，其丰饶与仁善，超出我们眼下想象的疆域。死者的教条与规条正在过去。伟大的*现代*大倒转——人类世界历世历代半盲半明地为它做着准备——如今，正在它全球范围的苏醒之中进行。民众的思想正在改变，正在收回它的同意、它的默许；民众之梦正在蜕变；哲学正变得属人，正把自己浸入生命的流变；科学正把幽灵们，赶回它们所自来的不可见之乡。世界正在阵痛之中，浑身血污，四顾刺刀的寒光；封建观念已达其疯狂的顶点；然而作为补偿，重重帷幕正迅速掀起——一切伪善与阴图的帷幕，一切貌似有理、阴柔入骨的言辞、宣传、背信与背叛的帷幕。身处这样的污浊，而信念坚定不移，需要勇气。然而，恰恰是这样的勇气，标示出处于其力量之中、作为自由精神的人。因为在这腐败之下，受启明者看见人类不朽的渴望，看见那永远渴慕的心，在寻索仁慈、和平，与它世界之内一个安全的锚地；对这样的人，悲悯者道出鼓励与预言的话语，纵使灰云仍从天际压到天际；预言这云终将消散，高天显出一朵闪耀的白云，在蔚蓝之中，宣告新的人，与新的信仰文化。

===Original===
It seems fitting, therefore, that this work should close with the same child-dream in which it began. The dream of a beauteous, beneficent power, which came when, winter past, the orchards burst into bloom, and the song of spring was heard in the land.

That dream has never ceased. That faith has never wearied. With the passage of the years, the dream, the faith, ever expanding in power, became all-inclusive; and with the progress of the dream and the faith, there emerged in confirmation a vague outline, growing year after year more luminous and clear. When the golden hour tolled, all mists departed, and there shone forth as in a vision, the reality of **man**, as Free Spirit, as Creator, as Container of illimitable powers, for the joy and the peace of mankind.

===Chinese===
因此，这部书以它开篇时的同一个孩童之梦来收束，看来是相宜的。那是一个美好而仁善之力的梦；当冬天过去，果园绽满花朵，大地上响起春之歌时，它来了。

那梦从未止息。那信念从未倦怠。随着岁月的推移，梦与信念在力量中不断扩涨，终至无所不包；而随着梦与信念的进展，有一道模糊的轮廓，确证般浮现，一年比一年更明亮、更清晰。当金色的时辰鸣响，一切雾霭散去，如异象般照耀而出的，是**人**的实在——作为自由精神，作为创造者，作为无限力量的容器——为了人类的喜乐，与安宁。

===Original===
It was this unseen nearby presence, messenger of Life in its flowing, that sang its song of spring to the child, and the child heard what no one heard; the child saw what no one saw.

===Chinese===
正是这近旁看不见的临在——生命在其流变中的信使——向那孩子唱了它的春之歌；孩子听见了无人听见的，孩子看见了无人看见的。

===Original===
It is questionable how much of social value one who has had access to the treasures of the past, access to the best and the worst in the thought of his day, may leave behind him in his fruitage, as a quantum﻿—an **idea**.

This narrator agrees, in such connection, that the initial instinct of the child, as set forth, is the basis of all fruitful ideas, and that the growth in power of such ideas is in itself a work of instinct; that, if it has been convincingly shown that instinct is primary and intellect secondary in all the great works of man, this portrayal is justified.

It is further the belief of this narrator, in this connection, that if he has succeeded in setting clearly forth the basic fruitful power of the **idea** permeating and dominating this narrative of a life-experience, physical and spiritual, he has done well in thus making a record in words to be pondered in the heart.

===Chinese===
一个曾得以亲近往昔的宝藏、得以亲近他时代思想之最好与最坏的人，在他的果实里，能为身后留下多少社会价值，作为一个份额——一个**观念**——是可疑的。

关于此点，本叙述者同意：如前所述的孩子最初的本能，是一切丰硕观念的基础；而这类观念在力量上的增长，本身就是一项本能的功业；倘若已令人信服地表明，在人的一切伟大功业中，本能居首、理智居次，那么，这幅写照便是正当的。

本叙述者更进一步相信：倘若他已成功地把那渗透并支配着这段身心生命历程叙事的**观念**之丰饶基础力量，清楚铺陈出来，那么，他以此用文字做成一份可供心中反复思量的记录，便算是做得好了。

## I The Child / 一　孩子

===Original===

Once upon a time there was a village in New England called South Reading. Here lived a little boy of five years. That is to say he nested with his grandparents on a miniature farm of twenty-four acres, a mile or so removed from the center of gravity and activity which was called Main Street. It was a main street of the day and generation, and so was the farm proper to its time and place.

===Chinese===

从前，新英格兰有个村子，叫南雷丁（South Reading）。村里住着一个五岁的小男孩。也就是说，他栖身于外祖父外祖母处，在那座 24 英亩的微型农场上；农场离那被称为「主街」（Main Street）的重心与活力之所，约莫一英里开外。那是一条属于那个时代、那一代人的主街；而这座农场，也恰是与它的时代和境地相称的农场。

===Original===

Eagerly the grandparents had for some time urged that the child come to them for a while; and after a light shower of mother tears﻿—the father indifferent﻿—consent was given and the child was taken on his way into the wilderness lying ten miles north of the city of Boston. The farm had been but recently acquired, and the child appeared, shortly thereafter, as a greedy parasite, to absorb that affection, that abundant warmth of heart which only Grandma and Grandpa have the intuitive folly to bestow. In short they loved him, and kept him bodily clean.

To the neighbors, he was merely another brat-nuisance to run about and laugh and scream and fight and bawl with the others﻿—all bent on joy and destruction. The peculiar kink in this little man’s brain, however, was this: he had no desire to destroy﻿—except always his momentary mortal enemies. His bent was the other way.

===Chinese===

外祖父外祖母热切地悬望了些时日，盼这孩子来他们家住上一阵；随后，在母亲一场轻疏的泪雨之后——父亲漠然置之——首肯给出，孩子便被送上了路，送进波士顿城以北十英里的那片荒野。农场是新近才置下的，此后不久，孩子便登场了，俨然一个贪馋的寄食者，来吮吸那份疼爱、那份心肠里丰沛的暖意——那样的疼爱，唯有外祖母和外祖父才有那份出于直觉的痴愚，肯倾心施与。总而言之，他们爱他，并把他身子拾掇得干干净净。

在邻里眼里，他不过是又一个讨厌的小捣蛋，同别的孩子一道跑来跑去、大笑、尖叫、打架、嚎哭——一个个全都心往欢乐与破坏。然而这个小家伙脑瓜里那一处特别的弯却是：他毫无破坏之欲——唯一的例外，永远是那些转瞬即逝的死敌。他的天性偏向另一头。

===Original===

Now lest it appear that this child had come suddenly out of nothing into being at the age of five, we must needs authenticate him by sketching his prior tumultuous life. He was born of woman in the usual way at 22 South Bennett Street, Boston, Mass., U.S.A., on the third day of September, 1856. And, for the benefit of the exigent and meticulous, it may be added, on the authority of the young mother, that the event occurred on the second floor: day Tuesday, hour 10 p.m., weight ten pounds. The mother, at that date, had arrived at the age of twenty-one years, while the father would be thirty-eight come Christmas.

The long interval of passing years has made it clear that this pink monstrosity came into the world possessed of a picture-memory. He remembers, even now, certain cradle indiscretions; and from that same cradle he recalls a dim vision of a ghostly lady in somber black, and veiled, entering through the open door and speaking in a voice strangely unlike the mellow tones of his nearby mother. He remembers that one night in midwinter, he was lifted from his warm cozy refuge, bundled up and taken to the third floor. Grandpa was already there, scraping the heavy frost from one of the small square window panes; finally, after the ecstasies of Mama and the awed tones of Grandpa, the child was lifted up and held close to the pane to see what?﻿—a long brilliant, cloud-like streak, which, he dimly fancied, must be unusual; but as it seemed to have no connection with the important concerns of his existence, he was glad to leave it to itself, whatever it was, and return to the warm spot from which he had been taken. This streak in the sky was Donati’s comet of 1858.

Before going further into the doings of this two-year-old, it may be well to give an outline of his mongrel origin.

===Chinese===

且说，为了不让人以为这孩子是五岁上凭空从虚无里一下子冒出来的，我们必得为他验明正身，把他此前那段动荡的生涯略述一个轮廓。他照常人之道由妇人而生，地点是美国马萨诸塞州波士顿南贝内特街 22 号（22 South Bennett Street），时在 1856 年 9 月 3 日。为那些苛刻讲究的人着想，还可以依年轻母亲的权威补上一句：此事发生在二楼；星期二，晚上 10 点，体重十磅。彼时母亲年方二十一岁，而父亲到圣诞节就满三十八了。

悠悠岁月的长长间隔已然表明，这个粉红色的怪物来到世上时，便天生怀有一种图像记忆（picture-memory）。直到如今，他还记得摇篮里的几桩失检小节；也是从那同一只摇篮里，他追忆起一个依稀的幻影：一位幽灵似的女士，一身素黑，蒙着面纱，从敞开的门里进来，说话的嗓音同近旁母亲那温润的声调出奇地不像。他记得一个仲冬之夜，他被从温暖舒适的窝里抱起来，裹得严严实实，抱到三楼。外祖父已经在那里了，正刮着一方小方格窗玻璃上厚厚的霜；末了，在妈妈的欢腾与外祖父敬畏的语声之后，孩子被举起来，贴近玻璃，看什么？——一道长长的、明亮的、云朵似的光带；他依稀觉得，这东西必定异乎寻常；但既然它似乎同他生存里的重大关切毫无干系，他也就乐得随它去——管它是什么——径自回到方才被抱离的那个暖处去了。天上的这道光带，是 1858 年的多纳蒂彗星（Donati's comet）。

在进一步叙述这个两岁小人的行状之前，不妨先把他那驳杂的家世源头勾一个轮廓。

===Original===

As to his father, Patrick Sullivan, no need for discussion﻿—he was Irish. As to the mother, Andrienne List Sullivan, she seemed French, but was not wholly so. She had the typical eyelids, expressive hazel eyes, an oval face, features mobile. She was a medium stature, trimly built, highly emotional, and given to ecstasies of speech. But *she also* had parents: her father, Henri List, was straight German of the Hanoverian type﻿—six feet tall, well proportioned, erect carriage, and topped by a domical head, full, clean-shaven face, thick lips, small gray eyes, beetling brows and bottle-nose. He was of intellectual mold, and cynically amused at men, women, children and all else. Her mother, a miniature woman of great sweetness and gentle poise, was Swiss-French, born in Geneva﻿—where also her three children were born. But her long Florentine nose suggested, unmistakably, an Italian strain. Her maiden name was Anna Mattheus. Like a true *mère de famille*, she ruled the roost, as was the custom in French society of the Middle Class. Her mind was methodic, her affection all-embracing.

===Chinese===

先说父亲帕特里克·沙利文（Patrick Sullivan），不必讨论——他是爱尔兰人。母亲安德丽安·李斯特·沙利文（Andrienne List Sullivan）呢，看着像法国人，却又不全是。她生着那种典型的眼睑，一双富于表情的淡褐色眼睛，一张鹅蛋脸，五官灵动。她中等身材，形体利落，情感炽烈，说起话来惯会忘情。可*她*也有父母：她的父亲亨利·李斯特（Henri List），是个不折不扣的汉诺威型德国人——六英尺高，比例匀称，腰背笔挺，头顶穹顶似的头颅，脸庞丰满，剃得干净，厚嘴唇，灰色的小眼睛，浓眉压目，外加一只酒糟鼻。他是智识的坯子，对男人、女人、孩子乃至世间一切，一律报以冷嘲的莞尔。她的母亲则是个小巧的女人，极为温婉娴静；她是瑞士裔法国人，生在日内瓦——她的三个孩子也都生在那里。但她那长长的佛罗伦萨式鼻子，明白无误地暗示着一份意大利血统。她的闺名是安娜·马特乌斯（Anna Mattheus）。像一位真正的*一家主母*（mère de famille），她执掌着家宅——这正是法国中产社会的习俗。她心思井然有序，慈爱无所不包。

===Original===

Henri List was reticent as to his past, but the family gossip had it that as a young man he was educated for the Catholic priesthood, rebelled at the job and ran away from home.

The intervening years between this hegira and his arrival in Geneva, Switzerland, are a blank. There seems to have been some lack of clearness as to his vocation in Geneva; was he a Professor of Greek in the University, or did he coach rich young English gentlemen through their university course? In any event he was highly educated, and he prospered. It was further gossiped that, having met Anna Mattheus﻿—considerably older than he﻿—who kept a store filled with a sumptuous stock of choice linens and laces, he courted her. It was sneeringly said he married her for her money. At any rate, they were well to do, and lived in a marble house with large grounds called La Maison des Paquis. Here three children were born to them, in order of arrival: Andrienne, Jennie and Jules. The narrator has in his possession a small oval card with perforated edge, on the plain field of which is drawn with colored pencils, a park-like view, with house half-hidden among the trees. On the back, in the handwriting of her mother, is the notation “*Terrace de la Maison des Paquis faites par Andrienne en 1849*”﻿—(that is at the age of fourteen). According also to family gossip, there seems to be no doubt that Henri List was tainted with cupidity. He speculated and finally lent ear to the wiles of a Jew. He ventured his all. The enterprise strangely and suddenly lost its credit, and the house of List tottered and collapsed in irretrievable ruin. Anna List borrowed money of her relatives to take the family to America, to forget the past and start anew in a strange land. Little wonder that Grandfather was reticent. It required a span of years for the narrator to pick up little by little the thread of the story.

===Chinese===

亨利·李斯特对自己的过去讳莫如深，但家人的闲话说，他年轻时受的培养是要当天主教神父，他却对这份差事反了骨，离家出走了。

从那次出走到他抵达瑞士日内瓦之间，隔着一片空白。他在日内瓦营生如何，似乎也不甚分明：他究竟是大学里的希腊语教授，还是专给有钱的英国青年绅士辅导大学课业？无论如何，他受过高等教育，而且发达了。家里的闲话又传说，他遇上了安娜·马特乌斯——比他年长不少——她开着一爿店，满架华贵的上等亚麻与蕾丝；他便向她献起殷勤。有人撇着嘴说，他娶她是图她的钱。无论如何，他们家境殷实，住进一座带大片园邸的大理石宅子，名叫帕基公馆（La Maison des Paquis）。他们的三个孩子相继在此降生，依先后为序：安德丽安、珍妮（Jennie）和朱尔（Jules）。叙述者手中存着一张椭圆的小卡片，边缘打着孔眼，素净的卡面上用彩色铅笔画着一幅园景，宅子半掩在树木中间。背面有她母亲手书的一行字：「*Terrace de la Maison des Paquis faites par Andrienne en 1849*」（帕基公馆露台，安德丽安作于 1849）——（那年她十四岁）。同样据家里闲话说，亨利·李斯特染有贪财之病，这一点似乎毋庸置疑。他做起了投机，末了听信了一个犹太人的花巧。他把全部家当都押了上去。那家企业离奇而突然地失了信用，李斯特门第摇摇欲坠，倾覆于无可挽回的败亡。安娜·李斯特向亲戚们借了钱，带全家远赴美洲，好忘掉过去，在一片陌生的土地上重新起步。难怪外祖父讳莫如深。叙述者费了好些年头，才一点一点把这故事的线头拾起来。

===Original===

As to Patrick Sullivan; he had no secrets, but his memory did not extend much back of his twelfth year. He said his father was a landscape painter, a widower, and he an only child. That together they used to visit the county fairs in Ireland. That at one of these fairs he lost his father in the crowd and never saw him again. Thus at the age of twelve he was thrown upon the world to make his way. With a curious little fiddle, he wandered barefoot about the countryside, to fiddle here and there for those who wished to dance; and of dancing there was plenty. Thus traveling he saw nearly all of Ireland. This wandering life must have covered a number of years. The period that emerges from the wander-period seems obscure in transition, but his attention must have focused on dancing as an art. As to the grim determination of his character, his pride and his ambition, there can be no doubt; but what chain of influences took him to London is not known. Arrived there, he placed himself under the tutelage of the best﻿—most fashionable﻿—masters, and in due time set up an academy of his own. Not content with this advance, which was successful, he must needs reach the heights of his art, and in Paris, the center of fashion, took instruction of the leading masters. In those days dancing was a social art of grace, of deportment, and of personal carriage. It had many branches of development, from the simple polka to highly figurative formations, in social functions, upward to its highest and most poetic reach in the romantic classical ballet. It was an art of elegance that has passed with the days of elegance. Artificial it largely was, yet humanizing, and beneficent. In such wise must the social value of the dance, of the dancing master, and the academy of a day long since past be visualized, to be understood in this day.

===Chinese===

再说帕特里克·沙利文；他没有秘密，只是他的记忆延伸不过十二岁之前多少年。他说他父亲是个风景画家，丧了偶，而他是独子。说父子俩从前常去逛爱尔兰的郡集市。说在其中一次集市上，他在人群里走失了父亲，从此再没见过他。就这样，十二岁上，他被抛到了世上，自谋生路。带着一把古灵精怪的小提琴，他赤着脚在乡野间流浪，东一处西一处，给想跳舞的人们拉琴；而跳舞的事，从来不缺。就这样一路行去，他几乎看遍了整个爱尔兰。这段漂泊生涯想必绵延了不少年头。从漂泊期里浮出的那段岁月，过渡处显得混沌，但他的心思想必已聚焦到舞蹈这门艺术上了。至于他性子里冷峻的决心、他的自尊与他的抱负，那是无可怀疑的；然而是哪一串影响把他带到了伦敦，则无人知晓。到了那里，他投身于最好——最时髦——的名师门下受教，时机一到，便开办起自己的学堂。这一步成功了，他却不以此为餍足，非要攀到他这门艺术的高峰不可，于是在时尚之都巴黎，又受教于一流名师。在那个年代，舞蹈是一门社交的艺术：关乎风致，关乎仪态，关乎个人的举止身段。它有许多发展的支脉，从简单的波尔卡，到社交场合里高度图案化的队形变舞，一路向上，直至浪漫古典芭蕾那最高、最富诗意的境界。那是一门优雅的艺术，已随优雅的岁月一同逝去。它大体上是矫饰的，却也陶冶人心，且有益于人。舞蹈、舞蹈教师和久已逝去的那个年代的学堂，其社会价值必须如此来构想，方能于今日为人理解。

===Original===

This young Irishman had another grand passion. To him the art of dancing was a fine art of symmetry, of grace, of rhythm; but parallel to this ran a hunger for Nature’s beauty. He must have been a pagan, this man, for in him Nature’s beauty, particularly in its more grandiose moods, inspired an ecstasy, a sort of waking trance, a glorious mystic worship. In this romantic quest, he had, through a series of years, footed it over a considerable part of Switzerland.

It seems strange at first glance that these highly virile and sensitive powers should be embodied in one so unlovely in person. His medium size, his too-sloping shoulders, his excessive Irish face, his small repulsive eyes﻿—the eyes of a pig﻿—of nondescript color and no flash, sunk into his head under rough brows, all seemed unpromising enough in themselves until it is remembered that behind that same mask resided the grim will, the instinctive ambition that had brought him, alone and unaided, out of a childhood of poverty.

Naturally enough he had not found time to acquire an “education,” as it was then called and is still called. He, however, wrote and spoke English in a polite way, and had acquired an excruciating French. Hence by the standards of his time in England he was no gentleman as that technical term went, but essentially a lackey, a flunkey or social parasite. Perhaps it was for this reason he revered book-learning and the learned. He knew no better.

===Chinese===

这个年轻的爱尔兰人还有另一桩盛大的热恋。于他，舞蹈这门艺术是关乎对称、风致与韵律的美术；可与之并行的，还有一份对自然之美的饥渴。这个人想必是个异教徒，因为自然之美——尤其是它那些更为宏丽的气象——在他心中激起的，是一种狂喜，一种清醒的出神，一场辉煌而神秘的崇拜。在这场浪漫的追寻里，他曾一连数年，徒步走过了瑞士相当大的一片土地。

乍看之下似乎奇怪：这般阳刚而敏锐的才具，竟寓于一个仪表如此不入眼的人身上。中等个头，双肩过于溜斜，一张爱尔兰味过重的脸，一双招人嫌的小眼睛——猪的眼睛——颜色说不清道不明，也毫无神采，深陷在粗硬的眉毛底下；这一切本身看来都够没出息的了——直到人想起，那副面具背后住着冷峻的意志，住着那股把他孤身一人、无依无傍地从贫苦童年里带出来的、出于本能的抱负。

自然而然，他无暇去求得那种「教育」——当时这么叫，如今也还这么叫。不过，他英文写得说得不失斯文，还学得一口叫人牙酸的法语。因此，按他那个时代英国的标准——照那个行话的说法——他算不得绅士，本质上是个仆役、跟班，或者说社交寄生者。也许正为了这个缘故，他才敬重书本学问和有学问的人。他原也不懂别的。

===Original===

It is probable that, about this time, the lure of America, goal of the adventurous spirit, the great hospitable, open-armed land of equality and opportunity, had been acting on his imagination. This is surmise. The fact, of which there is documentary evidence, is this: that on the 22nd day of July, 1847, he took passage at London for Boston in the good ship *Unicorn* of 550 tons register burden. This, in the eleventh year of the reign of Victoria; Louis Philippe nearing his political end; with revolution ripening in Germany; and the United States kindly relieving Mexico of its too heavy burden. And this, also, while a small prosperous family in a small European city was awaiting, all unconscious, the call to join him in the same city of the same far away land; and that but eleven brief years lay between them all and the advent of a child to whose story we must now begin a return. For the finger of fate was tracing a line in the air that was to lead on and on until it reached a finger tracing a line now and here.

===Chinese===

大约就在这个时候，美洲的诱惑——冒险精神的目标，那片伟大的、好客的、敞开怀抱的平等与机遇之邦——想必早已在他想象里发动了。这是揣测。有文献为凭的事实是：1847 年 7 月 22 日，他在伦敦搭船前往波士顿，乘的是那艘注册吨位 550 吨的好船「独角兽」号（*Unicorn*）。此时，维多利亚在位第十一年；路易·菲利普的政治末日将临；革命正在德国酝酿成熟；而美利坚合众国正好心地为墨西哥卸下它那过于沉重的负担。也正是在此时，一座欧洲小城里的一户小康人家，浑然不觉地等候着召唤，要到那同一片遥远土地上的同一座城里去与他相聚；而他们全体与一个孩子的降临之间，只隔着短短十一年——那个孩子的故事，我们如今必须开始回身讲起了。因为命运的手指正在空中描画一条线，那条线将一路引下去、引下去，直到触到另一根手指——一根此时此地正在描画着一条线的手指。

===Original===

Patrick Sullivan reached Boston in 1847, set up an academy and was successful. He always was successful. His probity was such that he could always command desirable influence and respect. He was familiar with polite forms. Later on, probably in 1850, the Geneva family also reached Boston. Somehow they met. The young Irishman, keen through training in the hard school of experience and self discipline was always wide awake; and this is what happened; he met the young girl, Andrienne, in the conventional way, was attracted by her grace of manner, her interesting broken English, her skilled piano playing; paid his court to her, professed love for her; they became engaged, and on the 14th of August, 1852, they were married. What is more likely is this; that he heard her playing of Chopin, Beethoven, et al., with approval, for he was fond of music; that he asked her to substitute dance music; that after the first few bars he was electrized﻿—he had found a jewel without price. Her sense of rhythm, of sweep, of accent, of the dance-cadence with its reinforcements and languishments, the *tempo rubato*﻿—was genius itself. He lost no time in marrying her as a business asset. She was lovable and he may have loved her. It is possible but hardly probable; for there is nothing in the record to show that he loved others, or that he loved himself. He was merely self-centered﻿—not even cold. He was moderate of habit; drank a little wine, smoked an occasional cigar, and was an enthusiast regarding hygiene. The stage-setting augured well for the coming child. The stock was sound. All the tribe were black-haired. So he came to pay his visit in due time, as recorded, believed by his mother to be an angel from Heaven, so great, so illusioning is the Mother-passion. But, as regarded from the viewpoint of the chronicler, he was not an angel from heaven. At the age of two he had developed temper, strong will, and obstinacy. He became at times a veritable howling dervish. He bawled, he shrieked, he blubbered, sobbed, whined and whimpered. He seemed to be obsessed by fixed ideas. Once in a while, as time passed, there came periods of relative calm within the pervading tempest, and now and then he was not wholly unlovable. A rising sun seemed to be dawning within him. He became interested in his bath, given daily in a movable tub. Grandma would allow none but herself to perform this rite, and as she sponged him down, he would sing to her something about *Marlbrouck s’en va-t’en guerre*, or tell what this giant did, or that fairy. Life was beginning to break in upon him from outside. A continuous breaking in from the outside and breaking out from the inside was to shape his destiny.

===Chinese===

帕特里克·沙利文于 1847 年抵达波士顿，开办了一所学堂，一炮而响。他从来都是成功的。他为人如此方正，总能博得可心的影响力与敬重。他熟谙种种斯文礼数。后来，大约在 1850 年，日内瓦那一家人也到了波士顿。不知怎么，两下里碰上了。这个年轻的爱尔兰人，经过经验与自律那所严酷学堂的磨砺，素来警醒；接下来发生的是这样：他以寻常的方式结识了年轻姑娘安德丽安，被她优雅的仪态、她那口有趣的磕磕绊绊的英语、她娴熟的钢琴技艺所吸引；他向她献殷勤，声言爱她；两人订了婚，1852 年 8 月 14 日，结为夫妇。更可能的情况却是这样：他怀着赞许听她弹肖邦、贝多芬等人——因为他爱好音乐；他请她换弹舞曲；头几个小节下来，他如遭电击——他觅到了一件无价的珍宝。她那份节奏感、那份奔放的气势、那份重音的拿捏、那份舞曲韵律的抑扬——昂扬处添力，缠绵处低回——还有那*弹性速度*（tempo rubato）——本身就是天才。他毫不耽搁地娶了她，当作一桩生意上的资产。她可爱，他或许也爱过她。可能，但不大像；因为记录里没有任何迹象表明他爱过别人，也不表明他爱过他自己。他只是以自我为中心——甚至算不上冷酷。他习性有节：喝一点葡萄酒，偶尔抽支雪茄，还是卫生之道的热心信奉者。这舞台布景，为即将登场的孩子预兆良好。血统是健全的。全部族的人都长着黑发。于是他如期前来造访，一如所录；他的母亲相信他是从天国来的天使——母性的迷狂（Mother-passion）就是这样盛大，这样令人目眩神迷。然而，从编年史家的视角看去，他并不是天国来的天使。两岁上，他已养出了脾气、铁一般的意志，和执拗。他时不时变成一个不折不扣的嚎叫旋转苦行僧。他嚎，他尖叫，他哭鼻子，抽噎，哼唧，呜咽。他像是被种种固念缠住了身子。随着时日推移，间或，在那场弥漫的风暴里也降临过几段相对的平静，他也偶尔并非全然不可爱。一轮朝阳仿佛正在他心里升起。他对洗澡发生了兴趣——那澡每天在一只可以搬动的浴盆里洗。外祖母只许她亲自来行这道仪式；她给他擦洗身子的时候，他就给她唱点什么，唱的是《马尔布鲁克出征记》（*Marlbrouck s'en va-t'en guerre*），或者讲讲这位巨人干了什么，那位仙女又干了什么。生活开始从外面闯到他身上来。这一场自外持续的闯入与自内持续的迸发，将塑造他的命运。

===Original===

He loved to look out of the window; to see people moving to and fro. But it was when first he saw the street-cleaners at work that there upsprung a life-fascination﻿—the sight, the drama of things being done. South Bennett Street ran from Washington Street to Harrison Avenue, a short block, but for him a large world. The street was paved with cobble stones; the sidewalks were of brick. He was there at the window when the work began. Came on the front rank; four men armed with huge watering cans painted red; these they swung in rhythm one-two, one-two. The thrill began, the child breathed hard. Then followed the second rank﻿—four men with huge brooms made of switches; they also, two-fisted, swung one-two, one-two, shaping a windrow in the gutter. Then came the glory of it all, the romantic, the utterly thrilling and befitting climax﻿—an enormous, a wonderful speckled gray Normandy horse, drawing a heavy tip cart, and followed as a retinue by two men, one sweeping the windrows into hillocks, the other with shovel and with mighty faith, moving these mountains into the great chariot. Thus appeared from Washington Street, thus passed in orderly action, thus disappeared into Harrison Avenue the Pageant of Labor, leaving the child, alone, thrilled with a sort of alarm of discovery, held by an utter infatuation. He had missed nothing; he had noted every detail. He had seen it whole and seen it steady. It is a close surmise that what actually passed in the child’s mind, aside from the romance, was a budding sense of orderly power. Indeed, the rhythm of it all! And then, to the wondering child, there began the dawn of a wonder-world.

===Chinese===

他爱趴在窗口张望；看人们来来去去。可直到他头一回看见清道夫们做工，一种终生的迷恋才訇然跃起——那景象，那事情正在做成的戏剧。南贝内特街从华盛顿街（Washington Street）通到哈里森大道（Harrison Avenue），短短一个街区，于他却是一个大千世界。街面用圆石铺就；人行道是砖砌的。开工的时候，他正在窗前。前排上场了；四条汉子，提着漆成红色的大洒水壶；一、二，一、二，他们合着节拍悠荡。战栗开始了，孩子呼吸粗重起来。接着第二排跟上——四条汉子，拿着细枝扎成的大扫帚；他们也双手齐挥，一、二，一、二，在街沟里扫拢出一条条长堆。随后登场的是全程的荣耀，那浪漫的、教人周身战栗而又是题中应有之义的高潮——一匹高大魁伟、灰底带花斑的诺曼底马，拖着一辆沉重的翻斗车，身后随行两名扈从：一个把长堆扫成小丘，另一个挥着铁锹、怀着浩大的信念，把这几座山移进那辆堂皇的大车里。就这样，劳动的盛典（Pageant of Labor）从华盛顿街登场，在秩序井然的动作中走过，又这样没入哈里森大道，留下那孩子独自一人，被一种发现的惊悸激得震颤，被一种全然的痴迷摄住。他什么也没漏掉；每个细节都记下了。他把这出戏看了个全，也看了个稳。一个贴切的揣测是：那孩子心里真正掠过的，除却浪漫之外，是一种正在破土的、对井然之力的感知。可不是嘛，这一切的节奏！于是，对这个满心惊奇的孩子，一个奇境（wonder-world）的黎明开始了。

===Original===

His mother often dandled him on her foot, holding his puny outstretched hands in hers, and in great glee and high spirits sang to him about *Le bon roi Dagobert, Le grand St. Elois*, and other heroes of the nursery. He felt these tales to be true, especially when the high points and low points of knee action were reached in a rushing climax. But one evening his mother took him for a visit; and on the return walk he tired and wailed. The mother raised him to her shoulder, and when the tears had dried he looked upward at the sky and beheld with delight the moon plowing its way through fleecy clouds. He called upon his mother to share in the joy. She too looked upward, yet told him that the moon was not plowing a path through the clouds, but that the clouds were driven by the wind across the face of the moon. This astounding statement he received as an affront to his common sense, and so stated. But the mother was adamant in her folly. He looked again skyward, to confirm himself. As by accident his eye fastened on the moon; the moon held steady and he was amazed to see the clouds go by. Then consciously he tried it on the clouds and the moon again plowed on. This process he reversed and reversed until he felt sure, and *then* it was he confided to his weary mother sagging under the intolerable burden of him, that he had made a *discovery*! He felt a sense of mastery and pride. He, **he**, had discovered this thing. In a world rising larger, difficulties appeared, and this particular thing was not quite what it seemed at first to be. But he had *mastered* it. That his mother knew all about it, had told him all about it, instantly faded; the child sank into sleep. The mother, weary unto death, reached her destination; she entered with her sleeping son while the clouds and the moon in the stillness of early night went their serene way undisturbed by further mundane intervention.

===Chinese===

母亲常常让他骑在自己脚背上颠着玩，把他伸出的纤细小手攥在自己手里，兴高采烈、意气风发地给他唱《好国王达戈贝尔》（*Le bon roi Dagobert*）、《伟大的圣埃卢瓦》（*Le grand St. Elois*），还有育儿歌谣里的别路英雄。他觉得这些故事千真万确，尤其是当膝头的一起一落一路奔涌着推向高潮的时候。可是一天傍晚，母亲带他出门做客；归途上他累了，哭闹起来。母亲把他扛上肩头；眼泪干了，他仰起脸望天，喜滋滋地看月亮在绒絮般的云朵间犁出自己的路。他唤母亲同享这份欢喜。她也抬头望了，却告诉他：月亮并不是在云中犁路，而是风赶着云，从月亮的脸上掠过。这句骇人听闻的话，他当作对他常识的一桩冒犯收下了，并且照此声明。可母亲在她的愚顽里寸步不让。他再望天，要验个明白。像是碰巧，他的眼睛钉住了月亮；月亮稳稳不动，云朵飞过，他看呆了。接着他有意拿云来试，月亮又犁起路来。这个过程他翻来覆去、颠颠倒倒地试验，直到确凿无疑；*于是*，才由他向母亲宣布——那母亲已被他这不堪承受的重负压得佝偻——他做了一项*发现*！他感到一阵驾御的得意与骄傲。他，**他**，发现了这件事。在一个愈升愈大的世界里，困难处处现身，而这桩特别的事，也全然不是它起初看起来的那副样子。可他已经*驾驭*了它。至于母亲早就一清二楚、早就对他说过，这一层，霎时间淡去了；孩子沉入睡眠。母亲，疲惫得要死，到了家；她抱着熟睡的儿子进了门，而初夜的静谧里，云和月亮各自安然赶路，不再受人间进一步的搅扰。

===Original===

Ever at the window pane, he liked to watch the snow, falling gently in large moist flakes and, in the little gusts, swirling and piling here and there, gathering curiously in odd nooks, and crannies, gathering on the window panes across the street, gathering on his own window panes, mantling the trees in a loving way, building far out in a roll from the top of a neighbor house﻿—and not breaking off (why did it not break off?). And the stillness, the muffled stillness, the lovely stillness. He was not satisfied to glance, he must look long, very long and steadily, he must see things move, he must follow the story, he must himself live the drama of dark things slowly changing into white things. It was all so real to him as he gazed through the window pane, alone and very quiet. And then when morning came, the hasty rattle and scoop and sip of the shovels, cleaning the sidewalks, heaping the snow in mountains in the street. Again the song of work, the song of action.

===Chinese===

他总是守在窗玻璃前，爱看下雪：大片大片湿润的雪花轻轻落下，又在阵阵小风里打着旋，这里一堆那里一堆，古怪地积进种种角落和缝隙，积在街对面的窗玻璃上，积在他自己的窗玻璃上，深情地给树木披上斗篷，从邻家的屋顶上远远探出一卷——却不坠落（它为什么不坠落呢？）。还有那静谧，那蒙着棉絮的静谧，那可爱的静谧。扫一眼他不满足，他必须久久地、久久而安安稳稳地看，他必须看见万物移动，必须追随那故事，必须亲自活进那出黑暗之物缓缓化作白色之物的戏剧。当他独自一人、安安静静地凝望窗外时，这一切于他如此真实。待到清晨来临，铁锹那急促的哗啦、铲刮与簌簌之声，清扫着人行道，把雪在街上堆成一座座山。又是劳动之歌，行动之歌。

===Original===

About this time a strange thing happened. It is the mother’s story repeated many times in after years. It seems one afternoon she was at the piano playing a nocturne with the fervor and melancholy sweetness that were her sometime mood. Lost in dreamland she played on and on, when of a sudden she seemed to hear a voice low-pitched like a sigh, a moan. She stopped, looked, listened; no one there. She seemed mistaken; then from under the very piano itself, came a true sob, a child sob and sigh. Why tell what happened? Her precious son in her arms pressed tight to her bosom; tears, tears, an ecstasy of tears, a turmoil of embraces, the flood gates open wide, a wonder, a joy, a happiness, an exultation, an exaltation supreme over all the world. The child did not understand. Why did he, unnoticed, enter the room; why secrete himself where he was found; why was he overcome and melted into lamentation? Had anyone else been playing, would he have thus responded? Had a new world begun to arise, this time a wonder-world within himself? Had there been awakened a new power within this child of three﻿—a power arising from the fountainhead of all tears?

===Chinese===

大约在这前后，一桩怪事发生了。这是母亲的故事，多年以后她讲了一遍又一遍。事情似乎是这样：一天下午，她坐在钢琴前，弹一首夜曲，带着那份热忱与忧郁的甜意——那是她时而会陷入的心绪。她迷失在梦境里，弹啊弹啊，忽然，她仿佛听见一个低低的声音，像一声叹息，一声呜咽。她停了手，看了看，听了听；没有人。她想是自己听岔了；紧接着，从那钢琴底下，传来一声真真切切的抽噎，一个孩子的抽噎与叹息。何必细说接下来发生了什么？她心肝般的儿子被搂进怀里，紧紧贴在胸口；眼泪，眼泪，一场眼泪的狂喜，一阵拥抱的纷乱，泪水的闸门豁然洞开，一个奇迹，一场欢喜，一种幸福，一阵欢腾，一种凌驾于全世界之上的无上飞扬。孩子不懂。他为什么悄没声地进了屋；为什么躲进后来被找到的那个地方；为什么他被击中、被融化，化作一场悲泣？换个人弹琴，他也会这样应和吗？一个新世界是否已经开始升起——这一回，是他自身之内的一个奇境？在这个三岁的孩子身上，是否有一种新的力量已被唤醒——一种从一切眼泪的泉源里升起来的力量？

### Folly Cove / 福利湾

===Original===

The family had decided to spend the summer on Cape Ann. They settled in a farm house of the very old fashioned kind, at a tiny spot called Folly Cove. The farm was a fairly large one and spread out to the rockbound coast. It had its weather-beaten orchards, its meadows and its fields, its barn and outbuildings, its barnyard with a well and bright tin bucket worked with a pulley and chain. There were also the farmer, a typical extra-nasal Yankee; the faded, shriveled, worn-out wife; the usual dozen or more children, and a farm hand. Also in the meadow was a well without a curb. Presently the child wanders into this meadow, picking the sparkling flowers, feeling the lush grass, glorying in the open. Quite incidentally in his floral march he walked into the well. It was rather deep, and amid his shrieks he felt that his blue flannel skirt seemed to float about him. His father and mother were away fishing; the farmer busy at a distance. Came the hired man on the run; a quick descent, a quick ascent of the boulder wall of the well, the child was saved. In the arms of the man he was hurried to the farm house and turned over to the womenfolk. The farm-man returned to his work. The children quickly gathered. The womenfolk rapidly stripped the chilly child, rubbed him down with harsh towels, and stood him naked with back to the glow in the huge fireplace. The children, all older than he, looked on curiously, pointed, giggled. For the first time he was aware of a vague sensitiveness. He felt, uncomfortably, that there was something in the air besides atmosphere. He turned aside. A new world was gestating in the depths.

===Chinese===

全家决定去安角（Cape Ann）消夏。他们在一座极老式的农舍里落了脚，那地方是个小到不能再小的去处，名叫福利湾（Folly Cove）。农场相当大，一路铺展开去，直抵岩石嶙峋的海岸。它有饱经风霜的果园，有草甸和田野，有谷仓和附属的棚屋，有场院，场院里一口井，配着滑轮、铁链和亮闪闪的马口铁吊桶。还有农场主，一个典型的鼻音格外浓重的扬基人；他那褪了色、干瘪瘪、操劳到枯竭的妻子；照例的一打或者更多的孩子；外加一个雇工。草甸上还有一口井，没有井栏。不一会儿，这孩子就逛进了这片草甸，摘着亮闪闪的野花，摸着丰茂的青草，在露天旷野里欢欣鼓舞。在他这场百花行军中，顺带便一脚走进了那口井。井相当深，在尖叫声中，他觉得他那条蓝色法兰绒小裙子仿佛在他周身漂浮。父母钓鱼去了；农场主在远处忙着。雇工飞跑而来；一口气的下，一口气的上，攀过井壁的顽石，孩子得救了。他躺那人怀里，被急急送进农舍，交给了女眷们。雇工回去干他的活了。孩子们很快围拢来。女眷们三下两下剥光这冻得冰凉的孩子，用粗硬的毛巾把他周身擦遍，让他赤条条站着，背对着巨大壁炉里的火光。孩子们——个个都比他大——好奇地看着，指指点点，咯咯地笑。他头一回觉出一种隐隐的敏感。他不自在地感到，空气里除了大气，还有些别的东西。他扭过身去。一个新世界正在深处孕育。

===Original===

Upon the return of the parents all was in turmoil again. Appalled thanks, gratitude, relief, amazement, the precious, the precious, and again the precious!

The father, more sedate, bethought him it would be righteous should he hold early communion with the lifesaver, the farm-man. They met. The father offered lucre in gratitude sincere enough. The offer was spurned. Would the farm-man, an American he-man, accept of gold for saving the life of an innocent child? He would not! Things looked bad. There was argument, persuasion, even supplication. Finally as by an inspiration he was asked if he would not accept something that was not money. The farm-man replied that if the father insisted and would not otherwise be calmed, he would with pleasure accept from him, as a casual gift, a plug of chewing tobacco. Thus was the value of a man-child ascertained.

===Chinese===

父母归来，屋里又是一片大乱。骇然的道谢，感激，宽慰，惊叹，心肝儿，心肝儿，又是心肝儿！

父亲较为持重，心想自己理应及早同那救命恩人——雇工——结一番交情。两人见了面。父亲奉上钱财，感激是足够诚挚的。好意被峻拒了。这位雇工，一条美国男子汉，岂肯为救一个无辜孩子的性命而收受金钱？他不干！局面眼看要僵。又是一番争辩、劝说，甚至恳求。终于，仿佛得了神启，有人问他，可肯收下一点不是钱的东西。雇工答道：倘若父亲执意如此、不这样就安不下心，那么他乐意作为一种随意的小礼，收他一块嚼烟。一个男孩的身价，就这样被核定。

===Original===

In the course of his exploration, he came to the other well, the one in the barn yard with pulley, chain, and big bright tin bucket. He was curious, and began huge experiments. Somehow the bucket got loose from the hook, struck the water with a splash and began to fill. He leaned over the edge in alarm. What was to be done? The bucket began its swaying descent, glinting this way, darkening that way, became dusky and was gone. In its place arose from the well an accusation seeming to say “guilty,” and there arose within and without the child a new world, the world of accountability.

===Chinese===

在四处勘探的途中，他来到了另一口井前——场院里那口，带滑轮、铁链和大大的亮马口铁吊桶的那口。他好奇心起，开始了庞大的实验。不知怎么，吊桶从挂钩上脱了开来，哗啦一声砸在水面上，开始进水。他惊慌地探身到井沿上。怎么办呢？吊桶开始了它摇摇晃晃的沉降，这边一闪，那边一暗，愈来愈暗，终于没了影。从井里升起来顶替它的，是一声控诉，仿佛说着「你有罪」；于是在这孩子的里里外外，升起了一个新世界——责咎的世界（world of accountability）。

===Original===

He spent most of his time with his father; the bond of union was the love of the great out-of-doors. Too young to philosophize and search his soul to discover sin, he took all things for granted. It seemed natural to him that there should be flowers, grass, trees, cows, oxen, sunshine and rains, the great open sky, the solid earth underfoot, men, women, children, the great ocean and its rockbound shore. All these he took at their face value﻿—they all belonged to him. He would sit beside his father on a great boulder watching him fish with pole and line. He would remain patiently there, inspirited by the salt breeze, listening to the joyous song of the sea as the ground swells reared and dashed upon the rocks with a mighty shouting, and a roaring recall, to form and break and form again. It seemed to lull him. It was mighty. It belonged to him. It was his sea. It was *his* father fishing.

One day as he was sitting alone on the boulder his father swung into sight in a row boat, and pulled for the open sea. The child did not know about rowboats, he had not discovered them, he did not understand how they went. Suddenly father and boat disappeared, the child gave a shriek of alarm, then as suddenly man and boat reappeared, to disappear again. The ground-swell running high, the breeze stiffening, the boat with the man grew smaller and smaller at each appearance; there was a flash each time. Smaller grew the boat until it became a speck, then it began to grow bigger and bigger. The child, dumbfounded, ran to meet his father, in wild excitement, at the landing. His father, very patient in such matters, explained it all as best he could, and the child listened eagerly, with some understanding. What was said must be true, because his father, who knew everything had said so. But what he knew, all of himself, and beyond the knowledge of others, was that the sea was a monster, a huge monster that would have swallowed up his father, like one of the giants he had told his grandmama about, if his father had not been such a big strong man. He felt this with terror and pride. Thus arose in prophecy the rim of another world, a world of strife and power, on the horizon verge of a greater sea.

===Chinese===

他大部分时光跟着父亲过；联结两人的纽带，是对浩大户外的那份爱。年纪太小，还不会玄思冥想，也不会搜寻灵魂去发现罪愆，他便把万事万物视作理所当然。世上有花，有草，有树，有母牛和犍牛，有阳光和雨水，有开阔的大天空，有脚下坚实的大地，有男人、女人、孩子，有浩瀚的大洋和它岩石嶙峋的海岸——这一切在他看来自然而然。这一切他都照单全收——全属于他。他会挨着父亲坐在一块巨大的圆石上，看他持竿垂钓。他会耐性地守在那里，被咸咸的海风鼓舞着，听大海欢畅的歌唱——涌浪昂起头来，大声呼吼着砸在岩石上，又轰鸣着召回，成形，破碎，再成形。它仿佛催他入眠。它是强大的。它属于他。那是他的海。垂钓的，是*他的*父亲。

一天，他正独自坐在圆石上，父亲摇着一条划艇荡进视野，朝着外海划去。孩子不认得划艇，他还没发现过这东西，不明白它们是怎么走的。忽然，父亲和船不见了，孩子惊叫了一声；随即人和船又忽然再现，跟着再度消失。涌浪愈掀愈高，风愈刮愈硬，连人带船每现一次就小一圈；每一次都有一星光亮。船愈来愈小，小到一粒斑点，然后又开始一圈圈变大。孩子目瞪口呆，狂喜地奔到上岸处去迎父亲。父亲——对这类事极有耐心——竭尽所能地解释了一遍，孩子听得急切，懂了个大概。说出口的必定是真的，因为无所不知的父亲这么说了。但他自个儿知道的、超出旁人所知的，是这海是一头巨怪，一头会把他父亲吞下去的巨怪——像他讲给外祖母听的那些巨人一样——倘若他父亲不是这样一条高大强壮的汉子的话。他怀着恐惧，也怀着骄傲，感到了这一点。就这样，另一个世界的边缘在预言中升起——一个争斗与力量的世界——立在更浩瀚之海的天际线上。

===Original===

For the remainder of the summer, nothing of special import occurred. The family returned to the city.

When all were settled, he was sent to the primary school of that district. He reported to the family at the end of the first day that teacher had called him to the platform to lead the singing. What a dreary prison the primary school of that day must have been. His recollection of his stay there is but a gray blank. Not one bright spot to recall, not one stimulus to his imagination, not one happiness. These he found only at home. He learned his letters, he followed the routine, that is all. Nor were there any especially memorable events at home until the matter of the farm came up and was discussed interminably. He had been merely enlarging his geographical boundaries, and exhausting the material. The primary school had, for the moment, dulled his faculties, slackened his frank eagerness, ignored his abundant imagination, his native sympathy. Even the family influence could not wholly antidote this. The neighborhood was growing disreputable. Next came the farm.

===Chinese===

那个夏天余下的日子里，没有发生什么特别要紧的事。一家人回了城。

一切安顿停当之后，他被送进了本区的初级学校。第一天散学，他向全家汇报说，老师把他叫上讲台，叫他领唱。那个年代的初级学校，想必是一座多么枯燥的牢狱。他对那段日子的回忆，只是一片灰蒙蒙的空白。没有一个亮点可追忆，没有一处激动他的想象，没有一桩快乐。这些他只在自家里找得到。他学会了字母，他跟着常规走，如此而已。家里也没有什么特别难忘的事，直到农场这桩事提上日程，被无休无止地讨论起来。他不过是在扩大自己的地理疆界，并把那里的材料用尽罢了。初级学校一时间磨钝了他的官能，弛缓了他那坦率的急切，无视了他丰沛的想象力和他天生的同情心。连家庭的影响也未能把这毒全数解掉。街区正日渐变得有失体面。接下来，登场的是农场。

## XIII The Garden City / 十三　花园城市

===Original===

There was a time a city some three hundred thousand strong stood beside the shore of a great and very wonderful lake with a wonderful horizon and wonderful daily moods. Above the rim of its horizon rose sun and moon in their times, the one spreading o’er its surface a glory of rubies; its companion, at the full, an entrancing sheen of mottled silver. At other times far to the west in the afterglow of sunset the delicate bright crescent poised in farewell slowly dimmed and passed from sight. Around this city, in ever-extending areas, in fancied semicircles, lay a beauteous prairie, born companion of the lake; while within this prairie, at distances of some seven to twelve miles from the center of the Garden City, were dotted villages, forming also an open-spaced semicircle, for each village nestled in the spacious prairie, and within its own companionable tree growth. To the north and west of the city there grew in abundance lofty elms and oaks; to the south the section-line dirt roads were double rowed with huge willows all swayed toward the northeast as the summer winds year by year had set them when sap was flowing strong; while scattered through this tract were ancient cottonwoods rising singly or in groups, in their immense and venerable strength. Further to the south, where the soil becomes sandy, there appeared fantastic dwarf pines and scrub oaks, while at the Lake Shore, neighboring them, stretched a mile or more of heavy oak groves that might be called a forest. Within it were winding trails; within it one seemed lost to the world.

===Chinese===

曾经有一个时候，一座约莫三十万人口的城市，伫立在一片浩瀚而奇妙无比的大湖岸边——湖有奇妙的地平线（horizon），有日日变幻的奇妙心绪。太阳与月亮按各自的时辰，从地平线的边缘升起：一个把一派红宝石的光华铺洒在湖面上；它的伴侣每逢圆满，则铺展一片斑驳银辉的醉人光泽。另有些时候，远远的西天，在落日的余晖里，那纤巧明亮的新月作别似的悬停着，慢慢黯淡下去，隐没不见。围绕这座城，在愈拓愈广的地带里，在想象中的半圆弧上，铺展着一片美丽的草原（prairie）——草原与湖乃是同生的伴侣；而在这草原之内，距花园城市（Garden City）中心约七到十二英里的地方，点缀着一座座村落，也连成一个疏朗的半圆，因为每座村落都安卧在开阔的草原里，安卧在自家亲伴相随的树丛里。城的北面和西面，繁茂地生长着高大的榆树与栎树；南面，沿测量分区线修的土路两旁，成双成行列着巨大的柳树，一律朝东北倾俯——年年岁岁，夏季的风都在树液奔涌正盛的时节把它们吹成了这个定势；而散布在这片土地上的，是一株株古老的三角叶杨，或孤植，或成群，以其巨大而可敬的伟力拔地而起。再往南去，土壤渐成沙质，那里现出奇形怪状的矮松与丛生栎；而在湖滨，与它们相邻，绵延着一英里上下的茂密栎树林，简直称得上森林。林中有蜿蜒的小径；身在林中，人恍若与整个世界相失。

===Original===

The city itself was more than a large village﻿—it was a village grown robust with an impelling purpose. In and near its central business district, residences held their own, and churches sent up their spires on Court House Square. There were few tramways. Horse-and-buggy was the unit; and on the Grand Boulevard fine victorias, blooded high-steppers noisily caparisoned in shining brass, liveried driver and footman, were daily on view to the populace﻿—wealth was growing breathlessly. The business section passed insensibly into the residential, then began tree growth and gardens﻿—the city bloomed in its season. In winter was the old time animation which came with heavy, lasting snows, with cutters, jangling bells, and horses of all shades and grades, and the added confusion of racing; for everybody who was anybody owned at least one horse.

===Chinese===

这座城本身可不只是一座大村子——它是一座因着一股奋进的宗旨而长得筋强骨壮的村子。在城的中心商务区里和近旁，住家宅院仍守得住自己的地盘，各色教堂把尖顶送上法院广场（Court House Square）的天空。有轨马车寥寥。一马一车的轻便马车是出行的单位；而在大林荫道（Grand Boulevard）上，精致的维多利亚式四轮马车、铮铮作响地披挂起亮闪闪黄铜饰具的名种高踏步骏马、穿号衣的车夫与跟班，天天陈列在民众眼前——财富正长得上气不接下气。商务区不知不觉间过渡为居住区，接着便起了树木与花园——这座城在它的花季里绽放。到了冬天，便有旧时那股欢腾劲儿，随厚重而经久的积雪一同到来：双人雪橇（cutter）、叮当乱响的铃铛、各色各样各等各级的马匹，还有赛马添上的乱哄哄；因为有头有脸的人物，谁家至少都养着一匹马。

===Original===

And then again came equinoctial spring; crocuses appeared; trees, each after its kind, put forth furtive leaves; for “April Showers” all too often were but chilling northeast rains. Indeed there was no Spring﻿—rather a wave-motion of subsiding winter and protesting summer. But in June the Garden City had come again into its own. From a distance one saw many a steeple, rising from the green, as landmarks, and in the distances the gray bulk of grain elevators.

The Garden City was triparted by a river with two branches; thus it had its three back yards of urgent commerce, where no gardens grew; and as well, its three shantytowns.

===Chinese===

随后，春分时节的春天又来了；番红花（crocus）冒出头来；树木各按各的品性，悄悄地绽出叶子；因为「四月的阵雨」十之八九不过是叫人打颤的东北冷雨。其实这里根本没有春天——有的只是退让中的冬天与抗议中的夏天之间的一阵波动。可是到了六月，花园城市便又恢复了它的本色。远远望去，只见一座座教堂尖塔从一派青绿中拔起，充作地标；更远处，是谷物升降塔（grain elevators）灰色的庞大身影。

花园城市被一条带着两条支流的河分作三瓣；于是它有了三片急煎煎做买卖的后院——那里长不出花园；也同样有了三片棚户区。

===Original===

On occasion, when a spell of hard weather had held the lumber fleet in port, one on watch might see the schooners pour in a stream from the river mouth, spread their wings, and in a great and beautiful flock, gleam in the sunlight as they moved with favoring wind, fan-like towards Muskegon and the northern ports.

The summer was dry. During September the land winds blew hot and steadily.

===Chinese===

偶尔，当一段恶劣天气把木材船队困在港中的时候，守望的人会看见双桅纵帆船（schooners）像一股洪流从河口涌出，展开翅膀，结成一大群美丽的鸟阵，乘着顺风，扇面似的向马斯基根（Muskegon）和北方的港口逶迤而去，在阳光里闪闪生辉。

那个夏天天旱。整个九月，陆风刮得又灼热又不停歇。

===Original===

The legend has it that a small flame, in a shanty town, destroyed the Garden City in two awful nights and days. The high winds did their carrier’s work. The Garden City vanished. With it vanished the living story, it had told in pride, of how it came to be. Another story now began﻿—the story of a proud people and their power to create﻿—a people whose motto was “I will”﻿—whose dream was commercial empire. They undertook to do what they willed and what they dreamed. In the midst of the epic of their striving, they were benumbed by the blow of a great financial panic, and when Louis returned from Paris the effect of this blow had not wholly passed﻿—though the time was nearing. The building industry was flat. Finding thus no immediate use for his newfangled imported education, and irking at the prospect of idleness, he bethought him to see what others might be doing in their lines, and at the same time get the lay of the land, something he had not found time to do during his first visit. Daily he made his twenty miles or more in the course of a systematic reconnaisance on foot. When this adventure had come to its end, he knew every nook and corner of the city and its environs, and had discovered undisturbed all that had formed the prairie setting of the living Garden City, and all that had remained undestroyed.

===Chinese===

传说讲的是：棚户区里的一点小火苗，在可怕的两个昼夜之间毁掉了花园城市。大风干完了它们搬运工的活计。花园城市消失了。随之一起消失的，还有它曾引以为豪、活生生讲述着的那个故事——它如何成形的身世。如今另一个故事开场了——一个骄傲的民族和他们创造力的故事——一个信条是「我要」（I will）的民族——一个梦想着商业帝国的民族。他们着手去做他们决意要做、梦想要做的事。在他们奋进的史诗正写到中途时，一场大金融恐慌的重击把他们打懵了；路易斯从巴黎归来时，这一击的效力还没有完全过去——虽然尾声已近。建筑业一蹶不振。他这才发现，自己那套新奇舶来的教育一时派不上用场，又不愿闲散度日，便想到去看看别人在各自行当里都在做什么，顺便摸一摸这片土地的底细——头一回来访时，他一直没腾出工夫做这件事。他天天徒步二十英里上下，做一场系统性的踏勘。这场探险收尾时，他已经认得了这座城和城郊的每一个犄角旮旯，并且原封不动地发现了当年构成活生生的花园城市之草原布景的一切，以及劫后残存的一切。

===Original===

Curiosity seemed to be Louis’s ruling passion; always he was seeking, finding something new, always looking for surprise sensations, always welcoming that which was fresh and gave joy to the sight. He had a skill in deriving joyful thoughts from close observation of what is often called the Commonplace. To him there was nothing commonplace﻿—everything had something to say. Everything suggested it be listened to and interpreted. He had followed the branches of the Chicago River, had located the lovely forest-bordered River Des Plaines, and the old-time historic portage. Had read Parkman’s vivid narrative of La Salle and the great Northwest, and his wonder stories of Marquette and Joliet, and he shared in mind the hardships of these great pioneers. Thus he came to know the why and wherefore of the City; and again he said: “*This is the Place for me!* This remnant scene of ruin is a prophecy!”

===Chinese===

好奇心仿佛是路易斯心中起统御作用的激情；他永远在寻寻觅觅、发现新东西，永远期待惊奇的感受，永远欢迎一切新鲜的、悦人眼目的事物。他有一手本领：从对那常被人称作「平凡琐细」（the Commonplace）之物的贴近观察里，衍生出快乐的思想。在他眼里没有什么是平凡琐细的——样样东西都有话说。样样东西都在示意，请人倾听它、解读它。他沿着芝加哥河的各条支流一一走过，找到了那条可爱的、森林镶边的德普莱恩斯河（River Des Plaines），找到了古时那条载入史册的连水陆路（portage）。他读了帕克曼（Parkman）关于拉萨尔（La Salle）与大西北的生动记述，读了他那关于马凯特（Marquette）与若利埃（Joliet）的奇迹故事，并在心里与这些伟大的拓荒先辈同甘共苦。于是他懂得了这座城的来龙去脉；他便又一次说道：「*这正是属于我的地方！*这劫后残景就是一则预言！」

===Original===

In a while the pulse of industry began the slow feeble beat of revival, and the interrupted story of imagination and will, again renewed its deep refrain in arousing energy. The Garden City had vanished with its living story. That tale could not be twice told; that presence could not be recalled. It had gone forever with the flames. Hence a new story must be told. Naught else than a new story could be told. Not again would the city be the same. It could not be the same﻿—men could not now be what they were. It was the approach of this new story that excited Louis; he would bide his time. He worked briefly now, at intervals, in the office of this or that architect, until he had nearly covered the field. These men were mostly of the elder generation, whose venerable clients clung to them for Auld Lang Syne. They were men of homely makeup, homely ways. Louis found them very human, and enjoyed their shop-talk, which was that of the graduate carpenter. He did not demur because they were not *diplômés* of the Beaux Arts. He preferred them as they were; much of their curious wisdom stuck to him. They were men of their lingering day. To them Louis was a marvel of speed. Indeed one of the younger of them, who laughed like a goat, remarked to his partner: “That Irish-*man* has ideas!”

===Chinese===

过了一些时候，工业的脉搏开始缓慢而微弱地搏动复苏，那中断了的想象力与意志的故事，又以深沉的复歌，在唤醒中的精力里重新唱起。花园城市连同它活生生的故事一同消失了。那个故事无法再讲第二遍；那份临在无法召回。它已随烈焰永逝。因此必须讲一个新的故事。除了新故事，别的也无从讲起。这座城再不会是从前的它了。它不可能是从前的它——如今的人也做不回从前的人了。正是这个新故事的临近令路易斯兴奋；他愿意静候时机。他如今只是间歇性地、短期地在这一位或那一位建筑师的事务所里做一做，直到把这一行几乎踏遍。这些人多半属于老一辈，他们那些可敬的老主顾念着旧日情分（Auld Lang Syne），死死跟定了他们。他们是一些家常做派、家常路数的人。路易斯觉得他们非常有人味儿，也喜欢听他们的行话——那是出师木匠的行话。他并不因为他们不是巴黎美术学院的*科班毕业生*（diplômés）而心存芥蒂。他倒喜欢他们本来的样子；他们那些古灵精怪的智慧，许多都粘在了他身上。他们是属于那个迟迟不肯谢幕的时代的人。在他们眼里，路易斯是个速度上的奇迹。他们中较年轻的一位确实——就是那个笑起来像山羊的——对合伙人说道：「那个爱尔兰*家伙*倒有点子！」

===Original===

He was a caustic joker and a man of brains, this same Frederick Baumann. Educated in Germany to the point of cynicism, he was master of one idea, which he embodied in a pamphlet entitled “A Theory of Isolated Pier Foundations,” published in 1873. The logic of this essay was so coherent, its common sense so sound, that its simple idea has served as the basis of standard practice continuously since its day. All honor therefore to Frederick Baumann, man of brains, exploiter of a new idea, which he made up out of his head. His vigorous years reached on to ninety-five, and as each one of them passed him by in defile, the world and its people seemed to his sharp, mirthful eye, to grow more and more ridiculous﻿—a conviction that gave him much comfort as his vertebrae began to curve. Louis met him frequently of evenings, at the gymnasium, and liked to talk to him to get his point of view, which he found to be not bitter, but Mephistophelian. He was most illuminating, bare of delusion, and as time went on Louis came to regard him as a goat-laughing teller of truths out of school﻿—but he, Louis, did not forget.

===Chinese===

这位弗雷德里克·鲍曼（Frederick Baumann），是个说话带刺的打趣者，也是个有头脑的人。他在德国受的教育，一直受出了愤世嫉俗的味道；他精通一个理念，并把它写成一本小册子，题作《孤立墩基理论》（A Theory of Isolated Pier Foundations），于 1873 年出版。这篇文章逻辑如此一贯，常识如此扎实，以致它那个简单的理念从问世之日起，便连续不断地充当着标准实务的基础。因此，向弗雷德里克·鲍曼致敬——一个有头脑的人，一个新理念的开拓者，这理念是他凭自己的脑袋凭空造出来的。他那精壮的年月一直活到九十五岁；那些年月一个个列队从他身旁走过时，在他那锐利而诙谐的眼睛看来，这世界和世上的人变得愈来愈可笑了——这个信念给了他莫大的安慰，当他的脊椎骨开始打弯的时候。路易斯常在晚间的体操房里遇见他，喜欢同他交谈，听听他的看法；他发现那看法并不怨毒，倒是梅菲斯托（Mephistophelian）式的。他最有启发性，全无幻觉；随着时间推移，路易斯渐渐把他看作一个笑如山羊、专爱说破不宜外传之真相的人——但他，路易斯，没有忘记。

===Original===

Reliable text books were few in those days. Due to this fact Louis made Trautwine’s *Engineers’ Pocket Book* his Bible, and spent long hours with it.
