The engineering journals kept close track of actual current doings, and thus Louis found himself drifting towards the engineering point of view, or state of mind, as he began to discern that the engineers were the only men who could face a problem squarely; who knew a problem when they saw it. Their minds were trained to deal with real things, as far as they knew them, as far as they could ascertain them, while the architectural mind lacked this directness, this simplicity, this singleness of purpose﻿—it had no standard of reference, no benchmark one might say. For he discerned that in truth the science of engineering is a science of *reaction*, while the science of architectural design﻿—were such a science to be presupposed﻿—must be a science of *action*. Thus Louis arranged in his mind the reciprocal values of the primary engineering and the primary architectural thought, and noted the curious antagonism existing between those who professed them. The trouble as he saw it was this: That the architect could not or would not understand the real working of the engineering mind because it was hidden in deadly literal attitude and results, because of the horrors it had brought forth as misbegotten stigmata; while the engineer regarded the architect as a frivolous person of small rule-of-thumb consequence. And both were largely right; both professions contained small and large minds﻿—mostly small or medium. Nevertheless they were all human beings, and therefore all ridiculous in the Mephistophelian sense of Frederick Baumann.

===Chinese===

那个年头，可靠的教科书寥寥无几。由于这个缘故，路易斯把特劳特温（Trautwine）的《工程师袖珍手册》（Engineers' Pocket Book）当作他的《圣经》，同它一道熬过了长长的时辰。工程期刊对当前的实际动态追踪甚紧；于是路易斯发觉自己正朝着工程的视角、或者说工程的心态漂移，因为他开始看出：工程师是唯一能直面问题的人；是见了问题便能认出问题的人。他们的头脑受过训练，去处理真实的事物——就他们所知的范围、就他们所能探明的范围；而建筑的头脑缺少这种直接，这种简单，这种目标的专一——它没有参照标准，可以说没有基准点（benchmark）。因为他辨出：工程学在实际上是一门*反应*（reaction）的科学，而建筑设计学——倘若预设有这样一门科学的话——必定是一门*行动*（action）的科学。就这样，路易斯在心里排布了初级工程思维与初级建筑思维的相互价值，并注意到信奉两者的人们之间存在着一种古怪的敌意。在他看来，症结是这样：建筑师不能、也不肯去理解工程头脑的真实运作，因为它藏在死板照字面行事的态度与结果之中，也因为它曾产出的那些畸形烙印般的可怖之物；而工程师则把建筑师看作一个轻浮的人，凭粗略的经验法则成不了什么气候。而两边大都有其道理；两种职业里都兼有小头脑与大头脑——以中小者居多。尽管如此，他们都是人，因此在弗雷德里克·鲍曼的梅菲斯托意义上，个个可笑。

===Original===

About this time two great engineering works were under way. One, the triple arch bridge to cross the Mississippi at St. Louis, Capt. Eades, chief engineer; the other, the great cantilever bridge which was to cross the Chasm of the Kentucky River, C. Shaler Smith, chief engineer, destined for the use of the Cincinnati Southern Railroad. In these two growing structures Louis’s soul became immersed. In them he lived. Were they not his bridges? Surely they were his bridges. In the pages of the *Railway Gazette* he saw them born, he watched them grow. Week by week he grew with them. Here was Romance, here again was man, the great adventurer, daring to think, daring to have faith, daring to do. Here again was to be set forth to view man in his power to create beneficently. Here were two ideas widely differing in kind. Each was emerging from a brain, each was to find realization. One bridge was to cross a great river, to form the portal of a great city, to be sensational and architectonic. The other was to take form in the wilderness, and abide there; a work of science without concession. Louis followed every detail of design, every measurement; every operation as the two works progressed from the sinking of the caissons in the bed of the Mississippi, and the start in the wild of the initial cantilevers from the face of the cliff. He followed each, with the intensity of personal identification, to the finale of each. Every difficulty encountered he felt to be his own; every expedient, every device, he shared in. The chief engineers became his heroes; they loomed above other men. The positive quality of their minds agreed with the aggressive quality of his own. In childhood his idols had been the big strong men who *did* things. Later on he had begun to feel the greater power of men who could *think* things; later the expansive power of men who could *imagine* things; and at last he began to recognize as dominant, the will of the Creative Dreamer: he who possessed the power of vision needed to harness Imagination, to harness the intellect, to make science do his will, to make the emotions serve him﻿—for without emotion nothing.

===Chinese===

大约在这个时候，两项伟大的工程正在施工。一项是圣路易斯（St. Louis）横跨密西西比河（Mississippi）的三孔拱桥，总工程师伊兹上校（Capt. Eades）；另一项是行将横跨肯塔基河（Kentucky River）峡谷的巨型悬臂桥，总工程师 C·沙勒·史密斯（C. Shaler Smith），供辛辛那提南方铁路（Cincinnati Southern Railroad）使用。路易斯的灵魂浸沉在这两座生长着的构筑物里。他活在它们里面。它们岂不正是他的桥吗？千真万确，它们正是他的桥。在《铁路报》（Railway Gazette）的书页里，他看着它们降生，看着它们生长。一周又一周，他随它们一同生长。这里是罗曼史；这里又一次是人，那个大冒险家——敢于思想，敢于怀信，敢于行事。这里又一次要向世人展示：人以创造行仁善的力量。这里是两个性质迥异的理念。各自正从一颗头脑里浮现，各自都将获得实现。一座桥要跨过大河，要做一座伟大城市的门廊，要轰动一时、要有建筑气象。另一座则要在荒野里成形，并在荒野里长驻；是一件绝无妥协的科学作品。路易斯亦步亦趋地追着设计的每一处细节、每一次测量、每一道工序——从密西西比河河床上沉箱（caissons）的下沉，到荒野中首批悬臂从崖壁迎面拔起的起步。他以切身认同的强度，逐一跟随到各自的终局。遭遇的每一重困难，他都感同身受，当作自己的；每一项权宜、每一件巧器，他都亲身与共。总工程师们成了他的英雄；他们巍然高出众人之上。他们头脑里那种斩钉截铁的品质，同他自己那股进取的品质正相投合。童年时，他的偶像本是那些*干*实事的壮硕大汉。后来他开始感到能*思考*事物的人身上更强大的力量；再后来，是能*想象*事物的人那种铺展的力量；最后，他开始认出那居于统御地位的：创造性梦想家（the Creative Dreamer）的意志——他拥有所需的远见之力，能驾驭想象力，驾驭才智，使科学奉行其意志，使情感为其所用——因为无情感则万无一成。

===Original===

This steadfast belief in the power of man was an unalloyed childhood instinct, an intuition and a childhood faith which never for a day forsook him, but grew stronger, like an indwelling daemon. As day by day passed on, he saw power grow before his eyes, as each unsuspected and new world arose and opened to his wonder eyes; he saw power intensify and expand; and ever grew his wonder at what men could do. He came in a manner to worship man as a being, a presence containing wondrous powers, mysterious hidden powers, powers so varied as to surprise and bewilder him. So that Man, the mysterious, became for him a sort of symbol of that which was deepest, most active in his heart. As months passed and the years went by, as world after world unfolded before him and merged within the larger world, and veil after veil lifted, and illusion after illusion vanished, and the light grew ever steadier, Louis saw power everywhere; and as he grew on through his boyhood, and through the passage to manhood, and to manhood itself, he began to see the powers of nature and the powers of man coalesce in his vision into an ***idea** of power*. Then and only then he became aware that this idea was a *new idea*﻿—a complete reversal and inversion of the commonly accepted intellectual and theological concept of the Nature of man.

===Chinese===

这一份对人的力量的坚定信念，是一种未掺假的童年本能，一种直觉，一种童年的信仰——它一天也不曾背弃他，反倒愈发强大，像一位内驻的守护神（daemon）。随着日子一天天过去，他看见力量在眼前生长，当每一个不曾料想的新世界升起、向他惊奇的双眼敞开的时候；他看见力量增浓、铺展；他对人所能为之事的那份惊叹，也愈来愈深。他竟至于以某种方式把人当作一个存在来崇拜——一个含有奇妙力量的临在，神秘而隐藏的力量，其花样繁多的程度，足以令他又惊又惑。于是，神秘的人（Man, the mysterious）对他而言成了一种象征，象征他心中最深、最活跃的东西。随着月份流逝、年岁推移，一个又一个世界在他面前展开、又汇入更大的世界；一层又一层帷幕掀起，一个又一个幻象消散，光愈来愈稳定；路易斯处处看见力量；而从他的少年时代，经过通往成年的关口，再到成年本身，他开始看见自然的力量与人的力量在他的视界里融合为一，凝成*力量的**理念**（an idea of power）*。直到那时，也唯有在那时，他才意识到：这个理念是一个*新理念*——一个对世人公认的、关于人的本性（the Nature of man）的理智概念与神学概念的彻底翻转与倒置。

===Original===

That **idea** which had its mystical beginning in so small a thing as a child’s heart, grew and nurtured itself upon that child’s varied consistently continuing and metamorphosing experiences in time and place, as has been most solicitously laid bare to view in detail, in the course of this recital. For it needs a long long time, and a rich soil of life-experience, to enable a simple, single idea to grow to maturity and solid strength. A French proverb has it that “Time will not consecrate that in which it has been ignored,” while the deep insight of Whitman is set forth in the line, “Nature neither hastens nor delays.”

===Chinese===

那个**理念**，它的神秘发端不过是一颗孩子的心这样一件小东西，却凭着这孩子在时间与空间中绵延不绝、层层蜕变的种种阅历，自养自长——这一切，本记述已如何关切备至地一一铺陈在读者眼前了。因为一个简单、单一的理念要长到成熟、长到坚实的力量，需要很长很长的时间，需要一片丰厚的生活阅历之土壤。法国有句谚语说：「时间不会把遭它冷落的事物奉为神圣」；而惠特曼（Whitman）的深见，则写在这一行诗里：「自然不疾行，亦不迟延。」

===Original===

Louis’s interest in engineering as such, and in the two bridges in particular, so captivated his imagination, that he briefly dreamed to be a great bridge engineer. The idea of spanning a void appealed to him as masterful in thought and deed. For he had begun to discern that among men of the past and of his day, there were those who were masters of ideas, and of courage, and that they stood forth solitary, each in a world of his own. But the practical effect of the bridges was to turn Louis’s mind from the immediate science of engineering toward science in general, and he set forth, with a new relish, upon a course of reading covering Spencer, Darwin, Huxley, Tyndall, and the Germans, and found a new, an enormous world opening before him, a world whose boundaries seemed destined to be limitless in scope, in content, in diversity. This course of reading was not completed in a month, or a year, or in many years; it still remains on the move.

===Chinese===

路易斯对工程本身的兴趣，尤其对那两座桥的兴趣，把他的想象力牢牢俘获了，以致有一阵子，他梦想成为一名伟大的桥梁工程师。跨越大空这一理念吸引着他，视之为思想与行事上的大师手笔。因为他已开始辨出：在往昔与当世的人当中，有那么一些驾驭理念与勇气的大师，他们卓然独立，各各住在自己的世界里。但那两座桥的实际效用，是把路易斯的心思从眼下的工程学引向了科学全体；他怀着一种新的兴味，启程研读斯宾塞（Spencer）、达尔文（Darwin）、赫胥黎（Huxley）、廷德尔（Tyndall）以及德国人的著作，发觉一个崭新的、巨大的世界在他面前打开——一个在疆域、内容、多样性上注定无边无际的世界。这一门功课，一个月读不完，一年读不完，许多年也读不完；它至今仍在进行之中。

===Original===

What Louis noted as uppermost in the scientific mind, was its honest search for stability in truth. Hitherto he had regarded his mathematics as an art; he had not followed far enough to see it as a science. Indeed he had hitherto regarded every constructive human effort as an art, and to this view he had been held through the consistent unfolding of the Idea. Inevitably this view was to return in time; through the channels of science itself. For that which at once impressed Louis as new to him and vital, was what was known as “The Scientific Method.” He saw in it a power of solution he long had fruitlessly been seeking. His key to an outlook took shape in the scientific method of approach to that which lay behind appearances; a relentless method whereby to arrive at the truth by tireless pursuit. He now had in his hands the instrument he wanted. He must learn to use it with a craftsman’s skill. For the scientific method was based on exact observation from which, by the inductive system of reasoning, an inference was drawn, an hypothesis framed, to be held tentatively in “suspended judgment” until the gathering of further data might raise it to the dignity of a theory, which theory, if it could stand up under further rigorous testing, would slowly pass into that domain of ordered and accepted knowledge we fondly believe to be Truth. Yet science, he foresaw, could not go either fast or far were it not for Imagination’s glowing light and warmth. By nature it is rigid and prosaic﻿—and Louis early noted that the free spirits within its field were men of vision﻿—masters of imagination, men of courage, great adventurers﻿—men of one big, dominant idea.

===Chinese===

路易斯在科学头脑里注意到的头等大事，是它对真理中之稳定的诚实求索。在此之前，他一直把自己的数学看作一门艺术；他追随得还不够远，还没把它看作一门科学。的确，他直到那时还把人的一切建设性努力都看作艺术，而这一看法，他是经由那个主理念的一贯展开而持守的。这一看法日后注定还要回来；经由科学自身的渠道回来。因为那立刻给路易斯留下印象、令他觉得既新鲜又攸关性命的东西，就是所谓的「科学方法」（The Scientific Method）。他在其中看见了一种求解之力——他长久徒劳寻求的那种。他通向一处眼界的钥匙，在科学的进路里成了形：循着它去抵达表象背后之物；一种不留情面的法门，以不懈的追索抵达真理。他如今手里有了他想要的器械。他还须学会以匠人的手艺来运用它。因为科学方法以精确的观察为基础，由此借助归纳推理体系，引出一个推断，立起一个假设，先暂且悬在「暂悬判断」（suspended judgment）里，待到进一步资料的集聚，把它提升到理论的尊位；而这个理论，倘若经得起更严格的检验，便会缓缓汇入那片井然而公认的知识疆域——我们满心相信那就是真理（Truth）。然而他也预见到：若没有想象力的灼灼光与热，科学走不快，也走不远。它天性刻板而无诗意——而路易斯早就注意到，这个领域里的自由精神，都是有远见的人——想象力的宗师，有勇气的人，大冒险家——心怀一个大写的、统御性理念的人。

---

===Original===

In the course of Louis’s daily working life, conditions were steadily improving. His engagements in offices grew longer, he began to prosper. The quality of work was improving. He had passed the day of his majority, and was now looking out for himself. His success in this regard made him proud. He was a man now, and he knew it. He knew he was equipped to hold his own in the world. He had made a reputation as a worker, and consorted now with a small aristocratic group of the highest paid draftsmen. They met at lunch in a certain favored restaurant. They talked shop; but Louis kept his major thoughts to himself. His plan was, in due time to select a middle-aged architect of standing and established practice, with the right sort of clientele; to enter such an office, and through his speed, alertness and quick ambitious wit, make himself so indispensable that partnership would naturally follow. But this was merely a broad plan. He had no direct selection in mind, but was looking the field over from the corner of his eye. He was in no hurry. He believed in the motto: “Be bold but prudent.” He wished events to shape themselves.

===Chinese===

在路易斯的日常工作生活里，境况稳步好转。他在各家事务所的聘期愈来愈长，他开始兴旺起来。工作的质量在提升。他已过了成年之日，如今自己照管自己了。他在这方面的成功令他自豪。他如今是个男子汉了，他自己知道。他知道他已有本领在世上立足。他挣得了一个能干者的名声，如今与一个小小的、最高薪绘图员中的贵族圈子过从。他们在一家中意的餐馆共进午餐。他们谈行话；但路易斯把他主要的想法留在自己心里。他的打算是：到适当的时机，选中一位有地位、有稳固业务、主顾对路的中年建筑师；进入这样一家事务所，凭他的速度、警觉与进取的敏思，把自己弄得不可或缺，合伙自然随之而来。不过这只是一个大概的规划。他心里并没有直接的选定对象，只是用眼角扫视着这一行。他不急。他信奉那句箴言：「大胆而谨慎。」他愿意让事件自行成形。

===Original===

Now John Edelmann returned. During the dull spell he had been away in Iowa trying to play the game of farming. The game played him instead. He showed up at lunch one winter day, clad *à l’outrance* as a farmer, for his usual theatrical effect. Instantly the room was filled with sound as he lustily proclaimed the joys of farming in Iowa, twenty miles from nowhere.

He entered the architectural office of a firm named Burling & Adler. The single, very large square office room he flooded with language; he literally “ate up” the work, as he spouted. Naturally he joined the aristocratic lunch-club, and made things lively. As usual he monopolized the conversation, unless rudely interrupted. One need not surmise to whom the sound of his voice was music from the spheres. He cut loose on his latest fad﻿—single tax﻿—and lauded Henry George in superlatives. He drew the long bow, he colored all things rosy, told Irish stories well in the broad brogue, and on the whole was a nuisance﻿—entertaining and agreeable.

===Chinese===

且说约翰·埃德尔曼（John Edelmann）回来了。清淡的时节里，他去了艾奥瓦（Iowa）乡下，想玩一玩务农这场游戏。结果这场游戏玩了他。一个冬日，他在午餐时露面，一身*登峰造极*（à l'outrance）的农夫打扮——照例要的就是那股戏剧效果。转眼间满屋子都是他的声音，他中气十足地宣讲着艾奥瓦农事的种种乐趣——那地方前不着村、后不着店，离哪儿都有二十英里。

他进了一家名为伯林与阿德勒（Burling & Adler）的建筑事务所。那间唯一的、方方正正的大办公室，叫他用语言淹了个满堂；他一面滔滔不绝，一面真把活计「吃」了个精光。他自然也加入了那个贵族午餐会，把场面搞得热热闹闹。他照例独霸话头，除非被人粗鲁地打断。他的嗓音对谁是天籁之乐，无须旁人猜度。他又对他的新风尚大发议论——单一税（single tax）——把亨利·乔治（Henry George）夸上了天。他信口开河，把万事万物涂成玫瑰色，用宽宽的爱尔兰土腔（brogue）把爱尔兰故事讲得出神入化；总而言之是个活宝——有趣，又讨人喜欢。

===Original===

One day, after lunch, John asked Louis to come over to the office to meet Adler, of whom he had spoken at times. Louis went along to please John. They entered the large bare room, drawing tables scattered about; in the center were two plain desks. Those who had business came and went unceremoniously. Both partners were present and busy. Louis thus had time to size them up. Burling was slouched in a swivel chair, his long legs covering the desk top; he wiggled a chewed cigar as he talked to a caller, and spat into a square box. He was an incredible, long and bulky nosed Yankee, perceptibly ageing fast, and of manifestly weakening will﻿—one of the passing generation who had done a huge business after the fire but whom the panic had hit hard.

===Chinese===

一天午餐后，约翰请路易斯到事务所去见一见阿德勒（Adler）——他时不时会提起此人。路易斯为讨约翰的欢心，就跟着去了。他们走进那间空旷的大屋子：绘图桌四散摆着；正中央是两张素净的书桌。有事的人来来去去，全无客套。两位合伙人都在，都忙着。路易斯于是有了端详他们的工夫。伯林（Burling）瘫在转椅里，两条长腿搭满桌面；他一边同来客说话，一边晃着一支嚼烂的雪茄，往一只方盒子里吐痰。他是个叫人难以置信的扬基人，鼻子又长又肥，眼见得老得很快，意志也分明在衰弱——他是那正在谢幕的一代：大火之后做过天大的买卖，却被恐慌重重击垮的一代。

===Original===

Further away stood Adler at a draftsman’s table, full front view, well lighted. He was a heavyset short-nosed Jew, well bearded, with a magnificent domed forehead which stopped suddenly at a solid mass of black hair. He was a picture of sturdy strength, physical and mental.

Louis was presented first to Burling who reached out a hand and said “Howdy,” in the distrait manner age so frequently bears toward strangely sprouting incomprehensible youth, separated by the gulf of years. Next, John led Louis to Adler whose broad serious face, and kindly brown efficient eyes, joined in a rich smile of open welcome. It did not take many ticks of the clock to note that Adler’s brain was intensely active and ambitious, his mind open, broad, receptive, and of an unusually high order. He was twelve years Louis’s senior, and in the pink of condition. Louis was of the exuberant age. Adler thought highly of John. The talk was brief and lively; Adler said nice things, questioned Louis as to his stay at the Beaux Arts. The little talk ended, Louis left; John remained in his preserve. This was the last that Louis saw of Adler for many moons. He was pleased to have met him and to have reason heartily to respect his vigorous personality. But he was no part of Louis’s program, hence he soon faded from view, and became almost completely forgotten. Louis was satisfied with things as they were going. He was ambitious but cautious; he was waiting for the right man to show up. He did not remain too long in any one place, and each time increased his salary.

===Chinese===

稍远处，阿德勒站在一张绘图桌前，正面全身，光线充足。他是个敦实的短鼻子犹太人，络腮胡蓄得整整齐齐，一副堂皇的穹顶式前额，到一头浓密的黑发处戛然而止。他本人便是一幅画：筋骨与头脑双重坚实的力量。

路易斯先被引见给伯林；伯林伸手一握，道了声「你好」，那心不在焉的做派，正是年岁对那些奇峰突起、无从索解的年轻人惯有的态度——中间隔着岁月的鸿沟。接着，约翰把路易斯领到阿德勒面前；阿德勒那张宽厚持重的脸，那双和善、棕色、干练的眼睛，一齐漾出一个热忱欢迎的丰润微笑。不消几秒钟便可看出：阿德勒的头脑极其活跃而雄心勃勃；他的心智开放、宽广、善纳，格调极其不凡。他比路易斯年长十二岁，正值状态的巅峰。路易斯正是血气方刚的年纪。阿德勒对约翰评价甚高。谈话简短而活泼；阿德勒说了些动听的话，又问起路易斯在巴黎美术学院的游历。小谈既毕，路易斯离去；约翰仍留在他的专属地盘里。此后好几个月，路易斯再没见过阿德勒。他很高兴结识了此人，也有理由由衷敬重他那旺盛的人格。但此人不在路易斯的规划之内，于是很快淡出了视野，几乎被忘了个干净。路易斯对时局的进展感到满意。他有雄心，但谨慎；他在等那个对的人现身。他在任何一处都待得不太久，而且每换一处，都涨一回薪水。

===Original===

Meanwhile his days were for work; his nights for study, for reflection, and gradual formulation of ideas subsidiary to the main Idea he was consciously now working out alone. This form of solitude did not disturb him. He saw that a “Clopet demonstration” meant a matter of years of work and growth. He was disturbed, however, by the elusive quality of the main thought he was pursuing, which seemed to recede and grow larger even as he grew abler to deal with it.

===Chinese===

与此同时，他的白昼用来工作；黑夜用来研读、沉思，用来把如今正自觉独力推演着的那条主理念（the main Idea）之下的种种从属理念，慢慢锤炼成形。这种样式的孤独并不搅扰他。他明白，一场「克洛佩式证明」（Clopet demonstration）意味着经年累月的功夫与生长。然而令他不安的，是他所追索的那条主思想的难以捉摸——它仿佛一面退远、一面长大，哪怕他应付它的本领也在一日日增长。

===Original===

On a recent Christmas his father had given him a copy of John Draper’s work on *The Intellectual Development of Europe*, in two volumes; still a notable work of the day. This he read and reread with absorbing interest, passing over its controversial trend, for the “war between science and religion” as it was called, was still raging. The broad division of the work into an “Age of faith,” and an “Age of reason” held his interest, as he saw set forth the emergence and the growth of science as the spirit of man sought and found freedom in the open. This coincided with his own belief, that man’s *spirit* must be free that his *powers* may be free to accomplish in beneficence. He had discovered, to his annoyance, that in the architectural art of his day, the spirit of man was not free, nor were his powers so liberated and so trained that he might create in beneficence. Not only this, but that for centuries it had been the case that art had been belittled in superstitions called traditions﻿—and lived on by virtue of a thin and baseless faith﻿—and John Edelmann’s theory of *suppressed functions*, recurred to him as broadly set forth, in confirmation, in Draper’s heavy work. Further than this, Louis felt as a result of reading Draper, that his thoughts concerning architecture must broaden into a perfected sympathy with mankind. That Man, past and present, must and would become more and more significant, would be found to have filled a greater role than any art, than any science. That man, perhaps and probably was the only real background that gave distinction to works appearing in the foreground as separated things﻿—or perhaps was it the invisible spirit of mankind that pervaded all things, all works, all civilizations, and informed them with the sense of actuality? That his, Louis’s true work, was now and henceforth to lie in the study of what man now thought, and had thought through the centuries. Thus the task for him grew larger, and the time required, longer﻿—for he was still in the plastic formative groping stage.

===Chinese===

前一个圣诞节，父亲送了他一部约翰·德雷珀（John Draper）的《欧洲智力发展史》（The Intellectual Development of Europe），两卷本；在当时仍是部名重一时的著作。他读了一遍又一遍，读得入了迷，对其中的论战倾向则略过不看——因为那场所谓「科学与宗教之战」仍在酣处。全书对「信仰时代」与「理性时代」的宽宏划分攫住了他的兴味，因为他看见科学如何兴起、如何生长，而人的精神如何到旷野里寻求自由、寻得自由。这与他自己的信念正相吻合：人的*精神*必须自由，他的*力量*才能自由地成就仁善。他已经发觉——发觉得颇为懊恼——在他那个时代的建筑艺术里，人的精神并不自由，他的力量也没有得到那样的解放与训练，好叫他创造于仁善之中。不仅如此：几个世纪以来，艺术一直被贬抑在名为「传统」的迷信里——靠着一息单薄而无根的信仰苟活——而约翰·埃德尔曼的*压抑功能论*（suppressed functions），又在他心头浮现，仿佛在德雷珀那部沉甸甸的著作里得到了广泛的印证。不止于此，路易斯觉得，读了德雷珀，他关于建筑的思索必须扩宽，达成一种对人类的圆成同情：人（Man），无论往昔还是当下，必定而且必将愈发意义重大，终将被人发现扮演过比任何艺术、任何科学都更伟大的角色；人，也许、而且多半正是那唯一真实的背景，赋予前景中一件件兀自独立的作品以光彩——又或者，那弥漫于万物、一切作品、一切文明之中、赋它们以实存感的，莫非是人族那不可见的精神？而他，路易斯的真正工作，从今往后就要落在研究人如今在想什么、千百年来都想过什么上面。于是他的任务愈见其大，所需的时间愈见其长——因为他仍处在那可塑的、成形的摸索阶段。

===Original===

In Darwin he found much food. The Theory of Evolution seemed stupendous. Spencer’s definition implying a progression from an unorganized simple, through stages of growth and differentiation to a highly organized complex, seemed to fit his own case, for he had begun with a simple unorganized idea of *beneficent power*, and was beginning to see the enormous complexity growing out of it, and enriching its meaning while insistently demanding room and nurture for further grown, until it should reach that stage of clarity through the depths of which the original idea might again be clearly seen, and its primal power more fully understood. Thus, Louis, while still in a haze, felt the courage to go on. He had been reading the works of men of matured and powerful thought, way beyond his years; but what he could grasp he hung on to. He felt the enthusiasm of one who is on the way, and who senses that his goal is real.

===Chinese===

在达尔文那里他找到了大量的食粮。演化论（Theory of Evolution）看来恢弘无比。斯宾塞的定义——从无组织的简单，经生长与分化的诸阶段，进到高度组织的复杂——似乎正合他自己的情形：因为他正是从一个简单而无组织的*仁善力量*（beneficent power）理念起步的，而且正开始看见从它里面生长出的巨大复杂性，看见它如何丰富着这一理念的意义，同时又执意索要进一步生长的空间与滋养，直到它抵达那种清澈的阶段——穿过清澈的深处，最初的理念得以重新看清，它太初的力量得以更充分地领悟。就这样，路易斯虽然还在雾里，却有了继续走下去的勇气。他一直在读那些思想成熟而有力、远超他年纪的人物的著作；但凡能抓住的，他都紧紧抓住不放。他感到一种正在路上的人的热情——那种感觉到自己目标真实存在的人的热情。

---

===Original===

One day John Edelmann, who meanwhile had entered into partnership with a man named Johnson, who did school work, sent for Louis to come over that evening﻿—said he had something to say. And this was his story: That Adler had cut loose from Burling, set up independently, and, in collaboration with George A. Carpenter, a resourceful promoter, had put through the New Central Music Hall, now nearing completion, and had other work on hand. The time was early in 1879. John urged that this was Louis’s opportunity. That Adler had all the strong points, but was feeble in design and knew it. That he had talked the matter over several times with Adler, that Adler was cautiously and eagerly interested, but timid in making advances.

===Chinese===

一天，约翰·埃德尔曼——他这时已与一位名叫约翰逊（Johnson）、做校舍营生的人合了伙——捎话叫路易斯当晚过去一趟，说他有话要讲。他讲的是这么一回事：阿德勒已同伯林分了手，自立了门户，又同一位精明强干的发起人乔治·A·卡彭特（George A. Carpenter）携手，把新中央音乐厅（New Central Music Hall）办成了——如今已近落成——手上还有别的工程。时间是 1879 年初。约翰力劝说，这正是路易斯的机会。阿德勒样样都强，唯独设计孱弱，而且自己心里有数。他说他同阿德勒把这件事反复谈过几回，阿德勒审慎而热切地动了心，只是不好意思先开这个口。

---

===Original===

Louis saw the point at once. So they made a second call on Adler. There ensued a mutual sizing up at close range, very friendly indeed. And it was then and there agreed that Louis was to take charge of Adler’s office, was to have a free hand, and, if all went well for a period, and they should get along together, there was something tangible in the background. Louis took hold and made things hum. Soon there came into the office three large orders; a six-story high grade office building﻿—the Borden Block; an up-to-date theatre, and a large substantial residence. Louis put through this work with the efficiency of combined Moses Woolson and Beaux Arts training. It was his first fine opportunity. He used it. He found in Adler a most congenial co-worker, open-minded, generous-minded, quick to perceive, thoroughgoing, warm in his enthusiasms, opening to Louis every opportunity to go ahead on his own responsibility, posting him on matters of building technique of which he had a complete grasp, and all in all treating Louis as a prize pet﻿—a treasure trove. Thus they became warm friends. Adler’s witticisms were elephantine. He said one day to Louis:

===Chinese===

路易斯当即听明白了。于是他们对阿德勒作了第二次拜访。接着是一场近距离的相互打量——实在是十分友好的。就在当时当地，双方说定：路易斯将执掌阿德勒的事务所，将放手大干；倘若一段时期一切顺遂、两人又处得来，幕后还有一点实实在在的东西。路易斯一上手，就把一切办得嗡嗡作响。不久，三笔大生意进了事务所：一幢六层的高档办公大楼——博登大楼（Borden Block）；一座最新式的剧院；还有一幢气派牢靠的大宅。路易斯以摩西·伍尔森与巴黎美术学院双重训练合成的效率，把这些工程一一办成。这是他头一个漂亮的机遇。他用足了它。他发现阿德勒是个再投契不过的同僚：心智开放，胸襟宽厚，见事敏捷，办事彻底，热心起来滚烫，把一切独当一面的机会都向路易斯敞开，在建筑技术的事体上——他对此有完整的把握——处处给他补课；总而言之，把路易斯当成一件得宠的珍宝——一座宝藏。就这样，他们成了热络的朋友。阿德勒的俏皮话是大象式的。一天，他对路易斯说：

===Original===

“How would you like to take me into partnership?”

Louis laughed.

===Chinese===

「你愿不愿意把我也收作合伙人？」

路易斯笑了。

===Original===

“All right,” said Adler, “draw up a contract for five years, beginning first of May. First year you one third, after that, even.”

Louis drew up a brief memorandum on a sheet of office stationery, which Adler read over once and signed.

===Chinese===

「好吧，」阿德勒说，「那就拟一份五年期的合同，从五月一日起。头一年你得三分之一，往后对半。」

路易斯在一张事务所的信纸上拟了一份简短的备忘录，阿德勒通读了一遍，签了字。

===Original===

On the first day of May, 1880, D. Adler & Co. moved into a fine suite of offices on the top floor of the Borden Block aforesaid. On the first day of May, 1881, the firm of Adler & Sullivan, Architects, had its name on the entrance door. All of which signifies, after long years of ambitious dreaming and unremitting work, that at the age of twenty-five, Louis H. Sullivan became a full-fledged architect before the world, with a reputation starting on its way, and in partnership with a man he had least expected as such; a man whose reputation was solidly secured in utter honesty, fine intelligence and a fund of that sort of wisdom which attracts and holds. Between the two there existed a fine confidence and the handling of the work was divided and adjusted on a temperamental basis﻿—each to have initiative and final authority in his own field, without a sharp arbitrary line being drawn that might lead to dissension. What was particularly fine, as we consider human nature, was Adler’s open frank way of pushing his young partner to the front.

===Chinese===

1880 年五月一日，D·阿德勒公司（D. Adler & Co.）迁入前述博登大楼顶层一套漂亮的办公用房。1881 年五月一日，阿德勒与沙利文建筑事务所（Adler & Sullivan, Architects）的大名，挂上了大门。这一切意味着：经过多年雄心勃勃的梦想与不懈的工作，二十五岁的路易斯·H·沙利文，在世人面前成为一位羽毛丰满的建筑师，名声正开始上路，而合伙人竟是他最不曾料想到会如此的那一个；此人的名声，稳稳立在彻底的诚实、精良的智慧与一份足以引人亦足以留人的智识财富之上。两人之间存在着一种美好的信任，工作的操持按性情分派调济——各人在自己的领域里有主动权与最终裁断权，不划可能惹起龃龉的生硬武断的界线。尤其难得的——若以人情而论——是阿德勒那副坦诚无忌的做派：把自己的年轻合伙人推到前台去。

===Original===

Now Louis felt he had arrived at a point where he had a foothold, where he could make a *beginning* in the open world. Having come into its responsibilities, he would face it boldly. He could now, undisturbed, start on the course of practical experimentation he long had in mind, which was to make an architecture that fitted its functions﻿—a realistic architecture based on well defined utilitarian needs﻿—that all practical demands of utility should be paramount as basis of planning and design; that no architectural dictum, or tradition, or superstition, or habit, should stand in the way. He would brush them all aside, regardless of commentators. For his view, his conviction was this: That the architectural art to be of contemporary immediate value must be *plastic*; all senseless conventional rigidity must be taken out of it; it must intelligently serve﻿—it must not suppress. In this wise the forms under his hand would grow naturally out of the needs and express them frankly, and freshly. This meant in his courageous mind that he would put to the test a formula he had evolved, through long contemplation of living things, namely that *form follows function*, which would mean, in practice, that architecture might again become a living art, if this formula were but adhered to.

===Chinese===

如今路易斯感到，他已抵达一个立住了脚跟的地方，一个可以在敞天敞地的世界里*开头*的地方。既已担起它的责任，他就要大胆面对它。他如今可以不受搅扰地启程了——踏上他酝酿已久的实务试验之路：造一种合乎其功能的建筑——一种奠基于明确界定的功利需求之上的现实主义建筑——一切功用的实际需求都应居于至高，充当规划与设计的基础；任何建筑的教条、传统、迷信或积习，都不得横加阻拦。他要把它们统统扫到一旁，任凭评头论足者说去。因为他的观点、他的信念是这样：建筑艺术要有当代的、当下的价值，就必须是*可塑的*（plastic）；一切无意义的因袭刻板都必须从中剔除；它必须明智地服务——它不得压抑。这样，他手下的形式便会从需求中自然长出，把它们坦白而清新地表达出来。在他那颗勇敢的心里，这意味着他将检验一条他经长久凝思活物而演化出的公式，即*形式追随功能*（form follows function）；这在实践中意味着：只要恪守这条公式，建筑或许能重新成为一门活的艺术。

===Original===

The building business was again under full swing, and a series of important mercantile structures came into the office, each one of which he treated experimentally, feeling his way toward a basic process, a grammar of his own. The immediate problem was increased daylight, the maximum of daylight. This led him to use slender piers, tending toward a masonry and iron combination, the beginnings of a vertical system. This method upset all precedent, and led Louis’s contemporaries to regard him as an iconoclast, a revolutionary, which was true enough﻿—yet into the work was slowly infiltrated a corresponding system of artistic expression, which appeared in these structures as novel and to some repellent, in its total disregard of accepted notions. But to all objections Louis turned a deaf ear. If a thousand proclaimed him wrong, the thousand could not change his course. As buildings varying in character came under his hand, he extended to them his system of form and function, and as he did so his conviction increased that architectural manipulation, as a homely art or a fine art must be rendered completely plastic to the mind and the hand of the designer; that materials and forms must yield to the mastery of his imagination and his will; through this alone could modern conditions be met and faithfully expressed. This meant the casting aside of all pedantry, of all the artificial teachings of the schools, of the thoughtless acceptance of inane traditions, of puerile habits of uninquiring minds; that all this mess, devoid of a center of gravity of thought, and vacant of sympathy and understanding, must be superseded by a sane philosophy of a living architecture, good for all time, founded on the only possible foundation﻿—Man and his powers. Such philosophy Louis had already developed in broad outline in the course of his many dissatisfactions and contemplations. He wished now to test it out in the broad daylight of action, and to perfect its form and content. This philosophy developed will be set forth in these closing chapters.

===Chinese===

建筑业又开足了马力，一系列重要的商业建筑进了事务所，他一概以试验待之，摸索着走向一道基本工序、一部他自己的语法。眼下的问题是增加日光，日光的最大化。这引他采用细长的墩柱，渐趋于砌体与铁的结合——一套竖向体系的发端。这套方法推翻了一切先例，使路易斯的同代人把他看作偶像破坏者（iconoclast）、一个革命者——倒也所言不虚——然而与此同时，一套相应的艺术表现体系也缓缓渗进了这些工程，在这些建筑里显得新奇，在某些人眼里显得可憎，因为它对公认的观念全然不屑一顾。但对一切异议，路易斯一概置若罔闻。纵有一千个人宣称他错了，那一千个人也改变不了他的航向。当性格各异的建筑一一到他手上，他便把自己的形式与功能体系推展于其上；而当他这样做时，他的信念愈发坚定：建筑的运作，无论作为一门家常艺术还是一门美术，都必须在设计师的头脑与手中彻底化为可塑；材料与形式必须屈服于他的想象力与意志的驾驭；唯有如此，现代的状况才能被应对、被忠实表达。这意味着把一切学究气、把学院里一切人为的教条、把对空洞传统的无思接受、把不求甚解的头脑那孩子气的积习，统统抛却；这一大团乱麻——思想的重心全无、同情与理解全空——必须被一种健全的活建筑哲学取而代之，一种万世皆宜的哲学，奠立在唯一可能的奠基石上——人及其力量。这门哲学，路易斯已在多年的不满与沉思中勾出了粗线条。他现在要把它放到行动的朗朗白日下检验，完善它的形式与内容。这门发展出来的哲学，将在最后这几章里得到铺陈。

===Original===

It is not to be supposed that Louis arrived directly at results as though by magic. Quite the contrary, he arrived slowly though boldly through the years, by means of incessant thought, self correction, hard work and dogged perseverance. For it was his fascinating task to build up a system of technique, a mastery of technique. And such a system could scarcely be expected to reach its fullness of development, short of maturity, assuming it would reach its fullness then, or could ever reach it; for the world of expression is limitless; the theory so deep in idea, so rich in content, as to preclude any ending of its beneficent, all-inclusive power. And we may here recall Monsieur Clopet, the book of descriptive geometry that went into the waste basket, and the thunderclap admonition: “Our demonstrations shall be such as to admit of no exception.”

===Chinese===

切不要以为路易斯像变戏法似的直取成果。恰恰相反，他是经年累月、缓慢而大胆地抵达的——靠的是不停的思考、自我修正、苦干与顽强的坚持。因为他的迷人任务，就是建立起一套技术的体系、一种对技术的精通。而这样一套体系，不到成熟的年纪，很难指望它达于发展的圆满——姑且假定它到那时会圆满，或者终究能够圆满；因为表现的世界无边无际；这门理论在理念上如此之深，在内容上如此之富，它那仁善而无所不包的力量不容任何终结。在此我们不妨回想一下克洛佩先生（Monsieur Clopet），回想那本进了废纸篓的画法几何（descriptive geometry），回想那声霹雳般的训诫：「我们的证明，必须做到不容任何例外。」

## VII Boston / 七　波士顿

===Original===
One day, in Boston, a boy of nine was walking northward on the east side of Washington Street. Just then “Yankee Doodle” came along whistling his tune to a brisk step, a pair of boots slung over one shoulder of his faded blue jeans; and, under a stovepipe hat, much battered in the strife of years, this agile elderly man wore a grey chin beard after the manner of Uncle Sam. And thus went Yankee Doodle tirelessly up and down Washington Street, always on the east side of it, day after day, year after year. In a legendary sense he was a cobbler. The boy watched his kindly face approaching, and for the hundredth time admired in despair the clear sharp whistle which he had tried in vain to emulate; and, as Yankee passed on southward the boy turned east into South Bennett Street following the south sidewalk. About midway to Harrison Avenue a paper bag struck the sidewalk in front of him, burst, and hard candies scattered over the pavement. The boy, startled, looked around, and then up. In a second story window, straight across the way, appeared two fat bare arms, an immense bosom, a heavy, broad, red face, topped with straight black hair. A fat finger beckoned to him; a fat mouth said something to him; and at the doorway of the house was the number 22﻿—the house he had been born in; but the silver nameplate marked P. Sullivan in black script was no longer there.

===Chinese===
一天，在波士顿，一个九岁的男孩正沿着华盛顿街（Washington Street）东侧向北走去。恰在此时，「扬基·杜德尔」（Yankee Doodle）来了，哼着他的调子，踏着轻快的步子，一双靴子挎在褪色蓝布工装的一边肩头；头上一顶高顶大礼帽，在岁月的争斗里磕碰得不成样子；这位矫健的老人蓄着灰色的下巴胡，是山姆大叔（Uncle Sam）的样式。扬基·杜德尔就这样不知疲倦地在华盛顿街上来来回回，永远走在这条街的东侧，日复一日，年复一年。在传说的意义上，他是个修鞋匠。男孩望着他和善的脸走近，第一百次绝望地艳羡那清亮尖利的口哨——他自己试着学过，怎么也学不像；扬基往南走远了，男孩便折向东，顺着南侧的人行道拐进南贝内特街（South Bennett Street）。走到去哈里森大道（Harrison Avenue）约莫一半的地方，一只纸袋啪地砸在他面前的人行道上，破了，硬糖撒了一地。男孩吓了一跳，四下张望，又抬起头来。正对着路的那扇二楼窗子里，探出两条肥胖的光胳膊，一座硕大无朋的胸脯，一张又重又宽的红脸盘，顶着一头乌黑的直发。一根胖手指朝他招了招；一张胖嘴对他说了句什么；房子门口是 22 号——他出生的那座房子；只是那块银铭牌不在了，上面原本用黑色花体字写着 P. Sullivan。

===Original===
He had been led to the spot, which he had not seen for years, by a revived memory of a sweet child named Alice Look, who lived next door when the two of them were three together. He had wished to see once more the sacred dwelling wherein she had lived and the walled yard in which she had mothered him and called him Papa in their play.

===Chinese===
他之来到这个多年不曾一见的地方，是被一段复苏的记忆引来的——记忆里有个甜美的孩子，名叫艾丽斯·卢克（Alice Look），两人三岁上在一起玩耍的时候，她就住在隔壁。他想再看一眼她住过的那座圣宅，再看一眼那座四面有墙的院落——在里面，她像小母亲似的照料他，游戏里唤他「爸爸」。

===Original===
Much troubled, he walked on to Harrison Avenue, where Bennett Street ends its one block of length. There he noticed that the stately trees were bare of leaves and sickly to the sight, while on the twigs and among the branches and even on the trunks were hundreds of caterpillar nests which made the trees look old, poor and forsaken. While he was counting the nests on a single tree, caterpillars now and then would come slowly downward from the heights. Some of them would remain for a time in midair, suspended invisibly, before completing their descent, perchance upon a passerby. The boy was examining one of these caterpillars undulating upon his coat sleeve, when his quick ear detected the sound of snare-drums. Crowds began to gather on the sidewalks. Slowly the drums beat out their increasing sadness, pulsing to a labored measure of weariness and finality, as a faint bluish mass appeared vaguely in the north. The sidewalk crowds became dense﻿—men, women and children stood very still. Onward, into distinctness and solidity, came the mass of faded blue undulating to the pathos of the drums. The drum corps passed﻿—and in the growing silence came on and passed ranks of wearied men in faded blue, arms at right shoulder, faces weather-beaten, a tired slow tread, measured as a time-beat on the pavement, the one-two of many souls. And to these men, as they marched, clung women shabbily clothed, with shawls drawn over their heads, moving on in a way tragically sad and glad, while to the skirts of many of these women clung dirty children. Thus moved in regular mass and in silence a regiment of veterans, their women, their children, passing onward between two tense rows of onlooking men, women and children, triple deep, many of them in tears. So vivid was this spectacle, so heartrending, so new this aching drama of return, that the boy, leaning against a caterpillared tree, overflowed with compassion. When he had ceased weeping upon his coat sleeve, Harrison Avenue was vacant; but not so the boy﻿—he in fullness of sympathy was ill with the thought of what all this might mean. What was the mystery that lay behind these men in faded blue? He found no sufficing answer. The men had been mustered out, he had been told; that was all.

===Chinese===
他满怀烦恼地走到哈里森大道——贝内特街那唯一一个街区的长度到那里为止。到了那里，他注意到那些气派的树木叶子落得精光，看上去病恹恹的；细枝上、枝杈间、甚至树干上，缀着数以百计的毛虫巢，把这些树弄得又苍老、又寒碜、又像遭了遗弃。他正数着一棵树上的巢，毛虫们不时从高处慢慢垂下来。有几只会先在半空悬停片刻，吊在看不见的丝上，然后才落完最后一段，说不定正落在哪个过路人身上。男孩正端详一只在他袖口上起伏蠕动的毛虫，灵敏的耳朵忽然捕到了小军鼓的声音。人群开始在人行道上聚拢。鼓声缓缓敲出愈来愈浓的悲怆，应和着一种吃力的、疲惫而终局的拍子；北方隐约浮起一小片微蓝。人行道上的人潮稠密起来——男人、女人和孩子伫立不动。那片褪色的蓝一路向前，愈来愈清晰、愈来愈凝实，随鼓声的哀婉起伏涌动。鼓队过去了——在渐深的静默里，走来又走过去的是一列列疲惫的人，穿着褪了色的蓝军装，枪抵着右肩，面孔饱经风霜，迈着疲乏迟缓的步子，像节拍一样落在路面上，是许多灵魂的一二、一二。这些人行进时，身上偎着衣衫寒酸的妇女，头巾罩在头上，以一种悲切而又欣喜的样子向前挪动；而这些妇女中许多人的裙边，又缠着脏兮兮的孩子。一个老兵团就这样成建制地、默然地行进着，带着他们的女人、他们的孩子，从两排紧张的围观者中间走过去——围观的男女老幼站了三层深，许多人在流泪。这一幕如此鲜明，如此叫人心碎；这场归来的痛楚戏剧如此新奇，男孩倚着一棵爬满毛虫的树，满怀的怜悯漫溢而出。等他把眼泪在袖口上哭干，哈里森大道已经空了；男孩却没有空——他满腔的同情，为这一切可能意味着什么而害了病。这些穿褪色蓝军装的人背后，究竟藏着什么奥秘？他找不到一个说得过去的答案。人家告诉他：这些人退伍了；仅此而已。

===Original===
He chafed until he got permission to go to South Reading for a week end; ostensibly to visit the grandparents, surreptitiously to visit Julia, to whom alone he could bare his heart. He knew in advance what Grandpa would say; he knew in advance what Grandma would say; he wished eagerly to learn what Julia might say. So after earnest greetings with Grandpa and Grandma he slipped quietly to the kitchen. Julia was not there. He moved to the barn; Julia was not there. Then, in dime-novel fashion he made a detour through the “old” orchard, dodging from tree to tree in Indian fashion, examining the grass, crawling slowly on all fours, bent on surprise, signalling to an imagined companion in the rear, cautiously advancing until he caught a glimpse of a broad back, topped with massy hair on fire. He approached at a flat crawl and, from behind the next tree, saw Julia sitting on a milking stool peeling potatoes. Now came the villain’s mad rush. Julia was seized savagely﻿—with an arm around her neck, her head pulled back, her face kissed all over, her hair roughly tousled, her shoulder pushed hard, her stool kicked from under her as Louis, in a warwhoop of joy, hailed her as Ireland’s hope, Queen of the orchard, and was greatly pleased.

===Chinese===
他一直闹腾磨缠，直到获准去南雷丁（South Reading）过上一个周末；明面上是探望外祖父外祖母，暗地里是去会朱莉娅（Julia）——只有对她一个人，他才敞得开心扉。他事先就知道外祖父会说什么；事先就知道外祖母会说什么；他热切想知道的是朱莉娅会说什么。于是同外祖父外祖母恳切地见礼之后，他便悄悄溜进厨房。朱莉娅不在。他挪到谷仓；朱莉娅也不在。于是，照十美分小说的路数，他绕道穿过「老」果园，学印第安人的样子从一棵树闪到另一棵树，察看草叶，四肢着地慢慢爬行，一心要攻其不备，还朝后方一位想象中的同伴打信号，小心翼翼地推进，直到瞥见一个宽阔的脊背，顶着一蓬火焰似的浓发。他贴地平爬过去，从旁边那棵树背后，看见朱莉娅正坐在挤奶凳上削土豆。恶徒的疯狂冲刺开始了。朱莉娅被凶猛地擒住——一条胳膊圈住她的脖子，头被往后扳起，满脸被亲了个遍，头发被粗暴地揉乱，肩膀被狠狠一推，挤奶凳被从身下一脚踢飞；路易斯在一声欢腾的战吼里，欢呼她是爱尔兰的希望、果园的王后，自己也得意非凡。

===Original===
Not so Erin’s daughter. Sitting broadly on the grass, shaking a clenched fist, she screamed: “Ye rat, ye vile spalpeen. To think o’ the likes o’ ye takin’ me unawares; and ye’ve upset the spuds and me pan of fresh water. May the divil fly away with ye. Get y’self out o’ here before I smash ye with the stool”; and Julia’s language became violent in a torrent of brogue, as, madly erect, she swung the stool and let fly while Louis danced about her singing an impudent Irish song he had learned from her. Then Julia sat largely down again in the grass, gasping for breath, while Louis went for the distant stool. Grandpa passed that way, remarking simply: “Ah, I *hear* you and Julia are visiting today.” Louis walked up to Julia and said, in a manner: “Julia Head, I now present you with this stool. It is far less beautiful than yourself, but in its humble way, it is as useful as your own valued activities, inasmuch as it, on many an occasion, has served as your main stay while you were drawing from our gentle kine the day’s accumulations. Will you accept this emblem of industry in the same simplicity of spirit with which it is offered you?” Julia, tired of ranting, laughed. “Sure,” she said, “ ’tis well ye know that had ye come at me dacently, it’s a hearty welcome I’d a given ye.” And she resumed operations, still sitting, the pan of spuds resting upon her enormous thighs. And Louis sat down meekly beside her, his small hand barely touching the expanse of freckled arm. He said he was sorry, and went on to pacify her. He used Gaelic words she had taught him, words romantically tender and sweet. Julia softened. With both hands she turned his face toward her; looked at him roguishly:

===Chinese===
艾琳（Erin）的女儿可不吃这一套。她大模大样地坐在草地上，摇着一只攥紧的拳头，尖声叫道：「你这耗子，你这下流的小无赖（spalpeen）！想想看，你这样的东西竟敢偷袭我；你还打翻了土豆，打翻了我一盆清水。叫魔鬼抓了你去。趁我还没拿凳子把你砸扁，快给我滚出去。」朱莉娅的言语在滔滔的爱尔兰土腔（brogue）里愈演愈烈；她狂怒地跳将起来，抡起凳子便掷，路易斯绕着她又蹦又跳，唱着一支从她那儿学来的无礼的爱尔兰歌子。随后朱莉娅又大剌剌地坐回草地上，喘着气，路易斯便去捡那把飞得远远的凳子。外祖父恰从那儿经过，平平地评了一句：「啊，我*听见*你跟朱莉娅今天在串门儿。」路易斯走到朱莉娅跟前，拿出一副腔调说道：「朱莉娅·海德（Julia Head），谨以此凳奉赠于您。它远不如您美丽，但以它谦卑的方式，它与您素来看重的种种营生同样有用：因为多少回，当您从我们温驯的乳牛身上汲取一日的积攒时，它都充当了您主要的倚靠。您可愿以它奉上时的同样单纯，收下这一勤劳的表征？」朱莉娅嚷嚷累了，笑起来。「可不，」她说，「亏你知道：你若体体面面地上我这儿来，我准热热闹闹地迎你。」她仍旧坐着，把土豆盆搁在那两条丰厚的大腿上，重新操办起来。路易斯乖乖地在她身旁坐下，小手轻轻挨着那大片洒满雀斑的胳膊。他说了对不起，又接着安抚她。他用上她教他的盖尔语单词，那些浪漫而温柔甜蜜的词。朱莉娅软了下来。她双手把他的脸转向自己，淘气地望着他：

===Original===
“Now what the divil is it ye want?”

“Julia, tell me a fairy story, won’t you? Just a little one, won’t you, Julia?”

“Divil a fairy tale there’ll be told this day! Tell me about Boston. I’ve a brother working there. I want ye to find how he’s getting on. His name’s Eugene Head. He’s younger than meself, he’s only here wan year. He’s tendin’ bar in a saloon on Tremont Street near King’s Chapel. I’ve heard he’s steady and don’t drink; and I’ve heard, too, that he knocks down quite a bit. Naw! I don’t mean that he knocks down people. I shouldn’t be talking such things t’ye anyway. It’s sorry I am I said a word. But Boston is a hell ye know.”

===Chinese===
「现在，你到底要什么？」

「朱莉娅，给我讲个童话吧，好不好？就讲一个小小的，好不好，朱莉娅？」

「今天一个童话也讲不成了！跟我讲讲波士顿吧。我有个兄弟在那儿做工。我要你去打听打听他过得怎么样。他叫尤金·海德（Eugene Head）。他比我小，到这儿才一年。他在特里蒙特街（Tremont Street）上、国王教堂（King's Chapel）附近的一家酒馆里当酒保。我听说他为人稳当，不喝酒；还听说他『私扣』下不少钱。不！我不是说他把人撂倒。这些事我本来就不该讲给你听。说漏了嘴，我真懊悔。可你要知道，波士顿是个地狱。」

===Original===
Then Louis opened the subject nearest his heart. He told her all about the soldiers in faded blue, and the wives and children hanging to them. What did it all mean? Why was it so sad; why did he have to cry?

“Well, Louis dear, ye know war’s a sad business; those men ye saw had just been mustered out of the army; they were good fighting men, but all tired out. From the shawls the women wore and the dirty childer, I know the whole crowd was Irish and poor; and as everyone knows, the Irish won the war. Think of it! Holy Virgin!﻿—the Irish fighting for the naygers! What will it be next time?”

“But, Julia, what was it all for? What was back of it all?”

===Chinese===
于是路易斯把贴着心口的那个话题打开了。他把穿褪色蓝军装的士兵、挂在士兵们身上的妻小，一五一十地讲给她听。这一切到底意味着什么？为什么这样悲伤；为什么他非哭不可？

「嗐，亲爱的路易斯，你知道，打仗是桩悲伤的营生；你见的那些人，才从军队里退了伍；他们是能打的好汉子，只是全都累垮了。从那些女人裹的头巾和那些脏孩子看，我知道那一整伙人都是爱尔兰人，都是穷人；再说人人都知道，这场仗是爱尔兰人打赢的。想想吧！圣母玛利亚啊——爱尔兰人竟替黑鬼打仗！下一回又该替谁打呢？」

「可是，朱莉娅，这一切究竟是为了什么？这一切背后又是什么？」

===Original===
“I’ll not be telling ye what was back of it all, though well I know. I’ll waste no breath on one who has no moind. Besides you’re too young and ye have no education. Ye wouldn’t understand. Why the divil don’t ye stick hard to yer books, and learn? What in the name of all the saints d’ye think your father is spending his good money on ye furr? Filling yer belly with food, giving ye a good, clane bed to sleep in, putting nice clothes on ye, buying ye books, except that he wants ye to have an education? The Irish are proud of education, and yer father’s a proud man, and he wants to be proud of his son. In God’s name why don’t ye do yure share? Ye remember the tale, I told ye of the man who looked too long at the moon? It’s a tender heart indade, ye had likewise to be lookin’ at thim dirty childer hangin’ to the mithers’ skirts! It’s a big heart ye had and a fine education ye have that ye didn’t think at wanst whin ye saw thim that ye haven’t a care in the world, that ye’ve niver known rale hunger, niver a rale sorrow, niver a heartbreak, niver despair; niver heard the wolf bark at the doore as yer blood went cold! And yerself, Louis, wid yere big heart and small head couldn’t see with yer own eyes and without any books at all, that thim very childer was part of what as ye say lies behind it all? God! me heart aches in the tellin; for the min ye saw come back wuz not all the min that wint out; but I’m through. I’ll tell ye no more of what lies behind it all; but I’ll tell ye some more about education, for I want to knock a bit of sinse into yure empty skull. Yere all sintiment, Louis, and no mercy. You’ve kissed the Blarney Stone right well, and ye kicked the milking stool from under me.

===Chinese===
「它背后是什么，我虽然一清二楚，却不打算告诉你。我才不把口舌浪费在一个没脑子的人身上。再说你太年轻，又没受过教育。你不会懂的。你到底为什么不肯死死咬住你的书本，学出个样来？你说，看在众位圣徒的份上，你父亲把辛苦钱花在你身上是为了什么？往你肚子里填饭，给你一张干净的好床睡，往你身上穿好衣裳，给你买书——除了要你受教育，还能为什么？爱尔兰人以教育为荣，你父亲是个心气高的人，他指望能为儿子骄傲。看在上帝份上，你为什么不尽自己那一份力？你还记得我给你讲过的那个人的故事吗——那个把月亮看得太久的人？你这颗心可真软，居然也要盯着那些吊在妈妈裙边的脏孩子看！好大的心肠，好棒的教养——见了他们，竟没有一下子想到：你是世上最没有牵挂的人，从没挨过一场真正的饿，从没受过一场真正的悲哀，从没有过心碎，从没陷过绝望；从没在你血都凉了的时候，听狼在门口嚎！而你这个人呢，路易斯，凭那颗大心肠和那个小脑袋，竟不能用自己的眼睛看——一页书都用不着——看不出那些孩子恰恰是你所说的『这一切背后』的一部分？老天！讲着讲着，我的心都疼了；因为你看见回来的那些人，并不是出去的那些人的全部；可我讲完了。它背后是什么，我不再告诉你了；不过教育我还要再讲一点，因为我要往你那空脑壳里敲进几分道理。你整个儿是感情，路易斯，一点狠劲也没有。你准是把布拉尼巧言石（Blarney Stone）亲了个十足，却一脚把我脚底下的挤奶凳踢飞了。

===Original===
“Now the story I’m to tell ye I got from one of me girl friends whose brother said he knew the man by reputayshun, and that he came from County Kerry where the Lakes of Killarney a’re I’ve told ye so mooch about, and I suppose ye’ve forgotten it all; and faith, I have me doubts, with yere scatter brains if ye can say fer a truth wither Ireland’s this side o’ the water or the other. Now its not meself as’ll make a short story long nor a long story short, so I’ll tell it in the words I heard it.

“This man from Kerry was in some way connected with the army, as most of the Irish were, for they’re natural fighting min from the oldest times. And wan day as he was out a-walking fer his health, and faring to and fro, he came upon a blanket lying on the ground; and at once he picked it up and with great loud laughter he sed, sed he: Sure I’ve found me blanket with me name upon it: *U* fer Patrick and *S* for McCarty; sure edication’s a foine thing, as me faather before me wud say.”

“Oh, Julia, I don’t believe that’s true. That’s just another Irish yarn.”

===Chinese===
「我现在要给你讲的这个故事，是从我一个女朋友那里听来的，她兄弟说，论名声他认得故事里这个人，还说此人老家在克里郡（County Kerry）——基拉尼湖（Lakes of Killarney）就在那个郡，那湖我跟你讲过不知多少回，我想你也忘了个精光；说真的，我满心犯疑：凭你那副散神的脑子，你到底说得出爱尔兰在大洋的这一边还是那一边。把短故事拉长、把长故事掐短，都不是我的做派；我就照我听来的原话讲给你听。」

「这个克里郡人跟军队多少沾着些关系——爱尔兰人多半如此，因为他们自古就是天生的战汉子。有一天，他出门散步，为的是锻炼身体，东走走，西逛逛，撞见一条毯子躺在地上；他当即把它拾起来，放声大笑，说道，他说道：可不，我可找着自己的毯子了，上头还写着我的名字：*U* 就是帕特里克（Patrick），*S* 就是麦卡蒂（McCarty）；教育真是个好东西，从前我爹也常这么说。」

「哦，朱莉娅，我不信这是真的。这不过是又一个爱尔兰牛皮罢了。」

===Original===
“Will, maybe it isn’t true and maybe it’s just a yarn; but I belave it’s true and I want to till ye this; the man from Kerry had a rale edication. Ye may think I’m a-jokin’ now, but when ye get older and have more sinse ye’ll be noticin’ that that’s the way everywan rades; and the higher educated they are, the more they rade just as Pat McCarty did, and add some fancy flourishes of their own. Now run along and carry in the wood, and do the chures. Me two feets is sore wid me weight. And take along the pans and the stool as ye go. I suppose it’s the whole batch of yees I’ll have to be feedin’; and I’ve a blister on me small toe, and me back is broke with handlin’ the wash tubs; an’ it’s little patience I have with ye, furr ye don’t seem to learn in school or out, and yit, be the powers, ye ask some mighty quare questions for a lad, so I suppose there’s something in the back of yer head that makes yer father support ye when ye ought to be wurkin’.”

===Chinese===
「唔，也许不是真的，也许只是个牛皮；可我相信它是真的；而且我要告诉你这个：那个克里郡人有的可是真正的学问。你当我这会儿是在说笑，等你长大了、多几分见识，你就会留意到：人人读书，都是帕特·麦卡蒂（Pat McCarty）那个读法；而且书读得越高的人，越是读成帕特·麦卡蒂那样，还要添上些自家花哨的花腔。现在快去吧，把柴火抱进屋，把杂活干了。我这两只脚，让我这份分量压得生疼。走的时候顺手把盆和凳子捎上。我看今天要我喂的就是你们这一整窝；我小脚趾上起了泡，搬洗衣盆又把我的腰都压断了；我对你是没什么耐心的，因为你在学校里也好、出了学校也好，好像什么也不肯学；可是，凭老天爷起誓，你这么个小小子，问出的问题倒出奇地刁钻，所以我想，你后脑壳里总归有点什么东西，才叫你父亲肯养着你——本来这年纪你早该去做工了。」

===Original===
And thus Julia grumbled on to the kitchen door and Louis did the chores. But his heart was not in them. Julia had told the story mockingly. She seemed to leave in it somewhere a sting he could feel but could not understand; and he mused as to what might perhaps be behind Julia, Irish to the core. She had set him vibrating at the suggestion of an unseen power and he became rigid in his resolve to penetrate the mystery that seemed to lie back of the tale she told.

===Chinese===
朱莉娅就这样一路咕哝着到厨房门口去了，路易斯便去做杂活。可他的心不在活计上。朱莉娅讲那个故事，是带着嘲弄讲的。她似乎在故事里的什么地方留了一根刺，他感觉得到，却捉摸不透；他寻思着，这个彻头彻尾的爱尔兰女人朱莉娅的背后，兴许藏着些什么。她在一个看不见的力量的暗示之下拨响了他，使他震颤；他于是横下一条心，定要钻透她所讲的故事背后仿佛横陈着的那桩秘密。


---
===Original===
Later on, say about the age of twelve, this same boy, to his own surprise, became aware that he had become interested in buildings; and over one building in particular he began to rave, as he detached it from the rest and placed it in his wonder-world. It stood at the northeast corner of Tremont and Boylston Streets. It was a Masonic Temple built of hewn granite, light gray in tone and joyous of aspect.

===Chinese===
后来，比如说，十二岁上下吧，还是这个男孩，叫他自家吃了一惊：他发觉自己对建筑物起了兴趣；对其中一座，他尤其开始如痴如狂，把它从其余房子里单拎出来，安放进他的奇境世界。它矗立在特里蒙特街与博伊尔斯顿街路口的东北角上。那是一座共济会圣殿（Masonic Temple），用凿方的花岗岩砌成，色调浅灰，神采飞扬。

===Original===
Boston, as a conglomerate of buildings, had depressed Louis Sullivan continuously since he became engulfed in it. These structures uttered to him as in chorus a stifling negation, a vast No!﻿—to his yea-cry for the lighthearted. In their varied utterance, they were to him unanimous in that they denied the flowers of the field. Some were austere, some gave forth an offensive effluvium of respectability, some fronted the crowded street as though they had always been there and the streets had come later; some seemed to thank God that they were not as other buildings, while others sighed: “I am aweary, aweary.” Most of them were old and some very new; and individually they impressed Louis, in their special ways, as of an uncanny particularity. He seemed to feel them as physiognomies, as presences, sometimes even as personalities; thus the State House with its golden dome seemed to him a thin, mean, stingy old woman; while Park Street Church seemed to tower as a loyal guardian above its ancient graveyard, and as friendly monitor of the crowds below. And one day as they looked at Faneuil Hall, Grandpa said of it: “The Wild Ass of the City stamps above its head but cannot break its sleep.” This sounded thrilling and imaginary to Louis, like a wild thing out of Julia’s land of enchantment; but Grandpa said he got it out of a book and that its meaning was too deep for the boy﻿—that he was talking to himself.

===Chinese===
作为一个建筑的集合体，波士顿自打路易斯·沙利文陷身其中以来，就一直叫他消沉。这些构筑物齐声向他发出一种令人窒息的否定，一声浩大的「不！」——否掉他为轻快生灵发出的那一声欢呼。它们的言辞尽管五花八门，在这一点上却众口一词：它们否定野地的花。有的严峻，有的散发出一股叫人反胃的体面气味，有的面对拥挤的街道摆出老资格的架子，仿佛它们自古便在此地，街道倒是后来的；有的似乎感谢上帝，庆幸自己不像别的房子；有的则叹息：「我倦了，倦了。」它们多半老旧，也有一些崭新；而每一座都以其独特的方式，给路易斯留下一种诡异的个殊之感。他仿佛把它们感受成一张张面相，一个个临场，有时甚至是人格：于是那金顶的州议会大厦（State House），在他眼里像一个瘦削、刻薄、小气的老太婆；而公园街教堂（Park Street Church）却像一位忠实的守护者，耸峙在它古老的墓园之上，又像下方人海的一位友善的班长。有一天，两人望着法纳尔大厅（Faneuil Hall），外祖父这样评说道：「城中的野驴在它头顶上跺蹄，却惊不破它的酣眠。」这话在路易斯听来惊心动魄、惝恍迷离，像朱莉娅的魔境里跑出来的一头野物；可外祖父说，他是从一本书里得来的，这话的意思对孩子太深了——说他是在自言自语。

===Original===
Thus buildings had come to speak to Louis Sullivan in their many jargons. Some said vile things, some said prudent things, some said pompous things, but none said noble things. His history book told him that certain buildings were to be revered, but the buildings themselves did not tell him so, for he saw them with a fresh eye, an ignorant eye, an eye unprepared for sophistries, and a mind empty of dishonesty. Nevertheless, a vague sense of doleful community among buildings slowly suffused him. They began to appear within his consciousness as a separate world in their way; a world of separated things seemed, in unison, to pass on to him a message from an unseen power. Thus immersed, he returned again and again to his wonder-building, the single one that welcomed him, the solitary one that gave out a perfume of romance, that radiated joy, that seemed fresh and full of laughter. How it gleamed and glistened in the afternoon sunlight. How beautiful were its arches, how dainty its pinnacles; how graceful the tourelle on the corner, rising as if by itself, higher and higher, like a lily stem, to burst at last into a wondrous cluster of flowering pinnacles and a lovely, pointed finial. Thus Louis raved. It has been often said that love is blind! If Louis chose to liken this new idol of his heart unto a certain graceful elm tree, the pulchritudinous virgin of an earlier day, surely that was his affair, not ours; for he who says that love is blind may be himself the blind﻿—and love clairvoyant.

===Chinese===
就这样，众建筑开始用它们五花八门的行话，对路易斯·沙利文说起话来。有的说下流话，有的说精明话，有的说浮夸话，却没有一座说高贵话。他的历史书告诉他，有些建筑当受敬畏；可这些建筑本身并没有对他这样说；因为他看它们，用的是一双新鲜的眼睛，一双无知的眼睛，一双未预备好接受诡辩的眼睛，和一颗不藏奸诈的心。尽管如此，一种众建筑之间悲戚的共同体之感，慢慢地浸透了他。它们开始在他的意识里显现为一个自成一格的世界；一个由彼此隔绝的事物组成的世界，似乎齐声向他递来一个出自无形力量的口信。他就这样浸在其中，一回又一回地回到他那座奇境之楼——唯一欢迎他的一座，唯一散发浪漫芬芳的一座；它焕发着欢乐，显得清新，满蕴笑声。午后的阳光里，它是何等熠熠生辉。它的拱何等美，它的尖顶何等精巧；转角上那座小塔楼（tourelle）何等优雅，仿佛自行拔地而起，愈升愈高，像一茎百合的花梗，终于迸发成一簇华美的开花尖塔，和一枚可爱的、挺秀的塔尖饰。路易斯就这样神魂颠倒。常言道，爱情是盲目的！倘若路易斯偏要把他心尖上这尊新偶像，比作某棵婀娜的榆树，比作早年间那位秀色动人的少女，那毕竟是他自家的事，与我们无干；因为说爱情盲目的人，自己说不定才是瞎子——而爱情却明察千里。


---
===Original===
One day, on Commonwealth Avenue, as Louis was strolling, he saw a large man of dignified bearing, with beard, top hat, frock coat, come out of a nearby building, enter his carriage and signal the coachman to drive on. The dignity was unmistakable, all men of station in Boston were dignified; sometimes insistently so, but Louis wished to know who and what was behind the dignity. So he asked one of the workmen, who said:

===Chinese===
有一天，在联邦大道（Commonwealth Avenue）上，路易斯正闲步溜达，看见一位身材魁伟、举止庄重的男人从附近一座建筑里走出来：蓄着胡须，戴高顶礼帽，穿燕尾礼服，登上自家的马车，示意车夫驱车前行。那份气派的庄重毋庸置疑——波士顿凡是有身份的男人个个庄重，有时还刻意坚持；可路易斯想知道的，是这份庄重背后，是谁、又是什么。于是他问一位工人，工人说：

===Original===
“Why he’s the archeetec of this building.”

“Yes? and what is an archeetec, the owner?”

“Naw; he’s the man what drawed the plans for this building.”

“What! What’s that you say: drawed the plans for this building?”

“Sure. He lays out the rooms on paper, then makes a picture of the front, and we do the work under our own boss, but the archeetec’s the boss of everybody.”

===Chinese===
「嗐，他就是这座楼的『阿基提克』（archeetec）。」

「是吗？阿基提克是干什么的，是业主吗？」

「不是；他就是给这座楼画图纸的那个人。」

「什么！你说什么：给这座楼画图纸？」

「可不。他在纸上一间一间把屋子摆出来，再画一张正面的图样；活儿是我们跟着自家工头干的，可『阿基提克』是所有人的总头儿。」

===Original===
Louis was amazed. So this was the way: The workmen stood behind their boss, their boss stood behind the archeetec﻿—but the building stood in front of them all. He asked the man if there had been an “archeetec” for the Masonic Temple, and the man said: “Sure, there’s an archeetec for every building.” Louis was incredulous, but if it were true it was glorious news. How great, how wonderful a man must have been the “archeetec” of his beloved temple! So he asked the man how the architect made the outside of the temple and the man said: “Why, he made it out of his head; and he had books besides.” The “books besides” repelled Louis: anybody could do that; but the “made it out of his head” fascinated him.

===Chinese===
路易斯惊呆了。原来如此：工人们站在自家工头背后，工头站在「阿基提克」背后——而那座建筑，则矗立在他们所有人前面。他问那人，共济会圣殿当年是否也有过一位「阿基提克」，那人说：「可不，哪座楼没有个『阿基提克』呢。」路易斯将信将疑；可这事若是真的，便是天大的好消息。他心爱的圣殿的那位「阿基提克」，该是何等伟大、何等奇妙的人物！于是他问那人，「建筑师」是怎么造出圣殿外表的，那人说：「嗐，是他打自己脑袋里造出来的；另外他还备着书。」那「另外还备着书」叫路易斯生反感：这个谁都会；而「打脑袋里造出来」却把他迷住了。

===Original===
How could a man make so beautiful a building out of his head? What a great man he must be; what a wonderful man. Then and there Louis made up his mind to become an architect and make beautiful buildings “out of his head.” He confined this resolve to the man. But the man said:

===Chinese===
一个人怎么能凭脑袋造出这么美的一座建筑？那得是多伟大的人物啊；多奇妙的人物啊。路易斯当机立断：要成为一名建筑师（architect），「凭脑袋」造出美丽的建筑。这个决心，他只透露给了那个人。可那人说：

===Original===
“I don’t know about that. You got to know a lot first. You got to have an education. Of course us mechanics has our books too. That’s the way we lay out stairs, rails and things like that. But you got to have more brains, more experience, more education and more books, especially more books, to be an archeetec. Can yer father keep yer at school long enough?”

“Yes; he says he’ll keep me at school until I’m twenty-one if I wish.”

“Well, that being so, yer may stand a chance of coming out ahead, but I honestly don’t think yer have the right kind of brains. That faraway look in yer eyes makes me think yer won’t be practical, and y’ *got* to be practical. I’m a foreman and that’s as far as I’ll get, and I’ve done work under a good many archeetecs; and some of them that’s practical ain’t much else. And some of them that’s fairly practical has so much education from books that they gets awful fussy, and are hard to get on with.” The latter part of this monologue interested Louis rather faintly, for he’d made up his mind. He thanked the foreman who said in parting: “Well, I dunno﻿—mebbe.”

===Chinese===
「这我说不好。你得先懂好多东西。你得受一份教育。当然喽，我们干手艺的也有我们的书。摆弄楼梯啊、栏杆啊这些个，就是这个路数。可要当『阿基提克』，你得多些脑子、多些阅历、多些教育，还要多些书——尤其是书。你父亲供得起你在学校里念那么久吗？」

「供得起；他说只要我愿意，他供我念到二十一岁。」

「唔，要是这样，你说不定还有指望出头；不过老实说，我看你的脑子不是那块料。你眼睛里那种望得远远的出神劲儿，叫我觉得你务实不起来，可你*非*务实不可。我是个工头，这辈子也就到这步了；我给好些个『阿基提克』做过工；他们里头有些人挺务实，别的不敢恭维。还有些人马马虎虎算得上务实，书念得太多，讲究得吓人，不好相处。」这段独白的后半截，路易斯听得兴味寥寥，因为他主意已定了。他谢过工头；工头临别说：「嗐，说不准——兴许吧。」


---
===Original===
Shortly before his father left Boston for Chicago, Louis confided to him his heart’s desire. The father seemed pleased, greatly pleased, that his son’s ambition was centering on something definite. He “allowed,” as they used to say in New England, that Architecture was a great art, the mother of all the arts, and its practice a noble profession, adding a word or two about Michelangelo. Then he offered a counter proposal that made Louis gasp. It was none other than this: That Louis was fond of the farm and the open, that he had shown himself a natural farmer with ready mastery of detail of common farming. Why not go further. After proper preparation he would send Louis to an agricultural college, he said, and thus Louis would be equipped as a *scientific farmer*. Louis was dazzled. The word *scientific* was electrical. Before him arose the woods, the fields, the cattle, the crops, the great grand open world as a narcotic phantom of delight. The father was eloquent concerning blooded stock, plant cross-fertilization, the chemistry of soils and fertilizers, underdrainage, and so forth; Louis wavered. He sat long in silence, on his father’s knee, lost to the world. Then he said: “**No:** *I have made up my mind.*”

===Chinese===
就在父亲离开波士顿、动身去芝加哥（Chicago）前不久，路易斯向他吐露了心底的愿望。父亲似乎很高兴，大为高兴，因为儿子的雄心正落在一桩明确的事情上。用新英格兰人当年的话头说，他「认可」了：建筑是一门伟大的艺术，是一切艺术之母，其实践是一桩高贵的职业；还添上一两句关于米开朗基罗（Michelangelo）的话。接着他提出一个反建议，叫路易斯倒抽一口气。原来不外乎这个：路易斯既喜爱农庄与旷野，又已经显出是个天生的庄稼人，寻常农活的细节一上手就通。何不再进一步呢。他说，经过适当的预备，他要把路易斯送进一所农业大学；这样，路易斯就会装备成一位*科学的*庄稼人（scientific farmer）。路易斯眼花缭乱了。*科学的*这个词像电一样击中了他。他眼前升起树林、田野、牛群、庄稼，那浩浩荡荡的露天大千世界，化作一片令人迷醉的欢乐幻象。父亲讲良种牲畜，讲植物的异花授粉，讲土壤与肥料的化学，讲地下排水，讲得口若悬河；路易斯动摇了。他久久坐在父亲膝上，默然无语，浑然忘世。然后他说：「**不**：*我主意已定。*」

===Original===
And thus it was agreed that Louis should remain in Boston to complete his General Education; after that to a Technical School; and, some day﻿—Abroad.

===Chinese===
于是就这样讲定了：路易斯应留在波士顿完成他的普通学业（General Education）；此后进一所技术学校；而有朝一日——出国。


---
===Original===
During the years preceding his decision, Louis, in practice, was essentially scatterbrained. His many and varied activities and preoccupations, physical, mental, emotional, his keen power of observation, his insatiable hunger for knowledge at first hand, his temperamental responses to externals, his fleeting mystic trances, his utterly childlike flashes of intuition, his welcoming of new worlds, opening upon him one after another, his perception that they must grow larger and larger, his imagination, unknown to him as such; all these things, impenetrable to him in their vast significance within the gigantic and diverse world of men and things and thoughts and acts, a world as yet sealed tight to him; all these things seemed to exist within him formless, aimless, a disconnected miscellany rich in impulse but devoid of order, of form, of intention.

===Chinese===
在作出这个决定之前的那些年里，路易斯实际上是个不折不扣的散漫性子。他那许多形形色色的活动与营营念念——身体的、头脑的、情绪的；他那敏锐的观察力；他对第一手知识永不餍足的饥渴；他对身外之物的气质性反应；他那倏忽来去的神秘出神；他那十足孩子气的直觉闪光；他对一个个新世界接踵向他敞开的欢迎；他觉出这些世界必将愈展愈大；还有他的想象力——他本人并不识得它为想象力：所有这一切，在人与物、思想与作为那个巨大而繁杂的世界里所具有的浩大意义，于他深不可测——那个世界当时对他还封得严严实实；所有这一切，似乎都在他之内无定形、无目标地存着，一堆互不相干的杂物，冲动丰盈，却全无秩序、全无形式、全无意向。

===Original===
Yet this was not precisely the fact. It was an ostensible fact, objectively, a non-fact, subjectively; for a presiding order, a primal impulse, was governing and shaping him through his own marvel at manifestations of power, his constant wonder at what men could do; at men’s power to do what they willed to do; and deeper than this moved a power he had heard in the Song of Spring, and which awakened within the glory of the sunrise.

===Chinese===
然而这并非确切的实情。就客观而言，它是一个表面的实情；就主观而言，它是一个非实情；因为有一种统御的秩序，一股原始的冲动，正借着他自己对力量之显现的惊叹、借着他对人所能为之事的经久惊奇——对人行其意愿之力的惊奇——在统摄他、塑造他；而在这之下更深的地方，还涌动着一股力量，那是他曾在春之歌里听见过的，是在日出的荣光里被唤醒过的。

===Original===
All this was vague enough, to be sure, but his memory was becoming tenacious and retroactive. Little given to introspection, as such, he was in daily conduct and appearance much like any boy, though perhaps he had a more stubborn will than is usual. His aversion from schools and books had been normal enough, becaused they failed in appeal. Nevertheless he began to swing around to an idea that there might be something useful to him in books, regardless of teachers; and this idea was vivified when he was transferred to the new Rice Grammar School building, the lightness and brightness and cleanliness of which put him at once in exceeding good humor.

===Chinese===
诚然，这一切都够含糊的；可他的记忆正变得执拗而能回溯。他素来不大内省；在日常的行止与外表上，他同随便哪个男孩大同小异——或许意志比常人更倔几分。他对学校与书本的反感本是够寻常的，因为它们招不起他的兴味。尽管如此，他开始转上一个念头：书里头也许有于他有用的东西，这与老师不相干；而当他转入新落成的赖斯文法学校（Rice Grammar School）大楼时，这个念头一下子活了——那楼房的轻盈、明亮与洁净，顿时叫他心绪大开。

===Original===
True to form he reacted to these cheerful externals, and at once became filled with a new eagerness. A cloud seemed to pass away from his brain, a certain inhibition seemed to relax its hold upon him. As by the waving of a magic wand, he made a sudden swerve in his course, and became an earnest, almost fanatical student of books, in the light and joy of the new schoolhouse. Teachers were secondary; and in habit he became almost a recluse. For the idea had clarified that in books might be found a concentration, an increase in power; that books might be﻿—and he later said they were﻿—storehouses of what men had done, an explanation of their power to do, and that the specific knowledge stored within them might be used as tools of the mind, as men used tools of the hand. Louis saw consequences with extreme rapidity and daring once the first light of an initial idea broke upon him. His enthusiasms were pramagtic. He lost no time, once he saw an objective. His grammar-book in particular fascinated him. Here for the first time in all his schooling a light began to shine within a book and illumine his brain. Here opened up to him, ever more startling, ever more inspiriting, the structure of the language he spoke; its whys and wherefores. Here opened, ever enlarging, a world of things said, and to be said. The rigid rules became plastic as he progressed, then they became fluent; grammar passed into romance; a dead book became a living thing. He could not go fast enough. When would he reach the end?

===Chinese===
他本性不改，对这些明朗的身外之物起了反应，顿时满怀一股新的热望。一片云仿佛从他脑中散去，某种抑制仿佛松开了攥着他的手。如魔杖一挥，他的行程猛地一转，在那新校舍的光明与欢乐里，成了一个认真的、几乎狂热的书本学生。老师们退居其次；他在习惯上几乎成了个隐士。因为那个念头已经澄清：书里或许藏着一种凝聚，一种力量的增长；书或许是——他后来说，它们确是——人类所行之事的库房，是其行力的一番解释；而书内储存的种种特定知识，或许可用作心智的工具，正如人使用手的工具。路易斯一旦望见一个初始念头破晓而来的第一线光，便以极端的迅捷与大胆看清它的重重后果。他的热望是务实的。他一见目标，便一刻也不耽搁。尤其是那本文法书迷住了他。在他全部的求学经历里，这是头一回，一盏灯在一本书里亮起，照亮他的头脑。这里向他打开的，愈来愈惊人、愈来愈振拔，是他日日说着的那种语言的构造，它的所以然与何所然。这里打开的，是一个不断扩大的世界：已经说出的、与有待说出的事物。僵死的规则随他的进步变得柔韧，继而变得流畅；文法化入了浪漫；一本死书成了活物。他嫌自己走得不够快。什么时候才能走到头呢？

===Original===
And as the end approached nearer and nearer, there came forth from the book as a living presence, as a giant from the world of enchantment, with shining visage, man’s power of speech. Louis saw it all, but it left him feeble. He had taken grammar at one dose. As usual his imagination had far outsped any possibility of reasonable accomplishment. For Louis, as usual, *saw too much at one time*. He saw, at a glance, ends that would require a lifetime of disciplined endeavor to reach. And so, in a measure, it was with his other studies, though not so ardently. There was little romance to be found in his arithmetic. It was in the main material and philistine. Yet he saw use in it. He accepted it as a daily task and plodded. It was not his fault but his misfortune that it was handed to him dry. Geography he took to kindly. He could visualize it as a diagram and it extended, on paper, his boundaries far and wide. Topographically and racially he could not see into it, even though he was informed, for instance, that the Japanese and Chinese were half-civilized. He asked what civilized meant and was told that we were civilized. There were various other things in the geography that were not clear; he found difficulty in making images of what he saw in the book. In his history book he was lied to shamefully, but he did not know it. Anyway, he had to take some things on faith. The history book did not interest him greatly because the people described did not seem human like the people he knew, and the story was mostly about wars. He got the idea that patriotism always meant fighting, and that the other side was always in the wrong.

===Chinese===
而随着尽头愈来愈近，从书里走出了一个大活人般的临在，像从魔境世界里走来的一位巨人，容光焕发——人的言语之力。路易斯把这一切都看在眼里，可它叫他浑身发软。他把文法一口吞服了。同往常一样，他的想象力远远跑过了任何合理成就的可能。因为路易斯照例*一次看得太多*。他一眼望见的，是需要一生自律奋斗方能抵达的种种终端。在别的功课上，情形大抵也如此，只是不那么炽烈。他的算术里寻不出多少浪漫。它大体是物质的、市侩的。然而他看出它有用。他把它当作日课领受，埋头苦干。它递到他手里时是干的，这不是他的过，而是他的不幸。地理他倒待见。他能把它看成一帧图解；它在纸上把他的疆界向四面八方推得辽远。论地形与种族，他看不进去，尽管人家告知他，比方说，日本人和中国人是半开化的。他问「开化」是什么意思，得到的回答是：我们是开化的。地理书里还有种种别的叫人糊涂的东西；他难于把书中所见在头脑里成像。历史书里，他被无耻地欺哄着，可他自己并不知道。反正，有些事情他只好凭信心领受。历史书不能叫他大感兴趣，因为书中所写的人物，不像他认识的那些人一样像活人，而故事又多半是打仗。他由此得了个观念：爱国总是意味着打仗，而对方总是理亏的。

===Original===
As to compositions, the pupils had to write one every so often, on a given topic. The first subject for Louis was “The Battle of Hastings.” He went at this dolefully, sought refuge in the encyclopedia, and in wabbly English produced a two-page essay weakly-hesitant and valueless; a mere task. He was marked low. The next subject was “A Winter Holiday in Boston.” Louis filled the air with snowflakes, merry bells, laughter, movement and cross movement, amusing episodes and accidents, all joyous, all lively. In simple boyish English, he made a hearty story of it, a word-picture; yes, the suggestion even of a prose poem, for it had structure. Within it was a dominant idea of winter that conveyed a sensation of color, of form. Louis was happy. He had hard work to confine himself to four pages. He was marked high. He was commended before the class. But the topics seldom fired him; as a rule they were academic, arid, artificial, having no relation to his life experiences, concerning which he might have said something worthwhile had he been given the chance. Another feature of the curriculum that went against the grain with Louis was the course in declamation, or “speaking pieces.” For Louis had a streak of bashfulness in his makeup, which, though invisible in his former street fights, came painfully into view when he must face the class and “speak out loud.” The ensuing torture of self-consciousness made him angry and rebellious. Besides, he had his opinions concerning various “pieces” and was not in the least backward in venturing them. He ridiculed the “Village Blacksmith” unmercifully.

===Chinese===
至于作文，学生们每隔一阵就得按指定的题目写一篇。路易斯领到的第一个题目是《黑斯廷斯之战》（The Battle of Hastings）。他愁眉苦脸地着手，躲进百科全书里求庇护，用歪歪扭扭的英文产出一篇两页的文章，怯弱而无力，毫无价值；纯粹是一桩差事。他得了个低分。下一个题目是《波士顿的冬日假期》（A Winter Holiday in Boston）。路易斯让满纸雪花飞舞，欢乐的铃铛、笑声、往复与交错的运动、逗趣的插曲与意外，样样欢腾，样样活泼。以朴素的男孩英文，他把它写成一篇酣畅的故事，一幅文字画；是的，甚至有了散文诗的意味，因为它有结构。内里有一个统御的冬日意念，传递出一种色彩感、一种形式感。路易斯快乐极了。他费了好大的劲，才把自己约束在四页之内。他得了高分，当着全班受了称赞。可这些题目很少点燃他；它们照例是学究气的、干瘪的、造作的，同他的生活经验毫无干系——倘蒙机会，对那些经验，他本可以说出些值得一听的东西。功课里另一桩与路易斯天性相拗的，是朗诵课，或曰「背诵选段」。因为路易斯的性子里有一缕羞怯，从前街头打架时看不见它，可一旦要他面对全班「放声讲出来」，它便痛苦地现了形。随之而来的自觉之刑叫他恼怒、叫他反叛。何况他对种种「选段」自有见解，发表起来也一点不怯。他把《乡村铁匠》（The Village Blacksmith）嘲笑了个够，毫不留情。

===Original===
His pet aversion was “Old Ironsides,” and it befell one day that he was to speak this very piece. As he approached the platform, he saw red; the class was invisible, no bashfulness now; teacher even, scarcely visible. His mind was made up; he mounted the platform, faced about; and in instant desperate acrimony, he shrieked: “*Ay, tear her tattered ensign down!!!!!!*” The class roared; teacher stopped him at once; sent him to his seat. She left the room. Louis boiled in his seat. In the hubbub he heard: “Now yer going to get it.” “Serves yer right.” “Yer made a fool of teacher.” “Serves yer right.” “Fatty’ll fix yer.” The teacher, Miss Blank, returning, stilled the storm, and said calmly: “Louis Sullivan, you are wanted in Mr. Wheelock’s office.” Mr. Wheelock, head master﻿—called “Fatty” for short﻿—was round, of middle height, kindly, with something of the cherub in his face. He wore a blond beard, had rather high color, merry blue eyes, a full forehead, sparsely covered with hair. He appeared not over thirty-five, had served in the army, and was judicial, considerate and human in his dealings.

===Chinese===
他的心头之恨是《老铁甲舰》（Old Ironsides）；事有凑巧，一天偏偏轮到他讲这一篇。他朝讲台走去，眼前发红；全班不见了，此刻再无羞怯；连老师也几乎看不见了。他主意已定；他登上讲台，转过身来；在一瞬孤注一掷的狂怒里，尖声嘶喊道：「*唉，扯下她那破碎的军旗吧！！！！！！*」全班哄堂大笑；老师当即喝止，把他打发回座位，随后离开了教室。路易斯在座位上煎熬。喧嚷声中他听见：「这下你可要挨收拾了。」「活该。」「你把老师当傻子耍了。」「活该。」「『胖子』要来收拾你。」老师布兰克小姐（Miss Blank）回到教室，平息了风暴，平静地说：「路易斯·沙利文，惠洛克先生（Mr. Wheelock）办公室有请。」惠洛克先生是校长——简称「胖子」——身材圆滚，中等个头，和气，脸上带几分小天使的神气。他蓄一部淡黄色的胡须，脸色相当红润，一双快活的蓝眼睛，额头饱满，头发稀疏。看去不出三十五岁，从过军，处事公正、体恤而通达人情。

===Original===
As Louis entered he saw, not this Mr. Wheelock, but a Mr. Wheelock, gray of face, sinister of eye, holding in his left hand a long rattan. “Miss Blank tells me you have grossly insulted her before the class. What have you to say for yourself?”

===Chinese===
路易斯进得门来，看见的不是这位惠洛克先生，而是另一位惠洛克先生：脸色灰败，目光阴狠，左手握着一根长长的藤条。「布兰克小姐告诉我，你当着全班的面粗暴地侮辱了她。你自己还有什么可说的？」

===Original===
Louis was fearless and aggressive by nature. He had crossed his Rubicon. He made a manly apology, wholly sincere as regarded Miss Blank. This cleared the ground but not the issue. He saw the rattan, and with steady eye and nerve he quickly wove about it his plan of action. The rod should never touch him; it was to be a battle of wits. He boldly made his opening with the statement that he regarded the poem as bunkum. Mr. Wheelock sneered. He then went on to take the poem to pieces, line by line, stanza by stanza. Mr. Wheelock looked puzzled; he eyed Louis quizzically. He edged about in his chair. Louis went on, more and more drastically. Mr. Wheelock’s eyes began to twinkle, calm returned to his face, he dropped the rod. He laughed heartily: “Where in the world did you dig that up?” Then Louis let go, he waxed eloquent, he spread out his views﻿—so long suppressed; he pleaded for the open, for honesty of thought for the lifting of a veil that hid things, for freedom of thought, for the right of interpretation, for freedom of utterance. He passionately unbosomed his longings. The head master, now sitting chin in hand, looked steadily at Louis, with grave, sad face. As Louis ceased, the master remained silent for a moment, then pulled himself together, relaxed, chuckled, and patting Louis on the shoulder said: “That was a pretty fine stump-speech, young man. When you got through with Holmes, you left his poem as tattered as his ensign. As for the rest: Irish accounts for that. I’m glad we had it out though. I might have thrashed you in anger. Go back to your class now, and hereafter be considerate of a woman’s feelings.” Louis returned to his room; before all the class he made full amends. Then, in his seat, he set to with a book. His plunge into grammar had not been in vain.

===Chinese===
路易斯生性无畏而好斗。他已渡过了自己的卢比孔河（Rubicon）。他作了一个有男子气的道歉，就布兰克小姐而言全然出于真诚。这清理了地面，却未了结争端。他看见那根藤条，便目光沉稳、心神不乱，飞快地围绕它织就了行动的计划：那根戒尺永远休想碰着他；这将是一场斗智。他大胆开局，宣称依他看那首诗纯属空话。惠洛克先生付之一哂。他接着把那首诗一行一行、一节一节地拆解开来。惠洛克先生显得困惑；他打趣般地打量路易斯，在椅子里挪来挪去。路易斯继续讲，愈讲愈峻急。惠洛克先生的眼睛开始发亮，平静回到了脸上，他把藤条放下了。他由衷地笑了：「你这都是从哪儿挖出来的？」于是路易斯开了闸，他口若悬河，铺展开自己久受压抑的种种见解；他为坦荡陈情，为思想的诚实陈情，为掀开遮蔽事物的帷幕陈情，为思想自由、为阐释的权利、为言论自由陈情。他激情满怀地倾吐了自己的渴望。校长手托下巴，面色凝重而悲哀，目不转睛地望着路易斯。路易斯说完了，校长默然片刻，而后振作起来，松快下来，轻声一笑，拍着路易斯的肩膀说：「这可真是一场不赖的竞选演说，年轻人。你把霍姆斯（Holmes）料理完，他这首诗就同他那面军旗一样，被你扯得千疮百孔了。至于其余：那是爱尔兰血统的账。不过我倒高兴，咱们把话说开了。刚才我本可以在火头上揍你一顿。现在回你的教室去，往后要体恤女人的感情。」路易斯回到自己的教室；当着全班，他作了充分的赔礼。然后，他在座位上埋头攻起书来。他那一头扎进文法的猛劲，没有白费。

===Original===
Thus Louis worked on and on, all by himself, as it were, digging into the solid vein of knowledge as a solitary miner digs; washing the alluvial sands of knowledge as a miner sifts﻿—a young prospector grub-staked by an absentee provider now settled on the shores of a vast Lake far in the West.

===Chinese===
就这样，路易斯独自一人干个不停，像个孤身的矿工，凿进知识坚实的矿脉；像矿工淘洗冲积的沙金一般，淘洗知识的沙——一个年轻的探矿人，由一位久不露面的供养人出资装备，而那位供养人，已定居在遥远的西方一片大湖的湖畔。

===Original===
Living again with his grandparents Louis felt at home once more. He had respites from the city bareness and baldness. He studied in the evenings, in the sitting room, unmindful of the family doings. He lost interest in playmates; waved aside all little girls as nuisances and inferior creatures﻿—they became nonexistent. He rose early, at all seasons and in all weathers, before the family were awake, walked the mile to the depot, took the train to Boston, walked a mile to breakfast and another mile to school. Many a night he was awakened by the rattling sash, and listened to the sharp wind moaning, groaning, shrieking, whistling through the crevices with many a siren rise and fall, from the depths of sorrow to the heights of madness, from double forte to piannissimo as this weird orchestra of the countryside lulled him again to sleep. And many a morning, in pitch darkness, he lit his little lamp, broke the skin of ice at the pitcher’s top, washed in arctic waters, donned his clothing, neatly folded over a chair as Grandmamma had taught him﻿—his stockings even, carefully turned in for orderliness, then left the house still in darkness and silence, to break his way, it may be, through fresh-fallen snow, knee-deep on the level, and as yet without a trail, his woolen cap drawn down, his woolen mittens well on, his books bound with a leather strap, held snug under the arm of his pea-jacket as the dim light at the depot shone nearer, and a distant double-toot announced the oncoming train, and the blinding headlight that shortly roared into view as he stood, waiting, on the platform.

===Chinese===
重新与外祖父外祖母住在一起，路易斯又有了在家的感觉。他从这座城市的赤裸与光秃中得了喘息。他晚间用功，坐在起居室里，不理会一家人的动静。他对玩伴失了兴趣；把所有的小姑娘当作讨厌的、低等的生物挥到一边——她们不复存在了。他起得早，无论什么季节、什么天气，赶在一家人醒来之前，走一英里到车站，搭火车进波士顿，再走一英里吃早饭，又走一英里到学校。多少个夜晚，哗哗作响的窗扇把他惊醒，他听着尖利的风在缝隙里呜咽、呻吟、嘶喊、呼啸，一阵阵塞壬式的起落，从悲哀的深处直上疯狂的绝顶，从倍强到几近无声——乡野的这支古怪乐队，就这样再把他哄入睡眠。多少个清晨，在漆黑里，他点亮自己的小灯，敲开水罐口上那层冰皮，在北极般的水里洗漱，穿上衣裳——那是照外祖母教的，齐齐整整叠搭在椅背上——连长袜也细心翻好，务求整饬——然后趁屋子还黑着、还静着便出门，去闯自己的路：也许要闯过新落的雪，平地没膝，还不见一行辙迹；毛线帽压得低低的，毛线手套戴得严严的，书本用一条皮带捆紧，妥帖地夹在厚呢短大衣（pea-jacket）的臂弯底下；车站的昏灯愈照愈近，远处一声双响的汽笛预告着来车；不多时，炫目的车头灯轰鸣着闯进视野，而他站在月台上，候着。

===Original===
Yet this was not heroism, but routine. It was an accepted part of the day’s doings, accepted without a murmur of other thought in days long since gone by.

===Chinese===
然而这并非英雄主义，而是日常。这是当日事务中被接受下来的一部分，在早已逝去的岁月里受下来时，连一声嘀咕、一个旁的念头都没有。

===Original===
Thus Louis worked, in gluttonous introspection, as one with a fixed idea, an unalterable purpose, whose goal lay beyond the rim of his horizon, and beyond the narrow confines of the casual and sterile thought of the day. Hence Louis was bound to be graduated with honors, as he was, the following June of 1870. There and then he received in pride, as a scholar, his first and last diploma. Never thereafter did he regard life with the gravity, the seriousness and the futility of a cloistered monk. That summer, he spent part of vacation time on the farm, and part of it within the primeval forest of Brown’s Track in the northern part of the State of New York. On his return to Boston in September, he passed the examinations, and at the age of fourteen entered the English High School, in Bedford Street﻿—there to expand.

===Chinese===
路易斯就这样用功着，在一种饕餮般的内省里，像怀着一个执念、抱定一个不可更易之目的的人，其目标远在地平线的边缘之外，远在当时那些浮泛而贫瘠的思想的狭仄之外。因此路易斯注定要以优等成绩毕业——事实上也果然如此——在 1870 年 6 月。就在那里、就在那时，他以一个学人的骄傲，领受了他生平第一张、也是最后一张文凭。自那以后，他再不曾以隐修教士那般的庄重、严肃与徒然来看待人生。那年夏天，他把假期的一部分消磨在农庄上，另一部分消磨在纽约州北部布朗氏林地（Brown's Track）的原始森林里。九月回到波士顿，他通过了各场考试，十四岁上进了贝德福德街（Bedford Street）的英语中学——去那里舒展壮大。
